- **Config / env:** Prerequisites: "Nix with flakes enabled — Determinate Nix recommended"; "API keys for the services you want to use (at minimum: an OpenRouter or Anthropic key)".
- **Edge cases / guards:** Tier 2 warning at the top of the page: "The flake and NixOS module documented here are maintained on a best-effort basis only. Commits to `main` may break these packages at any point in time."
- **Rebuild notes:** Publish multiple closure-size variants of the same package so users can trade size for capability.

### NixOS module (`services.hermes-agent`) — native mode  `id: docs-rest.nixos-module`
- **Surface:** Docs / Platform:Nix
- **Where:** Nix setup → **"## NixOS Module"** (flake output `nixosModules.default`).
- **What it does:** A full NixOS service module that "declaratively manages user creation, directories, config generation, secrets, documents, and service lifecycle".
- **How it works:** Flake input block verbatim (`inputs.hermes-agent.url = "github:NousResearch/hermes-agent"`, module `hermes-agent.nixosModules.default`). Minimal configuration verbatim:
  ```nix
  services.hermes-agent = {
    enable = true;
    settings.model.default = "anthropic/claude-sonnet-4";
    environmentFiles = [ config.sops.secrets."hermes-env".path ];
    addToSystemPackages = true;
  };
  ```
  "`nixos-rebuild switch` creates the `hermes` user, generates `config.yaml`, wires up secrets, and starts the gateway".
  **"### Choosing a Deployment Mode"** table (Native default vs Container): "How it runs" — "Hardened systemd service on the host" vs "Persistent Ubuntu container with `/nix/store` bind-mounted"; "Security" — "`NoNewPrivileges`, `ProtectSystem=strict`, `PrivateTmp`" vs "Container isolation, runs as unprivileged user inside"; "Agent can self-install packages" — "No — only tools on the Nix-provided PATH" vs "Yes — `apt`, `pip`, `npm` installs persist across restarts"; "Config surface" — "Same" / "Same"; "When to choose" — "Standard deployments, maximum security, reproducibility" vs "Agent needs runtime package installation, mutable environment, experimental tools".
- **Inputs / options:** Verification commands verbatim: `systemctl status hermes-agent`; `journalctl -u hermes-agent -f`; `hermes --version`; `hermes config`.
- **Outputs / side effects:** Creates the `hermes` user/group, `/var/lib/hermes`, `config.yaml`, `.env`, `.managed`, a systemd unit.
- **Config / env:** See `docs-rest.nix-options-reference`. `addToSystemPackages = true` "puts the `hermes` CLI on your system PATH **and** sets `HERMES_HOME` system-wide so the interactive CLI shares state (sessions, skills, cron) with the gateway service. Without it, running `hermes` in your shell creates a separate `~/.hermes/` directory."
- **Edge cases / guards:** **":::warning Secrets are required"** — the `environmentFiles` example assumes sops-nix or agenix; a plain non-world-readable file works as a starting point: `echo "OPENROUTER_API_KEY=sk-or-your-key" | sudo install -m 0600 -o hermes /dev/stdin /var/lib/hermes/env`.
- **Rebuild notes:** Expose the whole runtime config as a single deep-mergeable attrset and generate the on-disk config from it at activation, preserving user-added keys.

### Nix container mode and container-aware CLI routing  `id: docs-rest.nix-container-mode`
- **Surface:** Docs / Platform:Nix
- **Where:** Nix setup → **"### Container-aware CLI"**, **"## Container Architecture"**, and the `container.*` options.
- **What it does:** Runs Hermes inside a persistent Ubuntu container so the agent can install its own packages, and transparently routes every host `hermes` command into that container.
- **How it works:** Verbatim: "When `container.enable = true` and `addToSystemPackages = true`, **every** `hermes` command on the host automatically routes into the managed container… The routing is transparent: `hermes chat`, `hermes sessions list`, `hermes --version`, etc. all exec into the container under the hood; All CLI flags are forwarded as-is; If the container isn't running, the CLI retries briefly (5s with a spinner for interactive use, 10s silently for scripts) then fails with a clear error — no silent fallback; For developers working on the hermes codebase, set `HERMES_DEV=1` to bypass container routing and run the local checkout directly." `container.hostUsers` creates a `~/.hermes` symlink to the service state directory and auto-adds those users to the `hermes` group.
  Container layout, verbatim mounts: `/nix/store` ← host `/nix/store` (`ro`), `/data` ← `/var/lib/hermes` (`rw`), `/home/hermes` ← `${stateDir}/home` (`rw`), plus the container writable layer for `/usr`, `/usr/local`, `/tmp`. State-directory contents named by the doc: `current-package` symlink, `.gc-root`, `.container-identity` (sha256 hash), `.hermes/` (`.env`, `config.yaml`, `.managed`, `.container-mode` "routing metadata: backend, exec_user, etc.", `state.db`, `sessions/`, `memories/`, `mcp-tokens/`), `home/`, `workspace/`. Entrypoint: `/data/current-package/bin/hermes gateway run --replace`.
  Persistence matrix (Event | Container recreated? | `/data` | `/home/hermes` | Writable layer): `systemctl restart hermes-agent` — No / Persists / Persists / Persists; `nixos-rebuild switch` (code change) — "No (symlink updated)" / Persists / Persists / Persists; Host reboot — No / Persists / Persists / Persists; `nix-collect-garbage` — "No (GC root)" / Persists / Persists / Persists; Image change (`container.image`) — **Yes** / Persists / Persists / **Lost**; Volume/options change — **Yes** / Persists / Persists / **Lost**; `environment`/`environmentFiles` change — No / Persists / Persists / Persists. "The hash covers: schema version, image, `extraVolumes`, `extraOptions`, and the entrypoint script."
- **Inputs / options:** `container.enable` (bool, default `false`), `container.backend` (`"docker"` | `"podman"`, default `"docker"`), `container.image` (str, default `"ubuntu:24.04"`), `container.extraVolumes` (listOf str, `host:container:mode`), `container.extraOptions` (listOf str, "Extra args passed to `docker create`"), `container.hostUsers` (listOf str). Podman passwordless-sudo rule example given verbatim via `security.sudo.extraRules`.
- **Outputs / side effects:** Creates/recreates the `hermes-agent` container; writes `.container-identity`; creates a Nix GC root at `${stateDir}/.gc-root`.
- **Config / env:** `HERMES_DEV=1` bypasses routing.
- **Edge cases / guards:** **":::warning Writable layer loss"** — "When the identity hash changes (image upgrade, new volumes, new container options), the container is destroyed and recreated from a fresh pull of `container.image`. Any `apt install`, `pip install`, or `npm install` packages in the writable layer are lost." Container mode auto-enables `virtualisation.docker.enable` via `mkDefault`; Podman users set `container.backend = "podman"` and `virtualisation.docker.enable = false`. Container mode is not supported on Home Manager ("because it needs root and the Docker socket") and cannot be combined with `backend.mode`.
- **Rebuild notes:** Content-hash the container's *identity inputs* (image + mounts + options + entrypoint) and only recreate when that hash changes; bind-mount state so recreation is non-destructive.

### Nix declarative settings (`settings`, `configFile`) and the config merge  `id: docs-rest.nix-settings`
- **Surface:** Docs / Platform:Nix / Config
- **Where:** Nix setup → **"## Configuration"** → "### Declarative Settings", "### Escape Hatch: Bring Your Own Config", "### Customization Cheatsheet".
- **What it does:** Renders an arbitrary Nix attrset as `config.yaml`, deep-merged across module definitions, while preserving user-added keys.
- **How it works:** Verbatim: "The `settings` option accepts an arbitrary attrset that is rendered as `config.yaml`. It supports deep merging across multiple module definitions (via `lib.recursiveUpdate`)… Nix-declared keys always win over keys in an existing `config.yaml` on disk, but **user-added keys that Nix doesn't touch are preserved**. This means if the agent or a manual edit adds keys like `skills.disabled` or `streaming.enabled`, they survive `nixos-rebuild switch`." Discovery: "Run `nix build .#configKeys && cat result` to see every leaf config key extracted from Python's `DEFAULT_CONFIG`. You can paste your existing `config.yaml` into the `settings` attrset — the structure maps 1:1." Escape hatch: `services.hermes-agent.configFile = /etc/hermes/config.yaml;` — "This bypasses `settings` entirely — no merge, no generation. The file is copied as-is to `$HERMES_HOME/config.yaml` on each activation."
- **Inputs / options:** Full example keys shown in the doc: `model.base_url`, `model.default`, `toolsets`, `max_turns`, `terminal.{backend,cwd,timeout}`, `compression.{enabled,threshold,summary_model}`, `memory.{memory_enabled,user_profile_enabled}`, `display.{compact,personality}`, `agent.{max_turns,verbose}`, `plugins.enabled`, `memory.provider`. **"### Customization Cheatsheet"** rows (I want to… | Option | Example), all 16: "Change the LLM model" | `settings.model.default` | `"anthropic/claude-sonnet-4"`; "Use a different provider endpoint" | `settings.model.base_url` | `"https://openrouter.ai/api/v1"`; "Add API keys" | `environmentFiles`; "Give the agent an identity" | `hermesHomeFiles."SOUL.md"` | `"You are a terse ops assistant."`; "Add project context to the workspace" | `documents."AGENTS.md"`; "Run the backend for the desktop app or the dashboard" | `backend.mode` | `"serve"` or `"dashboard"`; "Add MCP tool servers" | `mcpServers.<name>`; "Enable Discord/Telegram/Slack" | `extraDependencyGroups` | `[ "messaging" ]`; "Mount host directories into container" | `container.extraVolumes` | `[ "/data:/data:rw" ]`; "Pass GPU access to container" | `container.extraOptions` | `[ "--gpus" "all" ]`; "Use Podman instead of Docker" | `container.backend` | `"podman"`; "Share state between host CLI and container" | `container.hostUsers` | `[ "sidbin" ]`; "Make extra tools available to the agent" | `extraPackages` | `[ pkgs.pandoc pkgs.imagemagick ]`; "Use a custom base image" | `container.image` | `"ubuntu:24.04"`; "Override the hermes package" | `package`; "Change state directory" | `stateDir` | `"/opt/hermes"`; "Set the agent's working directory" | `workingDirectory` | `"/home/user/projects"`.
- **Outputs / side effects:** Writes/merges `$HERMES_HOME/config.yaml` at activation.
- **Config / env:** All `services.hermes-agent.settings.*` keys map 1:1 to `config.yaml`.
- **Edge cases / guards:** **":::note Model naming"** — OpenRouter-style IDs by default; set `settings.model.base_url` for direct providers and use their native IDs; "When no `base_url` is set, Hermes defaults to OpenRouter."
- **Rebuild notes:** Declarative config must merge *into* the on-disk file (declared keys win, foreign keys survive) rather than overwrite, and expose a "bring your own file" escape hatch.

### Nix secrets management (sops-nix, agenix, `authFile`)  `id: docs-rest.nix-secrets`
- **Surface:** Docs / Platform:Nix / Config
- **Where:** Nix setup → **"## Secrets Management"**.
- **What it does:** Documents the supported ways to get API keys and OAuth credentials into a Nix-managed install without leaking them into the Nix store.
- **How it works:** **":::danger Never put API keys in `settings` or `environment`"** — "Values in Nix expressions end up in `/nix/store`, which is world-readable. Always use `environmentFiles` with a secrets manager." "Both `environment` (non-secret vars) and `environmentFiles` (secret files) are merged into `$HERMES_HOME/.env` at activation time (`nixos-rebuild switch`). Hermes reads this file on every startup, so changes take effect with a `systemctl restart hermes-agent` — no container recreation needed."
  **sops-nix** example verbatim: `sops.defaultSopsFile`, `sops.age.keyFile`, `sops.secrets."hermes-env" = { format = "yaml"; }`, then `services.hermes-agent.environmentFiles = [ config.sops.secrets."hermes-env".path ]`; secret file body example contains `OPENROUTER_API_KEY=`, `TELEGRAM_BOT_TOKEN=`, `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY=`.
  **agenix** example verbatim: `age.secrets.hermes-env.file = ./secrets/hermes-env.age;` then `environmentFiles = [ config.age.secrets.hermes-env.path ]`.
  **"### OAuth / Auth Seeding"** — `authFile = config.sops.secrets."hermes/auth.json".path;` and `authFileForceOverwrite = true;` — "The file is only copied if `auth.json` doesn't already exist (unless `authFileForceOverwrite = true`). Runtime OAuth token refreshes are written to the state directory and preserved across rebuilds."
- **Inputs / options:** `environmentFiles` (listOf str), `environment` (attrsOf str), `authFile` (null or path), `authFileForceOverwrite` (bool).
- **Outputs / side effects:** Merged `$HERMES_HOME/.env`; seeded `auth.json`.
- **Config / env:** As above.
- **Edge cases / guards:** `environment` values are visible in the Nix store; troubleshooting checks are `sudo -u hermes cat /var/lib/hermes/.hermes/.env` (native) and `docker exec hermes-agent cat /data/.hermes/.env` (container).
- **Rebuild notes:** Merge secrets at activation from a manager-owned path; never accept inline secret strings in the declarative surface.

### Nix documents: `documents` vs `hermesHomeFiles`  `id: docs-rest.nix-documents`
- **Surface:** Docs / Platform:Nix
- **Where:** Nix setup → **"## Documents"**.
- **What it does:** Two options for placing files, one into the agent's working directory and one into `HERMES_HOME`.
- **How it works:** Verbatim: "`documents` installs into the **working directory** of the agent, which is `workingDirectory`." "`hermesHomeFiles` installs into **`HERMES_HOME`**. Hermes reads the identity file and the memory files of the agent from that directory. `SOUL.md` and `memories/` work only from there. A `SOUL.md` in `documents` makes a workspace file. Hermes does not load that file as the identity". "Each value is a string or a path. A key in either option can contain subdirectories, and the module makes the parent directories. Each activation installs the files again." "`hermesHomeFiles` needs no `workingDirectory`, because the module owns the `HERMES_HOME` directory. Most users want `hermesHomeFiles`."
- **Inputs / options:** `documents."AGENTS.md" = ./documents/AGENTS.md;`; `documents."notes/oncall.md" = "Page #infra before restarting anything.";`; `hermesHomeFiles."SOUL.md" = "You are a helpful AI assistant.";`; `hermesHomeFiles."memories/USER.md" = ./documents/USER.md;`.
- **Outputs / side effects:** Files copied from the Nix store into the workspace / `HERMES_HOME` on each activation.
- **Config / env:** `workingDirectory` (required for `documents`).
- **Edge cases / guards:** **":::warning documents needs an explicit workingDirectory"** — "The module refuses `documents` until you set `workingDirectory`. The default of that option is different on each module. It is your home directory on Home Manager, and `${stateDir}/workspace` on NixOS. Thus an unset default puts the files in a directory that you did not select. A directory with the same path as the default is a correct selection, and it satisfies the rule."
- **Rebuild notes:** Refuse an ambiguous destination rather than silently defaulting; keep identity/memory files in a module-owned directory.

### Nix `mcpServers` declarative MCP configuration  `id: docs-rest.nix-mcp-servers`
- **Surface:** Docs / Platform:Nix / Config
- **Where:** Nix setup → **"## MCP Servers"**.
- **What it does:** Declaratively configures MCP servers (stdio or HTTP, optionally OAuth, optionally sampling) merged into `settings.mcp_servers`.
- **How it works:** **Stdio** example verbatim: `filesystem = { command = "npx"; args = [ "-y" "@modelcontextprotocol/server-filesystem" "/data/workspace" ]; }`, `github = { command = "npx"; args = [ "-y" "@modelcontextprotocol/server-github" ]; env.GITHUB_PERSONAL_ACCESS_TOKEN = "\${GITHUB_TOKEN}"; }` — "Environment variables in `env` values are resolved from `$HERMES_HOME/.env` at runtime." **HTTP** example: `remote-api = { url = "https://mcp.example.com/v1/mcp"; headers.Authorization = "Bearer \${MCP_REMOTE_API_KEY}"; timeout = 180; }`. **OAuth**: `auth = "oauth"` — "Hermes implements the full PKCE flow — metadata discovery, dynamic client registration, token exchange, and automatic refresh. Tokens are stored in `$HERMES_HOME/mcp-tokens/<server-name>.json` and persist across restarts and rebuilds." **Sampling**: `sampling = { enabled = true; model = "google/gemini-3-flash"; max_tokens_cap = 4096; timeout = 30; max_rpm = 10; }`.
- **Inputs / options:** All submodule fields: `command` (null|str), `args` (listOf str, default `[]`), `env` (attrsOf str, `{}`), `url` (null|str), `headers` (attrsOf str, `{}`), `auth` (null | `"oauth"`), `enabled` (bool, `true`), `timeout` (null|int, "Tool call timeout in seconds (default: 120)"), `connect_timeout` (null|int, "Connection timeout in seconds (default: 60)"), `tools` (null|submodule — "Tool filtering (`include`/`exclude` lists)"), `sampling` (null|submodule).
- **Outputs / side effects:** Merges into `settings.mcp_servers`; OAuth tokens under `$HERMES_HOME/mcp-tokens/`.
- **Config / env:** `mcp_servers.*` (see the MCP config reference).
- **Edge cases / guards:** Headless first-time OAuth: **Option A "Interactive bootstrap"** — `docker exec -it hermes-agent hermes mcp add my-oauth-server --url https://mcp.example.com/mcp --auth oauth` (container; "The container uses `--network=host`, so the OAuth callback listener on `127.0.0.1` is reachable from the host browser") or `sudo -u hermes HERMES_HOME=/var/lib/hermes/.hermes hermes mcp add …` (native). **Option B "Pre-seed tokens"** — complete on a workstation then `scp ~/.hermes/mcp-tokens/my-oauth-server{,.client}.json server:/var/lib/hermes/.hermes/mcp-tokens/`, "Ensure: chown hermes:hermes, chmod 0600".
- **Rebuild notes:** Model MCP servers as a typed submodule with transport-exclusive fields, and keep tokens in a per-server file outside the declarative surface.

### Nix "Managed Mode" CLI guards (`HERMES_MANAGED`, `.managed`)  `id: docs-rest.nix-managed-mode`
- **Surface:** Docs / Platform:Nix / Core
- **Where:** Nix setup → **"## Managed Mode"**.
- **What it does:** Blocks config-mutating CLI commands when the install is declaratively managed, and names the correct rebuild command in the refusal.
- **How it works:** Blocked-command table verbatim (Blocked command | Why): `hermes setup` | "Config is declarative — edit `settings` in your Nix config"; `hermes config edit` | "Config is generated from `settings`"; `hermes config set <key> <value>` | "Config is generated from `settings`"; `hermes gateway install` | "The systemd service is managed by NixOS"; `hermes gateway uninstall` | "The systemd service is managed by NixOS". Detection uses two signals, verbatim: "**The `HERMES_MANAGED` environment variable.** The service sets it, and the gateway process reads it." and "**The `.managed` marker file** in `HERMES_HOME`. The activation script writes it, and an interactive shell reads it. Thus the CLI also blocks a command such as `docker exec -it hermes-agent hermes config set ...`." "Both signals hold the name of the system that manages the install. Thus the refusal names the correct rebuild command. The NixOS module gives `sudo nixos-rebuild switch`. The Home Manager module gives `home-manager switch`."
- **Inputs / options:** n/a (guards).
- **Outputs / side effects:** Error message + non-zero exit; error string quoted in troubleshooting: **"Cannot save configuration: managed by NixOS"**.
- **Config / env:** `HERMES_MANAGED`; `$HERMES_HOME/.managed`.
- **Edge cases / guards:** Flake check `managed-guard` verifies `HERMES_MANAGED=true hermes config set ...` prints the NixOS error.
- **Rebuild notes:** Use both an env signal (for supervised processes) and an on-disk marker (for shells that bypass the env), and store the *manager's name* so the refusal can name the right remedy. This is the same mechanism as `docs-rest.managed-scope`.

### Nix Home Manager module (`services.hermes-agent`, `programs.hermes-agent`)  `id: docs-rest.nix-home-manager`
- **Surface:** Docs / Platform:Nix
- **Where:** Nix setup → **"## Home Manager Module"** (flake output `homeManagerModules.default`).
- **What it does:** Runs Hermes as a per-user service on any Home-Manager-supported distribution or macOS, with the same option set as the NixOS module.
- **How it works:** Differences table verbatim (NixOS module | Home Manager module): "Runs as" — "a system user that you declare, with `user`, `group` and `createUser`" vs "you"; "State directory" — "`stateDir` and `/.hermes`" vs "`hermesHome`, set directly. The default is `~/.hermes`."; "Service" — "`systemd.services`" vs "`systemd.user.services` on Linux, `launchd.agents` on macOS"; "CLI on the PATH" — "`addToSystemPackages`, which exports `HERMES_HOME` for the full system" vs "`programs.hermes-agent.enable`, which exports it for your session only"; "Desktop application" — "not supported, because a system service cannot own a user session" vs "`programs.hermes-agent.desktop.enable`"; "Container mode" — "supported" vs "not supported, because it needs root and the Docker socket".
  Minimal config verbatim: `imports = [ hermes-agent.homeManagerModules.default ];` + `services.hermes-agent = { enable = true; gateway.enable = true; settings.model.default = …; environmentFiles = […]; }`. "`home-manager switch` makes `~/.hermes`, writes `config.yaml`, builds `.env` and starts the gateway as a user service."
  `programs.hermes-agent` options (Home Manager only): `enable` (bool, `false`) — "Add the `hermes` CLI to `home.packages`, and export `HERMES_HOME` for your shells"; `package` (default `services.hermes-agent.package`) — "The default applies `extraPythonPackages` and `extraDependencyGroups` from the services, so both are one build."; `desktop.enable` (bool, `false`) — "Add the Hermes Desktop application, with a launcher entry on Linux"; `desktop.package` (default `package.hermesDesktop`).
- **Inputs / options:** `hermesHome` (str, default `"${config.home.homeDirectory}/.hermes"`), `gateway.enable` (bool, `false`), plus every shared option. `backend.sessionTokenFile` is used so "the application connects to the backend of the service instead of starting one of its own. Both sides read the file at start time, so the token enters no Nix store path. Without the option, each side runs its own backend."
- **Outputs / side effects:** `~/.hermes/` layout listed verbatim in the doc: `SOUL.md`, `config.yaml`, `.managed`, `.env`, `auth.json`, `memories/  sessions/  skills/  cron/  logs/  plugins/`; plus `~/AGENTS.md` from `documents` (default `workingDirectory` is the user's home).
- **Config / env:** `HERMES_HOME` exported via `home.sessionVariables` (shell-only) and separately baked into the desktop launcher.
- **Edge cases / guards:** **":::warning Enable linger, or the service stops at logout"** — "Without linger, systemd stops the user manager when your last session ends, and the gateway stops with it. Home Manager cannot set linger, because linger is a property of the account": `users.users.your-username.linger = true;` (NixOS) or `sudo loginctl enable-linger your-username` (elsewhere); "macOS has no equivalent option. A `launchd` agent with `RunAtLoad` starts at login and continues to run." Launcher-vs-shell trap: "A desktop menu reads no shell profile, so the value that `programs.hermes-agent.enable` exports with `home.sessionVariables` reaches an interactive shell only. Without the value in the launcher, the application opens `~/.hermes` while the services use `hermesHome`, and you see no sessions and no keys." Removed option: "`services.hermes-agent.installPackage` was removed by this split. A configuration that still sets it gets an error that names the replacement." Verification: `systemctl --user status hermes-agent` / `journalctl --user -u hermes-agent -f` (Linux); `launchctl list | grep hermes` / `tail -f ~/Library/Logs/hermes-agent.log` (macOS).
- **Rebuild notes:** Split "install the app for me" (`programs.*`) from "run the daemon" (`services.*`) and have the former default to the latter's package so there is exactly one runtime.

### Nix `backend.mode` (running `hermes serve` / `hermes dashboard`)  `id: docs-rest.nix-backend-mode`
- **Surface:** Docs / Platform:Nix
- **Where:** Nix setup → **"### Running the Desktop / Dashboard Backend"** and the **"### Backend (`hermes serve` / `hermes dashboard`)"** options table.
- **What it does:** Runs the process Hermes Desktop and the web dashboard connect to, alongside the messaging gateway.
- **How it works:** Verbatim: "`serve` runs without a user interface. It gives the `/api/ws` and `/api/pty` sockets that Hermes Desktop connects to, and it does not build the web application. `dashboard` gives all of that, and also serves the browser admin panel. Both processes use one `HERMES_HOME` with the gateway. Thus the sessions, the skills, the memory and the cron jobs are the same for all of them. `backend.mode` works in the same way on the NixOS module, but not in container mode."
- **Inputs / options:** `backend.mode` (`enum ["none" "serve" "dashboard"]`, default `"none"`), `backend.host` (str, default `"127.0.0.1"`), `backend.port` (port, default `9119`), `backend.extraArgs` (listOf str, `[]`), `backend.sessionTokenFile` (used in the Home Manager example).
- **Outputs / side effects:** Binds the backend on host:port; exposes `/api/ws` and `/api/pty`.
- **Config / env:** As above.
- **Edge cases / guards:** **":::warning Binding to an address other than loopback"** — "The default address is `127.0.0.1`. Each other address starts the authentication gate of the dashboard. The server also refuses each request with a `Host` header that is different from the address that the server bound to. This is a defence against DNS rebinding. Bind to the name or the address that your client uses." Cannot be combined with `container.enable`.
- **Rebuild notes:** Auto-enable authentication and strict `Host` checking the moment the bind address leaves loopback.

### Nix plugin options (`extraPlugins`, `extraPythonPackages`, `extraDependencyGroups`, `extraPackages`, overlay)  `id: docs-rest.nix-plugins`
- **Surface:** Docs / Platform:Nix
- **Where:** Nix setup → **"## Plugins"**.
- **What it does:** Four distinct extension mechanisms for a Nix-built install, plus an overlay for external flakes.
- **How it works:**
  - **"### Directory Plugins (`extraPlugins`)"** — "For plugins that are just a source tree with `plugin.yaml` + `__init__.py` (e.g., [hermes-lcm](https://github.com/stephenschoettler/hermes-lcm))". "Plugins are symlinked into `$HERMES_HOME/plugins/` at activation time. Hermes discovers them via its normal directory scan. Removing a plugin from the list and running `nixos-rebuild switch` removes the symlink." Built with `pkgs.fetchFromGitHub { owner; repo; rev; hash; }`.
  - **"### Entry-Point Plugins (`extraPythonPackages`)"** — "For pip-packaged plugins that register via `[project.entry-points.\"hermes_agent.plugins\"]` (e.g., [rtk-hermes](https://github.com/ogallotti/rtk-hermes))". "The package's `site-packages` is added to PYTHONPATH in the hermes wrapper. `importlib.metadata` discovers the entry point at session start." Example uses `pkgs.python312Packages.buildPythonPackage` with `format = "pyproject"` and `build-system = [ pkgs.python312Packages.setuptools ]`.
  - **"### Optional Dependency Groups (`extraDependencyGroups`)"** — "For optional extras declared in hermes-agent's `pyproject.toml`… This is required for any extra not in the default `[all]` set — on Nix, runtime installation into the read-only store is not possible." "This is resolved by uv alongside core dependencies — no PYTHONPATH patching, no collision risk." All 17 groups verbatim (Group | What it enables): `messaging` "Discord, Telegram, Slack"; `matrix` "Matrix/Element (mautrix with encryption; Linux only)"; `dingtalk` "DingTalk"; `feishu` "Feishu/Lark"; `voice` "Local speech-to-text (faster-whisper)"; `edge-tts` "Edge TTS provider"; `tts-premium` "ElevenLabs TTS"; `anthropic` "Native Anthropic SDK (not needed via OpenRouter)"; `bedrock` "AWS Bedrock (boto3)"; `azure-identity` "Azure Entra ID auth"; `honcho` "Honcho memory provider"; `hindsight` "Hindsight memory provider"; `modal` "Modal terminal backend"; `daytona` "Daytona terminal backend"; `exa` "Exa web search"; `firecrawl` "Firecrawl web search"; `fal` "FAL image generation".
  - **"When to use which"** table verbatim (Need | Option): "Enable a pyproject.toml optional extra" | `extraDependencyGroups`; "Add an external Python plugin not in pyproject.toml" | `extraPythonPackages`; "Add a system binary (pandoc, jq, etc.)" | `extraPackages`; "Add a directory-based plugin source tree" | `extraPlugins`.
  - **"### Using the Overlay"** — `nixpkgs.overlays = [ hermes-agent.overlays.default ];` then `pkgs.hermes-agent.override { extraPythonPackages = [...]; }` / `{ extraDependencyGroups = [ "hindsight" ]; }`.
  - **"### Plugin Configuration"** — plugins must still be enabled: `services.hermes-agent.settings.plugins.enabled = [ "hermes-lcm" "rtk-rewrite" ];`.
- **Inputs / options:** `extraPlugins` (listOf package, "Each must contain `plugin.yaml`"), `extraPythonPackages` (listOf package, "Build with `python312Packages`"), `extraDependencyGroups` (listOf str), `extraPackages` (listOf package, "Added to the hermes user's per-user profile so terminal commands, skills, and cron jobs all see them").
- **Outputs / side effects:** Symlinks under `$HERMES_HOME/plugins/`; PYTHONPATH additions; a rebuilt sealed venv.
- **Config / env:** `settings.plugins.enabled`.
- **Edge cases / guards:** **":::note"** — "A build-time collision check prevents plugin packages from shadowing core hermes dependencies. If a plugin provides a package already in the sealed venv, `nixos-rebuild` fails with a clear error." Troubleshooting: "Tool added via `extraPackages` not found in terminal" → "Requires `nixos-rebuild switch` to update the per-user profile".
- **Rebuild notes:** Distinguish four extension axes (source tree, Python distribution, optional extra, system binary) with one option each, and fail the build on dependency shadowing.

### Nix dev shell, direnv, and flake checks  `id: docs-rest.nix-dev-checks`
- **Surface:** Docs / Platform:Nix
- **Where:** Nix setup → **"## Development"**.
- **What it does:** Provides a reproducible development shell and six build-time verification checks.
- **How it works:** `nix develop` provides, verbatim: "Python 3.12 + uv (deps installed into .venv on first entry)", "Node.js 26, ripgrep, git, openssh, ffmpeg on PATH", "Stamp-file optimization: re-entry is near-instant if deps haven't changed". direnv: "The included `.envrc` activates the dev shell automatically" — `direnv allow` once.
- **Inputs / options:** `nix flake check` ("Run all checks") plus six individual checks, verbatim: `nix build .#checks.x86_64-linux.package-contents`, `.entry-points-sync`, `.cli-commands`, `.managed-guard`, `.bundled-skills`, `.config-roundtrip`. What each verifies (verbatim): `package-contents` "`hermes` and `hermes-agent` binaries exist and `hermes --version` runs"; `entry-points-sync` "Every `[project.scripts]` entry in `pyproject.toml` has a wrapped binary in the Nix package"; `cli-commands` "`hermes --help` exposes `gateway` and `config` subcommands"; `managed-guard` "`HERMES_MANAGED=true hermes config set ...` prints the NixOS error"; `bundled-skills` "Skills directory exists, contains SKILL.md files, `HERMES_BUNDLED_SKILLS` is set in wrapper"; `config-roundtrip` "7 merge scenarios: fresh install, Nix override, user key preservation, mixed merge, MCP additive merge, nested deep merge, idempotency".
- **Outputs / side effects:** `.venv` in the checkout; check derivations.
- **Config / env:** `HERMES_BUNDLED_SKILLS` (set in the Nix wrapper).
- **Edge cases / guards:** n/a
- **Rebuild notes:** Encode packaging invariants (entry points, CLI surface, guard behaviour, config merge) as build-time checks so packaging drift fails CI.

### Nix options reference and directory layouts  `id: docs-rest.nix-options-reference`
- **Surface:** Docs / Platform:Nix
- **Where:** Nix setup → **"## Options Reference"**, **"## Directory Layout"**, **"## Updating"**, **"## Troubleshooting"**.
- **What it does:** The complete `services.hermes-agent` option table plus the on-disk layout for all three modes and the troubleshooting matrix.
- **How it works:** **Core** options (Option | Type | Default | Description): `enable` bool `false` "Enable the hermes-agent service"; `package` package `hermes-agent` "The hermes-agent package to use"; `user` str `"hermes"` "System user"; `group` str `"hermes"` "System group"; `createUser` bool `true` "Auto-create user/group"; `stateDir` str `"/var/lib/hermes"` "State directory (`HERMES_HOME` parent)"; `workingDirectory` str `"${stateDir}/workspace"` "Agent working directory"; `addToSystemPackages` bool `false` "Add `hermes` CLI to system PATH and set `HERMES_HOME` system-wide". **Configuration**: `settings` attrs (deep-merged) `{}`; `configFile` null|path `null`. **Secrets & Environment**: `environmentFiles` listOf str `[]`; `environment` attrsOf str `{}` ("**Visible in Nix store**"); `authFile` null|path `null`; `authFileForceOverwrite` bool `false`. **Documents**: `documents` attrsOf (either str path) `{}`; `hermesHomeFiles` attrsOf (either str path) `{}`. **MCP Servers**: `mcpServers` and its 11 sub-fields (see `docs-rest.nix-mcp-servers`). **Service Behavior**: `extraArgs` listOf str `[]` "Extra args for `hermes gateway`"; `extraPackages`; `extraPlugins`; `extraPythonPackages`; `extraDependencyGroups`; `restart` str `"always"` "The systemd `Restart=` policy. macOS does not use it."; `restartSec` int `5`. **Backend**: `backend.mode`/`host`/`port`/`extraArgs`. **Home Manager only**: `hermesHome`, `gateway.enable`. **`programs.hermes-agent`**: `enable`, `package`, `desktop.enable`, `desktop.package`. **Container (NixOS only)**: `container.enable`/`backend`/`image`/`extraVolumes`/`extraOptions`/`hostUsers`.
  Directory layouts are given for **Native Mode** (`/var/lib/hermes/` with `.hermes/` containing `SOUL.md`, `config.yaml`, `.managed`, `.env`, `auth.json`, `gateway.pid`, `state.db`, `mcp-tokens/`, `sessions/`, `memories/`, `skills/`, `cron/`, `logs/`, plus `home/` and `workspace/`), **Home Manager**, and **Container Mode** (mount table).
  Updating: `cd /etc/nixos && nix flake update hermes-agent`; `sudo nixos-rebuild switch` (NixOS) or `home-manager switch`.
- **Inputs / options:** Troubleshooting commands verbatim: `journalctl -u hermes-agent -f`; `docker logs -f hermes-agent`; `systemctl status hermes-agent`; `docker ps -a --filter name=hermes-agent`; `docker inspect hermes-agent --format='{{.State.Status}}'`; `docker exec -it hermes-agent bash`; `docker exec hermes-agent readlink /data/current-package`; `docker exec hermes-agent cat /data/.container-identity`; force recreation `sudo systemctl stop hermes-agent && docker rm -f hermes-agent && sudo rm /var/lib/hermes/.container-identity && sudo systemctl start hermes-agent`; GC-root check `nix-store --query --roots $(docker exec hermes-agent readlink /data/current-package)`.
- **Outputs / side effects:** n/a
- **Config / env:** All options above.
- **Edge cases / guards:** **"### Common Issues"** table, all eight rows (Symptom | Cause | Fix): "Cannot save configuration: managed by NixOS" | "CLI guards active" | "Edit `configuration.nix` and `nixos-rebuild switch`"; "`No adapter available for discord` (or telegram/slack)" | "Messaging deps missing from the sealed Nix venv" | "Install `#messaging` variant… For NixOS module: `extraDependencyGroups = [ \"messaging\" ]`. Check `journalctl -u hermes-agent` for `FeatureUnavailable` or `requirements not met`"; "Container recreated unexpectedly" | "`extraVolumes`, `extraOptions`, or `image` changed" | "Expected — writable layer resets"; "`hermes --version` shows old version" | "Container not restarted" | "`systemctl restart hermes-agent`"; "Permission denied on `/var/lib/hermes`" | "State dir is `0750 hermes:hermes`" | "Use `docker exec` or `sudo -u hermes`"; "`nix-collect-garbage` removed hermes" | "GC root missing" | "Restart the service (preStart recreates the GC root)"; "`no container with name or ID \"hermes-agent\"` (Podman)" | "Podman rootful container not visible to regular user" | "Add passwordless sudo for podman"; "`unable to find user hermes`" | "Container still starting (entrypoint hasn't created user yet)" | "Wait a few seconds and retry — the CLI retries automatically". "### GC Root Protection": "The `preStart` script creates a GC root at `${stateDir}/.gc-root` pointing to the current hermes package."
- **Rebuild notes:** Publish every module option with type + default + one-line description in a generated table; the layout diagrams double as the storage contract.

## Integrations

### Integrations overview page  `id: docs-rest.integrations-index`
- **Surface:** Docs
- **Where:** Docs → **"Integrations"** (sidebar label **"Overview"**, `website/docs/integrations/index.md`, `sidebar_position: 0`).
- **What it does:** The index of every external system Hermes connects to, grouped into 12 categories.
- **How it works:** Intro verbatim: "Hermes Agent connects to external systems for AI inference, tool servers, IDE workflows, programmatic access, and more." Tip **":::tip Start here"** — "If you only have time to set up one integration, set up [Nous Portal] — a single OAuth login covers 300+ models plus the four Tool Gateway tools (web search, image generation, TTS, and browser automation)." Categories, verbatim headings: "## AI Providers & Routing" (AI Providers, Provider Routing, Fallback Providers); "## Tool Servers (MCP)"; "## Web Search Backends"; "## Browser Automation"; "## Voice & TTS Providers"; "## IDE & Editor Integration"; "## Programmatic Access"; "## Memory & Personalization"; "## Messaging Platforms" (+ "### Quick connect links"); "## Collaboration Workspaces"; "## Home Automation"; "## Plugins"; "## Training & Evaluation".
- **Inputs / options:** Browser automation backends named verbatim: "**Browser Use Cloud** — Managed Chromium with stealth, residential proxies, CAPTCHA solving, and reusable browser profiles"; "**Browserbase** — Alternative cloud browser provider with managed browsers, anti-bot tooling, CAPTCHA solving, and residential proxies"; "**Local Chromium-family CDP** — Connect to your running Chrome, Brave, Chromium, or Edge browser using `/browser connect`"; "**Local Chromium** — Headless local browser via the `agent-browser` CLI". Memory providers named: "Honcho (dialectic reasoning), OpenViking (tiered retrieval), Mem0 (cloud extraction), Hindsight (knowledge graphs), Holographic (local SQLite), RetainDB (hybrid search), ByteRover (CLI-based), and Supermemory". Home Assistant tools named: `ha_list_entities`, `ha_get_state`, `ha_list_services`, `ha_call_service` — "The Home Assistant toolset activates automatically when `HASS_TOKEN` is configured." Messaging platform list (28 links): Telegram, Discord, Slack, WhatsApp, Signal, Matrix, Mattermost, Email, SMS, DingTalk, Feishu/Lark, WeCom, WeCom Callback, Weixin, BlueBubbles, Buzz, QQ Bot, Yuanbao, Home Assistant, Microsoft Teams, Microsoft Teams Meetings, Microsoft Graph Webhook, Google Chat, LINE, ntfy, SimpleX, Open WebUI, Webhooks — introduced as "Hermes runs as a gateway bot on 27+ messaging platforms, all configured through the same `gateway` subsystem".
- **Outputs / side effects:** n/a
- **Config / env:** `HASS_TOKEN`.
- **Edge cases / guards:** n/a
- **Rebuild notes:** Group integrations by *what they replace* (inference, tools, transport, memory, UI) rather than by vendor. Handoff: per-integration detail lives in the docs-features shard and the platform shards.

### Web search backend matrix  `id: docs-rest.integrations-web-search-backends`

- **Where:** Integrations overview → **"## Web Search Backends"**.

- **How it works:** Intro verbatim: "The `web_search` and `web_extract` tools support eight backend providers, configured via `config.yaml` or `hermes tools`". Table rows (Backend | Env Var | Search | Extract | Crawl): **Firecrawl (default)** | `FIRECRAWL_API_KEY` | ✔ | ✔ | ✔; **SearXNG** | `SEARXNG_URL` | ✔ | — | —; **Brave (free tier)** | `BRAVE_SEARCH_API_KEY` | ✔ | — | —; **DuckDuckGo (ddgs)** | _(none)_ | ✔ | — | —; **Exa** | `EXA_API_KEY` | ✔ | ✔ | —; **Parallel** | `PARALLEL_API_KEY` | ✔ | ✔ | —; **xAI** | `XAI_API_KEY` | ✔ | — | —.
- **Inputs / options:** `web.backend` values enumerated in the doc's YAML comment: `firecrawl | searxng | brave-free | ddgs | keenable | exa | parallel | xai`.
- **Outputs / side effects:** Selects which HTTP backend the web tools call.
- **Config / env:** `web.backend`; `FIRECRAWL_API_URL` for self-hosted Firecrawl.
- **Edge cases / guards:** "If `web.backend` is not set, the backend is auto-detected from whichever API key is available."
- **Rebuild notes:** Model search/extract/crawl as three capability bits per backend and auto-select by available credentials, with an explicit override key.

Voice message transcription works across Telegram, Discord, WhatsApp, and other messaging platforms."
