### Nous Portal integration page  `id: docs-rest.nous-portal`
- **Surface:** Docs / Provider
- **Where:** Docs → Integrations → **"Nous Portal"** (`integrations/nous-portal.md`, `sidebar_position: 1`).
- **What it does:** Documents Nous Portal as "Nous Research's unified subscription gateway and **the recommended way to run Hermes Agent**" — one OAuth for 300+ models plus the Tool Gateway.
- **How it works:** Fast path verbatim: `hermes setup --portal` — "That single command runs the Portal OAuth, lets you pick a Nous model, sets Nous as your inference provider in `config.yaml`, and turns on the Tool Gateway." Signup: `https://portal.nousresearch.com/manage-subscription`. Model catalog table (Family | Models), all 14 rows: **Anthropic Claude** "Opus 4.7, Opus 4.6, Sonnet 4.6, Haiku 4.5"; **OpenAI** "GPT-5.5, GPT-5.5 Pro, GPT-5.4 Mini, GPT-5.4 Nano, GPT-5.3 Codex"; **Google Gemini** "Gemini 3 Pro Preview, Gemini 3 Flash Preview, Gemini 3.1 Pro Preview, Gemini 3.1 Flash Lite Preview"; **DeepSeek** "DeepSeek V4 Pro"; **Qwen** "Qwen3.7-Max, Qwen3.6-35B-A3B"; **Kimi / Moonshot** "Kimi K2.6"; **GLM / Zhipu** "GLM-5.1"; **MiniMax** "MiniMax M2.7"; **xAI** "Grok 4.3"; **NVIDIA** "Nemotron-3 Super 120B-A12B"; **Tencent** "Hunyuan 3 Preview"; **Xiaomi** "MiMo V2.5 Pro"; **StepFun** "Step 3.5 Flash"; **Hermes** "Hermes-4-70B, Hermes-4-405B (chat…)"; **+ everything else** "280+ additional models — the full agentic frontier".
- **Inputs / options:** Setup steps for a fresh install, verbatim numbered: "1. Opens your browser to portal.nousresearch.com for OAuth login; 2. Stores the refresh token at `~/.hermes/auth.json`; 3. Lets you pick a Nous model from the curated list (or skip to keep your current one); 4. Sets Nous as your inference provider in `~/.hermes/config.yaml` (when you pick a model); 5. Turns on the Tool Gateway (web, image, TTS, browser routing); 6. Returns you to your terminal ready to `hermes chat`". Existing install: `hermes model` → pick "Nous Portal".
- **Outputs / side effects:** Writes `model.provider: nous`, `model.default`, `model.base_url: https://inference-api.nousresearch.com/v1` in `config.yaml`; refresh token in `~/.hermes/auth.json`.
- **Config / env:** `model.provider`, `model.default`, `model.base_url`.
- **Edge cases / guards:** **":::note"** — "Because routing is per-model and not always through OpenRouter, OpenRouter-specific request extensions (such as `provider` routing preferences, `session_id` sticky routing, or top-level `cache_control`) are not part of the Portal's API contract and may be ignored depending on which backend serves the model." Headless setup points at `/guides/oauth-over-ssh`. Profiles: "the Portal refresh token is automatically shared across all profiles via a shared token store. Sign in once on any profile, and the rest pick it up automatically".
- **Rebuild notes:** A subscription gateway should front both inference and tool backends behind one OAuth, and share the refresh token across profiles from one store.

### Nous Tool Gateway backends (five partners)  `id: docs-rest.portal-tool-gateway-backends`
- **Surface:** Docs / Tool
- **Where:** Nous Portal page → "### The Nous Tool Gateway".
- **What it does:** Names the five tool backends the subscription unlocks and what each replaces.
- **How it works:** All five rows verbatim (Tool | Partner | What it does): **Web search & extract** | Firecrawl | "Agent-grade search and full-page extraction. No Firecrawl API key, no rate limit babysitting."; **Image generation** | FAL | "Nine models under one endpoint: FLUX 2 Klein 9B, FLUX 2 Pro, Z-Image Turbo, Nano Banana Pro (Gemini 3 Pro Image), GPT Image 1.5, GPT Image 2, Ideogram V3, Recraft V4 Pro, Qwen Image."; **Text-to-speech** | OpenAI TTS | "High-quality TTS without a separate OpenAI key. Enables voice mode across messaging platforms."; **Cloud browser automation** | Browser Use | "Headless Chromium sessions for `browser_navigate`, `browser_click`, `browser_type`, `browser_vision`. No Browserbase account needed."; **Cloud terminal sandbox** | Modal | "Serverless terminal sandboxes for code execution (optional add-on)."
- **Inputs / options:** Per-tool selection via `hermes tools` — the doc's worked example: "→ Web search → \"Nous Subscription\"; → Image generation → \"Nous Subscription\"; → Browser → \"Browserbase\" (your existing key); → TTS → \"Nous Subscription\"".
- **Outputs / side effects:** Writes `web.backend: nous`, `image_gen.provider: nous`, `tts.provider: nous`, `browser.cloud_provider: nous`.
- **Config / env:** `web.backend`, `image_gen.provider`, `tts.provider`, `browser.cloud_provider`.
- **Edge cases / guards:** "The Tool Gateway is opt-in per tool, not all-or-nothing. The managed backends show up in `hermes tools` whether or not you're logged into Nous Portal — if you pick \"Nous Subscription\" before authenticating, Hermes runs the Portal login inline (it won't change your inference provider or touch your other tools)." "The runtime always follows the stored selection — direct API keys left in `.env` are ignored while a category is set to `nous`, and picking a direct provider (e.g. `image_gen.provider: fal`) without its key produces a clear error rather than silently rerouting through the gateway. (Older configs used a legacy `use_gateway: true` flag; it is read as equivalent to `nous` but is no longer written.)"
- **Rebuild notes:** One selection key per tool category, never a global boolean; explicit selection must beat credential presence so routing is predictable.

### "A note on Hermes 4" — model-choice guidance  `id: docs-rest.portal-hermes4-note`
- **Surface:** Docs
- **Where:** Nous Portal page → **"## A note on Hermes 4"**.
- **What it does:** Tells users that Nous's own Hermes 4 chat models are available but not recommended inside Hermes Agent, and names four better picks.
- **How it works:** Verbatim: "Hermes 4 is tuned for chat and reasoning, not the rapid-fire tool-calling loop the agent relies on. Use them for research workflows or via the [subscription proxy] from other tooling — but for agent work, pick a frontier agentic model from the catalog instead". Recommended commands verbatim: `/model anthropic/claude-sonnet-4.6` ("best general-purpose agentic model"); `/model openai/gpt-5.5-pro` ("strong reasoning + tool calling"); `/model google/gemini-3-pro-preview` ("huge context window"); `/model deepseek/deepseek-v4-pro` ("cost-effective coder"). "The Portal's own [model info page](https://portal.nousresearch.com/info) carries the same warning".
- **Inputs / options:** The four `/model` commands.
- **Outputs / side effects:** n/a
- **Config / env:** n/a
- **Edge cases / guards:** n/a
- **Rebuild notes:** Publish a per-model "agentic suitability" flag in the catalog and warn at selection time rather than only in prose.

### `hermes portal` command family  `id: docs-rest.portal-cli`
- **Surface:** CLI / Docs
- **Where:** Nous Portal page → "## Using the Portal day-to-day" → "### Inspecting what's wired up".
- **What it does:** Five commands for Portal login, inspection, and subscription management.
- **How it works:** All five verbatim: `hermes portal` "log in to Nous Portal + set it up (one-shot onboarding)"; `hermes portal info` "login status, subscription info, model + gateway routing"; `hermes portal status` "alias for `portal info`"; `hermes portal tools` "detailed Tool Gateway catalog with per-tool routing"; `hermes portal open` "open the subscription management page in your browser". "`hermes portal` (with no subcommand) is the human-readable alias for `hermes auth add nous --type oauth`". Example `hermes portal info` output verbatim:
  ```
    Nous Portal
    ───────────
    Auth:    ✓ logged in
    Portal:  https://portal.nousresearch.com
    Model:   ✓ using Nous as inference provider

    Tool Gateway
    ────────────
    Web search & extract  via Nous Portal
    Image generation      via Nous Portal
    Text-to-speech        via Nous Portal
    Browser automation    via Nous Portal
    Cloud terminal        not configured
  ```
- **Inputs / options:** `portal`, `portal info`, `portal status`, `portal tools`, `portal open`; related `hermes auth add nous`, `hermes model`, `hermes tools`.
- **Outputs / side effects:** OAuth login writes `~/.hermes/auth.json`; `portal open` launches the browser.
- **Config / env:** n/a
- **Edge cases / guards:** Troubleshooting rows: "`hermes portal info` shows \"not logged in\"" → run `hermes portal` or `hermes model`; "Got a \"re-authentication required\" message mid-session" → `hermes auth add nous`; "Want to use a specific provider model that the Portal doesn't expose" → try the OpenRouter-style slug, e.g. `/model anthropic/claude-opus-4.6`; "Bills not appearing on my Portal account" → "Check `hermes portal info` first — if it shows you're using a different provider (`Model: currently openrouter` instead of `using Nous as inference provider`), your local config has drifted."
- **Rebuild notes:** Give every credentialed integration a `<name> info` command that prints auth + routing in one screen; that single view answers most billing/routing support tickets.

### Portal token handling and refresh-token quarantine  `id: docs-rest.portal-token-handling`
- **Surface:** Core / Docs
- **Where:** Nous Portal page → **"## Token handling"**; also the Codex note on `integrations/providers.md`.
- **What it does:** Mints short-lived JWTs per call from the stored refresh token and quarantines an invalidated refresh token so failures don't loop.
- **How it works:** Verbatim: "Hermes mints a short-lived JWT from your stored Portal refresh token on each inference call rather than reusing a long-lived API key. The token lifecycle is fully automatic — refresh, mint, retry on transient 401 — and you never see it. If the Portal invalidates the refresh token (password change, manual revoke, session expiry), the invalid refresh token is **quarantined locally** so Hermes stops replaying it and you don't see a stream of identical 401s. The next call surfaces a clear \"re-authentication required\" message. Run `hermes auth add nous` to log in again; the quarantine clears on the next successful login." The same quarantine mechanism is documented for OpenAI Codex: "If a token refresh fails with a terminal error (HTTP 4xx, `invalid_grant`, revoked grant, etc.), Hermes marks the refresh token as dead and stops replaying it… Run `hermes auth add openai-codex` (or `hermes model` → **ChatGPT or Codex Subscription**)".
- **Inputs / options:** `hermes auth add nous`, `hermes auth add openai-codex`.
- **Outputs / side effects:** `~/.hermes/auth.json`; a local quarantine marker.
- **Config / env:** Portal requests carry `client=hermes-client-v<version>` (e.g. `client=hermes-client-v0.13.0`) "on all Portal pathways — main chat loop, auxiliary calls, compression summarizer, web extraction".
- **Edge cases / guards:** Only the refresh token is on disk; JWTs are per-request and never persisted.
- **Rebuild notes:** Quarantine a credential after a *terminal* auth error (not a transient 401) and clear the quarantine only on a successful re-login; tag every request with a client version for server-side telemetry.

### Buzz integration — three connection paths  `id: docs-rest.buzz-integration`
- **Surface:** Docs / Platform:Buzz
- **Where:** Docs → Integrations → **"Buzz Integration"** (`integrations/buzz.md`, `sidebar_position: 4`).
- **What it does:** Compares the three ways to connect Hermes to Buzz — "Block's open-source, self-hostable workspace where humans and AI agents share the same channels… built on Nostr: every message is a signed event on a relay you own, and every participant — human or agent — is a keypair."
- **How it works:** Comparison table verbatim (① Desktop runtime | ② Relay bridge (ACP) | ③ Native gateway platform):
  - **"What it is"** — "Buzz Desktop spawns Hermes locally as a managed harness" | "Buzz's `buzz-acp` bridges a channel to `hermes acp` over stdio" | "Hermes' gateway joins Buzz as a first-class messaging platform".
  - **"Hermes runs"** — "On your desktop, launched by Buzz" | "On a server, launched by `buzz-acp`" | "In your own gateway, alongside Telegram/Discord/etc."
  - **"Best for"** — "Trying Hermes inside Buzz Desktop with zero config" | "A hosted agent identity when Buzz owns the transport" | "Full Hermes: memory, skills, approvals, cron, sessions".
  - **"Inbound"** — "ACP stdio" | "ACP stdio (via relay WebSocket)" | "NIP-42-authenticated Nostr WebSocket (poll fallback)".
  - **"Setup"** — "Automatic discovery" | "`buzz-acp` env vars" | "`hermes gateway setup` → Buzz".
  ① verbatim: "Buzz Desktop ships Hermes as a preset runtime. With Hermes installed the normal way, open **Settings → Runtimes** and Hermes appears automatically — discovery resolves the `hermes-acp` launcher on your login-shell PATH, which the installer writes to `~/.local/bin` (and `hermes update` self-heals on older installs)." ② topology verbatim: `Buzz relay <-- WebSocket --> buzz-acp <-- ACP over stdio --> Hermes Agent`; "The spawned Hermes uses the same config, credentials, memory, and skills as `hermes` on that host." ③ verbatim: "The bundled `buzz` platform plugin makes Buzz a normal Hermes messaging platform — channels, DMs, mention gating, threaded replies, reactions, images, and cron delivery (`deliver=buzz`), with Hermes' own approvals, memory, and session management intact. Inbound arrives over a persistent NIP-42-authenticated Nostr WebSocket (dependency-free BIP-340 signing) with automatic fallback to CLI polling; outbound goes through the `buzz` CLI."
- **Inputs / options:** `hermes gateway setup` → pick Buzz; env var named for the relay bridge: `BUZZ_ACP_RELAY_OBSERVER` ("owner-only telemetry"); cron delivery target `deliver=buzz`.
- **Outputs / side effects:** Joins channels/DMs on the configured relay under a Nostr keypair.
- **Config / env:** `BUZZ_ACP_RELAY_OBSERVER`; full reference at `/user-guide/messaging/buzz`.
- **Edge cases / guards:** "Buzz auto-approves tool permissions — keep agents owner-only". "①/② and ③ use different identities and transports; run ③ with its own dedicated Nostr keypair. The adapter takes a scoped lock on the relay+pubkey pair, so two Hermes profiles cannot accidentally drive one Buzz identity." Decision guidance verbatim: "Just exploring, Buzz Desktop user → ① works out of the box"; "Running a community relay and want an agent identity managed by Buzz → ②"; "You already run Hermes as your agent and want Buzz as another channel → ③. This is the deepest integration and the one that keeps every Hermes feature." Credits list: "@SHL0MS (PATH launcher + Desktop security audit), @NYTEMODEONLY (relay-bridge docs), @rob-coco (platform adapter), @ScaleLeanChris (Nostr WebSocket transport + NIP-42/BIP-340 signing), and @jethac (multi-agent verification)".
- **Rebuild notes:** When a partner ecosystem offers several embedding points, document all of them with an identity/transport comparison and take a scoped lock so two instances cannot share one identity.

### AI Providers page (`integrations/providers.md`)  `id: docs-rest.integrations-providers`
- **Surface:** Docs / Provider
- **Where:** Docs → Integrations → sidebar **"AI Providers"**, page title **"LLM and Model Providers"** (`integrations/providers.md`, `sidebar_position: 1`).
- **What it does:** The authoritative provider setup reference: the inference-provider table (48 rows), per-provider sections, custom/self-hosted endpoints, routing, and fallback.
- **How it works:** Intro verbatim: "This page covers setting up inference providers for Hermes Agent — from cloud APIs like OpenRouter and Anthropic, to self-hosted endpoints like Ollama and vLLM, to advanced routing and fallback configurations. You need at least one provider configured to use Hermes." The **"## Inference Providers"** table lists each provider with its internal provider id and aliases, e.g. "Ramp Router" (`router`; aliases `ramp-router`, `ramp`, `router.com`), "Fireworks AI" (`fireworks`; aliases `fireworks-ai`, `fw`), "Kimi / Moonshot (China)" (`kimi-coding-cn`; aliases `kimi-cn`, `moonshot-cn`), "Arcee AI" (`arcee`; aliases `arcee-ai`, `arceeai`), "GMI Cloud" (`gmi`; aliases `gmi-cloud`, `gmicloud`), "Nebius Token Factory" (`nebius-token-factory`; aliases `nebius`, `nebius-tf`, `tokenfactory`), "Actual Computer" (`actual`; aliases `actual-computer`, `actualcomputer`, `aci`), "Xiaomi MiMo" (`xiaomi`; aliases `mimo`, `xiaomi-mimo`), "Tencent TokenHub" (`tencent-tokenhub`; aliases `tencent`, `tokenhub`, `tencentmaas`), "Tencent TokenPlan" (`tencent-tokenplan`; aliases `tokenplan`, `tencent-lkeap`), "CommandCode" (`commandcode`, alias `commandcode-chat`; Claude via `commandcode-anthropic`, alias `commandcode-claude`), "OpenCode Free" (`opencode-free`, aliases `free`, `opencode_free`), "Hugging Face" (`huggingface`, alias `hf`), "Alibaba Cloud (Coding Plan)" (`alibaba-coding-plan`, alias `alibaba_coding`; CN `alibaba-coding-plan-cn`), "Alibaba Cloud (Token Plan)" (`alibaba-token-plan`; CN `alibaba-token-plan-cn`).
- **Inputs / options:** Section headings, all: "### Nous Portal"; "### Two Commands for Model Management"; "### Subscription plans: what your plan pays for"; "### Anthropic (Native)"; "### GitHub Copilot"; "### First-Class API-Key Providers"; "### xAI (Grok) — Responses API + Prompt Caching"; "### NovitaAI"; "### Ollama Cloud — Managed Ollama Models, OAuth + API Key"; "### AWS Bedrock"; "### Google Vertex AI"; "### Qwen Portal (OAuth)"; "### Alibaba Cloud (Coding Plan)"; "### MiniMax (OAuth)"; "### NVIDIA NIM"; "### GMI Cloud"; "### Actual Computer"; "### StepFun"; "### Hugging Face Inference Providers"; "## Custom & Self-Hosted LLM Providers"; "## Optional API Keys"; "## OpenRouter Provider Routing"; "## OpenRouter Pareto Code Router"; "## Fallback Providers".
- **Outputs / side effects:** Every documented path writes to `~/.hermes/.env` and/or `~/.hermes/config.yaml`.
- **Config / env:** `model.default` / `model.model` — **":::tip Model key alias"** — "In the `model:` config section, you can use either `default:` or `model:` as the key name for your model ID. Both `model: { default: my-model }` and `model: { model: my-model }` work identically."
- **Edge cases / guards:** **":::warning"** — "Even when using Nous Portal, Codex, or a custom endpoint, some tools (vision, web summarization, MoA) use a separate \"auxiliary\" model. By default (`auxiliary.*.provider: \"auto\"`), Hermes routes these tasks to your **main chat model**".
- **Rebuild notes:** Keep provider id + alias set in one registry and generate the doc table from it. Handoff: exhaustive per-provider config keys, base URLs, and auth flows are the `providers` shard's job.

### `hermes model` vs `/model` — two model commands  `id: docs-rest.providers-two-model-commands`
- **Surface:** Docs / CLI
- **Where:** Providers page → **"### Two Commands for Model Management"** and the **":::warning hermes model vs /model"** admonition under "### Switching Models with `/model`".
- **What it does:** Disambiguates the setup wizard from the in-session switcher.
- **How it works:** Table verbatim (Command | Where to run | What it does): **`hermes model`** | "Your terminal (outside any session)" | "Full setup wizard — add providers, run OAuth, enter API keys, configure endpoints"; **`/model`** | "Inside a Hermes chat session" | "Quick switch between **already-configured** providers and models". Verbatim: "`/model` … cannot add new providers, run OAuth, or prompt for API keys. If you've only configured one provider (e.g. OpenRouter), `/model` will only show models for that provider. **To add a new provider:** Exit your session (`Ctrl+C` or `/quit`), run `hermes model`, set up the new provider, then start a new session."
- **Inputs / options:** `/model custom:qwen-2.5`; `/model custom` ("Auto-detect the model from the endpoint"); `/model openrouter:claude-sonnet-4`; named-provider triple syntax `/model custom:local:qwen-2.5`, `/model custom:work:llama3`.
- **Outputs / side effects:** "When switching providers, Hermes persists the base URL and provider to config so the change survives restarts. When switching away from a custom endpoint to a built-in provider, the stale base URL is automatically cleared."
- **Config / env:** `model.provider`, `model.base_url`.
- **Edge cases / guards:** "`/model custom` (bare, no model name) queries your endpoint's `/models` API and auto-selects the model if exactly one is loaded."
- **Rebuild notes:** Keep credential-acquiring flows out of the in-session command; the in-session switcher should only select among already-authenticated providers, and must clear provider-scoped state (base URL, extra_body) on switch.

### Subscription-plan semantics table  `id: docs-rest.providers-subscription-plans`
- **Surface:** Docs / Provider
- **Where:** Providers page → **"### Subscription plans: what your plan pays for"**.
- **What it does:** States, per consumer subscription, whether Hermes can use it, what it consumes, what it does not, and the common billing surprise.
- **How it works:** Preamble verbatim: "Cells marked *not currently documented* mean exactly that: Hermes docs do not yet specify the behavior. Don't assume — check your provider's billing dashboard, and treat these as open questions." All five rows (Plan / path | Can Hermes use it? | What gets consumed | What does NOT get consumed | Common surprise):
  1. **Anthropic — Claude Max + OAuth** | "✅ Yes — `hermes model` → Anthropic OAuth. Requires Max **and** purchased extra usage credits" | "The **extra/overage credits** you've added on top of the Max plan" | "The **base Max plan allowance**" | "All Hermes usage bills as \"extra usage\" even while your included Max allowance sits untouched".
  2. **Anthropic — Claude Pro** | "❌ No — Pro subscribers cannot use the OAuth path" | "Nothing (path unavailable)" | "Your Pro subscription" | "Pro looks like it should work; it doesn't. Use an `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` instead".
  3. **OpenAI Codex — ChatGPT plan OAuth** | "✅ Yes — `hermes model` → **ChatGPT or Codex Subscription**" | "*Not currently documented*" | "*Not currently documented*" | "Docs cover auth and token refresh only; plan-quota semantics are not yet documented".
  4. **xAI — SuperGrok / X Premium+ OAuth** | "✅ Yes — browser OAuth, no API key needed" | "Your **subscription quota** … \"uses your subscription quota instead of API spend\"" | "`XAI_API_KEY` / pay-per-token API spend, when OAuth credentials are configured and preferred" | "`HTTP 403` after a successful login — xAI has restricted OAuth API access to specific SuperGrok tiers despite an active in-app subscription".
  5. **Google — Gemini consumer plan (Google AI Pro / Ultra)** | "❌ No documented path — the `gemini` provider is API-key only (`GOOGLE_API_KEY` / `GEMINI_API_KEY`); Vertex AI uses GCP billing" | "Your **API key's quota**" | "*Not currently documented*" | "Free-tier keys can be exhausted after a handful of agent turns, because Hermes may make several model calls per user turn".
- **Inputs / options:** n/a
- **Outputs / side effects:** n/a
- **Config / env:** `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`, `XAI_API_KEY`, `GOOGLE_API_KEY`/`GEMINI_API_KEY`.
- **Edge cases / guards:** "the same bearer token is reused by direct-to-xAI tools (TTS, image gen, video gen, transcription, X Search)".
- **Rebuild notes:** Documenting *what a subscription does not cover* prevents more support load than documenting what it does; mark unknowns explicitly rather than omitting them.

### Custom / self-hosted endpoint general setup  `id: docs-rest.providers-custom-endpoint`
- **Surface:** Docs / Config / Provider
- **Where:** Providers page → **"## Custom & Self-Hosted LLM Providers"** → "### General Setup".
- **What it does:** Points Hermes at any OpenAI-compatible endpoint, three ways.
- **How it works:** Verbatim: "Hermes Agent works with **any OpenAI-compatible API endpoint**. If a server implements `/v1/chat/completions`, you can point Hermes at it." Interactive: `hermes model` → "Custom endpoint (self-hosted / VLLM / etc.)" → "Enter: API base URL, API key, Model name". Manual YAML:
  ```yaml
  model:
    default: your-model-name
    provider: custom
    base_url: http://localhost:8000/v1
    api_key: your-key-or-leave-empty-for-local
  ```
- **Inputs / options:** `model.default`, `model.provider: custom`, `model.base_url`, `model.api_key`.
- **Outputs / side effects:** "Both approaches persist to `config.yaml`, which is the source of truth for model, provider, and base URL."
- **Config / env:** `OPENAI_BASE_URL` (only for `openai-api`), `CUSTOM_BASE_URL` (bare `provider: custom`).
- **Edge cases / guards:** **":::warning Legacy env vars"** — "`LLM_MODEL` in `.env` is **removed** — `config.yaml` is the single source of truth for model and endpoint configuration. `OPENAI_BASE_URL` is still honored, but **only** for the `openai-api` provider… If you have stale entries in your `.env`, they are automatically cleared on the next `hermes setup` or config migration."
- **Rebuild notes:** Make the config file the single source of truth for routing and actively clear superseded env keys during migration.

### Ollama local setup and the context-length trap  `id: docs-rest.providers-ollama`
- **Surface:** Docs / Provider
- **Where:** Providers page → **"### Ollama — Local Models, Zero Config"**.
- **What it does:** Documents pointing Hermes at a local Ollama server and the three ways to raise its context window above Hermes' 64K floor.
- **How it works:** Setup verbatim: `ollama pull qwen2.5-coder:32b`; `ollama serve` ("Starts on port 11434"); `hermes model` → "Custom endpoint (self-hosted / VLLM / etc.)" → URL `http://localhost:11434/v1` → skip API key → model name. YAML equivalent sets `model.default`, `model.provider: custom`, `model.base_url: http://localhost:11434/v1`, `model.context_length: 64000`.
  **":::caution Ollama defaults to very low context lengths"** table (Available VRAM | Default context): "Less than 24 GB" | **4,096 tokens**; "24–48 GB" | 32,768 tokens; "48+ GB" | 256,000 tokens.
- **Inputs / options:** Three fixes verbatim — Option 1 `OLLAMA_CONTEXT_LENGTH=64000 ollama serve` ("recommended"); Option 2 `sudo systemctl edit ollama.service` + `Environment="OLLAMA_CONTEXT_LENGTH=64000"` + `sudo systemctl daemon-reload && sudo systemctl restart ollama`; Option 3 Modelfile `echo -e "FROM qwen2.5-coder:32b\nPARAMETER num_ctx 64000" > Modelfile` then `ollama create qwen2.5-coder-64k -f Modelfile`. Verify with `ollama ps` ("Look at the CONTEXT column"). Also `ollama list`, `ollama pull <model>`.
- **Outputs / side effects:** n/a
- **Config / env:** `OLLAMA_CONTEXT_LENGTH`, `model.context_length`, `OLLAMA_HOST` (WSL2 NAT).
- **Edge cases / guards:** "**You cannot set context length through the OpenAI-compatible API** (`/v1/chat/completions`). It must be configured server-side or via a Modelfile. This is the #1 source of confusion when integrating Ollama with tools like Hermes."
- **Rebuild notes:** Detect the *served* context window from `/v1/models` and refuse with a precise remedy naming the server's own knob, rather than a generic "context too small".

### vLLM setup (tool-call parsers, context, reasoning parsers)  `id: docs-rest.providers-vllm`
- **Surface:** Docs / Provider
- **Where:** Providers page → **"### vLLM — High-Performance GPU Inference"**.
- **What it does:** Documents serving a model with vLLM for Hermes, including the flags without which tool calling silently fails.
- **How it works:** Launch command verbatim: `vllm serve meta-llama/Llama-3.1-70B-Instruct --port 8000 --max-model-len 65536 --tensor-parallel-size 2 --enable-auto-tool-choice --tool-call-parser hermes`. Hermes side: `hermes model` → custom endpoint → `http://localhost:8000/v1`.
- **Inputs / options:** Tool-calling flags table verbatim (Flag | Purpose): `--enable-auto-tool-choice` "Required for `tool_choice: \"auto\"` (the default in Hermes)"; `--tool-call-parser <name>` "Parser for the model's tool call format". Supported parsers verbatim: `hermes` (Qwen 2.5, Hermes 2/3), `llama3_json` (Llama 3.x), `mistral`, `deepseek_v3`, `deepseek_v31`, `xlam`, `pythonic`. Context flags: `--max-model-len`, `--max-model-len auto`, `--gpu-memory-utilization 0.95` ("default 0.9"). Size shorthand: "`--max-model-len 64k` (lowercase k = 1000, uppercase K = 1024)".
- **Outputs / side effects:** n/a
- **Config / env:** n/a
- **Edge cases / guards:** "Without these flags, tool calls won't work — the model will output tool calls as text." **Qwen reasoning parsers** verbatim: "Hermes preserves structured reasoning metadata such as `reasoning`, `reasoning_content`, and streamed reasoning deltas when OpenAI-compatible servers return them. That metadata is treated as reasoning/thinking trace data, not as a replacement for the assistant's visible answer… If `--reasoning-parser qwen3` leaves `content` empty in your deployment, either disable that parser or pass a server-supported request option such as `chat_template_kwargs.enable_thinking: false` through `extra_body`."
- **Rebuild notes:** Treat empty `content` with non-empty reasoning as a server misconfiguration and say so explicitly at runtime.

### SGLang setup  `id: docs-rest.providers-sglang`
- **Surface:** Docs / Provider
- **Where:** Providers page → **"### SGLang — Fast Serving with RadixAttention"**.
- **What it does:** Documents SGLang as a vLLM alternative "with RadixAttention for KV cache reuse. Best for: multi-turn conversations (prefix caching), constrained decoding, structured output."
- **How it works:** Launch verbatim: `pip install "sglang[all]"`; `python -m sglang.launch_server --model meta-llama/Llama-3.1-70B-Instruct --port 30000 --context-length 65536 --tp 2 --tool-call-parser qwen`. Hermes side: custom endpoint `http://localhost:30000/v1`.
- **Inputs / options:** `--context-length`; `SGLANG_ALLOW_OVERWRITE_LONGER_CONTEXT_LEN=1` ("If you need to exceed the model's declared maximum"); `--tool-call-parser` values verbatim: `qwen` (Qwen 2.5), `llama3`, `llama4`, `deepseekv3`, `mistral`, `glm`; `--default-max-tokens`; `--host 0.0.0.0`.
- **Outputs / side effects:** n/a
- **Config / env:** `SGLANG_ALLOW_OVERWRITE_LONGER_CONTEXT_LEN`.
- **Edge cases / guards:** **":::caution SGLang defaults to 128 max output tokens"** — "If responses seem truncated, add `max_tokens` to your requests or set `--default-max-tokens` on the server. SGLang's default is only 128 tokens per response if not specified in the request." "Without this flag, tool calls come back as plain text."
- **Rebuild notes:** Send an explicit `max_tokens` rather than relying on server defaults, which vary wildly across OpenAI-compatible servers.

### llama.cpp / llama-server setup  `id: docs-rest.providers-llamacpp`
- **Surface:** Docs / Provider
- **Where:** Providers page → **"### llama.cpp / llama-server — CPU & Metal Inference"**.
- **What it does:** Documents running quantized GGUF models on CPU/Metal/consumer GPUs behind an OpenAI-compatible server.
- **How it works:** Build/launch verbatim: `cmake -B build && cmake --build build --config Release`; `./build/bin/llama-server --jinja -fa -c 64000 -ngl 99 -m models/qwen2.5-coder-32b-instruct-Q4_K_M.gguf --port 8080 --host 0.0.0.0`. Hermes side: custom endpoint `http://localhost:8080/v1`, "Enter model name — or leave blank to auto-detect if only one model is loaded".
- **Inputs / options:** `--jinja`, `-fa`, `-c <ctx>`, `-ngl`, `-m`, `--port`, `--host`, `-np` (parallel slots).
- **Outputs / side effects:** n/a
- **Config / env:** n/a
- **Edge cases / guards:** Context verbatim: "Recent builds default to `0` which reads the model's training context from the GGUF metadata. For models with 128k+ training context, this can OOM trying to allocate the full KV cache. Set `-c` explicitly to at least 64,000 tokens for Hermes. If using parallel slots (`-np`), the total context is divided among slots — with `-c 64000 -np 4`, each slot only gets 16k, which is below Hermes' minimum per active session." **":::caution `--jinja` is required for tool calling"** — "Without `--jinja`, llama-server ignores the `tools` parameter entirely… you'll see raw JSON like `{\"name\": \"web_search\", ...}` printed as a message instead of an actual search." Native tool-calling models named: "Llama 3.x, Qwen 2.5 (including Coder), Hermes 2/3, Mistral, DeepSeek, Functionary". Verification: "checking `http://localhost:8080/props` — the `chat_template` field should be present". Tip: "Q4_K_M quantization offers the best balance of quality vs. memory usage."
- **Rebuild notes:** Probe `/props` at connect time and warn when the server lacks a chat template, before the first tool call fails silently.

### LM Studio setup and load modes  `id: docs-rest.providers-lmstudio`
- **Surface:** Docs / Provider / Config
- **Where:** Providers page → **"### LM Studio — Desktop App with Local Models"**.
- **What it does:** Documents the first-class `lmstudio` provider, model discovery, context handling, and the two load modes.
- **How it works:** Server start verbatim: LM Studio app "Developer tab → Start Server", or CLI `lms server start` ("Starts on port 1234") and `lms load qwen2.5-coder --context-length 64000`. Hermes side: `hermes model` → "LM Studio" → "Press Enter to use http://localhost:1234/v1" → "Pick one of the discovered models" → "If LM Studio server auth is enabled, enter LM_API_KEY when prompted". Context behaviour verbatim: "Hermes preserves the context of an already-loaded LM Studio instance. For an unloaded model in the default explicit mode, Hermes omits `context_length` unless you configured one in Hermes, so LM Studio can apply its own model setting. Hermes then uses only the context length LM Studio reports after loading."
- **Inputs / options:** Changing context in the GUI, verbatim steps: "1. Click the gear icon next to the model picker; 2. Set \"Context Length\" to at least 64000 for a smooth experience; 3. Reload the model for the change to take effect; 4. If your machine cannot fit 64000, consider using a smaller model with larger context lengths." CLI equivalents: `lms load model-name --context-length 64000`; `lms load model-name --context-length 64000 --estimate-only`; persistent defaults via "My Models tab → gear icon on the model → set context size". Load modes: `hermes config set model.lmstudio_load_mode jit` (let LM Studio's "Just-In-Time loading / Auto-Evict" manage loading) and `hermes config set model.lmstudio_load_mode explicit` (default preload).
- **Outputs / side effects:** Writes `model.lmstudio_load_mode`.
- **Config / env:** `model.lmstudio_load_mode` (`explicit` default | `jit`); `LM_API_KEY`; `LM_BASE_URL`.
- **Edge cases / guards:** "**Tool calling:** Supported since LM Studio 0.3.6. Models with native tool-calling training (Qwen 2.5, Llama 3.x, Mistral, Hermes) are auto-detected and shown with a tool badge. Other models use a generic fallback that may be less reliable." WSL2: "Enable **\"Serve on Network\"** in the Developer tab → Server settings".
- **Rebuild notes:** Where a local runtime manages its own model lifecycle, offer an explicit "let it manage" mode rather than always preloading.

### WSL2 networking for Windows-hosted model servers  `id: docs-rest.providers-wsl2-networking`
- **Surface:** Docs / Platform:Windows
- **Where:** Providers page → **"### WSL2 Networking (Windows Users)"**.
- **What it does:** Bridges the network gap when Hermes runs in WSL2 and the model server runs on the Windows host.
- **How it works:** Problem statement verbatim: "WSL2 uses a virtual network adapter with its own subnet, so `localhost` inside WSL2 refers to the Linux VM, **not** the Windows host." Tip: "If your model server also runs inside WSL2 (common for vLLM, SGLang, and llama-server), `localhost` works as expected — they share the same network namespace. Skip this section."
  **"#### Option 1: Mirrored Networking Mode (Recommended)"** (Windows 11 22H2+): edit `%USERPROFILE%\.wslconfig` with `[wsl2]` / `networkingMode=mirrored`; `wsl --shutdown`; verify `curl http://localhost:11434/v1/models`. Hyper-V firewall note verbatim: `Set-NetFirewallHyperVVMSetting -Name '{40E0AC32-46A5-438A-A0B2-2B479E8F2E90}' -DefaultInboundAction Allow`.
  **"#### Option 2: Use the Windows Host IP (Windows 10 / older builds)"**: `ip route show | grep -i default | awk '{ print $3 }'` (example output `172.29.192.1`), then `model.base_url: http://172.29.192.1:11434/v1`. Dynamic helper verbatim: `export WSL_HOST=$(ip route show | grep -i default | awk '{ print $3 }')`; mDNS alternative `sudo apt install libnss-mdns` then `curl http://$(hostname).local:11434/v1/models`.
  **"#### Server Bind Address (Required for NAT Mode)"** table (Server | Default bind | How to fix): **Ollama** `127.0.0.1` "Set `OLLAMA_HOST=0.0.0.0` environment variable before starting Ollama (System Settings → Environment Variables on Windows, or edit the Ollama service)"; **LM Studio** `127.0.0.1` "Enable **\"Serve on Network\"** in the Developer tab → Server settings"; **llama-server** `127.0.0.1` "Add `--host 0.0.0.0`"; **vLLM** `0.0.0.0` "Already binds to all interfaces by default"; **SGLang** `127.0.0.1` "Add `--host 0.0.0.0`". Ollama-on-Windows detail: "1. Open **System Properties** → **Environment Variables**; 2. Add a new **System variable**: `OLLAMA_HOST` = `0.0.0.0`; 3. Restart the Ollama service (or reboot)".
  **"#### Windows Firewall"**: `New-NetFirewallRule -DisplayName "Allow WSL2 to Model Server" -Direction Inbound -Action Allow -Protocol TCP -LocalPort 11434`. "Common ports: Ollama `11434`, vLLM `8000`, SGLang `30000`, llama-server `8080`, LM Studio `1234`."
  **"#### Quick Verification"**: `curl http://localhost:11434/v1/models` (mirrored) or `curl http://172.29.192.1:11434/v1/models` (NAT).
- **Inputs / options:** All commands above.
- **Outputs / side effects:** Modifies `.wslconfig`, Windows env vars, and firewall rules.
- **Config / env:** `OLLAMA_HOST`, `model.base_url`, `WSL_HOST` (helper var).
- **Edge cases / guards:** "The host IP can change on WSL2 restart." "Windows Firewall treats WSL2 as a separate network (in both NAT and mirrored mode)."
- **Rebuild notes:** Detect WSL2 and probe the default-gateway IP automatically when `localhost` refuses, then suggest the exact `.wslconfig` change.

### Troubleshooting local model servers  `id: docs-rest.providers-local-troubleshooting`
- **Surface:** Docs
- **Where:** Providers page → **"### Troubleshooting Local Models"** ("These issues affect **all** local inference servers when used with Hermes.").
- **What it does:** Five named failure modes with cause and per-server fix.
- **How it works:** All five, verbatim headings:
  1. **"#### \"Connection refused\" from WSL2 to a Windows-hosted model server"** → see WSL2 Networking.
  2. **"#### Tool calls appear as text instead of executing"** — "The model outputs something like `{\"name\": \"web_search\", \"arguments\": {...}}` as a message instead of actually calling the tool." Fix table (Server | Fix): **llama.cpp** "Add `--jinja`"; **vLLM** "Add `--enable-auto-tool-choice --tool-call-parser hermes`"; **SGLang** "Add `--tool-call-parser qwen` (or appropriate parser)"; **Ollama** "Tool calling is enabled by default — make sure your model supports it (check with `ollama show model-name`)"; **LM Studio** "Update to 0.3.6+ and use a model with native tool support".
  3. **"#### Model seems to forget context or give incoherent responses"** — "Cause: Context window is too small. When the conversation exceeds the context limit, most servers silently drop older messages. Hermes's system prompt + tool schemas alone can use 4k–8k tokens." Diagnosis commands verbatim: startup line "Context limit: X tokens"; `ollama ps` (CONTEXT column); `curl http://localhost:8080/props | jq '.default_generation_settings.n_ctx'`; "vLLM: check `--max-model-len` in startup args".
  4. **"#### \"Context limit: 2048 tokens\" at startup"** — "Hermes auto-detects context length from your server's `/v1/models` endpoint. If the server reports a low value (or doesn't report one at all), Hermes uses the model's declared limit which may be wrong." Fix: set `model.context_length: 64000` explicitly.
  5. **"#### Responses get cut off mid-sentence"** — causes verbatim: "1. **Low output cap (`max_tokens`) on the server** — SGLang defaults to 128 tokens per response. Set `--default-max-tokens` on the server or configure Hermes with `model.max_tokens` in config.yaml. Note: `max_tokens` controls response length only — it is unrelated to how long your conversation history can be (that is `context_length`). 2. **Context exhaustion** — The model filled its context window. Increase `model.context_length` or enable context compression in Hermes."
- **Inputs / options:** As above.
- **Outputs / side effects:** n/a
- **Config / env:** `model.context_length`, `model.max_tokens`.
- **Edge cases / guards:** n/a
- **Rebuild notes:** Print the resolved context limit and its *source* at startup so "Context limit: 2048" is immediately traceable.

### LiteLLM Proxy as a multi-provider gateway  `id: docs-rest.providers-litellm`
- **Surface:** Docs / Provider
- **Where:** Providers page → **"### LiteLLM Proxy — Multi-Provider Gateway"**.
- **What it does:** Documents fronting 100+ providers with LiteLLM and pointing Hermes at it as a custom endpoint.
- **How it works:** Verbatim: "[LiteLLM] is an OpenAI-compatible proxy that unifies 100+ LLM providers behind a single API. Best for: switching between providers without config changes, load balancing, fallback chains, budget controls." Commands: `pip install "litellm[proxy]"`; `litellm --model anthropic/claude-sonnet-4 --port 4000`; `litellm --config litellm_config.yaml --port 4000`. Hermes: `hermes model` → Custom endpoint → `http://localhost:4000/v1`.
- **Inputs / options:** Example `litellm_config.yaml` verbatim with `model_list` entries sharing `model_name: "best"` and `router_settings.routing_strategy: "latency-based-routing"`.
- **Outputs / side effects:** n/a
- **Config / env:** n/a
- **Edge cases / guards:** n/a
- **Rebuild notes:** Any OpenAI-compatible proxy is a valid provider; do not special-case it.

### ClawRouter cost-optimized routing  `id: docs-rest.providers-clawrouter`
- **Surface:** Docs / Provider
- **Where:** Providers page → **"### ClawRouter — Cost-Optimized Routing"**.
- **What it does:** Documents a local routing proxy that auto-selects the cheapest capable model.
- **How it works:** Verbatim: "[ClawRouter](https://github.com/BlockRunAI/ClawRouter) by BlockRunAI is a local routing proxy that auto-selects models based on query complexity. It classifies requests across 14 dimensions and routes to the cheapest model that can handle the task. Payment is via USDC cryptocurrency (no API keys)." Start: `npx @blockrun/clawrouter` ("Starts on port 8402"). Hermes: custom endpoint `http://localhost:8402/v1`, model name `blockrun/auto`.
- **Inputs / options:** Routing profiles table verbatim (Profile | Strategy | Savings): `blockrun/auto` "Balanced quality/cost" "74-100%"; `blockrun/eco` "Cheapest possible" "95-100%"; `blockrun/premium` "Best quality models" "0%"; `blockrun/free` "Free models only" "100%"; `blockrun/agentic` "Optimized for tool use" "varies". Health check: `npx @blockrun/clawrouter doctor`.
- **Outputs / side effects:** Requests route through BlockRun's backend API.
- **Config / env:** n/a
- **Edge cases / guards:** **":::note"** — "ClawRouter requires a USDC-funded wallet on Base or Solana for payment. All requests route through BlockRun's backend API."
- **Rebuild notes:** Expose routing profiles as model IDs so an existing model-switch UI can select them with no new surface.

- **Inputs / options:** YAML example sets `model.default`, `model.provider: custom`, `model.base_url`, `model.api_key`.
- **Outputs / side effects:** n/a

### Context-length detection chain (9 sources)  `id: docs-rest.providers-context-length-detection`
- **Surface:** Docs / Core / Config
- **Where:** Providers page → **"### Context Length Detection"**.
- **What it does:** Documents how Hermes resolves the context window for a model+provider pair, in priority order.
- **How it works:** All nine sources verbatim, in order: "1. **Config override** — `model.context_length` in config.yaml (highest priority); 2. **Custom provider per-model** — `providers.<name>.models.<id>.context_length`; 3. **Persistent cache** — previously discovered values (survives restarts); 4. **Endpoint `/models`** — queries your server's API (local/custom endpoints); 5. **Anthropic `/v1/models`** — queries Anthropic's API for `max_input_tokens` (API-key users only); 6. **OpenRouter API** — live model metadata from OpenRouter; 7. **Nous Portal** — suffix-matches Nous model IDs against OpenRouter metadata; 8. **[models.dev](https://models.dev)** — community-maintained registry with provider-specific context lengths for 3800+ models across 100+ providers; 9. **Fallback defaults** — broad model family patterns (128K default)." "The system is provider-aware — the same model can have different context limits depending on who serves it (e.g., `claude-opus-4.6` is 1M on Anthropic direct but 128K on GitHub Copilot)."
- **Inputs / options:** `model.context_length`; `providers.<name>.models.<id>.context_length`; "`hermes model` will prompt for context length when configuring a custom endpoint. Leave it blank for auto-detection."
- **Outputs / side effects:** Populates the persistent context-length cache.
- **Config / env:** `model.context_length`, `model.max_tokens`.
- **Edge cases / guards:** **":::note Two settings, easy to confuse"** — "**`context_length`** is the **total context window** — the combined budget for input *and* output tokens (e.g. 200,000 for Claude Opus 4.6). Hermes uses this to decide when to compress history and to validate API requests. **`model.max_tokens`** is the **output cap** — the maximum number of tokens the model may generate in a *single response*. It has nothing to do with how long your conversation history can be… Anthropic's native API has since renamed it `max_output_tokens` for clarity." When to set manually: "You're using Ollama with a custom `num_ctx` that's lower than the model's maximum"; "You want to limit context below the model's maximum (e.g., 8k on a 128k model to save VRAM)"; "You're running behind a proxy that doesn't expose `/v1/models`".
- **Rebuild notes:** Resolve context length through an explicit, ordered, cached chain keyed on (provider, model), and always report the winning source.

### Named custom providers (`providers:` dict)  `id: docs-rest.providers-named-custom`
- **Surface:** Docs / Config / Provider
- **Where:** Providers page → **"### Named Custom Providers"**.
- **What it does:** Defines multiple named custom endpoints in `config.yaml` and switches between them mid-session with a triple `/model` syntax.
- **How it works:** Example verbatim:
  ```yaml
  providers:
    local:
      api: http://localhost:8080/v1
      # api_key omitted — Hermes uses "no-key-required" for keyless local servers
    work:
      api: https://gpu-server.internal.corp/v1
      key_env: CORP_API_KEY
      transport: chat_completions
    anthropic-proxy:
      api: https://proxy.example.com/anthropic
      key_env: ANTHROPIC_PROXY_KEY
      transport: anthropic_messages
  ```
  Accepted fields, verbatim: "`api` (the endpoint base URL — `base_url`/`url` are accepted aliases), `name` (optional display name; defaults to the dict key), `key_env` or inline `api_key` or `key_cmd`, `transport` (`chat_completions` / `anthropic_messages` / `codex_responses`), `default_model`, `models`, `context_length`, `discover_models`, `extra_body`, `extra_headers`, `ssl_ca_cert` / `ssl_verify`, and `enabled: false` to hide an entry without deleting it."
- **Inputs / options:** Switching verbatim: `/model custom:local:qwen-2.5`; `/model custom:work:llama3-70b`; `/model custom:anthropic-proxy:claude-sonnet-4`. "You can also select named custom providers from the interactive `hermes model` menu." Native vision knob: `model.supports_vision: true` — "set `model.supports_vision: true` so Hermes routes attached images natively (as `image_url` parts) instead of pre-processing them through `vision_analyze`. Single knob — no need to also set `agent.image_input_mode: native`." Also honored per model: `providers.<name>.models.<id>.supports_vision`; "accepts standard YAML booleans (`true/false/yes/no/on/off/1/0`)".
- **Outputs / side effects:** Writes to `config.yaml` under `providers:`.
- **Config / env:** `providers.*`, `model.supports_vision`, `agent.image_input_mode`.
- **Edge cases / guards:** **":::note Legacy format"** — "Older configs used a top-level `custom_providers:` list instead. It still works — Hermes reads both — and `hermes update` auto-migrates it to the `providers:` dict (config v12). Field names differ slightly in the dict format: legacy `model` is `default_model`, and legacy `api_mode` is `transport`." "The `hermes model` → Custom Endpoint wizard now prompts for the API mode explicitly and persists your answer to `config.yaml` (as `transport` on the provider entry). URL-based auto-detection (e.g. `/anthropic` paths → `anthropic_messages`) still happens as a fallback when the field is left blank."
- **Rebuild notes:** Key custom providers by name in a dict (not a list), keep a legacy reader plus a versioned auto-migration, and make transport explicit with URL heuristics only as fallback.

### `key_cmd` — command-minted provider credentials  `id: docs-rest.providers-key-cmd`
- **Surface:** Config / Docs
- **Where:** Providers page → **"#### Command-minted credentials (`key_cmd`)"**.
- **What it does:** Runs a command that prints a short-lived bearer token and caches it until shortly before expiry, so enterprise SSO/IAM gateways keep working mid-session.
- **How it works:** Verbatim: "Enterprise gateways often issue short-lived bearer tokens (SSO/OIDC brokers, cloud IAM, internal auth proxies) rather than static API keys, so a token copied into `.env` goes stale mid-session and requests start returning 401. `key_cmd` names a command that *prints* a token; Hermes runs it and caches the result until shortly before expiry, so long sessions keep working with no restart." Example:
  ```yaml
  providers:
    my-gateway:
      base_url: "https://gateway.internal.example.com/v1"
      api_mode: chat_completions
      key_cmd: "my-auth-cli print-token --profile prod"
  ```
  Compatible helpers named verbatim: "`databricks auth token`, `gcloud auth print-access-token`, `az account get-access-token`, `vault read`, or Claude Code-style `apiKeyHelper` scripts."
- **Inputs / options:** `key_cmd` (string command). Output contract verbatim: "The command must print **only** the token on stdout: either bare, or as JSON with an `access_token` field (`expires_in` is honored; absolute `expiry`/`expiresOn` ISO timestamps too). Multi-line output is rejected rather than guessed at. If no expiry is advertised, the token is re-minted on a bounded window."
- **Outputs / side effects:** Spawns the helper process; caches the minted token in memory.
- **Config / env:** `providers.<name>.key_cmd`; contrast with `secrets.command`.
- **Edge cases / guards:** Precedence verbatim: "an explicit `--api-key` flag still wins; otherwise `key_cmd` beats a static `api_key`/`key_env` on the same entry. The minted credential applies to the main agent turn and to auxiliary tasks (title generation, compression, vision, embedding) alike." Disambiguation verbatim: "Not to be confused with `secrets.command`, which runs a helper **once at startup** to populate env vars process-wide. Use that for a vault/keychain helper handing back many secrets; use `key_cmd` when one provider's credential must be re-minted *during* a session."
- **Rebuild notes:** Support a token-minting hook per provider with a strict single-line/JSON output contract and an expiry-aware cache; reject ambiguous output instead of parsing heuristically.

### `extra_body` / `extra_headers` per custom provider  `id: docs-rest.providers-extra-body`
- **Surface:** Config / Docs
- **Where:** Providers page → "### Named Custom Providers" → the `extra_body` subsection.
- **What it does:** Merges provider-specific request-body fields into every chat-completions request for a given endpoint.
- **How it works:** Verbatim: "Some OpenAI-compatible endpoints need provider-specific request body fields. Add an `extra_body` map to the matching custom provider and Hermes will merge it into each chat-completions request for that endpoint." Examples verbatim: top-level `extra_body: { enable_thinking: true, reasoning_effort: high }`; nested `extra_body: { chat_template_kwargs: { enable_thinking: true } }` ("vLLM Gemma deployments and some NVIDIA NIM endpoints expect `enable_thinking` under `chat_template_kwargs`"); disabling `extra_body: { chat_template_kwargs: { enable_thinking: false } }` for Qwen reasoning models whose parser empties `content`.
- **Inputs / options:** `providers.<name>.extra_body`, `providers.<name>.extra_headers`.
- **Outputs / side effects:** Alters every outgoing request for that provider.
- **Config / env:** As above.
- **Edge cases / guards:** Lifetime verbatim: "The configured `extra_body` follows the provider everywhere: it is merged at agent construction, **survives every gateway turn** (including turns where `/fast` layers `service_tier`/`speed` overrides on top — those merge over your `extra_body` rather than replacing it), and is **re-derived on `/model` switches** — switching to a named custom provider applies its `extra_body`, and switching away clears it so it never leaks to another provider."
- **Rebuild notes:** Scope request-body overrides to the provider entry and re-derive them on every provider switch so they cannot leak.

### Cookbook: Together AI, Groq, Perplexity  `id: docs-rest.providers-cookbook`
- **Surface:** Docs / Config
- **Where:** Providers page → **"### Cookbook: Together AI, Groq, Perplexity"**.
- **What it does:** Three complete copy-paste recipes under the `providers:` dict, plus a combined config.
- **How it works:** **Together AI** verbatim: "Hosts open-weight models (Llama, MiniMax, Gemma, DeepSeek, Qwen) at prices significantly below first-party APIs. Good default for multi-model fleets." — `providers.together.api: https://api.together.xyz/v1`, `key_env: TOGETHER_API_KEY`, `model.default: MiniMaxAI/MiniMax-M2.7`, `model.provider: custom:together`; `.env`: `TOGETHER_API_KEY=your-together-key`; switches `/model custom:together:meta-llama/Llama-3.3-70B-Instruct-Turbo`, `/model custom:together:google/gemma-4-31b-it`, `/model custom:together:deepseek-ai/DeepSeek-V3`; "Together's `/v1/models` endpoint works, so `hermes model` can auto-discover available models."
  **Groq** verbatim: "Ultra-fast inference (~500 tok/s on Llama-3.3-70B). Small catalog but strong for latency-sensitive interactive use." — `api: https://api.groq.com/openai/v1`, `key_env: GROQ_API_KEY`, `model.default: llama-3.3-70b-versatile`, `model.provider: custom:groq`.
  **Perplexity** verbatim: "Useful when you want a model that does live web search and citation automatically. Strict about which models are available" — `api: https://api.perplexity.ai`, `key_env: PERPLEXITY_API_KEY`, `model.default: sonar`, `model.provider: custom:perplexity`.
  **"#### Multiple providers in one config"** — all three under `providers:` with `model.provider: custom:together` ("boot to Together; switch freely after").
- **Inputs / options:** `TOGETHER_API_KEY`, `GROQ_API_KEY`, `PERPLEXITY_API_KEY`; `/model custom:<name>:<model>`.
- **Outputs / side effects:** n/a
- **Config / env:** As above.
- **Edge cases / guards:** **":::tip Troubleshooting"** verbatim: "`hermes doctor` should print no `Unknown provider` warnings for any of these names after the CLI validator fixes in #15083."; "If a provider's `/v1/models` endpoint is unreachable (Perplexity is the common one), `hermes model` will persist the model with a warning rather than hard-reject — see #15136."; "To skip named providers entirely and use bare `provider: custom` with `CUSTOM_BASE_URL` env var, see #15103."
- **Rebuild notes:** Ship complete, runnable recipes (config + env + switch commands) rather than fragments.

### "Choosing the Right Setup" decision table  `id: docs-rest.providers-choosing`
- **Surface:** Docs
- **Where:** Providers page → **"### Choosing the Right Setup"**.
- **What it does:** Maps nine use cases to a recommended provider stack.
- **How it works:** All nine rows verbatim (Use Case | Recommended): "**Just want it to work**" | "OpenRouter (default) or Nous Portal"; "**Local models, easy setup**" | "Ollama"; "**Production GPU serving**" | "vLLM or SGLang"; "**Mac / no GPU**" | "Ollama or llama.cpp"; "**Multi-provider routing**" | "LiteLLM Proxy or OpenRouter"; "**Cost optimization**" | "ClawRouter or OpenRouter with `sort: \"price\"`"; "**Maximum privacy**" | "Ollama, vLLM, or llama.cpp (fully local)"; "**Enterprise / Azure**" | "Azure OpenAI with custom endpoint"; "**Chinese AI models**" | "z.ai (GLM), Kimi/Moonshot (`kimi-coding` or `kimi-coding-cn`), MiniMax, Xiaomi MiMo, or Tencent TokenHub (first-class providers)".
- **Inputs / options:** n/a
- **Outputs / side effects:** n/a
- **Config / env:** n/a
- **Edge cases / guards:** "You can switch between providers at any time with `hermes model` — no restart required. Your conversation history, memory, and skills carry over regardless of which provider you use."
- **Rebuild notes:** Provider choice is a constraint-satisfaction problem (privacy, hardware, region, cost); publish it as a table, not prose.

### Optional API keys table  `id: docs-rest.providers-optional-api-keys`
- **Surface:** Docs / Env
- **Where:** Providers page → **"## Optional API Keys"**.
- **What it does:** Names the non-inference features that need their own key.
- **How it works:** All eight rows verbatim (Feature | Provider | Env Variable): "Web scraping" | Firecrawl | `FIRECRAWL_API_KEY`, `FIRECRAWL_API_URL`; "Browser automation" | Browserbase | `BROWSERBASE_API_KEY`, `BROWSERBASE_PROJECT_ID`; "Image generation" | FAL | `FAL_KEY`; "Premium TTS voices" | ElevenLabs | `ELEVENLABS_API_KEY`; "OpenAI TTS + voice transcription" | OpenAI | `VOICE_TOOLS_OPENAI_KEY`; "Mistral TTS + voice transcription" | Mistral | `MISTRAL_API_KEY`; "Cross-session user modeling" | Honcho | `HONCHO_API_KEY`; "Semantic long-term memory" | Supermemory | `SUPERMEMORY_API_KEY`.
- **Inputs / options:** n/a
- **Outputs / side effects:** n/a
- **Config / env:** The nine env vars listed.
- **Edge cases / guards:** All are optional; the Tool Gateway removes the need for Firecrawl/FAL/Browser/OpenAI keys.
- **Rebuild notes:** Keep feature keys separate from inference keys so a user can reason about "what breaks if I remove this".

### Self-hosting Firecrawl  `id: docs-rest.providers-self-host-firecrawl`
- **Surface:** Docs / Config
- **Where:** Providers page → **"### Self-Hosting Firecrawl"**.
- **What it does:** Points Hermes' web tools at a locally-run Firecrawl instead of the cloud API.
- **How it works:** Trade-off verbatim: "**What you get:** No API key required, no rate limits, no per-page costs, full data sovereignty. **What you lose:** The cloud version uses Firecrawl's proprietary \"Fire-engine\" for advanced anti-bot bypassing (Cloudflare, CAPTCHAs, IP rotation). Self-hosted uses basic fetch + Playwright, so some protected sites may fail. Search uses DuckDuckGo instead of Google." Setup verbatim: "1. Clone and start the Firecrawl Docker stack (5 containers: API, Playwright, Redis, RabbitMQ, PostgreSQL — requires ~4-8 GB RAM): `git clone https://github.com/firecrawl/firecrawl`; `cd firecrawl`; In `.env`, set: `USE_DB_AUTHENTICATION=false`, `HOST=0.0.0.0`, `PORT=3002`; `docker compose up -d`. 2. Point Hermes at your instance (no API key needed): `hermes config set FIRECRAWL_API_URL http://localhost:3002`."
- **Inputs / options:** `FIRECRAWL_API_URL`, optionally `FIRECRAWL_API_KEY` "if your self-hosted instance has authentication enabled".
- **Outputs / side effects:** Web search/extract calls go to the local instance.
- **Config / env:** `FIRECRAWL_API_URL`, `FIRECRAWL_API_KEY`, `USE_DB_AUTHENTICATION`, `HOST`, `PORT`.
- **Edge cases / guards:** ~4–8 GB RAM for the five-container stack.
- **Rebuild notes:** Let every hosted backend be replaced by a base-URL override, and document precisely which capabilities degrade.

### OpenRouter provider routing (`provider_routing`)  `id: docs-rest.providers-openrouter-routing`
- **Surface:** Config / Docs
- **Where:** Providers page → **"## OpenRouter Provider Routing"**.
- **What it does:** Controls how OpenRouter distributes requests across upstream providers.
- **How it works:** YAML verbatim:
  ```yaml
  provider_routing:
    sort: "throughput"          # "price" (default), "throughput", or "latency"
    # only: ["anthropic"]      # Only use these providers
    # ignore: ["deepinfra"]    # Skip these providers
    # order: ["anthropic", "google"]  # Try providers in this order
    # require_parameters: true  # Only use providers that support all request params
    # data_collection: "deny"   # Exclude providers that may store/train on data
  ```
- **Inputs / options:** `provider_routing.sort` (`price` default, `throughput`, `latency`), `only`, `ignore`, `order`, `require_parameters`, `data_collection`. Shortcuts verbatim: "Append `:nitro` to any model name for throughput sorting (e.g., `anthropic/claude-sonnet-4:nitro`), or `:floor` for price sorting."
- **Outputs / side effects:** Adds a `provider` block to OpenRouter requests.
- **Config / env:** `provider_routing.*`.
- **Edge cases / guards:** Only applies on the OpenRouter provider.
- **Rebuild notes:** Pass provider-preference blocks through verbatim and support the vendor's model-name suffix shortcuts.

### OpenRouter Pareto Code Router (`min_coding_score`)  `id: docs-rest.providers-openrouter-pareto`
- **Surface:** Config / Docs
- **Where:** Providers page → **"## OpenRouter Pareto Code Router"**.
- **What it does:** Uses OpenRouter's `openrouter/pareto-code` router model with a tunable coding-quality floor.
- **How it works:** Verbatim: "OpenRouter ships an experimental coding-model router at `openrouter/pareto-code` that auto-routes requests to the cheapest model meeting a coding-quality bar (ranked by [Artificial Analysis](https://artificialanalysis.ai/))." Config verbatim: `model.provider: openrouter`, `model.model: openrouter/pareto-code`, `openrouter.min_coding_score: 0.65` ("0.0–1.0; higher = stronger (more expensive) coders. Default 0.65.").
- **Inputs / options:** `openrouter.min_coding_score`.
- **Outputs / side effects:** Sends a plugins block on OpenRouter requests.
- **Config / env:** `openrouter.min_coding_score`, `model.model`.
- **Edge cases / guards:** All four notes verbatim: "`min_coding_score` is **only** sent when `model.model` is `openrouter/pareto-code`. On any other model the value is a no-op."; "Set to empty string (or remove the line) to let OpenRouter pick the strongest available coder — its documented behavior when the plugins block is omitted."; "Selection is deterministic per score on a given day, but the actual model chosen can shift as the Pareto frontier moves (new models, benchmark updates)."; "To use the Pareto Code router for a specific **auxiliary task** (compression, vision, etc.) instead of the main agent, set `extra_body.plugins` under that task".
- **Rebuild notes:** Gate vendor-specific request extensions on the exact model id so they cannot leak to other models.

### Fallback provider chain (`fallback_providers`)  `id: docs-rest.providers-fallback-chain`
- **Surface:** Config / Docs
- **Where:** Providers page → **"## Fallback Providers"**.
- **What it does:** Configures an ordered chain of backup providers Hermes tries when the primary model fails.
- **How it works:** Canonical format verbatim:
  ```yaml
  fallback_providers:
    - provider: openrouter
      model: anthropic/claude-sonnet-4
    - provider: anthropic
      model: claude-sonnet-4
      # base_url: http://localhost:8000/v1    # optional, for custom endpoints
      # api_mode: chat_completions           # optional override
  ```
  Legacy single-pair form still accepted: `fallback_model: { provider: openrouter, model: anthropic/claude-sonnet-4 }`. Behaviour verbatim: "When activated, the fallback swaps the model and provider mid-session without losing your conversation. The chain is tried entry-by-entry; activation is one-shot per session."
- **Inputs / options:** Per-entry fields: `provider`, `model`, `base_url`, `api_mode`. Interactive setup: `hermes fallback`.
- **Outputs / side effects:** Swaps model+provider mid-session.
- **Config / env:** `fallback_providers`, `fallback_model`.
- **Edge cases / guards:** Supported providers list, all 41 verbatim: `openrouter`, `nous`, `novita`, `openai-codex`, `copilot`, `copilot-acp`, `anthropic`, `gemini`, `qwen-oauth`, `huggingface`, `zai`, `kimi-coding`, `kimi-coding-cn`, `minimax`, `minimax-cn`, `minimax-oauth`, `deepseek`, `nvidia`, `xai`, `xai-oauth`, `ollama-cloud`, `bedrock`, `ai-gateway`, `azure-foundry`, `opencode-zen`, `opencode-go`, `commandcode`, `commandcode-anthropic`, `kilocode`, `xiaomi`, `arcee`, `gmi`, `actual`, `stepfun`, `lmstudio`, `alibaba`, `alibaba-coding-plan`, `tencent-tokenhub`, `tencent-tokenplan`, `nebius-token-factory`, `router`, `custom`. "Fallback is configured exclusively through `config.yaml` — or interactively via `hermes fallback`."
- **Rebuild notes:** Model fallback as an ordered list with per-entry transport overrides, activate once per session, and preserve conversation state across the swap.

## Developer Guide

### Architecture overview (system map)  `id: docs-rest.dev-architecture`
- **Surface:** Docs / Core
- **Where:** Docs → **"Developer Guide"** (sidebar label from `developer-guide/_category_.json`: **"Developer Guide"**, position 3, generated-index description **"Contribute to Hermes Agent — architecture, tools, skills, and more."**) → **"Architecture"** (`developer-guide/architecture.md`, `sidebar_position: 1`).
- **What it does:** The top-level map of Hermes internals: entry points, the `AIAgent` core, subsystems, directory structure, data flows, and design principles.
- **How it works:** ASCII system diagram, verbatim structure: **Entry Points** — "CLI (cli.py)", "Gateway (gateway/run.py)", "ACP (acp_adapter/)", "Batch Runner", "API Server", "Python Library" — all feeding **AIAgent (run_agent.py)**, which contains "Prompt Builder (prompt_builder.py)", "Provider Resolution (runtime_provider.py)", "Tool Dispatch (model_tools.py)", plus "Compression & Caching", "3 API Modes chat_compl./codex_resp./anthropic", "Tool Registry (registry.py) 70+ tools 28 toolsets"; feeding **Session Storage** ("SQLite + FTS5, hermes_state.py, gateway/session.py") and **Tool Backends** ("Terminal (6 backends), Browser (5 backends), Web (4 backends), MCP (dynamic), File, Vision, etc.").
  Three data flows given verbatim: **CLI Session** — "User input → HermesCLI.process_input() → AIAgent.run_conversation() → prompt_builder.build_system_prompt() → runtime_provider.resolve_runtime_provider() → API call (chat_completions / codex_responses / anthropic_messages) → tool_calls? → model_tools.handle_function_call() → loop → final response → display → save to SessionDB". **Gateway Message** — "Platform event → Adapter.on_message() → MessageEvent → GatewayRunner._handle_message() → authorize user → resolve session key → create AIAgent with session history → AIAgent.run_conversation() → deliver response back through adapter". **Cron Job** — "Scheduler tick → load due jobs from jobs.json → create fresh AIAgent (no history) → inject attached skills as context → run job prompt → deliver response to target platform → update job state and next_run".
- **Inputs / options:** Directory structure, transcribed with the doc's own comments — root: `run_agent.py` "AIAgent — core conversation loop (large file)", `cli.py` "HermesCLI — interactive terminal UI (large file)", `model_tools.py` "Tool discovery, schema collection, dispatch", `toolsets.py` "Tool groupings and platform presets", `hermes_state.py` "SQLite session/state database with FTS5", `hermes_constants.py` "HERMES_HOME, profile-aware paths", `batch_runner.py` "Batch trajectory generation". `agent/`: `prompt_builder.py`, `context_engine.py` "ContextEngine ABC (pluggable)", `context_compressor.py` "Default engine — lossy summarization", `prompt_caching.py`, `auxiliary_client.py`, `model_metadata.py`, `models_dev.py`, `anthropic_adapter.py`, `display.py` "KawaiiSpinner, tool preview formatting", `skill_commands.py`, `memory_manager.py`, `memory_provider.py`, `trajectory.py`. `hermes_cli/`: `main.py`, `config.py` "DEFAULT_CONFIG, OPTIONAL_ENV_VARS, migration", `commands.py` "COMMAND_REGISTRY — central slash command definitions", `auth.py` "PROVIDER_REGISTRY, credential resolution", `runtime_provider.py`, `models.py`, `model_switch.py` "/model command logic (CLI + gateway shared)", `setup.py`, `skin_engine.py` "CLI theming engine", `skills_config.py`, `skills_hub.py`, `tools_config.py`, `plugins.py` "PluginManager — discovery, loading, hooks", `callbacks.py` "Terminal callbacks (clarify, sudo, approval)", `gateway.py`. `tools/`: `registry.py`, `approval.py` "Dangerous command detection", `terminal_tool.py`, `process_registry.py`, `file_tools.py` "read_file, write_file, patch, search_files", `web_tools.py`, `browser_tool.py` "10 browser automation tools", `code_execution_tool.py`, `delegate_tool.py`, `mcp_tool.py`, `credential_files.py`, `env_passthrough.py`, `ansi_strip.py`, `environments/` "(local, docker, ssh, modal, daytona, singularity)". `gateway/`: `run.py` "GatewayRunner — message dispatch (large file)", `session.py`, `delivery.py`, `pairing.py`, `hooks.py`, `mirror.py`, `status.py` "Token locks, profile-scoped process tracking", `builtin_hooks/` "Extension point for always-registered hooks (none shipped)", `platforms/` "signal, weixin, bluebubbles, qqbot, whatsapp_cloud, yuanbao, webhook, api_server". `plugins/platforms/`: "telegram, discord, slack, whatsapp, matrix, mattermost, email, sms, dingtalk, feishu, wecom, homeassistant, irc, line, teams, google_chat, buzz, ntfy, photon, raft, simplex". Plus `acp_adapter/`, `cron/` "(jobs.py, scheduler.py)", `plugins/memory/`, `plugins/context_engine/`, `skills/`, `optional-skills/`, `website/`, `tests/` "Pytest suite (~25,000 tests across ~1,250 files)".
  Recommended reading order, all ten verbatim: "1. This page; 2. Agent Loop Internals; 3. Prompt Assembly; 4. Provider Runtime Resolution; 5. Adding Providers; 6. Tools Runtime; 7. Session Storage; 8. Gateway Internals; 9. Context Compression & Prompt Caching; 10. ACP Internals".
- **Outputs / side effects:** n/a
- **Config / env:** n/a
- **Edge cases / guards:** Design principles table, all six verbatim (Principle | What it means in practice): **Prompt stability** "System prompt doesn't change mid-conversation. No cache-breaking mutations except explicit user actions (`/model`)."; **Observable execution** "Every tool call is visible to the user via callbacks. Progress updates in CLI (spinner) and gateway (chat messages)."; **Interruptible** "API calls and tool execution can be cancelled mid-flight by user input or signals."; **Platform-agnostic core** "One AIAgent class serves CLI, gateway, ACP, batch, and API server. Platform differences live in the entry point, not the agent."; **Loose coupling** "Optional subsystems (MCP, plugins, memory providers, RL environments) use registry patterns and check_fn gating, not hard dependencies."; **Profile isolation** "Each profile (`hermes -p <name>`) gets its own HERMES_HOME, config, memory, sessions, and gateway PID. Multiple profiles run concurrently." File dependency chain verbatim: "tools/registry.py (no deps — imported by all tool files) ↑ tools/*.py (each calls registry.register() at import time) ↑ model_tools.py (imports tools/registry + triggers tool discovery) ↑ run_agent.py, cli.py, batch_runner.py, environments/" — "tool registration happens at import time, before any agent instance is created."
- **Rebuild notes:** One platform-agnostic agent class behind many entry points; registration-at-import for tools; per-profile isolation of every stateful path. Better: split the "large file" god objects (`run_agent.py`, `cli.py`, `hermes_cli/main.py`) behind stable interfaces so the map stays true.

### Agent loop internals (`AIAgent`)  `id: docs-rest.dev-agent-loop`
- **Surface:** Docs / Core
- **Where:** Developer Guide → **"Agent Loop Internals"** (`developer-guide/agent-loop.md`, `sidebar_position: 3`).
- **What it does:** Documents the full turn lifecycle of `run_agent.py`'s `AIAgent` — prompt assembly, API mode selection, interruptible calls, tool dispatch, callbacks, budgets, fallback, compression, persistence.
- **How it works:** Core responsibilities, all eight verbatim: "Assembling the effective system prompt and tool schemas via `prompt_builder.py`"; "Selecting the correct provider/API mode (chat_completions, codex_responses, anthropic_messages)"; "Making interruptible model calls with cancellation support"; "Executing tool calls (sequentially or concurrently via thread pool)"; "Maintaining conversation history in OpenAI message format"; "Handling compression, retries, and fallback model switching"; "Tracking iteration budgets across parent and child agents"; "Flushing persistent memory before context is lost".
  Turn lifecycle, all nine steps verbatim: "1. Generate task_id if not provided; 2. Append user message to conversation history; 3. Build or reuse cached system prompt (prompt_builder.py); 4. Check if preflight compression is needed (>50% context); 5. Build API messages from conversation history — chat_completions: OpenAI format as-is; codex_responses: convert to Responses API input items; anthropic_messages: convert via anthropic_adapter.py; 6. Inject ephemeral prompt layers (budget warnings, context pressure); 7. Apply prompt caching markers if on Anthropic; 8. Make interruptible API call (_interruptible_api_call); 9. Parse response — If tool_calls: execute them, append results, loop back to step 5; If text response: persist session, flush memory if needed, return".
  Tool execution flow verbatim: "1. Resolve handler from tools/registry.py; 2. Fire pre_tool_call plugin hook; 3. Check if dangerous command (tools/approval.py) — If dangerous: invoke approval_callback, wait for user; 4. Execute handler with args + task_id; 5. Fire post_tool_call plugin hook; 6. Append {\"role\": \"tool\", \"content\": result} to history".
- **Inputs / options:** Two entry points verbatim: `agent.chat("Fix the bug in main.py")` ("Simple interface — returns final response string") and `agent.run_conversation(user_message=…, system_message=None, conversation_history=None, task_id="task_abc123")` ("Full interface — returns dict with messages, metadata, usage stats"); "`chat()` is a thin wrapper around `run_conversation()` that extracts the `final_response` field".
  API modes table verbatim (API mode | Used for | Client type): `chat_completions` "OpenAI-compatible endpoints (OpenRouter, custom, most providers)" `openai.OpenAI`; `codex_responses` "OpenAI Codex / Responses API" "`openai.OpenAI` with Responses format"; `anthropic_messages` "Native Anthropic Messages API" "`anthropic.Anthropic` via adapter". Mode resolution order verbatim: "1. Explicit `api_mode` constructor arg (highest priority); 2. Provider-specific detection (e.g., `anthropic` provider → `anthropic_messages`); 3. Base URL heuristics (e.g., `api.anthropic.com` → `anthropic_messages`); 4. Default: `chat_completions`".
  Callback surfaces, all eight verbatim (Callback | When fired | Used by): `tool_progress_callback` "Before/after each tool execution" "CLI spinner, gateway progress messages"; `thinking_callback` "When model starts/stops thinking" "CLI \"thinking...\" indicator"; `reasoning_callback` "When model returns reasoning content" "CLI reasoning display, gateway reasoning blocks"; `clarify_callback` "When `clarify` tool is called" "CLI input prompt, gateway interactive message"; `step_callback` "After each complete agent turn" "Gateway step tracking, ACP progress"; `stream_delta_callback` "Each streaming token (when enabled)" "CLI streaming display"; `tool_gen_callback` "When tool call is parsed from stream" "CLI tool preview in spinner"; `status_callback` "State changes (thinking, executing, etc.)" "ACP status updates".
  Agent-level tools table verbatim (Tool | Why intercepted): `todo` "Reads/writes agent-local task state"; `memory` "Writes to persistent memory files with character limits"; `session_search` "Queries session history via the agent's session DB"; `delegate_task` "Spawns subagent(s) with isolated context".
- **Outputs / side effects:** Session rows in SQLite; `MEMORY.md` / `USER.md` flushes; new session lineage IDs on compression.
- **Config / env:** `agent.max_turns` ("Default: 500 iterations"), `delegation.max_iterations` ("default 50"), `compression.protect_last_n` ("default: 20"), `fallback_providers`, `auxiliary.*`.
- **Edge cases / guards:** Message-alternation rules verbatim: "After the system message: `User → Assistant → User → Assistant → ...`; During tool calling: `Assistant (with tool_calls) → Tool → Tool → ... → Assistant`; **Never** two assistant messages in a row; **Never** two user messages in a row; **Only** `tool` role can have consecutive entries (parallel tool results). Providers validate these sequences and will reject malformed histories." Interruption verbatim: "The API thread is abandoned (response discarded); The agent can process the new input or shut down cleanly; No partial response is injected into conversation history." Concurrency verbatim: "**Single tool call** → executed directly in the main thread; **Multiple tool calls** → executed concurrently via `ThreadPoolExecutor` — Exception: tools marked as interactive (e.g., `clarify`) force sequential execution — Results are reinserted in the original tool call order regardless of completion order." Fallback verbatim: "When the primary model fails (429 rate limit, 5xx server error, 401/403 auth error): 1. Check `fallback_providers` list in config; 2. Try each fallback in order; 3. On success, continue the conversation with the new provider; 4. On 401/403, attempt credential refresh before failing over." Compression triggers verbatim: "**Preflight** (before API call): If conversation exceeds 50% of model's context window; **Gateway auto-compression**: If conversation exceeds 85%". Compression steps verbatim: "1. Memory is flushed to disk first (preventing data loss); 2. Middle conversation turns are summarized into a compact summary; 3. The last N messages are preserved intact; 4. Tool call/result message pairs are kept together (never split); 5. A new session lineage ID is generated (compression creates a \"child\" session)." Budget verbatim: "Each agent gets its own budget. Subagents get independent budgets capped at `delegation.max_iterations` (default 50) — total iterations across parent + subagents can exceed the parent's cap. At 100%, the agent stops and returns a summary of work done."
- **Rebuild notes:** Build the loop around (a) one internal message format with strict alternation, (b) an adapter per API dialect, (c) a thread-isolated interruptible call, (d) a callback bus so every surface renders progress from the same events, and (e) memory-flush-before-compress ordering. Better: make the loop async-native so interruption doesn't require abandoning a thread.

### Tools runtime — registry, discovery, dispatch  `id: docs-rest.dev-tools-runtime`
- **Surface:** Docs / Tool / Core
