Anything deeper is ignored."; "**Missing `__init__.py`:** Native packages need both `plugin.yaml` and `__init__.py` with a `register(ctx)` function…"; "**Wrong `kind`** — gateway adapters need `kind: platform` in their manifest. Memory providers are auto-detected as `kind: exclusive` and routed through the `memory.provider` config instead of `plugins.enabled`."
- **Outputs / side effects:** Tools/hooks/commands registered at startup; `~/.hermes/logs/agent.log` entries.
- **Config / env:** `plugins.enabled`, `plugins.entries.<id>.settings`, `HERMES_PLUGINS_DEBUG`.
- **Edge cases / guards:** **":::caution Third-party-product plugins ship standalone — not into the core tree"** verbatim: "Plugins that integrate **someone else's product or project** — observability/metrics backends, vendor SaaS connectors, analytics dashboards, paid-service tie-ins — are built and distributed as **standalone plugin repos**, not merged into `NousResearch/hermes-agent`… Promote it in the Nous Research Discord `#plugins-skills-and-skins` channel."
  Common mistakes, all four verbatim: "**Handler doesn't return JSON string**" (return `json.dumps(...)`, not a dict); "**Missing `**kwargs` in handler signature**"; "**Handler raises exceptions**" (catch and return `{"error": ...}`); "**Schema description too vague**" — Bad: `"description": "Does stuff"`; Good: `"description": "Evaluate a mathematical expression. Use for arithmetic, trig, logarithms. Supports: +, -, *, /, **, sqrt, sin, cos, log, pi, e."`.
- **Rebuild notes:** A plugin is a directory with a declarative manifest and one `register(ctx)` entry point; everything else is a method on `ctx`. Crash-isolate `register()` so a bad plugin never takes down the host, and ship a doctor command that runs the real discovery path.

### `hermes plugins doctor` — plugin validation  `id: docs-rest.dev-plugins-doctor`
- **Surface:** CLI / Docs
- **Where:** Build a Plugin → "### Validate with Plugin Doctor".
- **What it does:** Runs the real plugin discovery, manifest parse, import, `register(ctx)`, hook registry and tool registry against a plugin and reports problems.
- **How it works:** Verbatim: "`hermes plugins doctor [path-or-id]` runs the same directory discovery, manifest parser, namespaced import, `register(ctx)`, hook registry, and tool registry used by Hermes itself. It reports invalid hook names, callbacks that do not accept `**kwargs`, registration failures, and drift between declared and registered tools/hooks."
- **Inputs / options:** `[path-or-id]` positional; `--ci` ("Pass `--ci` to exit non-zero on an error"). Example: `hermes plugins doctor . --ci`.
- **Outputs / side effects:** Diagnostic report; non-zero exit with `--ci`.
- **Config / env:** Uses a temporary `HERMES_HOME`.
- **Edge cases / guards:** Verbatim: "Doctor uses a temporary `HERMES_HOME`, restores plugin registration state after the check, and blocks direct Python socket connections to catch accidental network access while registration runs. This is not a sandbox: plugin code still executes in-process with the current user's permissions and can spawn subprocesses, so only run Doctor on code you trust enough to import."
- **Rebuild notes:** Validate by running the real loader in an isolated home rather than by static analysis; block sockets during registration to catch side-effectful imports.

### Plugin manifest — v1, v2 fields, and capabilities  `id: docs-rest.dev-plugin-manifest`
- **Surface:** Docs / Config
- **Where:** Build a Plugin → "## Step 2: Write the manifest", "### Declaring capabilities", "### Manifest v2 reference".
- **What it does:** Defines every `plugin.yaml` field, the capability-consent model, and the additive v2 schema.
- **How it works:** Minimal v1 manifest verbatim: `name`, `version`, `description`, `provides_tools` (list), `provides_hooks` (list). Optional v1 fields verbatim: `author`; `requires_env` (simple string list, or rich entries with `name` / `description` / `url` / `secret`); `capabilities` (list of privileged host surfaces).
  v2 schema table, all nine fields verbatim (Field | Type | Meaning): `manifest_version` int "Manifest **file-format** version. Absent = `1`. Current max: `2`. Independent from `api_version`."; `api_version` int "Runtime **plugin API generation** the plugin targets (ctx surface / hook signatures). Deliberately a separate axis from `manifest_version`…"; `requires_plugins` list "Inter-plugin dependencies: `- id: other-plugin` with optional `version_range: \">=1.0,<2\"`. **Advisory**: a missing dependency logs a clear warning but the plugin still loads — probe at runtime with `ctx.has_plugin(\"other-plugin\")`. Load **order** honors these edges: when A requires B, B's `register()` runs before A's (topological sort, alphabetical tiebreak; cycles warn and fall back to alphabetical order)."; `python_dependencies` list of str "Declared pip requirements (e.g. `\"requests>=2.0,<3\"`). **Declaration seam only** — Hermes validates them, and `hermes plugins install` / `hermes plugins doctor` surface missing ones with a `pip install` hint, but Hermes **never auto-installs** them. Pin upper bounds."; `config_schema` mapping "JSON-schema-ish description of keys under `plugins.entries.<id>.settings`: `api_url: {type: str, default: \"\", description: \"...\", required: false}`. Validated at load; mismatches log actionable warnings naming the key and expected type — never load failures. Types: `str`, `int`, `float`, `bool`, `list`, `dict` (plus JSON-schema aliases)."; `license` str "SPDX-style license id (e.g. `MIT`)."; `homepage` str "Project URL."; `tags` list of str "Free-form discovery tags (e.g. `[gateway, telegram]`)."
  `requires_env` rich-format fields table verbatim (Field | Required | Description): `name` Yes "Environment variable name"; `description` No "Shown to user during install prompt"; `url` No "Where to get the credential"; `secret` No "If `true`, input is hidden (like a password field)". "Both formats can be mixed in the same list. Already-set variables are skipped silently."
- **Inputs / options:** Known capability ids verbatim: `tools.override`, `llm.provider_override`, `llm.model_override`, `llm.agent_id_override`, `llm.profile_override`, `llm.task_override` — "(see `hermes_cli/plugin_capabilities.py` for the canonical registry). Unknown ids are ignored." Probing pattern verbatim: `if ctx.has_capability("tools.override"): ctx.register_tool(..., override=True) else: ctx.register_tool(...)`. Pip declaration verbatim: entry-point group `hermes_agent.plugin_capabilities`, entries named `<plugin-id>.<capability-id>` pointing at the same object as the `hermes_agent.plugins` entry point — example `"calculator.tools.override" = "my_pkg:register"`.
- **Outputs / side effects:** Consent prompt at install/enable; `plugins.entries.<id>.settings` validation warnings.
- **Config / env:** `plugins.entries.<id>.settings.*`; deprecated per-capability keys `plugins.entries.<id>.allow_tool_override`.
- **Edge cases / guards:** Verbatim: "Undeclared or unconsented capabilities are simply off (fail closed), so **probe before using them and degrade gracefully**"; "if a later version adds a capability, the update flow asks again for just the addition"; "Capabilities are consent + audit, **not a sandbox**: they gate host API surfaces, nothing more"; "Unknown fields never break loading — they are ignored with a warning (forward compatibility), and a `manifest_version` newer than this Hermes understands still loads with a warning." **":::note pip-dependency isolation is deferred"** — "`python_dependencies` is intentionally declare-and-surface only. Installing arbitrary packages into Hermes' shared venv is a conflict and supply-chain surface…" (issues #64165, #15220; plugin packs #64166).
- **Rebuild notes:** Separate the manifest file-format version from the runtime API generation; make unknown fields non-fatal in both directions; capabilities are a consent screen, so keep the id registry small and canonical.

### Native plugin compatibility contract and deprecation policy  `id: docs-rest.dev-plugin-compat-contract`
- **Surface:** Docs / Core
- **Where:** Build a Plugin → "## Native plugin compatibility contract" and "### Deprecation policy".
- **What it does:** States the stability guarantees for `plugin.yaml` + `register(ctx)` plugins across Hermes upgrades.
- **How it works:** Verbatim: "Native `plugin.yaml` plus `register(ctx)` plugins are protected by behavior, not by one global plugin API number. Hermes does not expose a `PLUGIN_API_VERSION`, require a manifest-wide `api:` match, or attach an API version to unrelated values. A plugin that uses a documented behavior should continue to work after a normal Hermes upgrade." All five rules verbatim: "**Evolve additively.** Documented `PluginContext` methods are not removed or renamed. New parameters are optional, have defaults, and should be keyword-only. Existing return fields are not removed or silently retyped."; "**Hook payloads are keyword payloads.** New hook data is added as keyword fields, never by changing the meaning or position of an existing field. Hermes inspects callback signatures: a legacy callback receives the fields it declares, while a callback with `**kwargs` receives the complete current payload."; "**Manifests are open to additions.** Unknown `plugin.yaml` fields are ignored."; "**Provider interfaces grow through defaults.** New provider methods have a default implementation. New callback context is optional and forwarded only when signature inspection shows that a provider accepts it."; "**Version the contract that crosses a boundary.** A capability may carry its own schema version when it defines a wire payload or persisted format… Persisted plugin state and config must remain readable, or ship an explicit migration; resumed sessions written by the old format must still replay."
- **Inputs / options:** n/a
- **Outputs / side effects:** n/a
- **Config / env:** n/a
- **Edge cases / guards:** Deprecation policy, all four conditions verbatim: "1. a replacement and migration instructions in the plugin guide and release notes; 2. a warning emitted at most once per process, naming the replacement and the earliest removal release; 3. support for the old behavior through at least two subsequent minor releases; and 4. behavior-based compatibility coverage for both the legacy path and the replacement throughout that window." Enforcement verbatim: "Hermes enforces this contract with frozen external-plugin fixtures discovered from an isolated `HERMES_HOME`. Those tests load and invoke the plugin through `PluginManager`; they assert real registration and callback outcomes rather than internal symbol lists or source-code shape."
- **Rebuild notes:** Guarantee behavior, not a version number; inspect callback signatures so old and new callbacks can coexist; test the contract with frozen third-party fixtures, not internal introspection.

### Portable Agent Plugins v1 packages  `id: docs-rest.dev-portable-plugins`
- **Surface:** Docs / Core
- **Where:** Build a Plugin → "## Portable Agent Plugins v1 packages".
- **What it does:** A compatibility adapter that installs and loads directory packages targeting the Agent Plugins v1.0.0 format (`plugin.json` + `skills/` + `mcp.json`).
- **How it works:** Layout verbatim: `my-portable-plugin/` containing `plugin.json`, `skills/summarize/{SKILL.md,references/}`, `mcp.json`. Install flow verbatim: `hermes plugins install owner/repository --no-enable`; `hermes plugins list`; `hermes plugins enable <plugin-name>`. Behaviour verbatim: "Portable packages are disabled after installation unless you explicitly enable them. An enabled package may provide immediate `skills/*/SKILL.md` directories and stdio MCP servers from root `mcp.json`. Skills are read-only, namespaced, and loaded through `skills_list` plus `skill_view`. MCP commands are passed as one executable token with a separate argument list, never through a shell." Namespacing verbatim: "Portable skill namespaces have the deterministic form `agent-plugin-<slug>-<hash>`, derived from the discovered plugin key so sanitized names cannot collide."
- **Inputs / options:** `hermes plugins install <owner/repository> [--no-enable]`; `hermes plugins enable <plugin-name>`; `skills_list`, `skill_view`.
- **Outputs / side effects:** `PLUGIN_ROOT` points to the resolved package root; `PLUGIN_DATA` points to "a profile-scoped writable directory managed by Hermes".
- **Config / env:** `PLUGIN_ROOT`, `PLUGIN_DATA`.
- **Edge cases / guards:** Validation verbatim: "Hermes validates `plugin.json`, Agent Skills frontmatter, fixed component locations, `mcp.json`, resolved paths, and symlink containment locally. It does not fetch JSON schemas while loading a package. A bad skill or MCP entry is skipped at its own boundary when valid sibling components can still load." Secrets verbatim: "Values declared in portable MCP `env` are visible package data, not a secret storage mechanism. Do not place credentials in `mcp.json`." Transport subset verbatim: "The current portable subset supports stdio and Streamable HTTP MCP entries. Portable `streamable-http` entries are routed through Hermes' existing native remote MCP client…, with the v1 boundary rules enforced: the URL must be absolute http(s) with no user information or fragment, plain HTTP is accepted only for `localhost`/loopback hosts, and configured headers are never forwarded across a cross-origin redirect. Legacy `sse` entries are reported and skipped." Trust verbatim: "Agent Plugins v1 does not define trust, permissions, provenance, or a sandbox. Enabling a package grants its instructions and local executable the same full-trust posture as other installed Hermes plugins." Spec-status note verbatim: "Hermes keys behavior on the canonical v1.0.0 schema identifiers and normative text, not either mutable status label. This is an explicit supported subset, not a claim of full Agent Plugins conformance."
- **Rebuild notes:** Implement cross-agent plugin formats as an adapter over your own loader, validate locally (never fetch schemas at load time), fail per-component rather than per-package, and be explicit that a portable format carries no trust model.

### Plugin config, state, and durable storage  `id: docs-rest.dev-plugin-storage`
- **Surface:** Docs / Config / Core
- **Where:** Build a Plugin → "### Store settings and runtime state", "### Ship data files", "### Store durable state".
- **What it does:** Three distinct storage surfaces for a plugin — shipped files, user-visible settings, and plugin-owned runtime state.
- **How it works:** Settings verbatim: "Use plugin-relative config keys for user-visible behavior. Hermes resolves them under `plugins.entries.<plugin-id>.settings` and rejects global, cross-plugin, and traversal paths" — `ctx.get_config("endpoint", default=...)`, `ctx.get_config("retry.attempts", default=3)`, `ctx.set_config(...)`. State verbatim: `ctx.state.get("cursor", default={"page": 0})` / `ctx.state.set("cursor", ...)` — "State is profile-scoped, atomically replaced, safe across concurrent writers, and limited to 10 MiB per plugin. Portable packages share the same directory as their `PLUGIN_DATA`; native plugins receive a collision-resistant, Windows-safe namespace. Malformed existing state is reported and preserved." Ownership verbatim: "Config and state have different owners: settings are user-visible behavior in `config.yaml`, while state is plugin-owned runtime data under `<HERMES_HOME>/plugin-data/`. Neither API exposes another plugin's namespace."
  Durable storage helpers verbatim: `from plugins.plugin_storage import plugin_data_dir, plugin_db`; `plugin_data_dir("my-plugin") / "state.json"` ("`<hermes home>/plugin-data/<name>/` — created on first use"); `plugin_db("my-plugin")` ("a SQLite database at `<data dir>/data.db` (WAL mode, thread-friendly)"). Shipped files verbatim: read them at import time relative to `Path(__file__).parent`.
- **Inputs / options:** `ctx.get_config`, `ctx.set_config`, `ctx.state.get`, `ctx.state.set`, `plugin_data_dir()`, `plugin_db()`.
- **Outputs / side effects:** Files under `<HERMES_HOME>/plugin-data/<name>/`; keys under `plugins.entries.<id>.settings`.
- **Config / env:** `plugins.entries.<plugin-id>.settings.*`.
- **Edge cases / guards:** Verbatim: "Never write runtime state into your plugin directory: that's the install tree, and `hermes plugins update` / `remove` git-pull or delete it — your users' data dies with it." "Secrets don't belong here — credential reads go through the standard `.env` / secret-scope path like everywhere else." 10 MiB state cap per plugin.
- **Rebuild notes:** Three storage tiers with three different lifetimes (install tree = replaced on update; settings = user-owned; data dir = plugin-owned and survives updates), namespaced per plugin and per profile.

### Plugin skills, env gating, lazy deps and lazy singletons  `id: docs-rest.dev-plugin-extras`
- **Surface:** Docs / Core
- **Where:** Build a Plugin → "### Bundle skills", "### Gate on environment variables", "### Lazy-install optional Python dependencies", "### Thread-safe lazy singletons", "### Conditional tool availability".
- **What it does:** Four secondary plugin capabilities: shipping skills, gating on credentials, installing optional deps on first use, and building thread-safe cached singletons.
- **How it works:** **Skills** — layout `~/.hermes/plugins/my-plugin/skills/<name>/SKILL.md`; registration loop transcribed (`for child in sorted(skills_dir.iterdir()): if child.is_dir() and (child/"SKILL.md").exists(): ctx.register_skill(child.name, skill_md)`); use `skill_view("my-plugin:my-workflow")` vs `skill_view("my-workflow")` for the built-in. Key properties verbatim: "Plugin skills are **read-only** — they don't enter `~/.hermes/skills/` and can't be edited via `skill_manage`."; "Plugin skills are **not** listed in the system prompt's `<available_skills>` index — they're opt-in explicit loads."; "Bare skill names are unaffected — the namespace prevents collisions with built-in skills."; "When the agent loads a plugin skill, a bundle context banner is prepended listing sibling skills from the same plugin." Legacy note: "The old `shutil.copy2` pattern (copying a skill into `~/.hermes/skills/`) still works but creates name collision risk".
  **Env gating** — "If `WEATHER_API_KEY` isn't set, the plugin is disabled with a clear message. No crash, no error in the agent — just \"Plugin weather disabled (missing: WEATHER_API_KEY)\"." "When users run `hermes plugins install`, they're **prompted interactively** for any missing `requires_env` variables. Values are saved to `.env` automatically."
  **Lazy deps** — `from tools.lazy_deps import ensure, FeatureUnavailable`; call `ensure("my-plugin.my-backend")` inside the handler, catch `FeatureUnavailable`, then import. Two security rules verbatim (Rule | Why): "Your feature key must appear in the in-tree `LAZY_DEPS` allowlist" | "Prevents a malicious config from coaxing Hermes into installing arbitrary packages — only specs Hermes itself ships are eligible"; "Specs are PyPI-by-name only" | "No `--index-url`, `git+https://`, or file: paths. Pin versions with PEP 440 (`\"my-sdk>=1.2,<2\"`) inside the allowlist entry". Guidance verbatim: "For third-party plugins distributed via pip, declare the optional deps as `[project.optional-dependencies]` extras in your own `pyproject.toml`… The lazy-install dance is most useful for **bundled** plugins."
  **Lazy singletons** — `from plugins.plugin_utils import lazy_singleton, SingletonSlot`; `@lazy_singleton` decorator for zero-arg accessors (with `.reset()`), `SingletonSlot()` + `_slot.get(lambda: ...)` for accessors that take a build argument. Rationale verbatim: "Hermes runs multiple threads in one process (delegated tool calls, background workers, the self-improvement fork), so two threads can hit `get_client()` before `_client` is set, **both** pass the `is not None` check, **both** run the expensive build, and the second write clobbers the first — leaking whatever resource the loser opened". Behaviour verbatim: "Both serialize concurrent first calls with double-checked locking and run the factory at most once. If the factory raises, nothing is cached and the next call retries. The honcho memory plugin (`plugins/memory/honcho/client.py`) is the reference consumer." Rule of thumb verbatim: "any time you write `global _something` followed by a `is None` check and a build, reach for one of these instead."
  **Conditional availability** — `check_fn=lambda: _has_optional_lib()` ("False = tool hidden from model").
- **Inputs / options:** `ctx.register_skill(name, path)`; `requires_env` (simple/rich); `ensure(key)`; `lazy_singleton`, `SingletonSlot`; `check_fn`.
- **Outputs / side effects:** Namespaced skills; `.env` writes at install time; pip installs on first use (gated).
- **Config / env:** `security.allow_lazy_installs` — "When `security.allow_lazy_installs: false` is set globally, `ensure()` raises `FeatureUnavailable` immediately with a remediation hint — your plugin should catch it and degrade gracefully (return an error result, not crash the tool loop)."
- **Edge cases / guards:** As above.
- **Rebuild notes:** Namespace plugin-provided skills and keep them out of the always-loaded index; gate optional installs behind an in-tree allowlist plus a global kill switch; give plugin authors a correct lazy-singleton primitive so they don't hand-roll a racy one.

### Overriding a built-in tool from a plugin  `id: docs-rest.dev-plugin-tool-override`
- **Surface:** Docs / Config / Tool
- **Where:** Build a Plugin → "### Overriding a built-in tool".
- **What it does:** Lets a plugin replace a built-in tool's implementation, behind two explicit opt-ins.
- **How it works:** Registration verbatim: `ctx.register_tool(name="browser_navigate", toolset="plugin_my_browser", schema={...}, handler=my_custom_navigate, override=True)`. Verbatim: "Without `override=True`, the registry rejects any registration that would shadow an existing tool from a different toolset — this prevents accidental overwrites. Overriding a **built-in** tool additionally requires the operator to opt in via `plugins.entries.<plugin_id>.allow_tool_override: true` in `config.yaml`; without that gate, `register_tool(override=True)` raises `PluginToolOverrideError`. The override is logged so it's auditable in `~/.hermes/logs/agent.log`. Plugins load after built-in tools, so the registration order is correct: your handler replaces the built-in one."
- **Inputs / options:** `override=True`; config `plugins.entries.<id>.allow_tool_override: true`; CLI flags `--allow-tool-override` / `--no-allow-tool-override` on `hermes plugins enable <name>`.
- **Outputs / side effects:** A replaced tool handler; an audit line in `agent.log`.
- **Config / env:** `plugins.entries.<plugin_id>.allow_tool_override`.
- **Edge cases / guards:** Verbatim: "**Non-bundled plugins also need an operator grant.** For any plugin that does not ship with Hermes core (user, project, or pip source), `override=True` against an existing built-in tool additionally requires a per-plugin opt-in in `config.yaml`… Without the grant, `ctx.register_tool(..., override=True)` raises `PluginToolOverrideError`; since `register()` exceptions are caught by the loader, the plugin is disabled and Hermes continues. The gate exists because an enabled plugin that silently replaces a privileged built-in like `shell_exec` or `write_file` could intercept everything the model routes through it. Bundled plugins are exempt: an override there is a maintainer decision. If config cannot be loaded, the gate fails closed." "`hermes plugins enable <name>` asks whether to grant the capability when enabling a non-bundled plugin (defaulting to no)". "The same grant also gates `deregister()`: without it, a plugin cannot remove a tool it does not own (which would otherwise be a way around the override check)."
- **Rebuild notes:** Two-key rule — the plugin must declare the override *and* the operator must grant it — with a fail-closed default and an audit log line.

### Plugin hook reference (16 hooks)  `id: docs-rest.dev-plugin-hooks`
- **Surface:** Docs / Core
- **Where:** Build a Plugin → "### Register multiple hooks" and "### Hook reference".
- **What it does:** Enumerates every plugin hook, when it fires, its callback signature, and whether its return value is used.
- **How it works:** All 16 rows verbatim (Hook | Fires when | Callback signature | Returns):
  1. `pre_tool_call` | "Before any tool executes" | `tool_name: str, args: dict, task_id: str` | "optional directive: `{\"action\": \"block\", \"message\": ...}` vetoes the call; `{\"action\": \"approve\", \"message\": ...}` escalates to the human-approval gate".
  2. `post_tool_call` | "After any tool returns" | `tool_name: str, args: dict, result: str, task_id: str, duration_ms: int` | ignored.
  3. `pre_llm_call` | "Once per turn, before the tool-calling loop" | `session_id: str, user_message: str, conversation_history: list, is_first_turn: bool, model: str, platform: str` | context injection.
  4. `post_llm_call` | "Once per turn, after the tool-calling loop (successful turns only)" | `session_id: str, user_message: str, assistant_response: str, conversation_history: list, model: str, platform: str` | ignored.
  5. `pre_api_request` | "Before each raw provider API request (several per turn when the model calls tools)" | `session_id: str, model: str, provider: str, base_url: str, api_mode: str, api_call_count: int, message_count: int, tool_count: int, approx_input_tokens: int, max_tokens: int, request: dict` | ignored.
  6. `post_api_request` | "After each raw provider API request returns" | "`pre_api_request` fields plus `api_duration: float, finish_reason: str, response_model: str | None, usage: dict, response: dict, assistant_content_chars: int, assistant_tool_call_count: int`" | ignored.
  7. `api_request_error` | "A provider API call raised" | "correlation fields plus `status_code: int | None, retry_count: int | None, max_retries: int | None, retryable: bool | None, reason: str | None, error: dict, request: dict`" | ignored.
  8. `on_session_start` | "New session created (first turn only)" | `session_id: str, model: str, platform: str` | ignored.
  9. `on_session_end` | "End of every `run_conversation` call + CLI exit" | `session_id: str, completed: bool, interrupted: bool, model: str, platform: str` | ignored.
  10. `on_session_finalize` | "CLI/gateway tears down an active session" | `session_id: str | None, platform: str` | ignored.
  11. `on_session_reset` | "Gateway swaps in a new session key (`/new`, `/reset`)" | `session_id: str, platform: str` | ignored.
  12. `gateway_platform_event` | "An authorized platform-native event is normalized at the gateway boundary (Telegram reactions currently)" | `platform: str, event_type: str, payload: dict` | ignored.
  13. `kanban_task_claimed` | "A kanban task is claimed (dispatcher process, before the worker spawns)" | `task_id: str, board: str | None, assignee: str | None, run_id: int | None, profile_name: str` | ignored.
  14. `kanban_task_completed` | "A kanban task completes (worker process)" | `task_id, board, assignee, run_id, profile_name, summary: str | None` | ignored.
  15. `kanban_task_blocked` | "A kanban task is blocked (worker process)" | `task_id, board, assignee, run_id, profile_name, reason: str | None` | ignored.
- **Inputs / options:** `ctx.register_hook("<name>", callback)`.
- **Outputs / side effects:** Side effects are the plugin's own; only `pre_llm_call` and `pre_tool_call` returns are used.
- **Config / env:** n/a
- **Edge cases / guards:** Verbatim: "All callbacks should accept `**kwargs` for forward compatibility. If a hook callback crashes, it's logged and skipped. Other hooks and the agent continue normally." Kanban verbatim: "The kanban lifecycle hooks fire **after** the board DB change commits, so a callback always sees durable state and can never hold the SQLite write lock. Because kanban workers run as separate `hermes -p <profile> chat -q` subprocesses, `kanban_task_claimed` fires in the **dispatcher** process while `kanban_task_completed` / `kanban_task_blocked` fire in the **worker** process". API hooks verbatim: "The `request` and `response` kwargs are sanitized, size-capped JSON views of the provider payload (sensitive keys redacted, long strings truncated, SDK objects normalized), and `usage` is a plain token-summary dict. Every payload carries the correlation fields `turn_id`, `api_request_id`, `task_id`, `session_id`, and `api_call_count`, so a plugin can stitch requests, tool calls, and turns together."
- **Rebuild notes:** Keyword-only payloads plus signature inspection makes hooks additively extensible; crash-isolate every callback; sanitize any payload that mirrors a provider request.

### `pre_llm_call` context injection and oversized-context spill  `id: docs-rest.dev-pre-llm-injection`
- **Surface:** Docs / Core / Config
- **Where:** Build a Plugin → "### `pre_llm_call` context injection".
- **What it does:** The one hook whose return value is used — it appends text to the current turn's *user message*, never the system prompt.
- **How it works:** Return formats verbatim: `return {"context": "Recalled memories:\n- User prefers dark mode\n- Last project: hermes-agent"}`; a plain string ("equivalent to the dict form"); `return None` ("no injection (observer-only)"). "Any non-None, non-empty return with a `\"context\"` key (or a plain non-empty string) is collected and appended to the user message for the current turn."
  Why the user message, verbatim: "**Prompt cache preservation** — the system prompt stays identical across turns. Anthropic and OpenRouter cache the system prompt prefix, so keeping it stable saves 75%+ on input tokens in multi-turn conversations. If plugins modified the system prompt, every turn would be a cache miss."; "**Ephemeral** — the injection happens at API call time only. The original user message in the conversation history is never mutated, and nothing is persisted to the session database."; "**The system prompt is Hermes's territory** — it contains model-specific guidance, tool enforcement rules, personality instructions, and cached skill content."
  Three worked examples are transcribed in the doc: a memory-recall plugin (`httpx.post(f"{MEMORY_API}/recall", …, timeout=3)`, returning `{"context": text}` or `None`), a guardrails plugin (returns a fixed `POLICY` string every turn), and an observer-only analytics hook (logs and returns nothing).
- **Inputs / options:** Spill config verbatim:
  ```yaml
  hooks:
    output_spill:
      enabled: true          # default: true
      max_chars: 10000       # default; set higher to opt out of spilling
      preview_head: 500      # chars shown at the top of the preview
      preview_tail: 500      # chars shown at the bottom of the preview
      # directory: null      # default: $HERMES_HOME/hook_outputs
  ```
- **Outputs / side effects:** Spill files at `$HERMES_HOME/hook_outputs/<session_id>/<uuid>.txt`.
- **Config / env:** `hooks.output_spill.{enabled,max_chars,preview_head,preview_tail,directory}`.
- **Edge cases / guards:** Spill verbatim: "Per-hook context is capped at `10,000` characters by default. Anything above the cap is written to `$HERMES_HOME/hook_outputs/<session_id>/<uuid>.txt` and replaced with a head/tail preview plus the saved path. The model can read the full content via `read_file` or `terminal` if it genuinely needs it. This keeps a runaway plugin from inflating every subsequent turn's prompt and blowing out the prompt cache prefix." Multiple plugins verbatim: "their outputs are joined with double newlines and appended to the user message together. The order follows plugin discovery order (alphabetical by plugin directory name)."
- **Rebuild notes:** Injected context belongs on the mutable end of the prompt (the user turn), never the cached prefix; cap it and spill the overflow to a file the model can read on demand.

### Plugin middleware (`tool_request`, `llm_request`, `tool_execution`, `llm_execution`)  `id: docs-rest.dev-plugin-middleware`
- **Surface:** Docs / Core
- **Where:** Build a Plugin → "### Middleware: change what happens". Canonical list: `VALID_MIDDLEWARE` in `hermes_cli/middleware.py`.
- **What it does:** Lets a plugin rewrite the effective payload before anything downstream sees it, or wrap the actual tool/provider call.
- **How it works:** Distinction verbatim: "Hooks observe the agent loop (with the few documented steering shapes above). **Middleware changes what happens**: request middleware rewrites the effective payload before anything downstream sees it, and execution middleware wraps the actual call." Registration: `ctx.register_middleware("tool_request", cap_find_output)`. Worked example transcribed: a `tool_request` middleware that appends `| head -100` to `terminal` commands starting with `find `, returning `{"args": {...}, "source": "my-plugin", "reason": "cap find output"}`.
  All four kinds verbatim (Kind | Receives | Return contract): `tool_request` | "`tool_name`, `args`, `original_args`, context kwargs" | "Return `{\"args\": {...}}` to replace the effective tool arguments before hooks, guardrails, approvals, and execution see them. Return `None` to leave the call unchanged."; `llm_request` | "`request`, `original_request`, context kwargs" | "Return `{\"request\": {...}}` to replace the effective provider kwargs before Hermes sends them."; `tool_execution` | "the payload plus `next_call`" | "Wraps tool execution. Call `next_call(payload)` exactly once to run the downstream chain (or skip it to short-circuit) and return the result."; `llm_execution` | "the payload plus `next_call`" | "Same shape, wrapping the provider call."
- **Inputs / options:** `ctx.register_middleware(kind, callback)`; optional returned keys `source`, `reason`, `name`.
- **Outputs / side effects:** Rewritten tool args / provider kwargs; entries in the middleware trace.
- **Config / env:** n/a
- **Edge cases / guards:** All five rules verbatim: "Request middleware chains: each callback sees the payload as rewritten by earlier callbacks, while `original_args` / `original_request` always carries the pre-middleware copy. Payloads are copied between callbacks, so mutate freely."; "You can include `source`, `reason`, and `name` strings in the returned dict. They land in the middleware trace, which downstream observer hooks receive as the `middleware_trace` kwarg."; "`next_call` in execution middleware is **single-use**. Calling it twice raises, because it would re-run the provider or tool."; "A middleware callback that raises is logged and skipped; the chain continues. A downstream failure raised after your `next_call` propagates as itself. Middleware can never break the base runtime path."; "Middleware payloads carry `middleware_schema_version` (`hermes.middleware.v1`) alongside the observer telemetry fields."; "Unknown kinds register with a warning instead of failing, so a plugin written against a newer Hermes still loads on an older one."
- **Rebuild notes:** Two middleware shapes (rewrite vs wrap), an immutable `original_*` copy for auditing, single-use `next_call`, and unknown-kind tolerance for forward compatibility.

### Plugin CLI commands, slash commands and `dispatch_tool`  `id: docs-rest.dev-plugin-commands`
- **Surface:** Docs / CLI / Gateway/Telegram
- **Where:** Build a Plugin → "### Register CLI commands", "### Register slash commands", "### Dispatch tools from slash commands", "### Act from inside a hook (profile + tools)".
- **What it does:** Three command surfaces a plugin can add, plus the supported way to invoke tools from them.
- **How it works:** **CLI** — `ctx.register_cli_command(name="my-plugin", help="Manage my plugin", setup_fn=_setup_argparse, handler_fn=_my_command)`; the setup fn builds an argparse subtree (`subs = subparser.add_subparsers(dest="my_command")`, `subs.add_parser("status", help=...)`, `subparser.set_defaults(func=...)`). "After registration, users can run `hermes my-plugin status`, `hermes my-plugin config`, etc."
  **Slash** — `ctx.register_command(name, handler, description="", args_hint="")`. Parameter table verbatim: `name` `str` "Command name without the leading slash (e.g. `\"lcm\"`, `\"mystatus\"`)"; `handler` `Callable[[str], str | None]` "Called with the raw argument string. May also be `async`."; `description` `str` "Shown in `/help`, autocomplete, and Telegram bot menu". "After registration, users can type `/mystatus` in any session. The command appears in autocomplete, `/help` output, and the Telegram bot menu."
  Comparison table verbatim (`register_command()` | `register_cli_command()`): "Invoked as" `/name` in a session | `hermes name` in a terminal; "Where it works" "CLI sessions, Telegram, Discord, etc." | "Terminal only"; "Handler receives" "Raw args string" | "argparse `Namespace`"; "Use case" "Diagnostics, status, quick actions" | "Complex subcommand trees, setup wizards".
  **`dispatch_tool`** — signature verbatim `ctx.dispatch_tool(name: str, args: dict, *, parent_agent=None) -> str`; parameters: `name` "Tool name as registered in the tool registry (e.g. `\"delegate_task\"`, `\"file_edit\"`)"; `args` "Tool arguments, same shape the model would send"; `parent_agent` "Optional override. When omitted, resolves from the current CLI agent (or degrades gracefully in gateway mode)". Runtime behaviour verbatim: "**CLI mode:** `parent_agent` is resolved from the active CLI agent so workspace hints, spinner, and model selection inherit as expected. **Gateway mode:** There is no CLI agent, so tools degrade gracefully — workspace is read from the configured terminal working directory and no spinner is shown. **Explicit override:** If the caller passes `parent_agent=` explicitly, it is respected and not overwritten." "The dispatched tool goes through the normal approval, redaction, and budget pipelines — it's a real tool invocation, not a shortcut around them."
  **Acting from a hook** verbatim: "`ctx._cli_ref` is only populated in an **interactive CLI** session. It is `None` in the gateway, in non-interactive `hermes chat -q` runs, and in **kanban-spawned worker sessions**". Two stable APIs verbatim: "**`ctx.profile_name`** — the active profile name (e.g. `\"default\"`, or the assignee profile in a kanban worker). Derived from `HERMES_HOME`, so it works everywhere with no `_cli_ref` dependency."; "**`ctx.dispatch_tool(name, args)`** — invoke any registered tool (built-in or plugin), including the `kanban_*` tools, `delegate_task`, `terminal`, `read_file`, etc. Works from hook callbacks regardless of which process the hook fires in."
- **Inputs / options:** As above; async slash handlers supported ("The gateway dispatch automatically detects and awaits async handlers").
- **Outputs / side effects:** New `hermes <name>` subcommand; new `/name` command in every session surface.
- **Config / env:** n/a
- **Edge cases / guards:** Conflict protection verbatim: "If a plugin tries to register a name that conflicts with a built-in command (`help`, `model`, `new`, etc.), the registration is silently rejected with a log warning. Built-in commands always take precedence." Memory-provider CLI convention verbatim: "**Memory provider plugins** use a convention-based approach instead: add a `register_cli(subparser)` function to your plugin's `cli.py` file… **Active-provider gating:** Memory plugin CLI commands only appear when their provider is the active `memory.provider` in config." Headless note verbatim: "For running a full `hermes <subcommand>` (e.g. `hermes kanban show`), shell out with the `terminal` tool via `ctx.dispatch_tool(\"terminal\", {\"command\": \"hermes kanban show ...\"})` — there is no in-process slash-command bridge for headless worker sessions". "Plugins should not reach into `ctx._cli_ref.agent` or similar private state."
- **Rebuild notes:** Two command registries (terminal argparse vs in-session raw-string) plus one tool-dispatch API that reuses the parent agent's approval/credential context, so plugin commands are indistinguishable from model-driven calls.

### Native platform handlers and Slack action handlers  `id: docs-rest.dev-plugin-platform-handlers`
- **Surface:** Docs / Gateway/Telegram
- **Where:** Build a Plugin → "### Handle Slack Block Kit button clicks" and "### Register native platform handlers (any platform)".
- **What it does:** Lets a plugin receive platform-native events the core adapter doesn't route — extra update types, native buttons, reaction/member events, custom webhook routes.
- **How it works:** Slack signature verbatim: `ctx.register_slack_action_handler(action_id, callback) -> None`; parameters: `action_id` "`str | re.Pattern | dict` — Whatever `slack_bolt.App.action()` accepts: a literal `action_id`, a compiled regex matching multiple ids, or a constraint dict like `{\"action_id\": \"...\", \"block_id\": \"...\"}`"; `callback` "async callable — Receives `(ack, body, action)` per the slack_bolt convention". Runtime verbatim: "The handler is queued at plugin-load time and wired into the adapter's `slack_bolt.AsyncApp` when the Slack platform connects. Each callback is wrapped defensively: if your handler raises, the gateway logs the error and best-effort-acks the click so Slack stops retrying. Standard slack_bolt rules apply — `await ack()` within 3 seconds… For multi-workspace deployments the handler fires for clicks from any connected workspace; use `body[\"team\"][\"id\"]` if you need to scope behaviour."
  Generic signature verbatim: `ctx.register_platform_handler(platform, factory) -> None`; `platform` "Gateway platform name, lowercase (`\"telegram\"`, `\"discord\"`, `\"slack\"`, `\"matrix\"`, ...)"; `factory` "Receives `(native, adapter)` at connect time".
  `native` object per platform, all rows verbatim (Platform | `native` object | Typical hooks): `telegram` "PTB `Application`" "`add_handler` — any update type, pattern-scoped callbacks"; `discord` "`discord.ext.commands.Bot`" "`add_listener` — reactions, member events, threads, voice"; `slack` "`slack_bolt.AsyncApp`" "`app.event()` / `app.action()` / `app.command()`"; `matrix` "Matrix client" "event callbacks"; `teams` "Teams `App`" "`on_message` / `on_card_action` decorators"; `dingtalk` "`DingTalkStreamClient`" "`register_callback_handler` for other stream topics"; `feishu` "lark_oapi client" "API calls; event routing"; `line`, `api_server`, `msgraph_webhook` "aiohttp `web.Application`" "`router.add_get/post` — custom routes (wired before the router freezes)"; "everything else (whatsapp, signal, irc, email, sms, ntfy, wecom, weixin, bluebubbles, yuanbao, ...)" `None` "connect-time hook; work through the `adapter` handle".
- **Inputs / options:** Back-compat alias verbatim: "`ctx.register_telegram_handler(factory)` is a back-compat alias for `ctx.register_platform_handler(\"telegram\", factory)`." Two worked examples transcribed: Telegram `CallbackQueryHandler(_on_button, pattern=r"^myplugin:")`; Discord `bot.add_listener(on_raw_reaction_add, "on_raw_reaction_add")`.
- **Outputs / side effects:** Handlers attached to the platform's native client at connect time.
- **Config / env:** n/a
- **Edge cases / guards:** Runtime rules verbatim: "Factories are queued at plugin-load time and invoked when the platform connects — for platforms where dispatch order matters (Telegram, Slack, Teams, aiohttp routers) they run **before** the core handlers register, so scoped plugin handlers take precedence and everything else falls through."; "**Always scope handlers you add to first-match dispatch tables.** On Telegram, use `CallbackQueryHandler(..., pattern=r\"^myplugin:\")` — an unscoped handler would swallow the core button flows (exec approvals, model picker, clarify prompts)."; "Each factory is isolated: if it raises, the error is logged and the platform still connects."; "Import platform SDKs inside the factory body, not at module level — `register()` must work when the SDK isn't installed."; "One plugin can register factories for several platforms; each fires only when its platform connects." "Older plugins may patch `SlackAdapter.connect`; prefer this API instead."
- **Rebuild notes:** Hand the plugin the platform's native client at connect time instead of proxying every event type; run plugin handlers before core ones in first-match tables and require them to be pattern-scoped.

### Specialized plugin types (5 categories) and non-Python extension surfaces  `id: docs-rest.dev-plugin-specialized`
- **Surface:** Docs / Core
- **Where:** Build a Plugin → "## Specialized plugin types" and "## Non-Python extension surfaces".
- **What it does:** Sketches the authoring pattern for each specialized plugin category and each non-Python extension style.
- **How it works:** Verbatim: "Hermes has five specialized plugin types beyond the general surface. Each ships as a directory under `plugins/<category>/<name>/` (bundled) or `~/.hermes/plugins/<category>/<name>/` (user)."
  **Model provider** — `plugins/model-providers/<name>/__init__.py` calling `register_provider(ProviderProfile(name=, aliases=, display_name=, env_vars=, base_url=, auth_type=, default_aux_model=, fallback_models=))`; manifest `kind: model-provider`. "Lazy-discovered the first time anything calls `get_provider_profile()` or `list_providers()` — `auth.py`, `config.py`, `doctor.py`, `models.py`, `runtime_provider.py`, and the chat_completions transport auto-wire to it. User plugins override bundled ones by name."
  **Platform** — `plugins/platforms/<name>/adapter.py` subclassing `BasePlatformAdapter` with `connect()`, `send(chat_id, text)`, `disconnect()`, plus `check_requirements()` and `_env_enablement()`; registration `ctx.register_platform(name=, label=, adapter_factory=, check_fn=, required_env=, env_enablement_fn=, cron_deliver_env_var=, emoji=, platform_hint=)`; manifest fields `name`, `label`, `kind: platform`, `version`, `description`, `requires_env` (with `password: true`), `optional_env`. "Look at `plugins/platforms/irc/` for a stdlib-only working example."
  **Memory provider** — `plugins/memory/<name>/__init__.py` subclassing `MemoryProvider` with `name` property, `is_available()`, `initialize(session_id, **kwargs)`, `sync_turn(user_content, assistant_content, *, session_id="", messages=None)`, `prefetch(query, *, session_id="")`, `get_tool_schemas()` ("required @abstractmethod"); registered via `ctx.register_memory_provider(...)`. "Memory providers are single-select — only one is active at a time, chosen via `memory.provider` in `config.yaml`."
  **Context engine** — `plugins/context_engine/<name>/__init__.py` subclassing `ContextEngine` with `name`, `update_from_response(usage)`, `should_compress(prompt_tokens=None)`, `compress(messages, current_tokens=None, focus_topic=None, force=False, memory_context="")`; registered via `ctx.register_context_engine(...)`. "Context engines are single-select — chosen via `context.engine` in `config.yaml`."
  **Image generation** — `plugins/image_gen/<name>/__init__.py` subclassing `ImageGenProvider` with `name`, `is_available()`, `generate(prompt, aspect_ratio="landscape", **kwargs) -> dict` returning `success_response(...)` / `error_response(...)`; manifest `kind: backend`; registered via `ctx.register_image_gen_provider(...)`. Reference examples verbatim: "`plugins/image_gen/openai/` (DALL-E / GPT-Image via OpenAI SDK), `plugins/image_gen/openai-codex/`, `plugins/image_gen/xai/` (Grok image gen)."
  Non-Python surfaces, all five verbatim: **MCP servers** ("Declare them in `~/.hermes/config.yaml`" — `mcp_servers.filesystem.{command,args,timeout}`, `mcp_servers.linear.{url,auth.type}`; "Hermes connects to each server at startup, lists its tools, and registers them alongside built-ins."); **Gateway event hooks** (`~/.hermes/hooks/<name>/HOOK.yaml` with `name`/`description`/`events`, plus `handler.py` defining `async def handle(event_type: str, context: dict) -> None`; events "`gateway:startup`, `session:start`, `session:end`, `session:reset`, `agent:start`, `agent:step`, `agent:end`, and wildcard `command:*`"); **Shell hooks** (`hooks:` list in `config.yaml` with `event`, `command` (supports `{tool_name}`), `when.tools`; "Supports all the same events as Python plugin hooks (`pre_tool_call`, `post_tool_call`, `pre_llm_call`, `post_llm_call`, `on_session_start`, `on_session_end`, `pre_gateway_dispatch`) plus structured JSON output for `pre_tool_call` blocking decisions."); **Skill sources / taps** (`hermes skills tap add myorg/skills-repo`, `hermes skills search my-workflow --source myorg/skills-repo`, `hermes skills install myorg/skills-repo/my-workflow`; "Publishing your own tap is just a GitHub repo with `skills/<skill-name>/SKILL.md` directories — no server or registry signup needed."); **TTS / STT command templates** (`tts.providers.<name>.{type: command, command, output_format, voice_compatible}`; `HERMES_LOCAL_STT_COMMAND` "argv-tokenized template… It runs without implicit shell interpretation; wrap it in `sh -c`, `cmd /c`, or PowerShell explicitly if the trusted local command requires shell syntax." Placeholders verbatim: "`{input_path}`, `{output_path}`, `{format}`, `{voice}`, `{model}`, `{speed}` (TTS); `{input_path}`, `{output_dir}`, `{language}`, `{model}` (STT). Any path-interacting CLI is automatically a plugin.").
- **Inputs / options:** As above.
- **Outputs / side effects:** Registered providers/adapters/engines.
- **Config / env:** `memory.provider`, `context.engine`, `mcp_servers.*`, `hooks`, `tts.provider`/`tts.providers.*`, `HERMES_LOCAL_STT_COMMAND`.
- **Edge cases / guards:** Single-select categories (memory, context engine) are chosen by config key, not by `plugins.enabled`.
- **Rebuild notes:** One directory convention per extension category with a category-specific ABC and a single `ctx.register_<category>(...)` call; config-driven surfaces (MCP, shell hooks, TTS/STT command templates) let non-programmers extend the agent with no code.

### Distributing plugins (pip entry points, NixOS)  `id: docs-rest.dev-plugin-distribution`
- **Surface:** Docs
- **Where:** Build a Plugin → "## Distribute via pip" and "## Distribute for NixOS".
- **What it does:** The two supported distribution channels for third-party plugins.
- **How it works:** pip verbatim: `[project.entry-points."hermes_agent.plugins"]` with `my-plugin = "my_plugin_package"`, then `pip install hermes-plugin-calculator` — "Plugin auto-discovered on next hermes startup". NixOS verbatim: entry-point plugins via `services.hermes-agent.extraPythonPackages` with `pkgs.python312Packages.buildPythonPackage` (`pname`, `version`, `src = pkgs.fetchFromGitHub {owner, repo, rev, hash}`, `format = "pyproject"`, `build-system = [ pkgs.python312Packages.setuptools ]`; hash via `nix-prefetch-url --unpack`); directory plugins via `services.hermes-agent.extraPlugins` with a bare `fetchFromGitHub`.
- **Inputs / options:** As above.
- **Outputs / side effects:** Discovery via `importlib.metadata` entry points or a symlink into `$HERMES_HOME/plugins/`.
- **Config / env:** n/a
- **Edge cases / guards:** **":::warning Nix is no longer explicitly supported"** — "This section is kept for users already deploying on NixOS."
- **Rebuild notes:** Support both a code-packaged channel (entry points) and a file-drop channel (directory), so a plugin can be shipped with or without packaging.

### Model Provider Plugins  `id: docs-rest.dev-model-provider-plugin`
- **Surface:** Docs / Provider
- **Where:** Developer Guide → **"Model Provider Plugins"** (`developer-guide/model-provider-plugin.md`, `sidebar_position: 10`), heading **"Building a Model Provider Plugin"**.
- **What it does:** Documents the "drop a directory, declare a profile, no repo edits" way to add an inference backend.
- **How it works:** Discovery verbatim: "`providers/__init__.py._discover_providers()` runs lazily the first time any code calls `get_provider_profile()` or `list_providers()`. Discovery order: 1. **Bundled plugins** — `<repo>/plugins/model-providers/<name>/`; 2. **User plugins** — `$HERMES_HOME/plugins/model-providers/<name>/` — drop in any directory; no restart required for subsequent sessions; 3. **Legacy single-file** — `<repo>/providers/<name>.py` — back-compat for out-of-tree editable installs." "**User plugins override bundled plugins of the same name** because `register_provider()` is last-writer-wins."
  Directory layout verbatim: `__init__.py` "Calls register_provider(profile) at module-level"; `plugin.yaml` "kind: model-provider + metadata (optional but recommended)"; `README.md` "Setup instructions (optional)". "The only required file is `__init__.py`."
  Auto-wiring table, all nine rows verbatim (Integration | Where | What it gets): "Credential resolution" `hermes_cli/auth.py` "`PROVIDER_REGISTRY[\"acme-inference\"]` populated from profile"; "`--provider` CLI flag" `hermes_cli/main.py` "Accepts `acme-inference`"; "`hermes model` picker" `hermes_cli/models.py` "Appears in `CANONICAL_PROVIDERS`, model list fetched from `{base_url}/models`"; "`hermes doctor`" `hermes_cli/doctor.py` "Health check for `ACME_API_KEY` + `{base_url}/models` probe"; "`hermes setup`" `hermes_cli/config.py` "`ACME_API_KEY` appears in `OPTIONAL_ENV_VARS` and the setup wizard"; "URL reverse-mapping" `agent/model_metadata.py` "Hostname → provider name for auto-detection"; "Auxiliary model" `agent/auxiliary_client.py` "Uses `default_aux_model` for compression / summarization"; "Runtime resolution" `hermes_cli/runtime_provider.py` "Returns correct `base_url`, `api_key`, `api_mode`"; "Transport" `agent/transports/chat_completions.py` "Profile path generates kwargs via `prepare_messages` / `build_extra_body` / `build_api_kwargs_extras`".
- **Inputs / options:** `ProviderProfile` fields, all 16 verbatim (Field | Type | Purpose): `name` str "Canonical id — matches `model.provider` in `config.yaml` and the `--provider` flag"; `aliases` `tuple[str, ...]` "Alternative names resolved by `get_provider_profile()` (e.g. `grok` → `xai`)"; `api_mode` str "`chat_completions` | `codex_responses` | `anthropic_messages` | `bedrock_converse`"; `display_name` str "Human label shown in `hermes model` picker"; `description` str "Picker subtitle"; `signup_url` str "Shown during first-run setup (\"get an API key here\")"; `env_vars` `tuple[str, ...]` "API-key env vars in priority order; a final `*_BASE_URL` entry is used as the user base-URL override"; `base_url` str "Default inference endpoint"; `models_url` str "Explicit catalog URL (falls back to `{base_url}/models`)"; `auth_type` str "`api_key` | `oauth_device_code` | `oauth_external` | `copilot` | `aws_sdk` | `external_process`"; `fallback_models` `tuple[str, ...]` "Curated list shown when live catalog fetch fails"; `default_headers` `dict[str, str]` "Sent on every request (e.g. Copilot's `Editor-Version`)"; `fixed_temperature` Any "`None` = use caller's value; `OMIT_TEMPERATURE` sentinel = don't send temperature at all (Kimi)"; `default_max_tokens` `int | None` "Provider-level max_tokens cap (Nvidia: 16384)"; `default_aux_model` str "Cheap model for auxiliary tasks (compression, vision, summarization)". Also referenced elsewhere: `supported_reasoning_efforts`.
  Overridable hooks, all four verbatim: `prepare_messages(messages)` "Provider-specific message preprocessing. Runs after codex sanitization, before developer-role swap. Default: pass-through."; `build_extra_body(*, session_id=None, **context)` "Provider-specific extra_body fields merged into the API call. Context includes: session_id, provider_preferences, model, base_url, reasoning_config. Default: empty dict."; `build_api_kwargs_extras(*, reasoning_config=None, **context)` "Returns (extra_body_additions, top_level_kwargs). Needed when some fields go top-level (Kimi's reasoning_effort, OpenRouter's verbosity for adaptive Anthropic models) and some go in extra_body (OpenRouter's reasoning dict). Default: ({}, {})."; `fetch_models(*, api_key=None, base_url=None, timeout=8.0)` "Live catalog fetch. Default hits {models_url or base_url}/models with Bearer auth. Override for: custom auth (Anthropic), no REST endpoint (Bedrock → None), or public/unauthenticated catalogs (OpenRouter)."
  Reference plugins verbatim (Plugin | Why look): `plugins/model-providers/openrouter/` "Aggregator with provider preferences, public model catalog"; `plugins/model-providers/gemini/` "`thinking_config` translation (native + OpenAI-compat nested forms)"; `plugins/model-providers/kimi-coding/` "`OMIT_TEMPERATURE`, `extra_body.thinking`, top-level `reasoning_effort`"; `plugins/model-providers/qwen-oauth/` "Message normalization, `cache_control` injection, VL high-res"; `plugins/model-providers/nous/` "Attribution tags, \"omit reasoning when disabled\""; `plugins/model-providers/custom/` "Ollama `num_ctx` + `think: false` quirks"; `plugins/model-providers/bedrock/` "`api_mode=\"bedrock_converse\"`, `fetch_models` returns None (no REST endpoint)".
  `api_mode` selection order verbatim: "1. User explicit override (`config.yaml` `model.api_mode` when set); 2. OpenCode's per-model dispatch (`opencode_model_api_mode` for Zen and Go); 3. URL auto-detection — `/anthropic` suffix → `anthropic_messages`, `api.openai.com` → `codex_responses`, `api.x.ai` → `codex_responses`, `/coding` on Kimi domains → `chat_completions`; 4. **Profile `api_mode`** as a fallback when URL detection finds nothing; 5. Default `chat_completions`."
  Auth types table, all six verbatim (`auth_type` | Meaning | Who uses it): `api_key` "Single env var carries a static API key" "Most providers"; `oauth_device_code` "Device-code OAuth flow" "—"; `oauth_external` "User signs in elsewhere, tokens land in `auth.json`" "Anthropic OAuth, MiniMax OAuth, Qwen Portal, Nous Portal"; `copilot` "GitHub Copilot token refresh cycle" "`copilot` plugin only"; `aws_sdk` "AWS SDK credential chain (IAM role, profile, env)" "`bedrock` plugin only"; `external_process` "Auth handled by a subprocess the agent spawns" "`copilot-acp` plugin only".
- **Outputs / side effects:** A registered profile visible to auth, CLI, doctor, setup, aux routing, and the transport.
- **Config / env:** `model.provider`, `model.api_mode`, `plugins.enabled` / `plugins.disabled` (for pip entry-point providers).
- **Edge cases / guards:** pip distribution verbatim: entry-point group `hermes_agent.plugins`; the target may be "a **callable** (`module:func`) — invoked with no arguments; it should call `register_provider(profile)`" or "a **bare module** (`module`) — imported for its module-level `register_provider(...)` side effect". Two rules verbatim: "**Opt-in required.** The same `plugins.enabled` allow-list (and `plugins.disabled` deny-list) from `config.yaml` governs this scan. A pip package is never imported just because it is installed"; "**Lowest precedence.** Entry-point plugins are discovered **before** filesystem plugins: because `register_provider()` is last-writer-wins, a bundled or `$HERMES_HOME` profile of the same name always overrides a pip-installed one. A pip package can add a genuinely new provider, but cannot silently hijack a first-party provider name." "Targets that require arguments (a general plugin's `register(ctx)`) are skipped by the provider scan… A broken entry point is isolated — it is logged at warning level and skipped." PluginManager relationship verbatim: "The general `PluginManager`… **sees** model-provider plugins but does not import them — `providers/__init__.py` owns their lifecycle… the manager auto-coerces it to `kind: model-provider` via a source-text heuristic — so the plugin still routes correctly even without `plugin.yaml`." `auth_type` gating verbatim: "if it's not `api_key`, the PluginManager still records the manifest but Hermes' CLI-level automation (doctor checks, `--provider` flag, setup wizard delegation) may skip over it." Testing recipe transcribed with `export HERMES_HOME=/tmp/hermes-plugin-test` and `hermes -z "hello" --provider my-provider -m some-model`; inspection via `from providers import list_providers`.
- **Rebuild notes:** A provider is a declarative profile plus four optional transform hooks; discovery is lazy and last-writer-wins across three layered sources, so users can shadow any built-in without patching the repo.

### Adding a first-class (core) provider  `id: docs-rest.dev-adding-providers`
- **Surface:** Docs / Provider
- **Where:** Developer Guide → **"Adding Providers"** (`developer-guide/adding-providers.md`, `sidebar_position: 5`).
- **What it does:** The end-to-end checklist for wiring a built-in inference provider, including when *not* to.
- **How it works:** When to add verbatim: "Do not add a built-in provider unless you want first-class UX for that service: provider-specific auth or token refresh; a curated model catalog; setup / `hermes model` menu entries; provider aliases for `provider:model` syntax; a non-OpenAI API shape that needs an adapter. If the provider is just \"another OpenAI-compatible base URL and API key\", a named custom provider may be enough."
  Mental model verbatim: "1. `hermes_cli/auth.py` decides how credentials are found. 2. `hermes_cli/runtime_provider.py` turns that into runtime data: `provider`, `api_mode`, `base_url`, `api_key`, `source`. 3. `run_agent.py` uses `api_mode` to decide how requests are built and sent. 4. `hermes_cli/models.py` and `hermes_cli/main.py` make the provider show up in the CLI. (`hermes_cli/setup.py` delegates to `main.py` automatically — no changes needed there.) 5. `agent/auxiliary_client.py` and `agent/model_metadata.py` keep side tasks and token budgeting working."
  api_mode notes verbatim: "Most providers use `chat_completions`. Codex and Meta Model API (`api.meta.ai` — Muse Spark) use `codex_responses` (auto-sends `prompt_cache_retention: 24h` for prompt caching; `api.meta.ai` achieves 93–99% cache hits only on `/v1/responses`). Ramp Router (`api.router.com`) also uses `codex_responses` — Responses is Router's native wire (`/v1/chat/completions` is only a minimal compatibility shim), and it validates `reasoning.effort` per model, which the router profile handles by declaring each model's vocabulary from the live catalog (`ProviderProfile.supported_reasoning_efforts`). Anthropic uses `anthropic_messages`."
  Ten steps verbatim: "Step 1: Pick one canonical provider id"; "Step 2: Add auth metadata in `hermes_cli/auth.py`"; "Step 3: Add model catalog and aliases in `hermes_cli/models.py`"; "Step 4: Resolve runtime data in `hermes_cli/runtime_provider.py`"; "Step 5: Wire the CLI in `hermes_cli/main.py`"; "Step 6: Keep auxiliary calls working"; "Step 7: If the provider is native, add an adapter and `run_agent.py` support"; "Step 8: Tests"; "Step 9: Live verification"; "Step 10: Update user-facing docs".
- **Inputs / options:** Required file checklist verbatim: "1. `hermes_cli/auth.py`; 2. `hermes_cli/models.py`; 3. `hermes_cli/runtime_provider.py`; 4. `hermes_cli/main.py`; 5. `agent/auxiliary_client.py`; 6. `agent/model_metadata.py`; 7. tests; 8. user-facing docs under `website/docs/`" plus for native providers "10. `agent/<provider>_adapter.py`; 11. `run_agent.py`; 12. `pyproject.toml` if a provider SDK is required". Runtime dict shape verbatim: `{"provider": ..., "api_mode": "chat_completions", "base_url": ..., "api_key": ..., "source": "env|portal|auth-store|explicit", "requested_provider": requested_provider}`. `hermes_cli/main.py` edits verbatim: "`provider_labels` dict; `providers` list in `select_provider_and_model()`; provider dispatch (`if selected_provider == ...`); `--provider` argument choices; login/logout choices if the provider supports those flows; a `_model_flow_<provider>()` function, or reuse `_model_flow_api_key_provider()` if it fits". `hermes_cli/models.py` edits verbatim: "`_PROVIDER_MODELS`; `_PROVIDER_LABELS`; `_PROVIDER_ALIASES`; provider display order inside `list_available_providers()`; `provider_model_ids()` if the provider supports a live `/models` fetch". Native-provider `run_agent.py` audit list verbatim: "`__init__` chooses the new `api_mode`; client construction works for the provider; `_build_api_kwargs()` knows how to format requests; `_interruptible_api_call()` dispatches to the right client call; interrupt / client rebuild paths work; response validation accepts the provider's shape; finish-reason extraction is correct; token-usage extraction is correct; fallback-model activation can switch into the new provider cleanly; summary-generation and memory-flush paths still work." Search targets verbatim: `PROVIDER_REGISTRY`, `_PROVIDER_ALIASES`, `_PROVIDER_MODELS`, `resolve_runtime_provider`, `_model_flow_`, `select_provider_and_model`, `api_mode`, `_API_KEY_PROVIDER_AUX_MODELS`, `self.client.`. Test files verbatim: `tests/hermes_cli/test_runtime_provider_resolution.py`, `tests/cli/test_cli_provider_resolution.py`, `tests/hermes_cli/test_model_switch_custom_providers.py` (+ adjacent `test_model_switch_*.py`), `tests/hermes_cli/test_setup_model_provider.py`, `tests/run_agent/test_provider_parity.py`, `tests/run_agent/test_run_agent.py`, `tests/test_<provider>_adapter.py`. Commands verbatim: `python -m pytest tests/hermes_cli/test_runtime_provider_resolution.py tests/cli/test_cli_provider_resolution.py tests/hermes_cli/test_setup_model_provider.py tests/run_agent/test_provider_parity.py -q`; `python -m pytest tests/ -n0 -q`; `python -m hermes_cli.main chat -q "Say hello" --provider your-provider --model your-model`; `python -m hermes_cli.main model`; `python -m hermes_cli.main setup`. Docs to update verbatim: `website/docs/getting-started/quickstart.md`, `website/docs/user-guide/configuration.md`, `website/docs/reference/environment-variables.md`.
- **Outputs / side effects:** A provider available across auth, CLI, runtime, aux, and docs.
- **Config / env:** As above.
- **Edge cases / guards:** Seven "Common pitfalls" verbatim: "1. Adding the provider to auth but not to model parsing — That makes credentials resolve correctly while `/model` and `provider:model` inputs fail. 2. Forgetting that `config[\"model\"]` can be a string or a dict. 3. Assuming a built-in provider is required. 4. Forgetting auxiliary paths — The main chat path can work while summarization, memory flushes, or vision helpers fail because aux routing was never updated. 5. Native-provider branches hiding in `run_agent.py` — Search for `api_mode` and `self.client.`. 6. Sending OpenRouter-only knobs to other providers. 7. Updating `hermes model` but not `hermes setup`." Wire-format note verbatim: "Hermes stores conversation history in the OpenAI chat-completions shape internally, so the `chat_completions` transport's `convert_messages` / `convert_tools` (`agent/transports/chat_completions.py`) are near-identity, and every other transport converts *from* that shape into its native protocol." Fast-path list of 12 things that auto-wire from `register_provider()` is transcribed in the doc.
- **Rebuild notes:** Publish both a "fast path" (declarative plugin) and a "full path" (core integration) checklist, and enumerate every file a provider touches so half-wired providers are impossible.

### Context Engine Plugins  `id: docs-rest.dev-context-engine-plugin`
- **Surface:** Docs / Core
- **Where:** Developer Guide → **"Context Engine Plugins"** (`developer-guide/context-engine-plugin.md`, `sidebar_position: 9`), heading **"Building a Context Engine Plugin"**.
- **What it does:** Documents replacing the built-in `ContextCompressor` with an alternative context-management strategy (e.g. "a Lossless Context Management (LCM) engine that builds a knowledge DAG instead of lossy summarization").
- **How it works:** Selection verbatim: "Only **one** context engine can be active at a time. Selection is config-driven: `context.engine: \"compressor\"` (default built-in) / `context.engine: \"lcm\"` (activates a plugin engine named \"lcm\"). Plugin engines are **never auto-activated** — the user must explicitly set `context.engine` to the plugin's name." Layout: `plugins/context_engine/<name>/{__init__.py, plugin.yaml, ...}`.
  Required methods verbatim: `name` property ("Short identifier, e.g. 'lcm'. Must match config.yaml value."); `update_from_response(usage: dict) -> None` ("Called after every LLM call with the usage dict. Update self.last_prompt_tokens, self.last_completion_tokens, self.last_total_tokens from the response."); `should_compress(prompt_tokens: int = None) -> bool` ("Return True if compaction should fire this turn."); `compress(messages, current_tokens=None, focus_topic=None) -> list` ("Compact the message list and return a new (possibly shorter) list. The returned list must be a valid OpenAI-format message sequence. `focus_topic` is an optional topic string from manual `/compress <focus>`").
  Required class attributes verbatim: `last_prompt_tokens: int = 0`; `last_completion_tokens: int = 0`; `last_total_tokens: int = 0`; `threshold_tokens: int = 0` ("when compression triggers"); `context_length: int = 0` ("model's full context window"); `compression_count: int = 0` ("how many times compress() has run").
  Optional methods table, all ten verbatim (Method | Default | Override when): `on_session_start(session_id, **kwargs)` "No-op" "You need to load persisted state (DAG, DB)"; `on_session_end(session_id, messages)` "No-op" "You need to flush state, close connections"; `on_session_reset()` "Resets token counters" "You have per-session state to clear"; `update_model(model, context_length, ...)` "Updates context_length + threshold" "You need to recalculate budgets on model switch"; `get_tool_schemas()` "Returns `[]`" "Your engine provides agent-callable tools (e.g., `lcm_grep`)"; `handle_tool_call(name, args, **kwargs)` "Returns error JSON" "You implement tool handlers"; `should_compress_preflight(messages)` "Returns `False`" "You can do a cheap pre-API-call estimate"; `get_status()` "Standard token/threshold dict" "You have custom metrics to expose"; `select_context(request_messages, *, conversation_messages, incoming_message, budget_tokens)` "Returns `None` (no-op)" "You select/route which context enters **this** request (retrieval, topic routing)"; `on_turn_complete(messages, usage=None, **kwargs)` "No-op" "You ingest/index/observe the finished turn".
- **Inputs / options:** `select_context()` docstring verbatim: "Choose/replace the context for THIS request, before dispatch. Return a new message list to use for this one provider call (retrieval, topic routing, role/branch switching), or None to leave it unchanged. Request-only: the persisted conversation history is never mutated." `on_turn_complete()` docstring verbatim: "Observe a finished turn after the assistant/tool loop completes. Receives a shallow copy of the finalized transcript plus the turn's canonical usage dict (or None if no provider response was reached), so the engine can ingest/index/summarize for the next select_context(). The return value is ignored."
- **Outputs / side effects:** Replaces the compression strategy for the whole agent; may add agent-callable tools.
- **Config / env:** `context.engine`.
- **Edge cases / guards:** Contract verbatim: "**No-op by default, fail-open.** Both default to `return None`. A missing hook, an exception, or an invalid return value leaves the request untouched — so a failing engine is never worse than not installing one. The host also identity-checks for the inherited ABC default and skips it entirely, so non-implementing engines (including the built-in compressor) pay no per-request work at all."; "**`select_context()` is request-only.** The returned list replaces the messages for a single provider call; persisted history is never written. Returning `None`, `[]`, a non-list, or a list containing non-dicts all fall open to the unmodified request."; "**Ordering / cache stability.** The hook runs **before** prompt cache-control and every request sanitizer… Evaluated per provider request (re-runs on retries)."; "**`on_turn_complete()`** is post-turn observation only; treat `messages` as read-only. **Coverage is best-effort:** it fires from the standard turn-finalization seam. Some abnormal early-return paths in the loop (e.g. a content-policy block or a provider terminal failure) persist and return without routing through finalization, so they do not currently emit this hook."
- **Rebuild notes:** Separate "shorten because it's too long" (`compress`) from "choose what enters this request" (`select_context`) and "learn from the finished turn" (`on_turn_complete`); make every optional hook fail-open and identity-check the ABC default so non-implementers pay nothing.

### Memory Provider Plugins  `id: docs-rest.dev-memory-provider-plugin`
- **Surface:** Docs / Core
- **Where:** Developer Guide → **"Memory Provider Plugins"** (`developer-guide/memory-provider-plugin.md`, `sidebar_position: 8`), heading **"Building a Memory Provider Plugin"**.
- **What it does:** Documents building a cross-session knowledge backend beyond built-in `MEMORY.md` / `USER.md`.
- **How it works:** Discovery sources table, all four verbatim (Source | Location | Notes): Bundled `plugins/memory/<name>/` "Ships with Hermes. Closed to new providers — see CONTRIBUTING."; User `$HERMES_HOME/plugins/<name>/` "Dropped in by the user, per profile."; Project `./.hermes/plugins/<name>/` "Opt-in via `HERMES_ENABLE_PROJECT_PLUGINS=1`."; Package "`hermes_agent.memory_providers` entry point" "`pip install`, nothing to copy." Precedence verbatim: "Earlier sources win on a name collision, so a directory dropped into a working tree can never shadow a shipped provider." Note verbatim: "This is the reverse of the general plugin system's later-wins order. A memory provider is activated by *name* (`memory.provider`), so shadowing would silently redirect the agent's memory rather than merely override a tool." "Discovery only *enumerates* — it never imports a provider. Nothing runs until `memory.provider` names it."
  Required methods verbatim (Method | When Called | Must Implement?): `name` (property) "Always" **Yes**; `is_available()` "Agent init, before activation" "**Yes** — no network calls"; `initialize(session_id, **kwargs)` "Agent startup" **Yes**; `get_tool_schemas()` "After init, for tool injection" **Yes**; `handle_tool_call(tool_name, args, **kwargs)` "When agent uses your tools" "**Yes** (if you have tools)". Config methods: `get_config_schema()` "Declare config fields for `hermes memory setup`" **Yes**; `save_config(values, hermes_home)` "Write non-secret config to native location" "**Yes** (unless env-var-only)".
  Optional hooks table, all eight verbatim (Method | When Called | Use Case): `system_prompt_block()` "System prompt assembly" "Static provider info"; `prefetch(query, *, session_id="")` "Before each API call" "Return recalled context"; `queue_prefetch(query, *, session_id="")` "After each turn" "Pre-warm for next turn"; `sync_turn(user, assistant, *, session_id="", messages=None)` "After each completed turn" "Persist conversation"; `on_session_end(messages)` "Conversation ends" "Final extraction/flush"; `on_pre_compress(messages)` "Before context compression" "Save insights before discard"; `on_memory_write(action, target, content)` "Built-in memory writes" "Mirror to your backend"; `shutdown()` "Process exit" "Clean up connections".
  Config-schema field keys verbatim: `key`, `description`, `secret` ("→ written to .env"), `required`, `env_var` ("explicit env var name"), `url` ("where to get it"), `default`, `choices`. "Fields with `secret: True` and `env_var` go to `.env`. Non-secret fields are passed to `save_config()`."
- **Inputs / options:** Entry point verbatim: `def register(ctx): ctx.register_memory_provider(MyMemoryProvider())`; optional read-only skills via `ctx.register_skill("maintenance", SKILLS_DIR / "maintenance" / "SKILL.md", "Maintain the provider's memory store")` — "With the `my-provider` entry point active, the skill is available as `my-provider:maintenance` through `skill_view()`." `plugin.yaml` fields: `name`, `version`, `description`, `hooks` ("list hooks you implement"). pip entry point verbatim: `[project.entry-points."hermes_agent.memory_providers"]` with `my-provider = "my_provider:register"` — "A package entry point gets everything a directory install does, including the two files Hermes reads from disk rather than importing — `config_schema.py` (the dashboard config panel) and `cli.py` (your `hermes <provider>` subcommands)."
  CLI registration verbatim: "1. Add a `cli.py` file to your plugin directory; 2. Define a `register_cli(subparser)` function that builds the argparse tree; 3. The memory plugin system discovers it at startup via `discover_plugin_cli_commands()`; 4. Your commands appear under `hermes <provider-name> <subcommand>`." "**Active-provider gating:** Your CLI commands only appear when your provider is the active `memory.provider` in config." Reference: "`plugins/memory/honcho/cli.py` for a full example with 13 subcommands, cross-profile management (`--target-profile`), and config read/write."
- **Outputs / side effects:** Provider tools injected into the agent; writes to the provider's own store and optionally `$HERMES_HOME/<provider>.json`.
- **Config / env:** `memory.provider`, `compression.checkpoint_required` (default `false`), `compression.codex_responses_native`, `compression.micro_compact`, `HERMES_ENABLE_PROJECT_PLUGINS`.
- **Edge cases / guards:** **Pre-compress checkpoints (API v2)** verbatim: "`on_pre_compress()` is best-effort by default: if your provider raises, the host logs the failure and compression proceeds… For that case the host offers an opt-in checkpoint contract (API v2)": set `pre_compress_checkpoint_api_version = 2` and implement `on_pre_compress(self, messages, *, require_checkpoint=False)`. Enforcement verbatim: "With the gate on, compression **fails closed** before any lossy rewrite unless an active provider advertising the API completed its checkpoint: the uncompressed transcript is preserved, the compaction attempt errors with `BLOCKED_MISSING_PREREQUISITE`, and it can be retried once your store recovers." Scope verbatim: "The gate binds to every compaction authority, not just the Hermes summarizer: server-side native compaction (`compression.codex_responses_native`) is suppressed while the gate is armed, post-turn micro-compaction (`compression.micro_compact`) is forced off at agent init…, and the `codex_app_server` API mode is refused at agent init". Payload difference verbatim: "Version 1 providers (the implicit default…) keep the historical contract: the raw message list… Version 2 checkpoint providers receive normalized direct evidence instead: user/assistant text rows only — tool results, system messages, the `tool_calls` payload of assistant messages (their prose is kept), and prior compaction summaries are filtered host-side. Prior summaries are recognized via a persistent `_compressed_summary` message marker that survives process restarts." Idempotency verbatim: "**Checkpoints must be idempotent.** After a fail-closed block, the next compaction attempt calls `on_pre_compress()` again with the same transcript… Key your archive writes by content (for example a transcript digest) and upsert." Contract tests: `tests/agent/test_pre_compress_checkpoint_contract.py`.
  Threading verbatim: "**`sync_turn()` MUST be non-blocking.** If your backend has latency (API calls, LLM processing), run the work in a daemon thread" (worked example joins a previous thread with `timeout=5.0`). "`messages` is optional OpenAI-style conversation context as of the completed turn… Cloud providers should document what parts of `messages` are sent off-device. Tool calls and tool results may contain file paths, command output, or other workspace data."
