Profile isolation verbatim: "All storage paths **must** use the `hermes_home` kwarg from `initialize()`, not hardcoded `~/.hermes`" — CORRECT `get_hermes_home() / "my-provider"`, WRONG `Path("~/.hermes/my-provider").expanduser()`. Single-provider rule verbatim: "Only **one** external memory provider can be active at a time. If a user tries to register a second, the MemoryManager rejects it with a warning. This prevents tool schema bloat and conflicting backends." Setup-schema tip verbatim: "Providers with many options should keep the schema minimal — only include fields the user **must** configure… See the Supermemory provider for an example — it only prompts for the API key; all other options live in `supermemory.json`." Test entry points: `tests/agent/test_memory_provider.py`, `tests/agent/test_memory_session_switch.py`, `tests/agent/test_memory_user_id.py`, `tests/run_agent/test_memory_provider_init.py`; harness `MemoryManager().add_provider(...)`, `initialize_all(session_id=..., platform="cli")`, `handle_tool_call(...)`, `sync_all(...)`, `on_session_end([])`, `shutdown_all()`.
- **Rebuild notes:** Enumerate-but-don't-import discovery with earlier-source-wins precedence (because activation is by name), a non-blocking `sync_turn`, and a versioned fail-closed pre-compression checkpoint contract are the three non-obvious parts worth copying.

### Image Generation Provider Plugins  `id: docs-rest.dev-image-gen-plugin`
- **Surface:** Docs / Provider
- **Where:** Developer Guide → **"Image Generation Provider Plugins"** (`developer-guide/image-gen-provider-plugin.md`, `sidebar_position: 11`).
- **What it does:** Documents registering a backend that services every `image_generate` tool call.
- **How it works:** Discovery verbatim: "1. **Bundled** — `<repo>/plugins/image_gen/<name>/` (auto-loaded with `kind: backend`, always available); 2. **User** — `~/.hermes/plugins/image_gen/<name>/` (opt-in via `plugins.enabled`); 3. **Pip** — packages declaring a `hermes_agent.plugins` entry point." "Each plugin's `register(ctx)` function calls `ctx.register_image_gen_provider(...)` — that puts it into the registry in `agent/image_gen_registry.py`. The active provider is picked by `image_gen.provider` in `config.yaml`; `hermes tools` walks users through selection." Built-ins named verbatim: "OpenAI, OpenAI-Codex, xAI, FAL, Krea, DeepInfra, OpenRouter".
  ABC reference table, all seven verbatim (Member | Required | Default | Purpose): `name` ✅ — "Stable id used in `image_gen.provider` config"; `display_name` — `name.title()` "Label shown in `hermes tools`"; `is_available()` — `True` "Gate for missing creds/deps"; `list_models()` — `[]` "Catalog for `hermes tools` model picker"; `default_model()` — "first from `list_models()`" "Fallback when no model is configured"; `get_setup_schema()` — "minimal" "Picker metadata + env-var prompts"; `generate(prompt, aspect_ratio, **kwargs)` ✅ — "The call". Also documented: `capabilities()` — "Declare whether this backend supports image-to-image / editing… Default (if you omit this) is text-only: `{\"modalities\": [\"text\"], \"max_reference_images\": 0}`."
  Helpers imported verbatim from `agent.image_gen_provider`: `DEFAULT_ASPECT_RATIO`, `ImageGenProvider`, `error_response`, `normalize_reference_images`, `resolve_aspect_ratio`, `save_b64_image`, `success_response`.
- **Inputs / options:** `success_response(image=<url-or-absolute-path>, model=, prompt=, aspect_ratio="landscape"|"square"|"portrait", provider=, extra={...})`; `error_response(error=, error_type="provider_error"|"invalid_input"|"<exception class name>", provider=, model=, prompt=, aspect_ratio=)`. `list_models()` entry keys shown: `id`, `display`, `speed`, `strengths`, `price`. `get_setup_schema()` keys: `name`, `badge` ("optional; shown as a short tag in the picker"), `tag`, `env_vars` (each with `key`, `prompt`, `url`). `generate()` signature keywords: `prompt`, `aspect_ratio`, `image_url`, `reference_image_urls`, `**kwargs`. Manifest: `name`, `version`, `description`, `author`, `kind: backend`, `requires_env`.
- **Outputs / side effects:** Base64 output saved via `save_b64_image()` to `$HERMES_HOME/cache/images/<prefix>_<timestamp>_<uuid>.<ext>`; "Gateway delivery (Telegram photo bubble, Discord attachment) recognizes both URLs and absolute paths."
- **Config / env:** `image_gen.provider`, `plugins.enabled`.
- **Edge cases / guards:** "If no provider is registered, the tool surfaces a helpful error pointing at `hermes tools`." User overrides verbatim: "Drop a user plugin at `~/.hermes/plugins/image_gen/<name>/` with the same `name` property as a bundled one and enable it via `hermes plugins enable <name>` — the registry is last-writer-wins, so your version replaces the built-in." Testing recipe transcribed (`export HERMES_HOME=/tmp/hermes-imggen-test`, `hermes plugins enable my-backend`, append `image_gen: provider: my-backend` to config, then `hermes -z "Generate an image of a corgi in a spacesuit"`). Reference implementations verbatim: "`plugins/image_gen/openai/__init__.py` — gpt-image-2 at low/medium/high tiers as three virtual model IDs sharing one API model with different `quality` params"; "`plugins/image_gen/xai/__init__.py` — Grok Imagine via xAI. Different shape (URL output, simpler catalog)"; "`plugins/image_gen/openai-codex/__init__.py` — Codex-style Responses API variant reusing the OpenAI SDK with a different routing base URL."
- **Rebuild notes:** One ABC with two required members, envelope helpers so every backend returns the same dict, and a `capabilities()` declaration that feeds the tool's dynamic schema so the model only sees honored parameters.

### Video Generation Provider Plugins  `id: docs-rest.dev-video-gen-plugin`
- **Surface:** Docs / Provider
- **Where:** Developer Guide → **"Video Generation Provider Plugins"** (`developer-guide/video-gen-provider-plugin.md`, `sidebar_position: 12`).
- **What it does:** Documents registering a backend for `video_generate`, with one tool exposing both text-to-video and image-to-video.
- **How it works:** Unified surface verbatim: "**Text-to-video** — call with `prompt` only. The provider routes to its text-to-video endpoint. **Image-to-video** — call with `prompt` + `image_url`. The provider routes to its image-to-video endpoint. Edit and extend are intentionally out of scope." Discovery: `plugins/video_gen/<name>/` (bundled, `kind: backend`), `~/.hermes/plugins/video_gen/<name>/` (user, `plugins.enabled`), pip entry point; registration `ctx.register_video_gen_provider(...)`; selection `video_gen.provider`. "Unlike `image_generate`, there is no in-tree legacy backend — every provider is a plugin." Built-ins verbatim: "xAI, FAL, DeepInfra".
  `capabilities()` keys verbatim: `modalities`, `aspect_ratios`, `resolutions`, `min_duration`, `max_duration`, `supports_audio`, `supports_negative_prompt`, `max_reference_images`.
- **Inputs / options:** `video_generate` schema, all ten parameters verbatim (Parameter | What it does): `prompt` "Text instruction (required)"; `image_url` "When set → image-to-video; when omitted → text-to-video"; `reference_image_urls` "Style/character refs (provider-dependent)"; `duration` "Seconds — provider clamps"; `aspect_ratio` "`\"16:9\"`, `\"9:16\"`, `\"1:1\"`, ... — provider clamps"; `resolution` "`\"480p\"` / `\"540p\"` / `\"720p\"` / `\"1080p\"` — provider clamps"; `negative_prompt` "Content to avoid (Pixverse/Kling only)"; `audio` "Native audio (Veo3 / Pixverse pricing tier)"; `seed` "Reproducibility"; `model` "Override the active model/family". `generate()` signature keywords transcribed: `prompt`, `model`, `image_url`, `reference_image_urls`, `duration`, `aspect_ratio="16:9"`, `resolution="720p"`, `negative_prompt`, `audio`, `seed`, `**kwargs` ("always ignore unknown kwargs for forward-compat").
  Selection precedence verbatim: "1. `model=` keyword from the tool call; 2. `<PROVIDER>_VIDEO_MODEL` env var; 3. `video_gen.<provider>.model` in `config.yaml`; 4. `video_gen.model` in `config.yaml` (when it's one of your IDs); 5. Provider's `default_model()`."
- **Outputs / side effects:** Success keys verbatim: "`success`, `video` (URL or absolute path), `model`, `prompt`, `modality` (`\"text\"` or `\"image\"`), `aspect_ratio`, `duration`, `provider`, plus `extra`". Error keys verbatim: "`success`, `video` (None), `error`, `error_type`, `model`, `prompt`, `aspect_ratio`, `provider`". Artifact helpers verbatim: "If your backend returns base64, use `save_b64_video()` to write under `$HERMES_HOME/cache/videos/`. For raw bytes from a follow-up HTTP fetch, use `save_bytes_video()`. Otherwise return the upstream URL directly."
- **Config / env:** `video_gen.provider`, `video_gen.model`, `video_gen.<provider>.model`, `<PROVIDER>_VIDEO_MODEL`.
- **Edge cases / guards:** Model-family pattern verbatim (the FAL pattern): "When your backend has multiple endpoints per \"model\" — like FAL, where every family (Veo 3.1, Pixverse v6, Kling O3) has both a `/text-to-video` and an `/image-to-video` URL — represent each **family** as one catalog entry. Your `generate()` picks the right endpoint based on whether `image_url` was passed… The user picks `veo3.1` once in `hermes tools`. The agent never thinks about endpoints." "The agent sees the active backend's capabilities in the tool description, dynamically rebuilt when the user changes backend via `hermes tools`." Testing verbatim: "Drop a smoke test under `tests/plugins/video_gen/test_<name>_plugin.py`… register, verify catalog, exercise routing both with and without `image_url`, assert clean error responses on missing auth."
- **Rebuild notes:** One tool, two modalities selected by the presence of one parameter; model *families* (not endpoints) in the catalog; capabilities feed the dynamic schema so unsupported parameters never reach the model.

### Web Search Provider Plugins  `id: docs-rest.dev-web-search-plugin`
- **Surface:** Docs / Provider
- **Where:** Developer Guide → **"Web Search Provider Plugins"** (`developer-guide/web-search-provider-plugin.md`, `sidebar_position: 12`).
- **What it does:** Documents registering a backend for `web_search`, `web_extract`, and deep crawl (a mode of `extract()`).
- **How it works:** Built-ins verbatim: "Firecrawl, SearXNG, Exa, Parallel, Keenable, Brave Search (free tier), xAI, and DDGS — all ship as plugins under `plugins/web/<name>/`." Discovery: bundled `<repo>/plugins/web/<name>/`, user `~/.hermes/plugins/web/<name>/`, pip entry point; registration `ctx.register_web_search_provider(...)` into `agent/web_search_registry.py`.
  Capability→config table verbatim (Capability | Config key | Falls back to): `web_search` | `web.search_backend` | `web.backend`; `web_extract` | `web.extract_backend` | `web.backend`; "Deep crawl modes inside `web_extract`" | `web.extract_backend` | `web.backend`. "When neither key is set, Hermes auto-detects the backend from whichever API key/URL is present in the environment."
  Tool wiring verbatim: "1. Read the relevant config key…; 2. Ask the registry for the provider with that `name`; 3. Check `is_available()` and the matching `supports_*()` flag; 4. Dispatch to `search()` / `extract()` (deep crawl runs as a mode inside `extract()`), awaiting if the method is a coroutine; 5. JSON-serialize the response envelope and hand it back to the LLM."
- **Inputs / options:** ABC members: `name`, `display_name`, `is_available()`, `supports_search()`, `supports_extract()`, `search(query, limit=5)`, `extract(...)`. Manifest keys table verbatim (Key | Purpose): `kind: backend` "Routes the plugin through the backend-loading path"; `provides_web_providers` "List of provider `name`s this plugin registers — used by the loader to advertise the plugin in `hermes tools` even before `register()` runs"; `requires_env` "Interactive credential prompt during `hermes plugins install`". Search response shape transcribed: `{"success": True, "data": {"web": [{"title", "url", "description", "position"}]}}`; extract rows carry `raw_content`, optional `metadata`, optional per-URL `error`; failure envelope `{"success": False, "error": "human-readable message"}`.
- **Outputs / side effects:** A JSON envelope handed to the LLM.
- **Config / env:** `web.backend`, `web.search_backend`, `web.extract_backend`, `security.allow_lazy_installs`.
- **Edge cases / guards:** `is_available()` verbatim: "Cheap check — env var present, optional dep importable, etc. MUST NOT make network calls (runs on every `hermes tools` paint)." Async verbatim: "Both `search()` and `extract()` may be `async def` — the dispatcher detects coroutine functions via `inspect.iscoroutinefunction` and awaits accordingly." Capability flags verbatim: "If your provider only supports one capability, leave the other flags at their default (`False`) and the registry will skip it for that tool — users won't see misleading \"provider X failed\" errors." Multi-provider example verbatim: `web: {search_backend: "brave-free", extract_backend: "firecrawl"}`. Lazy deps verbatim: "If your provider wraps a third-party SDK (like DDGS does with the `ddgs` package), don't `import` it at module top level. Use `tools.lazy_deps.ensure(...)` inside `is_available()` or `search()`." Reference implementations verbatim: "`plugins/web/brave_free/` — small, API-key-gated, search-only HTTP provider. Good starting template."; "`plugins/web/ddgs/` — no-key provider that lazy-installs its SDK."; "`plugins/web/firecrawl/` — full multi-capability provider (search + extract + crawl) with multiple format modes."; "`plugins/web/searxng/` — self-hosted, URL-configured backend with no auth."; "`plugins/web/xai/` — LLM-backed search via Grok's server-side `web_search` tool."
- **Rebuild notes:** Split search and extract into independently-selectable backends with a shared fallback key, declare capabilities as booleans so the registry can skip a provider per tool, and keep availability checks network-free.

### Browser Provider Plugins  `id: docs-rest.dev-browser-provider-plugin`
- **Surface:** Docs / Provider
- **Where:** Developer Guide → **"Browser Provider Plugins"** (`developer-guide/browser-provider-plugin.md`, `sidebar_position: 13`).
- **What it does:** Documents registering a **cloud browser backend** that supplies CDP sessions for the `browser_*` toolset.
- **How it works:** Key insight verbatim: "A browser provider does **not** implement browsing. It implements **session lifecycle**: create a remote browser session, hand back a CDP websocket URL, and tear the session down. Hermes' own browser stack (`agent-browser` + `tools/browser_tool.py`) connects to whatever CDP URL you return and drives the page from there — every provider gets the full `browser_*` toolset for free." Selection verbatim: "The active provider is selected by `browser.cloud_provider` in `config.yaml`; the dispatcher in `tools/browser_tool.py` is a pure registry lookup with no per-provider conditionals." Discovery: bundled `<repo>/plugins/browser/<name>/`, user `~/.hermes/plugins/browser/<name>/`, pip entry point; registration `ctx.register_browser_provider(...)` into `agent/browser_registry.py`. Built-ins verbatim: "Browserbase, Browser Use, and Firecrawl".
  ABC methods verbatim: `name` ("the browser.cloud_provider config value"); `display_name` ("shown in `hermes tools`"); `is_available()` ("Cheap check only — env var present, dep importable. NO network calls: runs at tool-registration time and on every `hermes tools` paint."); `create_session(task_id) -> dict`; `close_session(session_id) -> bool` ("Terminate by provider session ID. Log-and-return-False on error — never raise, so the dispatcher's cleanup loop keeps moving."); `emergency_cleanup(session_id) -> None` ("Best-effort teardown from atexit/signal handlers. Must not raise."); `get_setup_schema() -> dict`.
- **Inputs / options:** Session-metadata contract verbatim: `session_name` ("unique agent-browser session name"), `bb_session_id` ("provider session ID (for cleanup)"), `cdp_url` ("CDP websocket URL"), `features` ("feature flags you enabled"); optional `external_call_id` ("supports managed-gateway billing"). `get_setup_schema()` keys: `name`, `badge`, `tag`, `env_vars` (`key`, `prompt`, `url`), `post_setup: "agent_browser"` ("ensures local Chromium is installed (agent-browser itself resolves via npx)"). Manifest keys: `name`, `version`, `description`, `author`, `kind: backend`, `provides_browser_providers`.
- **Outputs / side effects:** A remote browser session and its CDP URL.
- **Config / env:** `browser.cloud_provider`.
- **Edge cases / guards:** Verbatim: "**`bb_session_id` is a legacy key name** kept verbatim for backward compatibility with `tools/browser_tool.py` — it holds *your* provider's session ID regardless of vendor. Don't rename it."; "`create_session()` **may raise** — `ValueError` for missing credentials, `RuntimeError` for network/API failures. The dispatcher surfaces these to the user. This differs from `close_session`/`emergency_cleanup`, which must never raise." Project standard verbatim: "if a backend can't be selected and configured through `hermes tools`, it isn't done — \"set this env var manually\" is not an integration." Checklist verbatim: "`name` is lowercase and stable (it's a config value users write); `is_available()` makes zero network calls; `create_session()` returns the full metadata contract (`bb_session_id` key name intact); `close_session()` / `emergency_cleanup()` never raise; `get_setup_schema()` exposes your env vars so `hermes tools` can configure the backend; `plugin.yaml` declares `kind: backend` + `provides_browser_providers`." Reference implementations verbatim: "`firecrawl` (simplest), `browser_use`, and `browserbase` (stealth/proxy/keep-alive feature flags with graceful fallback when paid features are unavailable)."
- **Rebuild notes:** Make the vendor supply only a CDP endpoint and lifecycle; keep all automation in one in-house driver so every backend inherits the whole toolset with zero per-vendor branching.

### Secret Source Plugins  `id: docs-rest.dev-secret-source-plugin`
- **Surface:** Docs / Config / Core
- **Where:** Developer Guide → **"Secret Source Plugins"** (`developer-guide/secret-source-plugin.md`, `sidebar_position: 9`).
- **What it does:** Documents building a backend that resolves credentials from an external secret manager into environment variables at startup.
- **How it works:** Placement verbatim: "after `~/.hermes/.env` loads, before Hermes reads credentials. Bitwarden, 1Password, and a generic command-helper source ship in-tree; **every other backend is a plugin**." Bootstrap timing verbatim: "`load_hermes_dotenv()` often runs at import time **before** plugins register. Hermes then re-pulls secrets after plugin discovery when any **enabled** plugin secret source is configured. Enablement uses the source's `is_enabled(cfg)` contract; the standard form is `secrets.<name>.enabled: true`… Re-pull is idempotent and fail-open (never blocks startup)."
  Ownership split table verbatim (Framework owns | You own): "Source ordering, mapped-vs-bulk precedence" | "Fetching values from your backend"; "First-claim-wins conflict handling + warnings" | "Validating your reference format"; "`override_existing` semantics (never crosses sources)" | "Talking to your CLI/SDK/API"; "Protected bootstrap tokens" | "Declaring which env var IS your bootstrap token"; "Per-source wall-clock timeout" | "Keeping `fetch()` reasonably fast"; "Per-var provenance + `(from X)` labels" | "A human-readable `label`"; "`os.environ` writes" | "Nothing — you never touch the environment".
  ABC class attributes verbatim: `name` ("config section key: secrets.myvault"), `label` ("used in startup lines + provenance labels"), `shape` ("\"mapped\" (explicit VAR→ref map) or \"bulk\" (project dump)"), `scheme` ("optional: unique URI scheme you own (mv://...)"), `api_version`. Required method: `fetch(cfg: dict, home_path: Path) -> FetchResult` ("Resolve secrets. MUST NOT raise. MUST NOT prompt.").
  Optional hooks table verbatim (Method | Default | Override when): `is_enabled(cfg)` `cfg.get("enabled")` "Custom activation logic"; `override_existing(cfg)` `cfg.get("override_existing", False)` "You want a different default (both bundled sources default `True` for rotation)"; `protected_env_vars(cfg)` "empty" "You have a bootstrap token (you almost certainly do)"; `fetch_timeout_seconds(cfg)` "120s" "Your backend needs a different budget"; `config_schema()` `{}` "Declare config keys for setup surfaces"; `remediation(kind, cfg)` "generic per-`ErrorKind` hints" "You want failure warnings to point at your own fix-it command… Must be a pure kind→string mapping: no I/O, never raises. Return `\"\"` to suppress the hint."
- **Inputs / options:** `run_secret_cli(argv, allow_env=[...], timeout=30)` — "argv-only (no `shell=True`), a **minimal allowlisted child environment** (by the time sources run, `os.environ` holds every credential Hermes knows — never hand that to a child process), `NO_COLOR` + ANSI-scrubbed stderr, stdin closed, timeout → clean `RuntimeError`. Pass user-supplied reference strings after a `--` terminator in your argv so they can never parse as flags." Registration: `def register(ctx): ctx.register_secret_source(MyVaultSource())`. User config verbatim: `secrets: {sources: [myvault, bitwarden], myvault: {enabled: true, ...}}`.
  `ErrorKind` reference, all eight verbatim (Kind | Meaning): `NOT_CONFIGURED` "Enabled but missing token / project / map"; `BINARY_MISSING` "Helper CLI not found or not executable"; `AUTH_FAILED` / `AUTH_EXPIRED` "Bad / expired credentials"; `REF_INVALID` "A secret reference failed validation"; `NETWORK` "Transport-level failure"; `EMPTY_VALUE` "Backend returned nothing for a ref — never apply `\"\"` over a good credential"; `TIMEOUT` "Fetch exceeded its budget"; `INTERNAL` "Anything else (bug, unexpected shape)".
- **Outputs / side effects:** A `FetchResult` whose `secrets` mapping the orchestrator applies to `os.environ`; provenance labels like `(from My Vault)`.
- **Config / env:** `secrets.sources`, `secrets.<name>.enabled`, `secrets.<name>.timeout_seconds`, `secrets.<name>.override_existing`.
- **Edge cases / guards:** Enforced contract rules verbatim: "**`fetch()` never raises.** Errors go in `result.error` + `result.error_kind`. A raising fetch is contained by the orchestrator and reported as `INTERNAL` — a contract violation, not a feature."; "**`fetch()` never prompts.** Startup runs in non-TTY contexts (gateway, cron, Docker). `run_secret_cli()` closes stdin so a prompting helper fails fast."; "**Sync, within budget.** The orchestrator enforces a wall-clock timeout (default 120s, user-tunable via `secrets.<name>.timeout_seconds`). Exceeding it reports `TIMEOUT` and your result is discarded."; "**You fetch; the orchestrator applies.** … Never write `os.environ` yourself — you'd bypass precedence, conflict detection, and provenance."; "**API versioning.** `SecretSource.api_version` defaults to the current `SECRET_SOURCE_API_VERSION`. The registry skips (with a warning) sources built against a different version instead of crashing startup." Shape choice verbatim: "`mapped` — the user explicitly binds env-var names to references in config (like 1Password's `env:` map). Strongest intent: mapped claims beat bulk claims on contested vars. `bulk` — you inject a whole project/folder of secrets implicitly (like Bitwarden BSM). Yields to mapped sources." Registration rejection verbatim: "Registration is rejected (with a log warning, never a crash) for: non-`SecretSource` instances, invalid/duplicate names, a `scheme` another source owns, wrong `api_version`, or a `shape` outside `mapped`/`bulk`." Conformance kit verbatim: "Subclass the kit from the Hermes repo (`tests/secret_sources/conformance.py`)… It checks the rules that break other people when violated: never-raises on malformed config, machine-readable error kinds, disabled-by-default, positive timeouts, valid protected-var names, and a full `apply_all()` round trip." Trust note verbatim: "the trust boundary remains \"enabled plugins run with agent privilege\"."
- **Rebuild notes:** The plugin fetches, the framework applies — that single split makes precedence, conflict detection, provenance, and protected bootstrap tokens un-buggable by third parties. Ship a conformance test kit so "contract-compliant" is mechanically checkable.

### Terminal Environment Provider Plugins  `id: docs-rest.dev-terminal-env-plugin`
- **Surface:** Docs / Config / Core
- **Where:** Developer Guide → **"Terminal Environment Provider Plugins"** (`developer-guide/terminal-environment-plugin.md`).
- **What it does:** Documents registering a third-party sandbox as a `terminal.backend` alongside the built-ins.
- **How it works:** Built-ins verbatim: "local, Docker, Singularity, Modal, Daytona, Vercel Sandbox, SSH… live in the core repo under `tools/environments/`. Third-party sandbox vendors integrate as **plugins** instead — a standalone plugin repo installed under `~/.hermes/plugins/`, registering a backend the user selects exactly like a built-in one via `terminal.backend` in `config.yaml`."
  Surfaces a provider drives, all ten verbatim (Surface | Driven by): "Command dispatch (`terminal`, `execute_code`, file tools)" | `create_environment()`; "`hermes setup` backend picker" | "`display_name`, `description`, `setup_instructions()`, `post_setup()`"; "Dashboard terminal-backend picker (probe status)" | `probe()`; "`hermes status` / `hermes doctor`" | `doctor_checks()`; "System-prompt environment hints" | "`is_remote`, `env_description`"; "Dangerous-command approval skipping" | `skip_container_guards`; "Container path/cwd handling" | `is_container`; "Synced cache-file path translation" | `cache_path_base`; "Secret stripping from spawned subprocesses" | `strip_env_keys`; "Per-session sandbox isolation (`container_persistent: false`)" | `session_isolated_when_nonpersistent`. Rationale verbatim: "Declaring these flags on the provider closes the classic \"new backend missed classification site N\" bug class — the core consults the registry at each site instead of a hardcoded list of names."
- **Inputs / options:** `create_environment(*, cwd, timeout, task_id="default", image=None, container_config=None, **kwargs)`; registration `ctx.register_terminal_environment_provider(AcmeBoxProvider())`; manifest `name`, `version`, `description`, `kind: backend`. Enable/select verbatim: `hermes plugins enable acmebox`; `hermes config set terminal.backend acmebox`. Environment object contract verbatim: "`execute(command, timeout=None, ...)` → `{\"output\": str, \"exit_code\": int}`; `cleanup()` — release resources; called on session teardown / idle reaping; Optional: persistence hooks mirroring the built-in cloud backends. Subclassing `BaseEnvironment` is recommended (you inherit the shared file-sync and background-process plumbing) but not required."
- **Outputs / side effects:** Commands execute in the vendor sandbox instead of on the host.
- **Config / env:** `terminal.backend`, `terminal.container_persistent`.
- **Edge cases / guards:** All five rules verbatim: "**Reserved names.** Registrations that collide with a built-in backend name (`local`, `docker`, `singularity`, `modal`, `managed_modal`, `daytona`, `vercel_sandbox`, `ssh`) are rejected. Plugins extend the backend set; they never shadow in-tree backends."; "**`create_environment` must accept `**kwargs`** and ignore unknown keys — the forward-compat contract that lets the factory signature evolve without breaking older plugins."; "**`is_available()` / `probe()` must be cheap.** No network calls — they run during requirement checks and UI paints."; "**Fail-soft everywhere.** A provider attribute that raises is treated as its default by the core (e.g. a raising `skip_container_guards` keeps the approval layer ON)."; "**Secrets belong in `strip_env_keys`.** Your vendor token must never be readable by a model-authored shell command; listing it strips it from every spawned subprocess unconditionally, like the built-in `MODAL_*` / `DAYTONA_API_KEY` handling." Session isolation verbatim: "If your sandbox is **resumed by name** (a durable VM the backend re-attaches to), set `session_isolated_when_nonpersistent = True`. With `terminal.container_persistent: false`, each session then gets its own sandbox identity instead of sharing one — without this, two independent ephemeral runs could attach one live VM and delete it out from under each other."
- **Rebuild notes:** Make every backend-classification decision a declared provider attribute consulted by the core, never a hardcoded name list; reserve built-in names; strip vendor tokens from every child process by declaration.

### Public Subagent Lifecycle API  `id: docs-rest.dev-subagent-lifecycle-api`
- **Surface:** Docs / Core
- **Where:** Developer Guide → **"Public Subagent Lifecycle API"** (sidebar label **"Subagent lifecycle API"**, `developer-guide/subagent-lifecycle-api.md`).
- **What it does:** Lets a plugin launch and supervise fresh Hermes child sessions without importing `tools.delegate_tool`, gateway internals, TUI state, or `AIAgent` fields.
- **How it works:** Verbatim: "The service resolves its parent from the current agent turn, so it works in CLI, gateway, non-interactive, and kanban-worker sessions. Launching outside an active agent turn fails closed with `No active Hermes parent session`." Usage transcribed: `from agent.subagent_lifecycle import SubagentLaunchRequest`; `service = ctx.subagent_lifecycle`; `handle = service.launch(SubagentLaunchRequest(goal=..., context=..., role="leaf", correlation_id="review-42", allowed_toolsets=("file",)))`; `service.wait(handle, timeout_seconds=2).timed_out`; `service.result(handle)`; `handle.to_dict()`.
  Relationship to `delegate_task` verbatim: "This API is lifecycle-managed asynchronous execution. Child construction and completion use the same host-owned path as `delegate_task`, including parent tool-resolution restoration, memory notification, serialized `subagent_stop` hooks, resource cleanup, and child-cost rollup. It does not change the synchronous `delegate_task` tool, batch delegation, or its gateway/TUI display."
- **Inputs / options:** `SubagentLaunchRequest` fields shown: `goal`, `context`, `role`, `correlation_id`, `allowed_toolsets`. Service methods: `launch`, `status`, `wait`, `cancel`, `result`, `reconnect`. States, all nine verbatim: `PENDING`, `STARTING`, `RUNNING`, `SUCCEEDED`, `FAILED`, `INTERRUPTED`, `CANCEL_REQUESTED`, `CANCELLED`, `UNKNOWN`.
- **Outputs / side effects:** A serializable `SubagentHandle` ("carries a versioned, opaque capability"); terminal results.
- **Config / env:** n/a
- **Edge cases / guards:** Verbatim: "malformed or forged handles return `UNKNOWN`/`UNKNOWN_HANDLE` and cannot access a child."; "`cancel(handle, reason=...)` is cooperative: it asks the child agent to interrupt at its next safe boundary and returns `CANCEL_REQUESTED`; it never claims completion until `wait` or `result` observes a terminal state."; "Terminal results are immutable, idempotent, bounded to 32k characters, omit transcripts and hidden reasoning, and include a stable result hash."; "The initial implementation retains metadata and terminal results in-process for one hour. After a process restart, `reconnect` returns `RECONNECT_UNAVAILABLE` and never starts a replacement child. Running Python threads also cannot survive process exit; callers must treat those handles as interrupted by process exit."; "Requests are fail-closed: goal/context/metadata sizes are capped, unknown or parent-broadening toolsets are rejected, and per-tool blocks, working-directory overrides, and per-launch timeouts are explicitly rejected until Hermes can support them without weakening isolation. Use `allowed_toolsets` to narrow a child; Hermes's existing unsafe-tool block remains enforced."
- **Rebuild notes:** Expose child-session supervision as an opaque, versioned capability handle rather than object references; make cancel cooperative and never optimistic; cap and hash terminal results so they can be replayed and compared.

### Codebase Ownership Map  `id: docs-rest.dev-codebase-ownership`
- **Surface:** Docs
- **Where:** Developer Guide → **"Codebase Ownership Map"** (`developer-guide/codebase-ownership.md`).
- **What it does:** Maps each subsystem to its source directories and the docs page to read before changing it.
- **How it works:** All 18 rows verbatim (Subsystem | Source directories | Docs entry point): "Agent core (loop, transports, compression)" | `agent/`, `run_agent.py` | Agent Loop, Context Compression & Caching; "Prompt assembly" | `agent/prompt_builder.py`, `agent/system_prompt.py` | Prompt Assembly; "Model providers & transports" | `agent/transports/`, `plugins/model-providers/`, `hermes_cli/models.py` | Adding Providers, Model Provider Plugins, Provider Runtime; "Built-in tools" | `tools/` | Adding Tools, Tools Runtime; "Messaging gateway" | `gateway/`, `plugins/platforms/` | Gateway Internals, Adding Platform Adapters; "CLI" | `hermes_cli/` | Extending the CLI; "Plugins system" | `plugins/` | Build a Hermes Plugin; "Skills (bundled & optional)" | `skills/`, `optional-skills/` | Creating Skills; "Cron / scheduled jobs" | `cron/` | Cron Internals; "Session storage" | `hermes_state.py` | Session Storage; "Browser stack" | `tools/browser_tool.py`, `tools/browser_supervisor.py`, `tools/browser_cdp_tool.py` | Browser Supervisor; "Egress firewall" | `agent/proxy_sources/iron_proxy.py` | Egress Internals; "ACP (IDE integration)" | `acp_adapter/` | ACP Internals; "Desktop app" | `apps/desktop/` | Desktop Plugin SDK, Worktree UI Development; "TUI" | `ui-tui/`, `tui_gateway/` | Worktree UI Development; "Docs site" | `website/` | Contributing; "Tests" | `tests/`, `tests-js/` | Contributing → Before Submitting.
- **Inputs / options:** n/a
- **Outputs / side effects:** n/a
- **Config / env:** n/a
- **Edge cases / guards:** Three conventions verbatim: "**Changes should stay inside their subsystem.** A plugin that needs to edit core files is a design smell — widen the generic plugin surface instead (see the contribution rubric in the repository's `AGENTS.md`)."; "**Run the mirror test directory for every source directory you touch.** A change to `plugins/platforms/telegram/` needs `tests/plugins/platforms/` green, not just the test file you happened to think of."; "**When two subsystems are involved, the narrower one owns the change.** Prefer a fix in an adapter or plugin over a branch in the agent core; the core is a narrow waist, and every addition there is paid for on every API call." Test-mirroring rule verbatim: "tests mirror source: code in `tools/` is tested in `tests/tools/`, plugins in `tests/plugins/<type>/`, and so on".
- **Rebuild notes:** Publish an ownership map keyed on directory, with a mirrored test tree so "what do I run" is mechanical.

### Contributing guide (dev setup, style, security, PR process)  `id: docs-rest.dev-contributing`
- **Surface:** Docs
- **Where:** Developer Guide → **"Contributing"** (`developer-guide/contributing.md`, `sidebar_position: 4`).
- **What it does:** The complete contributor onboarding: priorities, dev setup, code style, cross-platform rules, security expectations, and the PR process.
- **How it works:** Contribution priorities, all seven in order verbatim: "1. **Bug fixes** — crashes, incorrect behavior, data loss; 2. **Cross-platform compatibility** — macOS, different Linux distros, WSL2; 3. **Security hardening** — shell injection, prompt injection, path traversal; 4. **Performance and robustness** — retry logic, error handling, graceful degradation; 5. **New skills** — broadly useful ones; 6. **New tools** — rarely needed; most capabilities should be skills; 7. **Documentation** — fixes, clarifications, new examples."
  Prerequisites table verbatim (Requirement | Notes): **Git** "With the `git-lfs` extension installed"; **Python 3.11–3.13** "uv will install it if missing"; **uv** "Fast Python package manager"; **Node.js 26+** "Optional — needed for browser tools and WhatsApp bridge (matches root `package.json` engines)".
  Standard dev bootstrap verbatim: `curl -fsSL https://hermes-agent.nousresearch.com/install.sh | bash`; `cd "${HERMES_HOME:-$HOME/.hermes}/hermes-agent"`; `uv pip install -e ".[all,dev]"`; `npm install`; `git checkout -b fix/description`; `scripts/run_tests.sh`. Sandbox verbatim: `scripts/dev-sandbox.sh python -m hermes_cli.main`; `scripts/dev-sandbox.sh --persistent python -m hermes_cli.main desktop` ("state survives restarts, but lives in the worktree :)").
  Manual clone fallback verbatim, including the venv-location warning: "Create the venv **outside** the cloned source tree. A venv that lives inside the directory the agent operates from can be wiped by a relative-path command the agent runs against its own checkout (`rm -rf venv`, `uv venv venv`, etc.), which silently destroys the running runtime mid-session." Commands: `uv venv ~/.hermes/venvs/hermes-dev --python 3.11`; `export VIRTUAL_ENV=...`; `export PATH="$VIRTUAL_ENV/bin:$PATH"`; `uv pip install -e ".[all,dev]"`.
  Dev config verbatim: `mkdir -p ~/.hermes/{cron,sessions,logs,memories,skills}`; `cp cli-config.yaml.example ~/.hermes/config.yaml`; `touch ~/.hermes/.env`; `echo 'OPENROUTER_API_KEY=sk-or-v1-your-key' >> ~/.hermes/.env`.
- **Inputs / options:** Code style rules verbatim: "**PEP 8** with practical exceptions (no strict line length enforcement)"; "**Comments**: Only when explaining non-obvious intent, trade-offs, or API quirks"; "**Error handling**: Catch specific exceptions. Use `logger.warning()`/`logger.error()` with `exc_info=True` for unexpected errors"; "**Cross-platform**: Never assume Unix"; "**Profile-safe paths**: Never hardcode `~/.hermes` — use `get_hermes_home()` from `hermes_constants` for code paths and `display_hermes_home()` for user-facing messages."
  Cross-platform rules, all five verbatim: "**Don't add unguarded `signal.SIGKILL` references.** It's not defined on Windows. Either route through `gateway.status.terminate_pid(pid, force=True)` (the centralized primitive that does `taskkill /T /F` on Windows and SIGKILL on POSIX), or fall back with `getattr(signal, \"SIGKILL\", signal.SIGTERM)`."; "**Catch `OSError` alongside `ProcessLookupError` on `os.kill(pid, 0)` probes.** Windows raises `OSError` (WinError 87, \"parameter is incorrect\") for an already-gone PID instead of `ProcessLookupError`."; "**Don't force the terminal to POSIX semantics.** `os.setsid`, `os.killpg`, `os.getpgid`, `os.fork` all raise on Windows — gate them with `if sys.platform != \"win32\":` or `if os.name != \"nt\":`."; "**Open files with an explicit `encoding=\"utf-8\"`.** The Python default on Windows is the system locale (often cp1252), which mojibakes or crashes on non-Latin text."; "**Use `pathlib.Path` / `os.path.join` — never manually concat with `/`.**" Windows lint: `scripts/check-windows-footguns.py`. PTY gate verbatim: "the dashboard's embedded PTY terminal pane (`/chat` tab) needs a POSIX PTY (Linux, macOS, or WSL2)"; "Native Windows uses Git Bash (from Git for Windows) for shell commands."
  Branch naming verbatim: `fix/description` "Bug fixes"; `feat/description` "New features"; `docs/description` "Documentation"; `test/description` "Tests"; `refactor/description` "Code restructuring". Commit types verbatim (Type | Use for): `fix` "Bug fixes"; `feat` "New features"; `docs` "Documentation"; `test` "Tests"; `refactor` "Code restructuring"; `chore` "Build, CI, dependency updates". Scopes verbatim: `cli`, `gateway`, `tools`, `skills`, `agent`, `install`, `whatsapp`, `security`. Examples verbatim: "fix(cli): prevent crash in save_config_value when model is a string"; "feat(gateway): add WhatsApp multi-user session isolation"; "fix(security): prevent shell injection in sudo password piping".
- **Outputs / side effects:** A dev checkout, a PR.
- **Config / env:** `HERMES_HOME`, `VIRTUAL_ENV`, `PATH`.
- **Edge cases / guards:** Existing security protections table, all seven verbatim (Layer | Implementation): "**Sudo password piping**" "Uses `shlex.quote()` to prevent shell injection"; "**Dangerous command detection**" "Regex patterns in `tools/approval.py` with user approval flow"; "**Cron prompt injection**" "Scanner blocks instruction-override patterns"; "**Write deny list**" "Protected paths resolved via `os.path.realpath()` to prevent symlink bypass"; "**Skills guard**" "Security scanner for hub-installed skills"; "**Code execution sandbox**" "Child process runs with API keys stripped"; "**Container hardening**" "Docker: all capabilities dropped, no privilege escalation, PID limits". Security-contribution rules verbatim: "Always use `shlex.quote()` when interpolating user input into shell commands; Resolve symlinks with `os.path.realpath()` before access control checks; Don't log secrets; Catch broad exceptions around tool execution; Test on all platforms if your change touches file paths or processes." Before-submitting checklist verbatim: "1. **Run tests**: `scripts/run_tests.sh` for CI-parity…; 2. **Test manually**: Run `hermes` and exercise the code path you changed; 3. **Check cross-platform impact**…; 4. **Keep PRs focused**: One logical change per PR." Issue-report requirements verbatim: "Include: OS, Python version, Hermes version (`hermes --version`), full error traceback; Include steps to reproduce; Check existing issues before creating duplicates; For security vulnerabilities, please report privately." Community: Discord `discord.gg/NousResearch`, GitHub Discussions, Skills Hub. License: MIT.
- **Rebuild notes:** Publish an explicit contribution priority order and a cross-platform footgun list with a lint script that enforces it; the "venv outside the tree" rule is a genuinely load-bearing safety property for a self-modifying agent.

### Repo-local review checklists (`.agents/checks/*.md`)  `id: docs-rest.dev-agents-checks`
- **Surface:** Docs / Core
- **Where:** Contributing → **"### Repo-local review checklists: `.agents/checks/*.md`"**.
- **What it does:** A convention for storing per-concern reviewer checklists inside a repository so a review agent loads the project-specific bar.
- **How it works:** Layout verbatim: `.agents/checks/security.md` ("e.g. \"grep the diff for shell interpolation; check subprocess calls quote args\""), `.agents/checks/migrations.md` ("e.g. \"every schema change ships a backfill and a rollback note\""), `.agents/checks/public-api.md` ("e.g. \"exported signatures changed? flag for semver review\"").
- **Inputs / options:** Conventions verbatim: "**One concern per file**, named after the concern. Small files get read in full; a monolithic `checklist.md` gets skimmed."; "**Write checks as verifiable actions** (\"run X and confirm Y\"), not aspirations (\"code should be secure\")."; "**State the trigger at the top** — which paths or change types the checklist applies to — so an agent (or human) can skip irrelevant ones cheaply."; "Keep them in version control next to the code they guard: they evolve with the codebase, and a PR that changes the rules changes the checklist in the same diff."
- **Outputs / side effects:** A review report against the named checklists.
- **Config / env:** n/a
- **Edge cases / guards:** Verbatim: "When you ask Hermes to review a PR in a repository that has `.agents/checks/`, tell it (or teach it via a skill) to read the relevant checklists first and report against them."
- **Rebuild notes:** Small, trigger-annotated, action-phrased checklist files versioned with the code are the cheapest way to give a review agent project-specific standards.

### Programmatic integration — three protocols  `id: docs-rest.dev-programmatic-integration`
- **Surface:** Docs / API
- **Where:** Developer Guide → **"Programmatic Integration"** (`developer-guide/programmatic-integration.md`, `sidebar_position: 8`).
- **What it does:** Compares the three protocols for driving Hermes from an external program and says which to pick.
- **How it works:** Comparison table verbatim (Protocol | Transport | Best for | Defined by): **ACP** "JSON-RPC over stdio" "IDE clients (VS Code, Zed, JetBrains) that already speak the Agent Client Protocol" `acp_adapter/`; **TUI gateway** "JSON-RPC over stdio (or WebSocket)" "Custom hosts that want fine-grained control of sessions, slash commands, approvals, and streaming events" `tui_gateway/server.py`; **API server** "HTTP + Server-Sent Events" "OpenAI-compatible frontends (Open WebUI, LobeChat, LibreChat…) and language-agnostic web clients" `gateway/platforms/api_server.py`. "All three drive the same `AIAgent` core."
  Selection guidance verbatim: "**You're writing an IDE plugin and the IDE already speaks ACP** → ACP. Zero protocol work on the IDE side."; "**You're writing a custom desktop / web / TUI host and want every Hermes feature** (slash commands, approvals, clarify, multi-agent, session branching) → TUI gateway JSON-RPC."; "**You want any OpenAI-compatible frontend, a language-agnostic HTTP client, or curl-driven automation** → API server."; "**You want a Python in-process embed without a subprocess** → import `run_agent.AIAgent` directly."
- **Inputs / options:** ACP commands verbatim: `hermes acp` ("serve ACP on stdio"), `hermes acp --check` ("verify ACP dependencies and adapter imports"), `hermes acp --setup` ("interactive provider/model setup for ACP terminal auth"). ACP capabilities verbatim: "session creation, prompt submission, streaming agent message chunks, tool-call events, permission requests, session fork, cancel, and authentication. Tool output is rendered into ACP `Diff`/`ToolCall` content blocks the IDE understands."
  API server endpoint list, all 15 verbatim: `POST /v1/chat/completions` "OpenAI Chat Completions (streaming via SSE)"; `POST /v1/responses` "OpenAI Responses API (stateful)"; `POST /v1/runs` "Start a run, returns run_id (202)"; `GET /v1/runs/{id}` "Run status"; `GET /v1/runs/{id}/events` "SSE stream of lifecycle events"; `POST /v1/runs/{id}/approval` "Resolve a pending approval"; `POST /v1/runs/{id}/steer` "Inject mid-run guidance at the next tool boundary"; `POST /v1/runs/{id}/stop` "Interrupt the run"; `GET /v1/capabilities` "Machine-readable feature flags"; `POST /v1/browser-control/register` "Register a browser controller"; `GET /v1/browser-control/ws` "Browser-controller WebSocket"; `GET /v1/models` "Lists hermes-agent"; `GET /api/model/options` "Provider-aware picker inventory"; `GET /health`, `/health/detailed`. Headers named: `X-Hermes-Session-Id`, `X-Hermes-Session-Key`, `X-Hermes-Session-Token`.
  Model hot-swapping verbatim: "**CLI / TUI:** `/model claude-sonnet-4` or `/model openrouter:anthropic/claude-sonnet-4.6`; **TUI gateway RPC:** `command.dispatch` with `{\"command\": \"/model claude-sonnet-4\"}`; **ACP:** the IDE sends the slash command as a prompt; the agent dispatches it; **API server:** include a `model` field in the request body." Implementation: `hermes_cli/model_switch.py`.
  Model-catalog surfaces verbatim: "API server REST: `GET /api/model/options` with the API-server bearer key; Dashboard backend REST: `GET /api/model/options` with `X-Hermes-Session-Token`; TUI gateway RPC: `model.options`." Probe policy verbatim: "Normal open: probe only the current custom provider so offline saved endpoints do not stall the picker. Explicit refresh (`refresh=1` or `refresh: true`): bust the provider-model cache and probe all saved custom providers so live catalogs repopulate fully."
- **Outputs / side effects:** Protocol-specific responses and event streams.
- **Config / env:** n/a
- **Edge cases / guards:** Steering semantics verbatim: "`POST /v1/runs/{id}/steer` is the HTTP equivalent of Hermes `/steer`: it does not create a new user turn or immediately rewrite the assistant output already in flight. Instead, the text is appended to the live run and becomes visible to the agent after the next tool boundary"; "only accepted while the run status is `running`. Queued, approval-paused, stopping, cancelled, failed, and completed runs return `409 run_not_accepting_steer`"; "A `200` (and the `run.steered` event) means the text was **queued**, not that the agent consumed it. If a steer lands after the agent's final response… the undelivered text is returned as `pending_steer` on the terminal `run.completed` event and run status." `/v1/models` scope verbatim: "the OpenAI-compatible API intentionally keeps `GET /v1/models` minimal: it is the compatibility endpoint frontends expect, not the full Hermes provider/model picker catalog." Non-existent flag verbatim: "Hermes does not have a `--mode rpc` flag."
- **Rebuild notes:** Offer exactly three integration surfaces mapped to three consumer classes, all over one core; keep the compatibility endpoint minimal and put the rich catalogue behind an authenticated first-party route.

### TUI gateway JSON-RPC — method catalog and rewind protocol  `id: docs-rest.dev-tui-gateway-rpc`
- **Surface:** Docs / API / TUI
- **Where:** Programmatic Integration → **"## TUI Gateway JSON-RPC"** (`tui_gateway/server.py`, WebSocket via `tui_gateway/ws.py`).
- **What it does:** Documents the protocol the Ink TUI and the dashboard PTY bridge speak, and the destructive-rewrite (rewind) contract on `prompt.submit`.
- **How it works:** Method catalog, all methods verbatim: `prompt.submit`, `prompt.background`, `session.steer`, `session.create`, `session.list`, `session.active_list`, `session.activate`, `session.close`, `session.interrupt`, `session.history`, `session.compress`, `session.branch`, `session.title`, `session.usage`, `session.status`, `clarify.respond`, `sudo.respond`, `secret.respond`, `approval.respond`, `config.set` / `config.get`, `commands.catalog`, `command.resolve`, `command.dispatch`, `cli.exec`, `reload.mcp`, `reload.env`, `process.stop`, `delegation.status`, `subagent.interrupt`, `subagent.steer`, `spawn_tree.save` / `list` / `load`, `terminal.resize`, `clipboard.paste`, `image.attach`, plus `model.options` and `session.resume` referenced elsewhere on the page.
  Events streamed back, all verbatim: `message.delta`, `message.complete`, `tool.start`, `tool.progress`, `tool.complete`, `approval.request`, `clarify.request`, `sudo.request`, `sudo.expire`, `secret.request`, `secret.expire`, `gateway.ready`, "plus session lifecycle and error events. Expiry events carry the original `{ request_id }`; external hosts should clear only the matching pending prompt."
  Pi-style RPC mapping table, all 11 rows verbatim (Pi command | Hermes equivalent): `prompt` | "`prompt.submit` (or ACP `session/prompt`)"; `steer` | `session.steer`; `follow_up` | "`prompt.submit` queued after current turn"; `abort` | `session.interrupt`; `set_model` | "`command.dispatch` for `/model <provider:model>` (mid-session, persistent)"; `compact` | `session.compress`; `get_state` | `session.status`; `get_messages` | `session.history`; `switch_session` | `session.resume`; `fork` | `session.branch`; `ui_request` / `ui_response` | "`clarify.respond` / `sudo.respond` / `secret.respond` / `approval.respond`".
- **Inputs / options:** Rewind parameters, all four verbatim (Parameter | Meaning): `truncate_before_user_ordinal` "Zero-based index of the user turn to cut at. Everything from that turn onward is dropped. Display-only timeline rows (`display_kind`) are not counted. Must be a real integer — a JSON boolean is refused with code `4004`."; `truncate_before_row_id` "Integer SQLite row ID (`messages.id` / `row_id`) of the target user turn to cut at. Preferred durable address. When both ordinal and row ID are provided, gateway verifies they match (returning `4030` on mismatch). An unknown/stale row ID is refused with `4018` — it does **not** fall back to the ordinal."; `confirm_truncate` "Required whenever an ordinal, message ID, or row ID is sent. Declares that this submit really is a rewind, not an ordinary send that happens to carry leftover parameters. Sending it without a target is refused with code `4004`."; `confirm_empty_truncate` "Additionally required when the cut would leave the transcript empty (ordinal `0`)."
- **Outputs / side effects:** On a successful truncating submit, `survivor_user_row_ids` — verbatim: "the fresh post-rewrite row IDs of the surviving user turns, in visible-user-ordinal order. The rewrite re-inserts the kept prefix as new rows, so every row ID the host cached before the rewind is stale afterward; rebind cached IDs from this list (a `null` entry means that turn has no durable ID — drop the cached one) or the next rewind targeting an older surviving turn will be refused with `4018`."
- **Config / env:** n/a
- **Edge cases / guards:** Error codes named: `4004`, `4018`, `4029`, `4030`. Verbatim: "A truncation parameter without `confirm_truncate` is refused with code `4004` or `4029` and nothing is written. Hosts that implement rewind must set the flag at the moment the user asks for it, and must never keep truncation parameters in state across ordinary submits. Prefer `truncate_before_row_id` (from resume `row_id` / `_row_id`) over ordinals". Live vs saved sessions verbatim: "`session.active_list`, `session.activate`, and `session.close` are the process-local live-session controls used by the TUI session switcher. Use `session.list` / `/resume` for saved transcript discovery; use the active-session methods only for sessions that are currently open in the TUI gateway process."
- **Rebuild notes:** Require an explicit intent flag for any destructive transcript rewrite, prefer durable row IDs over positional ordinals, and hand back the post-rewrite IDs so the client can rebind — this is the whole rewind-safety design in three rules.

### `htui` / `hgui` — TUI & Desktop from git worktrees  `id: docs-rest.dev-worktree-ui`
- **Surface:** Docs / Desktop app / TUI
- **Where:** Developer Guide → **"TUI & Desktop from Worktrees"** (`developer-guide/worktree-ui-dev.md`, `sidebar_position: 5`).
- **What it does:** Two shell helpers that launch the Ink TUI and the Electron desktop app from the current git worktree while borrowing `node_modules` from one canonical checkout.
- **How it works:** Problem verbatim: "The Python core runs fine from any git worktree — `cd` in and `hermes` just works. The two TypeScript surfaces do not: `ui-tui/` and `apps/desktop/` each need a populated `node_modules`, and a fresh `npm ci` per worktree is slow and duplicates gigabytes across every branch you have checked out." Deps model verbatim: "One checkout is the **deps checkout** — the one place you actually run `npm install`. Every other worktree links against it, and only re-installs locally when its lockfile diverges." Flow (mermaid, transcribed): "htui / hgui in a worktree → package-lock.json matches deps checkout? → yes: symlink node_modules from deps checkout / no: local npm ci in this worktree → launch surface". "They're developer conveniences, not shipped commands. Drop them in `~/.zshrc`; adapt paths to taste."
  The full `htui`, `hgui`, `_hermes_root`, `_hermes_link_deps`, and `_hermes_gui_cleanup` shell functions are transcribed verbatim in the doc.
- **Inputs / options:** Env vars table verbatim (Variable | Meaning): `HERMES_MAIN_CHECKOUT` "The deps checkout — where `node_modules` really lives, and whose `.venv/bin/python` runs the backend."; `HERMES_GUI_DEPS_CHECKOUT` "Where the desktop deps (`apps/desktop/node_modules`) live. Defaults to `HERMES_MAIN_CHECKOUT`; override only if you keep desktop deps elsewhere." "Neither is read by Hermes itself — they're private to these helpers."
  Desktop env vars `hgui` sets, all four verbatim (Variable | Role in `hgui`): `HERMES_DESKTOP_HERMES_ROOT` "Runs the backend from **this worktree**, not the packaged/PATH `hermes`."; `HERMES_DESKTOP_PYTHON` "Reuses the deps checkout's venv instead of re-resolving a Python."; `HERMES_DESKTOP_IGNORE_EXISTING` "Ignores any `hermes` on `PATH` so it can't shadow the worktree."; `HERMES_DESKTOP_CWD` "Opens the desktop chat rooted at the worktree."
- **Outputs / side effects:** A symlinked `node_modules`, a Vite dev server on port 5174, an Electron shell, and an ephemeral `dashboard --port 0` backend.
- **Config / env:** `HERMES_MAIN_CHECKOUT`, `HERMES_GUI_DEPS_CHECKOUT`, `HERMES_TUI_DIR`, `HERMES_DESKTOP_*`.
- **Edge cases / guards:** **":::warning `--dev` and `HERMES_TUI_DIR` are mutually exclusive"** — "`HERMES_TUI_DIR` points Hermes at a *prebuilt* bundle (Nix, system packages), which has no source to hot-reload. If it's set in your shell, `hermes --tui --dev` exits with an error. Run `unset HERMES_TUI_DIR` before `htui`." Two `hgui` footguns verbatim: "**Port `5174` is fixed.** A second `hgui` collides with the first's Vite server; the helper kills the stale one first."; "**Orphaned children.** Electron frequently survives `Ctrl+C` through `concurrently` without reaping the ephemeral `dashboard --port 0` backend or the Vite process. The `EXIT`/`INT`/`TERM` trap runs a cleanup that terminates the Electron shell, the `:5174` listener, and any `--port 0` dashboard it spawned." Linking rule verbatim: "A symlink to a divergent `node_modules` is worse than no install — the worktree would build against packages its own lockfile never declared. Byte-comparing `package-lock.json` is the cheap, exact guard: same lock ⇒ safe to borrow; different lock ⇒ `npm ci` locally. Vite realpaths symlinks before enforcing `server.fs.allow`, which is why `apps/desktop/vite.config.ts` whitelists the real `node_modules` location." Note: "`killport` is a small helper of your own (`lsof -ti:$1 | xargs kill`)".
- **Rebuild notes:** Share `node_modules` across worktrees by symlink, gated on a byte-identical lockfile; trap EXIT/INT/TERM around any Electron+Vite dev launcher because Electron does not reap its children.

### Browser CDP Supervisor  `id: docs-rest.dev-browser-supervisor`
- **Surface:** Docs / Tool / Core
- **Where:** Developer Guide → **"Browser CDP Supervisor"** (`developer-guide/browser-supervisor.md`, `sidebar_position: 18`).
- **What it does:** Documents the persistent CDP connection that detects/answers native JS dialogs and enables interaction with cross-origin iframes.
- **How it works:** The two gaps verbatim: "1. **Native JS dialogs** (`alert`/`confirm`/`prompt`/`beforeunload`) block the page's JS thread. Without supervision, the agent has no way to know a dialog is open — subsequent tool calls hang or throw opaque errors. 2. **Cross-origin iframes (OOPIFs)** are invisible to top-level `Runtime.evaluate`."
  `CDPSupervisor` state verbatim: "**Dialog queue** — `List[PendingDialog]` with `{id, type, message, default_prompt, session_id, opened_at}`"; "**Frame tree** — `Dict[frame_id, FrameInfo]` with parent relationships, URL, origin, whether cross-origin child session"; "**Session map** — `Dict[session_id, SessionInfo]` so interaction tools can route to the right attached session for OOPIF operations"; "**Recent console errors** — ring buffer of the last 50 for diagnostics". Subscriptions verbatim: "`Page.enable` — `javascriptDialogOpening`, `frameAttached`, `frameNavigated`, `frameDetached`; `Runtime.enable` — `executionContextCreated`, `consoleAPICalled`, `exceptionThrown`; `Target.setAutoAttach {autoAttach: true, flatten: true}` — surfaces child OOPIF targets; supervisor enables `Page`+`Runtime` on each." "One `asyncio.Task` running in a background daemon thread per Hermes `task_id`." "Thread-safe state access via a snapshot lock; tool handlers (sync) read the frozen snapshot without awaiting."
  Lifecycle verbatim: "**Start:** `SupervisorRegistry.get_or_start(task_id, cdp_url)` — called by `browser_navigate`, Browserbase session create, `/browser connect`. Idempotent. **Stop:** session teardown or `/browser disconnect`. Cancels the asyncio task, closes the WebSocket, discards state. **Rebind:** if the CDP URL changes (user reconnects to a new Chrome), the old supervisor is stopped and a fresh one started — state is never reused across endpoints."
  Backend support table verbatim (Backend | Dialog detect | Dialog respond | Frame tree | OOPIF `Runtime.evaluate` via `browser_cdp(frame_id=...)`): "Local Chrome (`--remote-debugging-port`) / `/browser connect`" ✓ | "✓ full workflow" | ✓ | ✓; "Browserbase" "✓ (via bridge)" | "✓ full workflow (via bridge)" | ✓ | ✓; "Camofox" "✗ no CDP (REST-only)" | ✗ | "partial via DOM snapshot" | ✗.
  Browserbase bridge verbatim: "Browserbase's CDP proxy uses Playwright internally and auto-dismisses native dialogs within ~10ms, so `Page.handleJavaScriptDialog` can't keep up. The supervisor injects a bridge script via `Page.addScriptToEvaluateOnNewDocument` that overrides `window.alert`/`confirm`/`prompt` with a synchronous XHR to a magic host (`hermes-dialog-bridge.invalid`). `Fetch.enable` intercepts those XHRs before they touch the network — the dialog becomes a `Fetch.requestPaused` event the supervisor captures, and `respond_to_dialog` fulfills via `Fetch.fulfillRequest` with a JSON body the injected script decodes."
- **Inputs / options:** `browser_dialog(action, prompt_text=None, dialog_id=None)` — verbatim: "`action=\"accept\"` / `\"dismiss\"` → responds to the specified or sole pending dialog (required); `prompt_text=...` → text to supply to a `prompt()` dialog; `dialog_id=...` → disambiguate when multiple dialogs are queued (rare). Tool is response-only." Dialog policies verbatim: "**`must_respond`** (default) — capture, surface in `browser_snapshot`, wait for explicit `browser_dialog(action=...)` call. After a 300s safety timeout with no response, auto-dismiss and log."; "`auto_dismiss` — record and dismiss immediately; agent sees it after the fact via `browser_state` inside `browser_snapshot`."; "`auto_accept` — record and accept (useful for `beforeunload`…)". "Policy is per-task; no per-dialog overrides."
- **Outputs / side effects:** Three new `browser_snapshot` fields, transcribed verbatim: `pending_dialogs` (`{id, type, message, opened_at}`), `recent_dialogs` (adds `closed_at`, `closed_by`), `frame_tree` (`top` with `frame_id`/`url`/`origin`; `children` with `frame_id`/`url`/`is_oopif`/`session_id`; `truncated`). `closed_by` values verbatim: "`\"agent\"` (we responded), `\"auto_policy\"` (local auto_dismiss/auto_accept), `\"watchdog\"` (must_respond timeout hit), or `\"remote\"` (browser/backend closed it on us, e.g. Browserbase)".
- **Config / env:** `browser.dialog_policy`, `browser.dialog_timeout_s` (defaults in `hermes_cli/config.py`).
- **Edge cases / guards:** Limits verbatim: "`recent_dialogs` — ring buffer of up to 20 recently-closed dialogs"; "`frame_tree` — Capped at 30 entries + OOPIF depth 2 to bound snapshot size on ad-heavy pages. `truncated: true` surfaces when limits were hit; agents needing the full tree can use `browser_cdp` with `Page.getFrameTree`." Availability gating verbatim: "Both surfaces gate on `_browser_cdp_check` (supervisor can only run when a CDP endpoint is reachable). On Camofox / no-backend sessions, the dialog tool is hidden and the snapshot omits the new fields — no schema bloat." OOPIF routing verbatim: "For same-origin iframes (no dedicated CDP session), the agent uses `contentWindow`/`contentDocument` from a top-level `Runtime.evaluate` instead — the supervisor surfaces an error pointing at that fallback when `frame_id` belongs to a non-OOPIF. On Browserbase, this is the only reliable path for iframe interaction — stateless CDP connections (opened per `browser_cdp` call) hit signed-URL expiry." Non-goals verbatim: "Detection/interaction for Camofox (upstream gap; tracked separately); Streaming dialog/frame events live to the user (would require gateway hooks); Persisting dialog history across sessions (in-memory only); Per-iframe dialog policies; Replacing `browser_cdp` — it stays as the escape hatch for the long tail (cookies, viewport, network throttling)." File layout verbatim: `tools/browser_supervisor.py` ("`CDPSupervisor`, `SupervisorRegistry`, `PendingDialog`, `FrameInfo`"), `tools/browser_dialog_tool.py`, `tools/browser_tool.py`, `toolsets.py` ("registers `browser_dialog` in `browser`, `hermes-acp`, `hermes-api-server`, and core toolsets (gated on CDP reachability)"), `hermes_cli/config.py`. Tests: `tests/tools/test_browser_supervisor.py` with "an asyncio mock CDP server".
- **Rebuild notes:** Hold one long-lived CDP socket per task, mirror its state into the existing snapshot rather than adding new tools, and when a backend swallows dialogs, intercept them at the page level with an injected shim plus request interception.

### Egress proxy internals (iron-proxy)  `id: docs-rest.dev-egress-internals`
- **Surface:** Docs / Core / Config
- **Where:** Developer Guide → **"Egress proxy internals"** (`developer-guide/egress-internals.md`, `sidebar_position: 14`).
- **What it does:** Documents the architecture, lifecycle, and load-bearing security invariants of the credential-injection egress firewall.
- **How it works:** Module layout verbatim: `agent/proxy_sources/iron_proxy.py` "Core: binary install, CA gen, config build, subprocess lifecycle, mappings I/O, PID/nonce defense. Pure-function surface where possible."; `hermes_cli/proxy_cli.py` "Wizard + slash command handlers. `hermes egress {install,setup,start,stop,status,disable,config}`."; `hermes_cli/main.py:_dispatch_egress` "Top-level subparser dispatcher. dest='egress_command' (intentionally disjoint from the inbound OAuth `hermes proxy` subparser, which uses dest='proxy_command')."; `hermes_cli/config.py: proxy schema` "The `proxy:` block in DEFAULT_CONFIG."; `tools/environments/docker.py` — `_egress_proxy_args_for_docker()` "Builds the volume_args / env_overrides / host_args triple that the Docker backend injects when `proxy.enabled: true`" and `DockerEnvironment.__init__` "Docker-side merge logic: collision detection against critical egress vars, NODE_OPTIONS append-merge via the `_HERMES_EGRESS_NODE_OPTIONS_APPEND` sentinel, enforce_on_docker precedence."; tests `tests/test_iron_proxy.py` (~70 hermetic), `tests/test_iron_proxy_cli.py` (~20), `tests/test_iron_proxy_e2e.py` ("gated on HERMES_RUN_E2E=1").
  Lifecycle for each command is transcribed step-by-step in the doc: `hermes egress install` (pinned tarball + `checksums.txt` from GitHub Releases, SHA-256 verify before extraction, `tarfile.extract(..., filter="data")` on Python 3.12+ with `_pick_tar_member` sanitisation fallback, stage into `~/.hermes/bin/.iron-proxy_XXXX`, chmod 755, `os.replace` atomic); `hermes egress setup` (5 steps: find/install binary; `ensure_ca_cert()` writing the CA key via `os.open(O_WRONLY|O_CREAT|O_TRUNC|O_NOFOLLOW, 0o600)` + `os.replace` and the cert 0o644; `discover_provider_mappings()` or BWS `fetch_bitwarden_secrets()` + `merge_mappings(...)` + `discover_uncovered_providers()`; `ensure_audit_log()` + `build_proxy_config(...)` + `write_proxy_config(cfg)` + `write_mappings(...)` all atomic 0o600; enable + credential_source preservation + `save_config`); `hermes egress start` (pre-checks, `_build_proxy_subprocess_env`, nonce plant `sha256(urandom(16))` into `NONCE_ENV`, log open with `O_NOFOLLOW` + 0o600 + st_uid check, `Popen(stdin=DEVNULL, stdout=log_fd, stderr=STDOUT, start_new_session=True)`, `_write_pidfile_safely` with `O_EXCL|O_NOFOLLOW` + uid check + nonce sidecar, SIGINT/SIGTERM handlers, do-while poll loop against the configured bind host); `hermes egress stop` (`_read_pid` + `_pid_alive`, starttime capture, SIGTERM, 5s grace, re-check starttime + alive, suppress SIGKILL on recycle, `_cleanup_state_files`).
- **Inputs / options:** CLI verbs verbatim: `hermes egress {install, setup, start, stop, status, disable, config}`; flags named: `--force` (install), `--from-bitwarden`, `--no-bitwarden`, `--rotate-tokens` (setup). Extension points verbatim: `_BEARER_PROVIDERS` ("maps env var name -> tuple of upstream hosts"), `_HEADER_AUTH_PROVIDERS` ("`hosts`, `match_headers`, `aliases`" — "Use `aliases` ONLY for interchangeable env-var names of the *same* credential… aliased names collapse into a single mapping, because two `require: true` rules on the same host reject each other's requests"), `_NON_BEARER_PROVIDERS` ("SigV4 / SDK-minted OAuth / request signatures… so the wizard and `hermes egress status` warn about it"), `_DEFAULT_ALLOWED_HOSTS`.
  Non-Docker backend wiring, all six steps verbatim: "1. Reads `load_config().get(\"proxy\", {})`; returns empty args if `enabled` is false. 2. Calls `iron_proxy.get_status()`; surfaces `enforce` semantics on `configured` / `pid` / `listening` / `ca_cert_path` failure paths. 3. Calls `iron_proxy.load_mappings()`; refuses to mount if empty AND `enforce_on_docker: true`. 4. Sets the seven env vars (HTTPS_PROXY, NO_PROXY, REQUESTS_CA_BUNDLE, SSL_CERT_FILE, CURL_CA_BUNDLE, NODE_EXTRA_CA_CERTS, HERMES_EGRESS_PROXY) and the per-mapping `HERMES_PROXY_TOKEN_<NAME>` vars. 5. Distributes the CA cert into the sandbox at a path the runtime will trust (typically `/etc/ssl/certs/hermes-egress-ca.crt`). 6. Implements collision detection against the user's backend-specific env config." "The Docker implementation is ~150 lines; expect similar volume for Modal / Daytona / SSH."
- **Outputs / side effects:** Files under `~/.hermes/proxy/`: `ca.key`, `ca.crt`, `proxy.yaml`, `mappings.json`, `iron-proxy.pid`, `iron-proxy.nonce`, `audit.log`, `iron-proxy.log`; the binary at `~/.hermes/bin/iron-proxy`.
- **Config / env:** `proxy.enabled`, `proxy.enforce_on_docker`, `proxy.allow_env_fallback`, `proxy.credential_source`, `proxy.tunnel_port`, `HERMES_RUN_E2E`, `HERMES_EGRESS_PROXY`, `HERMES_PROXY_TOKEN_<NAME>`.
- **Edge cases / guards:** Filesystem perms table, all nine rows verbatim (Path | Mode | Test): `~/.hermes/proxy/` (dir) `0o700` `test_proxy_state_dir_is_0o700`; `ca.key` `0o600` `test_ca_key_created_with_0o600`; `ca.crt` `0o644`; `proxy.yaml` `0o600`; `mappings.json` `0o600`; `iron-proxy.pid` `0o600`; `iron-proxy.nonce` `0o600`; `audit.log` `0o600` `test_ensure_audit_log_creates_with_0o600`; `iron-proxy.log` `0o600`. Rule verbatim: "All write paths use `os.open(O_WRONLY | O_CREAT | O_NOFOLLOW, 0o600)` + `os.fstat().st_uid` check. `shutil.copy2` + `os.chmod` is forbidden because it leaks a default-umask window."
  Other invariants verbatim: **Subprocess env minimisation** — "`_build_proxy_subprocess_env` MUST NOT use `os.environ.copy()`. The allowlist is `_PROXY_SUBPROCESS_ENV_ALLOWLIST` (PATH, HOME, locale, etc.) plus the env names referenced by `load_mappings()`." **Bind policy** — "`_default_http_listen` returns a single-element list: on Linux the docker bridge gateway IP…; on macOS/Windows Docker Desktop, loopback… Never `0.0.0.0`, never `:PORT` (INADDR_ANY)." plus "`_detect_docker_bridge_ip` validates via `ipaddress.IPv4Address` and rejects `is_unspecified` / `is_loopback` / `is_multicast` / `is_reserved` / `is_link_local` / `is_global`." **v0.39 schema constraint** — "`tunnel_listen` is the CONNECT + MITM listener (what `HTTPS_PROXY` traffic hits); `http_listen` only handles absolute-form plain-HTTP forwards… `build_proxy_config` therefore binds `tunnel_listen` on `tunnel_port` and `http_listen` on `tunnel_port + 1`… The Docker backend sets `HTTPS_PROXY` to `tunnel_port` and `HTTP_PROXY` to `tunnel_port + 1`." **Metrics port collision** — "`metrics.listen` defaults to `:9090` in iron-proxy v0.39 — the SAME port as Hermes's default `tunnel_port: 9090`. `build_proxy_config` MUST explicitly pin `metrics.listen: 127.0.0.1:0`." **Default deny CIDRs** — "covers loopback (v4 + v6), link-local (incl. IMDS at 169.254.169.254 and the IPv4-mapped-v6 form), RFC1918, IPv6 ULA, CGNAT, and the RFC2544 benchmark range… only an explicit empty list opts out." **Audit log fail-loud** — "`ensure_audit_log` raises `RuntimeError` on any `OSError`… `log.audit_path` is NOT a field in iron-proxy v0.39's `config.Log` struct". **Bitwarden mode fail-loud** — "Missing access token env var -> `cmd_start` refuses. Missing `project_id` -> `cmd_start` refuses. `bws secret list` returns no values for one or more mapped providers -> `_build_proxy_subprocess_env` raises." **docker_env collision detection** — "When `enforce_on_docker: true`, `docker_env` overrides on any of the egress-controlling vars… OR any mapped `real_env_name`… raises `RuntimeError` BEFORE the container starts." **PID recycling defense** — "`_pid_alive` MUST consult either the in-process `_proxy_nonce`… OR the on-disk `iron-proxy.nonce`… before trusting an `argv[0]` basename match. `stop_proxy` MUST re-check `/proc/<pid>/stat` starttime before SIGKILL and suppress the signal on starttime drift." **Token preservation** — "`merge_mappings(existing, discovered, rotate=False)` MUST return prior tokens for providers that overlap. Re-running `hermes egress setup` cannot silently 401 running sandboxes." **`credential_source` preservation** — "`cmd_setup` MUST NOT downgrade `credential_source: bitwarden` to `env` on re-run without an explicit `--no-bitwarden` flag." Every invariant names its regression test verbatim in the doc.
- **Rebuild notes:** Every file write goes through `O_NOFOLLOW` + explicit mode + uid check; the daemon's environment is allowlisted, never copied; PID identity is proven with a nonce, not a name match; and the bind host is derived from the container-reachability model rather than defaulting to loopback or `0.0.0.0`.

### Context compression — dual system, config, and 4-phase algorithm  `id: docs-rest.dev-compression`
- **Surface:** Docs / Core / Config
- **Where:** Developer Guide → **"Context Compression and Caching"** (`developer-guide/context-compression-and-caching.md`). Source files verbatim: `agent/context_engine.py` (ABC), `agent/context_compressor.py` (default engine), `agent/prompt_caching.py`, `gateway/run.py` (session hygiene), `run_agent.py` (`_compress_context`).
- **What it does:** Documents the two independent compression layers, every `compression.*` key, and the compressor's four-phase algorithm.
- **How it works:** Dual system verbatim: "**1. Gateway Session Hygiene (85% threshold)** — Located in `gateway/run.py`… a **safety net** that runs before the agent processes a message. It prevents API failures when sessions grow too large between turns (e.g., overnight accumulation in Telegram/Discord). **Threshold**: Fixed at 85% of model context length. **Token source**: Prefers actual API-reported tokens from last turn; falls back to rough character-based estimate (`estimate_messages_tokens_rough`). **Fires**: Only when `len(history) >= 4` and compression is enabled. **Purpose**: Catch sessions that escaped the agent's own compressor." Rationale verbatim: "Setting it at 50% (same as the agent) caused premature compression on every turn in long gateway sessions." "**2. Agent ContextCompressor (50% threshold, configurable)** — Located in `agent/context_compressor.py`. This is the **primary compression system** that runs inside the agent's tool loop with access to accurate, API-reported token counts."
  Engine resolution verbatim: "1. Check `plugins/context_engine/<name>/` directory; 2. Check general plugin system (`register_context_engine()`); 3. Fall back to built-in `ContextCompressor`. Plugin engines are **never auto-activated**."
  Four-phase algorithm verbatim: "**Phase 1: Prune Old Tool Results (cheap, no LLM call)** — Old tool results (>200 chars) outside the protected tail are replaced with `[Old tool output cleared to save context space]`."; "**Phase 2: Determine Boundaries** — `[0..2] ← protect_first_n (system + first exchange)`, `[3..N] ← middle turns → SUMMARIZED`, `[N..end] ← tail (by token budget OR protect_last_n)`. Tail protection is **token-budget based**: walks backward from the end, accumulating tokens until the budget is exhausted. Falls back to the fixed `protect_last_n` count if the budget would protect fewer messages. Boundaries are aligned to avoid splitting tool_call/tool_result groups. The `_align_boundary_backward()` method walks past consecutive tool results to find the parent assistant message."; "**Phase 3: Generate Structured Summary**" using an eight-section template transcribed verbatim (`## Goal`, `## Constraints & Preferences`, `## Progress` with `### Done` / `### In Progress` / `### Blocked`, `## Key Decisions`, `## Relevant Files`, `## Next Steps`, `## Critical Context`) — budget "Formula: `content_tokens × 0.20` (the `_SUMMARY_RATIO` constant); Minimum: 2,000 tokens; Maximum: `min(context_length × 0.05, 12,000)` tokens"; "**Phase 4: Assemble Compressed Messages** — 1. Head messages (with a note appended to system prompt on first compression); 2. Summary message (role chosen to avoid consecutive same-role violations); 3. Tail messages (unmodified). Orphaned tool_call/tool_result pairs are cleaned up by `_sanitize_tool_pairs()`: Tool results referencing removed calls → removed; Tool calls whose results were removed → stub result injected."
  Iterative re-compression verbatim: "On subsequent compressions, the previous summary is passed to the LLM with instructions to **update** it rather than summarize from scratch… The `_previous_summary` field on the compressor instance stores the last summary text."
  Computed defaults verbatim (200K model): `context_length = 200,000`; `threshold_tokens = 200,000 × 0.50 = 100,000`; `tail_token_budget = 100,000 × 0.20 = 20,000`; `max_summary_tokens = min(200,000 × 0.05, 12,000) = 10,000`.
- **Inputs / options:** Every parameter verbatim (Parameter | Default | Range | Description): `threshold` `0.50` `0.0-1.0` "Compression triggers when prompt tokens ≥ `threshold × context_length`"; `model_thresholds` `{}` map "Per-model overrides of `threshold`. Keys are substring-matched against the model name (longest match wins). The small-context floor still applies on top"; `target_ratio` `0.20` `0.10-0.80` "Controls tail protection token budget: `threshold_tokens × target_ratio` (legacy mode only — `lean` uses its own clamp)"; `tail_mode` `lean` `lean, legacy` — "`legacy` keeps a `target_ratio`-sized verbatim tail (~100K+ tokens on big-window models). `lean` keeps a clamped tail of `2.5% × context window` (10K floor, 25K cap) and instead carries continuity in the summary: a detailed identifier-preserving session log (produced by the same single summary request — lean compaction makes exactly one auxiliary LLM call per attempt), a mechanically extracted anchor index (PR numbers, SHAs, paths, error strings — regex, never paraphrased), every real user message quoted verbatim (newest-first budget), and a `session_search` recovery pointer… Result on 500K-token real sessions: ~49K retained vs ~162K… Old tool results inside the lean tail are demoted to one-line stubs carrying a recovery pointer"; `protect_last_n` `20` `≥1` "Minimum number of recent messages always preserved"; `min_tail_user_messages` `1` `≥1` "Minimum number of REAL (actionable) user messages guaranteed to survive in the uncompressed tail… Blank platform echoes, compaction handoffs, and synthetic continuation rows never count toward N. The guarantee wins over the tail token budget"; `protect_first_n` `3` (hardcoded) "System prompt + first exchange always preserved"; `idle_compact_after_seconds` `0` `≥0 seconds` "Opt-in: compact up front when a session resumes after this many seconds idle (0 = disabled). Skips when context ≤ threshold × target_ratio; honors cooldown/anti-thrash/lock guards"; `codex_gpt55_autoraise` `true` bool; `codex_gpt55_autoraise_notice` `true` bool; `codex_app_server_auto` `native` `native, hermes, off`; `codex_responses_native` `false` bool; `codex_responses_compact_threshold` `null` "`null` or positive integer — `null` follows the resolved local compression trigger with an 8,192 token safety margin… Automatic mode falls back to `200000` when no usable local trigger exists"; `in_place` `true` bool "Compact on the same session id instead of rotating to a new one".
