Failure semantics: "If delivery fails, the job still runs — it just won't send anywhere."
- **Rebuild notes:** Order troubleshooting by the layer that fails silently first (existence → schedule → ticker → clock → target → suppression → permissions → wrapping), and make every silent failure produce a specific error string rather than a misleading generic one.

### AWS Bedrock setup guide  `id: docs-rest.guide-aws-bedrock`
- **Surface:** Docs / Provider
- **Where:** Docs → Guides → **"AWS Bedrock"** (`guides/aws-bedrock.md`).
- **What it does:** Sets up Amazon Bedrock as a native provider "with full access to the Bedrock ecosystem: IAM authentication, Guardrails, cross-region inference profiles, and all foundation models."
- **How it works:** Per-family routing table verbatim (Model family | API route | Why): "Anthropic Claude" | "Anthropic SDK (`AnthropicBedrock`)" | "Prompt caching, thinking budgets, adaptive thinking — features not exposed via Converse"; "OpenAI GPT-5.5 / GPT-5.6 (Sol, Terra, Luna)" | "Bedrock Mantle **OpenAI Responses** endpoint (`bedrock-mantle.<region>.api.aws/openai/v1`)" | "These models are Mantle-only — their model cards list bedrock-runtime/Converse as unsupported"; "Everything else (Nova, DeepSeek, Llama, GPT-OSS, …)" | "Native **Converse API** (`bedrock-runtime`)" | "Full Bedrock feature set: Guardrails, inference profiles, streaming". Auth verbatim: "All three routes share the same AWS credential chain and region resolution… Requests to the Mantle endpoint are authenticated with `AWS_BEARER_TOKEN_BEDROCK` when set, or SigV4-signed via the standard boto3 credential chain otherwise."
- **Inputs / options:** Prerequisites verbatim: "**AWS credentials** — any source supported by the boto3 credential chain"; "**boto3** — install with `cd ~/.hermes/hermes-agent && uv pip install -e \".[bedrock]\"`"; "**IAM permissions**". Quick start: `hermes model` → "More providers..." → "AWS Bedrock" → region and model. Model table verbatim (Model | ID | Notes): Claude Sonnet 4.6 `us.anthropic.claude-sonnet-4-6` "Recommended — best balance of speed and capability"; Claude Opus 4.6 `us.anthropic.claude-opus-4-6-v1` "Most capable"; Claude Haiku 4.5 `us.anthropic.claude-haiku-4-5-20251001-v1:0` "Fastest Claude"; OpenAI GPT-5.6 Sol `openai.gpt-5.6-sol` "OpenAI frontier model (via Bedrock Mantle)"; OpenAI GPT-5.6 Terra `openai.gpt-5.6-terra` "Balanced (via Bedrock Mantle)"; OpenAI GPT-5.6 Luna `openai.gpt-5.6-luna` "Fast, affordable (via Bedrock Mantle)"; OpenAI GPT-5.5 `openai.gpt-5.5` "Previous OpenAI flagship (via Bedrock Mantle)"; Amazon Nova Pro `us.amazon.nova-pro-v1:0` "Amazon's flagship"; Amazon Nova Micro `us.amazon.nova-micro-v1:0` "Fastest, cheapest"; DeepSeek V3.2 `deepseek.v3.2` "Strong open model"; Llama 4 Scout 17B `us.meta.llama4-scout-17b-instruct-v1:0` "Meta's latest".
- **Outputs / side effects:** Writes provider/model/region into `~/.hermes/config.yaml`.
- **Config / env:** `AWS_BEARER_TOKEN_BEDROCK`; region "Set the AWS region in any of these ways (highest priority first)"; Guardrails config block; model-discovery config block.
- **Edge cases / guards:** Prefix semantics verbatim: "Models prefixed with `us.` use cross-region inference profiles, which provide better capacity and automatic failover across AWS regions. Models prefixed with `global.` route across all available regions worldwide. OpenAI `openai.*` model IDs are served by Bedrock Mantle in the configured region and don't use inference-profile prefixes." Prompt caching verbatim: "Hermes automatically applies prompt caching on the Bedrock **Converse API** path by inserting `cachePoint` markers after the system prompt, tool definitions, and the latest message. Because sending a `cachePoint` block to a model that doesn't support it raises a `ValidationException`, markers are only added for models on a known-good allowlist (Anthropic Claude and Amazon Nova model IDs); unknown models default to no cache markers." Context-window probing verbatim: "Hermes can probe the real limit by sending oversized requests at fixed tiers (~1.3M and ~2.2M tokens) and parsing the `maximum` reported in Bedrock's length-validation error. Probed values feed the same metadata cache as the static table; stale cached entries that under-report a model's window… are dropped automatically in favor of the larger known value." Zero-config path verbatim: "On AWS compute, attach an IAM role with `AmazonBedrockFullAccess` and you're done. No API keys, no `.env` configuration — Hermes detects the instance role automatically."
- **Rebuild notes:** Route per model family to whichever API exposes that family's features, share one credential chain across all routes, and gate provider-specific request extras (cache points) behind a known-good allowlist because unsupported fields hard-fail.

### Microsoft Foundry / Azure Foundry setup guide  `id: docs-rest.guide-azure-foundry`
- **Surface:** Docs / Provider
- **Where:** Docs → Guides → **"Microsoft Foundry"** (`guides/azure-foundry.md`), provider id `azure-foundry`.
- **What it does:** Configures Microsoft Foundry (formerly Azure AI Foundry) and Azure OpenAI, auto-detecting which of two wire formats the endpoint uses.
- **How it works:** Two transports verbatim: "**OpenAI-style** — `POST /v1/chat/completions` on endpoints like `https://<resource>.openai.azure.com/openai/v1`. Used for GPT-4.x, GPT-5.x, Llama, Mistral, and most open-weight models."; "**Anthropic-style** — `POST /v1/messages` on endpoints like `https://<resource>.services.ai.azure.com/anthropic`. Used when Microsoft Foundry serves Claude models via the Anthropic Messages API format." "The setup wizard probes your endpoint and auto-detects which transport it uses, which deployments are available, and each model's context length."
  Entra ID implementation verbatim: "OpenAI-style uses the OpenAI Python SDK's native callable `api_key=` contract — the SDK mints a fresh JWT per request automatically."; "Anthropic-style uses an `httpx.Client` with a request event hook installed by `agent.azure_identity_adapter.build_bearer_http_client`, because the Anthropic SDK does not accept callable `auth_token` natively. The hook rewrites `Authorization: Bearer <fresh-jwt>` per outbound request. Same Microsoft RBAC, same Foundry scope — the SDK contract is the only difference."
- **Inputs / options:** Prerequisites verbatim: "A Microsoft Foundry or Azure OpenAI resource with at least one deployment; The deployment's endpoint URL; **Either** an API key (from the Azure Portal under \"Keys and Endpoint\") **or** the **Azure AI User** RBAC role on the Foundry resource if you plan to use Microsoft Entra ID… Some tenants may show the role as **Foundry User** during Microsoft's rename rollout." Wizard flow verbatim: `hermes model` → "Select \"Azure Foundry\"" → "Enter your endpoint URL" → "Choose Authentication: 1. API key / 2. Microsoft Entra ID (managed identity / workload identity / az login)" → "(Entra) Hermes probes DefaultAzureCredential; on success it never asks for a key" → "(API key) Enter your API key" → "Pick a model from the list (or type a deployment name manually)"; Entra extras "(optional) user-assigned managed identity client ID", "(optional) Azure tenant ID". Config knob verbatim: "**`scope`** — the OAuth resource scope. Defaults to Microsoft's documented inference scope (`https://ai.azure.com/.default`). Override only if your resource was provisioned against a non-standard audience."
- **Outputs / side effects:** Writes an `azure-foundry` provider block into `config.yaml`; "No secrets land in `~/.hermes/.env` for Entra mode — `azure-identity` caches tokens in-process (and where available, in your OS keychain / `~/.IdentityService`)."
- **Config / env:** `AZURE_FOUNDRY_API_KEY`, `AZURE_FOUNDRY_BASE_URL`, and the standard `AZURE_*` variables read by `azure-identity`.
- **Edge cases / guards:** Preflight verbatim: "The wizard runs a bounded preflight probe (10 s timeout). On failure it offers to \"save anyway, validate later\" — useful when configuring on a machine that doesn't yet have credentials but will at runtime (e.g. preparing config for a managed-identity deployment)." Entra rationale verbatim: "No long-lived API keys to rotate or revoke.; RBAC-driven access — grant or remove `Azure AI User` on the Foundry resource, no config rewrite needed.; Access and audit logs are segmented by assignee instead of all callers sharing one static key.; Single auth surface for Azure VMs, AKS pods, App Service, Functions, Container Apps, and Foundry Agent Service via managed identity.; Workload identity and service-principal flows for CI/CD pipelines." Context length verbatim: "resolved via Hermes' standard metadata chain (`models.dev`, provider metadata, and hardcoded family fallbacks) and stored in `config.yaml`."
- **Rebuild notes:** Probe the endpoint to pick the transport instead of asking the user; when an SDK won't accept a callable credential, install a per-request auth hook rather than caching a token.

### Google Gemini (AI Studio) setup guide  `id: docs-rest.guide-google-gemini`
- **Surface:** Docs / Provider
- **Where:** Docs → Guides → **"Google Gemini"** (`guides/google-gemini.md`).
- **What it does:** Configures Gemini through the native Google AI Studio / Gemini API rather than the OpenAI-compatible endpoint.
- **How it works:** Translation layer verbatim: "Hermes still keeps the agent loop in OpenAI-shaped messages, then translates each request to Gemini's native schema: `messages[]` → Gemini `contents[]`; system prompts → Gemini `systemInstruction`; tool schemas → Gemini `functionDeclarations`; tool results → Gemini `functionResponse` parts; streaming responses → OpenAI-shaped stream chunks for the Hermes loop." Thought signatures verbatim: "For Gemini 3 tool use, Hermes preserves the `thoughtSignature` values attached to function-call parts and replays them on the next tool turn. That covers the validation-critical path for multi-step agent workflows. Gemini 3 may also attach thought signatures to other response parts. Hermes' native adapter is optimized for agent tool loops today, so it does not yet replay every non-tool-call signature with full part-level fidelity."
- **Inputs / options:** Prerequisites verbatim: "**Google AI Studio API key** — create one at aistudio.google.com/apikey; **Billing-enabled Google Cloud project** — recommended for agent use. Gemini's free tier is too small for long-running agent sessions because Hermes may make several model calls per user turn; **Hermes installed** — no extra Python package is required for the native Gemini provider." Setup: set `GOOGLE_API_KEY` or `GEMINI_API_KEY` ("Hermes checks both names for the `gemini` provider"), then `hermes model` → "More providers..." → "Google AI Studio". Model table verbatim (Model | ID | Notes): Gemini 3.7 Flash `gemini-3.7-flash` "Recommended default balance of speed, capability, and multimodal understanding"; Gemini 3.1 Pro Preview `gemini-3.1-pro-preview` "Most capable reasoning, math, and coding model"; Gemini 3.5 Flash Lite `gemini-3.5-flash-lite` "Fastest and lowest-cost option for lightweight tasks"; Gemini 2.5 Flash `gemini-2.5-flash` "Previous generation fast model with thinking capabilities"; Gemini 2.5 Pro `gemini-2.5-pro` "Previous generation complex reasoning model". Aliases verbatim: "`gemini-pro-latest` and `gemini-flash-latest` are useful when you want Google to advance the model automatically without changing your Hermes config. Note that your usage charges may be affected if newer models introduce different rates."
- **Outputs / side effects:** `model.provider: gemini` + base URL in `config.yaml`; key in `.env`.
- **Config / env:** `GOOGLE_API_KEY`, `GEMINI_API_KEY`, `GEMINI_BASE_URL`.
- **Edge cases / guards:** Endpoint choice verbatim: "For Hermes agent sessions, prefer the native Gemini endpoint above… The OpenAI-compatible endpoint is still useful when you specifically need OpenAI API compatibility. If you previously set `GEMINI_BASE_URL` to the `/openai` URL, remove it or change it: `GEMINI_BASE_URL=https://generativelanguage.googleapis.com/v1beta`". Model-ID form verbatim: "Use Gemini's native model IDs such as `gemini-3.7-flash`, not OpenRouter-style IDs like `google/gemini-3.7-flash`, when `provider: gemini`." Availability verbatim: "Model availability changes over time. If a model disappears or is not enabled for your key, run `hermes model` again."
- **Rebuild notes:** Keep one internal message shape and translate at the transport boundary; preserve and replay any provider-issued opaque continuation tokens (thought signatures) or multi-step tool turns fail validation.

### Google Vertex AI setup guide  `id: docs-rest.guide-google-vertex`
- **Surface:** Docs / Provider
- **Where:** Docs → Guides → **"Google Vertex AI"** (`guides/google-vertex.md`).
- **What it does:** Runs Gemini models on Google Cloud Vertex AI through Vertex's OpenAI-compatible endpoint, with GCP billing and enterprise rate limits.
- **How it works:** Token model verbatim: "Vertex has **no static API key** for the standard endpoint. Every request needs a short-lived **OAuth2 access token** (≈1 hour TTL) minted from either a service-account JSON or Application Default Credentials (ADC). Hermes mints and **auto-refreshes** these tokens for you — you never paste a token by hand. This is why pasting a temporary token into a custom provider's `api_key` field does not work: it expires mid-session." Config split verbatim: "The **credential path** is a pointer to a secret and lives in `~/.hermes/.env`. **Project ID and region** are non-secret routing settings and live in `~/.hermes/config.yaml`."
- **Inputs / options:** Prerequisites verbatim: "**A Google Cloud project** with the **Vertex AI API enabled** and billing active."; "**Credentials**, one of:"; "**`google-auth`** — installed automatically the first time you select Vertex (lazy install). Run `hermes setup` to repair a managed install if that fails." Setup verbatim: Option A service-account JSON ("recommended for servers / gateways"), Option B ADC ("good for local dev"); `hermes model` → "More providers..." → "Google Vertex AI" → "Enter your GCP project ID (or leave blank to use the one in your credentials)" → "Choose a region (default: global)" → "Select a Gemini model". Model table verbatim (Model | ID): Gemini 3.1 Pro Preview `google/gemini-3.1-pro-preview`; Gemini 3 Pro Preview `google/gemini-3-pro-preview`; Gemini 3 Flash Preview `google/gemini-3-flash-preview`; Gemini 3.1 Flash Lite Preview `google/gemini-3.1-flash-lite-preview`; Gemini 2.5 Pro `google/gemini-2.5-pro`; Gemini 2.5 Flash `google/gemini-2.5-flash` — "Vertex requires the `google/` vendor prefix on model IDs."
- **Outputs / side effects:** `vertex.project_id` / `vertex.region` in `config.yaml`; credential path in `.env`.
- **Config / env:** `VERTEX_CREDENTIALS_PATH`, `GOOGLE_APPLICATION_CREDENTIALS` ("One of these (checked in this order); omit both to use ADC"), `VERTEX_REGION`, `vertex.project_id`, `vertex.region`.
- **Edge cases / guards:** Region verbatim: "The Gemini 3.x preview models are served through the `global` endpoint. Regional endpoints (`us-central1`, etc.) may 404 them. Leave `region: global` unless you have a specific reason to pin a region." Reasoning verbatim: "Vertex exposes Gemini's thinking budget through the OpenAI-compatible surface. Hermes maps its reasoning-effort setting onto `extra_body.google.thinking_config` automatically, so `reasoning_effort` works the same way it does on other Gemini surfaces." Troubleshooting entries verbatim: "**\"Vertex AI credentials could not be resolved\"** — Hermes found neither a service-account JSON nor working ADC. Either set `VERTEX_CREDENTIALS_PATH`…, or run `gcloud auth application-default login`."; "**`google-auth` not installed**"; "**404 on Gemini 3.x models** — You are probably on a regional endpoint. Set `region: global`"; "**403 / permission denied** — The service account (or your ADC identity) needs the `roles/aiplatform.user` role on the project, and the Vertex AI API must be enabled." Diagnostics: `hermes doctor` "reports whether Vertex credentials can be resolved (service-account path or ADC) and whether the provider is configured."
- **Rebuild notes:** For token-minting providers, own the refresh loop in the client and refuse to accept a pasted bearer as an "API key" — an expiring credential in a static field is a guaranteed mid-session failure.

### MiniMax OAuth setup guide  `id: docs-rest.guide-minimax-oauth`
- **Surface:** Docs / Provider
- **Where:** Docs → Guides → **"MiniMax OAuth"** (`guides/minimax-oauth.md`).
- **What it does:** Logs into MiniMax with a browser PKCE OAuth flow — no API key or credit card.
- **How it works:** Overview table verbatim (Item | Value): "Provider ID" `minimax-oauth`; "Display name" "MiniMax (OAuth)"; "Auth type" "Browser OAuth (PKCE redirect flow)"; "Transport" "Anthropic Messages-compatible (`anthropic_messages`)"; "Models" "`MiniMax-M2.7`, `MiniMax-M2.7-highspeed`"; "Global endpoint" `https://api.minimax.io/anthropic`; "China endpoint" `https://api.minimaxi.com/anthropic`; "Requires env var" "No (`MINIMAX_API_KEY` is **not** used for this provider)". Transport rationale verbatim: "The transport reuses the `anthropic_messages` adapter (MiniMax exposes an Anthropic Messages-compatible endpoint at `/anthropic`), so all existing tool-calling, streaming, and context features work without any adapter changes."
- **Inputs / options:** Prerequisites verbatim: "Python 3.9+; Hermes Agent installed; A MiniMax account at minimax.io (global) or minimaxi.com (China); A browser available on the local machine (or use `--no-browser` for remote sessions)." Quick start: `hermes model` → "Select \"MiniMax (OAuth)\"" → browser authorization → "Select a model (MiniMax-M2.7 or MiniMax-M2.7-highspeed)". Headless: `--no-browser` — "Hermes will print the verification URL and user code — open the URL on any device and enter the code when prompted." Status check via the `◆ Auth Providers` section.
- **Outputs / side effects:** "credentials are stored under `~/.hermes/auth.json` and are refreshed automatically before each session."
- **Config / env:** `MINIMAX_CN_API_KEY`, `MINIMAX_CN_BASE_URL` (for the China provider).
- **Edge cases / guards:** China region verbatim: "If your account is on the China platform (`minimaxi.com`), use the API-key-based `minimax-cn` provider instead — `minimax-cn` is registered with `auth_type=\"api_key\"` only (no OAuth flow)." Refresh verbatim: "Token refresh (standard OAuth `refresh_token` grant) runs automatically at each session start when the access token is within 60 seconds of expiry."
- **Rebuild notes:** Reuse an existing transport adapter whenever a vendor ships a compatible wire format; refresh proactively on a small expiry margin at session start rather than reactively on 401.

### xAI Grok OAuth (SuperGrok / X Premium+) setup guide  `id: docs-rest.guide-xai-grok-oauth`
- **Surface:** Docs / Provider
- **Where:** Docs → Guides → **"xAI Grok OAuth (SuperGrok / X Premium+)"** (`guides/xai-grok-oauth.md`).
- **What it does:** Signs into xAI Grok with a device-code OAuth flow against `accounts.x.ai` using a SuperGrok or X Premium+ subscription instead of an API key.
- **How it works:** Overview table verbatim (Item | Value): "Provider ID" `xai-oauth`; "Display name" "xAI Grok OAuth (SuperGrok / X Premium+)"; "Auth type" "Browser OAuth 2.0 device code"; "Transport" "xAI Responses API (`codex_responses`)"; "Default model" `grok-4.6`; "Endpoint" `https://api.x.ai/v1`; "Auth server" `https://accounts.x.ai`; "Requires env var" "No (`XAI_API_KEY` is **not** used for this provider)"; "Subscription" "SuperGrok or X Premium+". Cross-surface reuse verbatim: "The same OAuth bearer token is also reused by every direct-to-xAI surface in Hermes — TTS, image generation, video generation, and transcription — so a single login covers all four." Subscription linking verbatim: "When you sign in with an X account that has Premium+, xAI automatically links the subscription status to your xAI session, so the OAuth flow works the same as it does for direct SuperGrok subscribers."
- **Inputs / options:** Prerequisites verbatim: "Python 3.9+; Hermes Agent installed; An active **SuperGrok** subscription on your xAI account, **or** an **X Premium+** subscription on the X account you sign in with; A browser available anywhere you can open the printed verification URL." Quick start: `hermes model` → "Select \"xAI Grok OAuth (SuperGrok / X Premium+)\"" → verification URL/code → "Pick a model (grok-4.6 is at the top)". Status: `◆ Auth Providers` section "will show the current state of every provider, including `xai-oauth`."
- **Outputs / side effects:** "credentials are stored under `~/.hermes/auth.json` and refreshed automatically before they expire"; a provider block in `config.yaml`.
- **Config / env:** none required; `XAI_API_KEY` explicitly unused by this provider.
- **Edge cases / guards:** Headless verbatim: "On servers, containers, browser-only consoles (Cloud Shell, Codespaces, EC2 Instance Connect), or SSH sessions where Hermes cannot open a browser locally, Hermes prints the xAI verification URL and user code. Open the URL in any browser on your laptop or in the cloud console, enter the code if prompted, and Hermes will keep polling until xAI approves the login. No SSH tunnel or local callback listener is required. The same device-code flow applies when you sign in from the web dashboard or the desktop app." Known failure (from the providers page): "`HTTP 403` after a successful login — xAI has restricted OAuth API access to specific SuperGrok tiers despite an active in-app subscription"; workaround is an `XAI_API_KEY`.
- **Rebuild notes:** Device-code flows are strictly better than loopback-callback OAuth for headless and remote hosts — no tunnel, no listener; and one issued token should serve every surface that talks to the same vendor.

### Run Local LLMs on Mac (llama.cpp vs MLX/omlx)  `id: docs-rest.guide-local-llm-on-mac`
- **Surface:** Docs / Provider / Platform:macOS
- **Where:** Docs → Guides → **"Run Local LLMs on Mac"** (`guides/local-llm-on-mac.md`).
- **What it does:** Compares the two Apple-Silicon local-inference backends, gives full launch commands, and benchmarks them.
- **How it works:** Backend table verbatim (Backend | Install | Best at | Format): **llama.cpp** `brew install llama.cpp` "Fastest time-to-first-token, quantized KV cache for low memory" GGUF; **omlx** "omlx.ai" "Fastest token generation, native Metal optimization" "MLX (safetensors)". Model-variant table verbatim (Variant | Size on disk | RAM needed (128K context) | Backend): "Qwen3.5-9B-Q4_K_M (GGUF)" "5.3 GB" "~10 GB with quantized KV cache" llama.cpp; "Qwen3.5-9B-mlx-lm-mxfp4 (MLX)" "~5 GB" "~12 GB" omlx. Memory rule verbatim: "model size + KV cache. A 9B Q4 model is ~5 GB. The KV cache at 128K context with Q4 quantization adds ~4-5 GB. With default (f16) KV cache, that balloons to ~16 GB. The quantized KV cache flags in llama.cpp are the key trick for memory-constrained systems."
- **Inputs / options:** llama.cpp flag table verbatim (Flag | Purpose): `-ngl 99` "Offload all layers to GPU (Metal). Use a high number to ensure nothing stays on CPU."; `-c 131072` "Context window size (128K tokens). Reduce this if you're low on memory."; `-np 1` "Number of parallel slots. Keep at 1 for single-user use — more slots split your memory budget."; `-fa on` "Flash attention. Reduces memory usage and speeds up long-context inference."; `--cache-type-k q4_0` "Quantize the key cache to 4-bit. **This is the big memory saver.**"; `--cache-type-v q4_0` "Quantize the value cache to 4-bit. Together with the above, this cuts KV cache memory by ~75% vs f16."; `--host 0.0.0.0` "Listen on all interfaces. Use `127.0.0.1` if you don't need network access." KV-cache memory table verbatim (KV cache type | KV cache memory (128K ctx, 9B model)): "f16 (default)" "~16 GB"; "q8_0" "~8 GB"; "**q4_0**" "**~4 GB**". Page sections: "Option A: llama.cpp" (Install / Download the model / Start the server / Memory optimization for constrained systems / Test it / Get the model name); "Option B: MLX via omlx" (Install / Download the model / Start the server / Test it / List available models); "Connect to Hermes"; "Timeouts".
- **Outputs / side effects:** A local OpenAI-compatible server Hermes points at.
- **Config / env:** n/a (see the custom-endpoint config).
- **Edge cases / guards:** Benchmark table verbatim (Metric | llama.cpp (Q4_K_M) | MLX (mxfp4) | Winner): "**TTFT (avg)**" "**67 ms**" "289 ms" "llama.cpp (4.3x faster)"; "**TTFT (p50)**" "**66 ms**" "286 ms" "llama.cpp (4.3x faster)"; "**Generation (avg)**" "70 tok/s" "**96 tok/s**" "MLX (37% faster)"; "**Generation (p50)**" "70 tok/s" "**96 tok/s**" "MLX (37% faster)"; "**Total time (512 tokens)**" "7.3s" "**5.5s**" "MLX (25% faster)". Interpretation verbatim: "**llama.cpp** excels at prompt processing — its flash attention + quantized KV cache pipeline gets you the first token in ~66ms…"; "**MLX** generates tokens ~37% faster once it gets going…"; "Both backends are **extremely consistent** — variance across runs was negligible." Recommendation table verbatim (Use case | Recommendation): "Interactive chat, low-latency tools" llama.cpp; "Long-form generation, bulk processing" "MLX (omlx)"; "Memory-constrained (8-16 GB)" "llama.cpp (quantized KV cache is unmatched)"; "Serving multiple models simultaneously" "omlx (built-in multi-model support)"; "Maximum compatibility (Linux too)" llama.cpp.
- **Rebuild notes:** Publish measured TTFT-vs-throughput numbers rather than prose claims, and surface the one flag pair (quantized KV cache) that changes the memory envelope by 4×.

### Run Hermes Locally with Ollama — Zero API Cost  `id: docs-rest.guide-local-ollama-setup`
- **Surface:** Docs / Provider
- **Where:** Docs → Guides → **"Run Hermes Locally with Ollama — Zero API Cost"** (`guides/local-ollama-setup.md`).
- **What it does:** An eight-step walkthrough from installing Ollama to running a fully local agent, optionally as a gateway bot.
- **How it works:** Outcome verbatim: "Ollama serving one or more open-weight models; Hermes connected to Ollama as a custom endpoint; A working local agent that can edit files, run commands, and browse the web; Optional: a Telegram/Discord bot powered entirely by your own hardware." Steps verbatim: "Step 1: Install Ollama"; "Step 2: Pull a Model"; "Step 3: Configure Hermes"; "Step 4: Start Using Hermes"; "Step 5: Pick the Right Model for Your Task"; "Step 6: Optimize for Speed"; "Step 7: Run as a Gateway Bot (Optional)"; "Step 8: Set Up Fallbacks (Optional)".
- **Inputs / options:** Hardware table verbatim (Component | Minimum | Recommended): **RAM** "8 GB (for 3B models)" "32+ GB (for 27B+ models)"; **Storage** "5 GB free" "30+ GB (for multiple models)"; **CPU** "4 cores" "8+ cores (AMD EPYC, Ryzen, Intel Xeon)"; **GPU** "Not required" "NVIDIA GPU with 8+ GB VRAM speeds things up significantly". Model table verbatim (Model | Size on Disk | RAM Needed | Tool Calling | Best For): `gemma4:31b` "~20 GB" "24+ GB" "Yes" "Best quality — strong tool use and reasoning"; `gemma2:27b` "~16 GB" "20+ GB" "No" "Conversational tasks, no tool use"; `gemma2:9b` "~5 GB" "8+ GB" "No" "Fast chat, Q&A — cannot call tools"; `llama3.2:3b` "~2 GB" "4+ GB" "No" "Lightweight quick answers only". Hermes connection verbatim: "**Base URL:** `http://localhost:11434/v1`; **API Key:** Leave empty or type `no-key` (Ollama doesn't need one); **Model:** `gemma4:31b` (or whichever model you pulled)". Task-to-model table verbatim (Task | Recommended Model | Why): "File edits, code, terminal commands" `gemma4:31b` "Only model with reliable tool calling"; "Quick Q&A (no tool use needed)" `gemma2:9b` "Fast responses for conversational tasks"; "Lightweight chat" `llama3.2:3b` "Fastest, but very limited capabilities". Speed subsections verbatim: "Increase Ollama's Context Window" (Modelfile extending context); "Keep the Model Loaded" (`OLLAMA_KEEP_ALIVE=24h`, systemd `override.conf` at `/etc/systemd/system/ollama.service.d/override.conf`); "Use GPU Offloading (If Available)".
- **Outputs / side effects:** A local agent; optionally a bot.
- **Config / env:** `HERMES_API_TIMEOUT=1800` (in `~/.hermes/.env`), `OLLAMA_KEEP_ALIVE`.
- **Edge cases / guards:** Troubleshooting verbatim: "**\"Connection refused\" on startup**"; "**Slow responses**" — "**Check model size vs RAM:** If your model needs more RAM than available, it swaps to disk…; **Check `ollama ps`:** If no GPU layers are offloaded, responses are CPU-bound…; **Reduce context:** Large conversations slow down inference. Use `/compress` regularly, or set a lower compression threshold in config."; "**Slow first response (prefill)**" — "**Keep the model loaded** — Ollama unloads idle models after 5 minutes, adding a full reload before the next prefill. Set `OLLAMA_KEEP_ALIVE=24h`; **Widen the API timeout** — set `HERMES_API_TIMEOUT=1800`; **Measure and trim the fixed prompt** — run `hermes prompt-size` for a byte breakdown of the system prompt and tool schemas, then disable unused toolsets with `hermes tools` and uninstall skills you don't need with `hermes skills`; **Use GPU offloading**"; "**Model doesn't follow tool calls**".
- **Rebuild notes:** Ship a `prompt-size` command so users can see and shrink the fixed prompt cost — on local models the constant system-prompt prefill dominates perceived latency.

### Run Nemotron 3 Ultra free in Hermes Agent  `id: docs-rest.guide-nemotron-free`
- **Surface:** Docs / Provider
- **Where:** Docs → Guides → **"Run Nemotron 3 Ultra free in Hermes Agent"** (`guides/run-nemotron-3-ultra-free.md`).
- **What it does:** Two paths (desktop app, CLI) to running the free-tier Nemotron 3 Ultra model through Nous Portal.
- **How it works:** **"Option A — Desktop app (recommended)"** steps verbatim: "1. Download and install; 2. Connect Nous Portal; 3. Pick the free Nemotron 3 Ultra model; 4. Start chatting". **"Option B — Command line"** steps verbatim: "1. Install Hermes Agent; 2. Run Quick Setup; 3. Create a Nous Portal account; 4. Connect your account; 5. Select the free Nemotron 3 Ultra model; 6. Start chatting".
- **Inputs / options:** Switching later verbatim: "**Desktop app:** open the model picker, search for **nemotron 3 ultra**, and select the **Free tier** variant."; "**CLI / TUI:** switch any time from inside a session with `/model nvidia/nemotron-3-ultra:free`, or run `/model` to open the picker".
- **Outputs / side effects:** Model selection persisted in config.
- **Config / env:** n/a
- **Edge cases / guards:** Troubleshooting verbatim: "**Don't see the model in the list?** Make sure you finished the Nous Portal connection and that you're on the **Free** plan. In the CLI, `hermes portal info` confirms you're logged in and routing through Nous."; "**Picked the wrong variant?** Re-select `nvidia/nemotron-3-ultra:free` — the `:free` suffix is required to stay on the no-cost tier."; "**Browser didn't open / you're on a remote host (CLI)?** See OAuth over SSH / Remote Hosts for port-forwarding workarounds."
- **Rebuild notes:** When a free tier is a model-id suffix, say so explicitly — users silently fall onto the paid variant otherwise.

### Run Hermes Agent with Nous Portal (full walkthrough)  `id: docs-rest.guide-run-with-nous-portal`
- **Surface:** Docs / Provider
- **Where:** Docs → Guides → **"Run Hermes Agent with Nous Portal"** (`guides/run-hermes-with-nous-portal.md`).
- **What it does:** The end-to-end Portal setup: subscription, one-shot setup, verification, first conversation, model choice, Tool Gateway routing, voice, cron, profiles, troubleshooting.
- **How it works:** Sections verbatim: "1. Get a subscription"; "2. Run the one-shot setup" (+ "What if I'm SSH'd into a server?"); "3. Verify it worked"; "4. Run your first conversation"; "5. Pick the model you actually want" (+ "Don't pick Hermes-4 for agent work"); "6. (Optional) Customize Tool Gateway routing"; "7. (Optional) Enable voice mode"; "8. (Optional) Cron + always-on workflows"; "Profiles and multi-user setups"; "Troubleshooting". Prerequisites verbatim: "Hermes Agent installed; A web browser on the machine you're setting up (or SSH port forwarding); About 5 minutes."
- **Inputs / options:** SSH options verbatim: "Option A: SSH port forwarding (preferred)"; "Option B: device-code login (works from Cloud Shell, Codespaces, EC2 Instance Connect)" then "re-run `hermes setup --portal` to wire the provider + gateway". Tool-routing example verbatim: "→ Web search → \"Nous Subscription\" (recommended); → Image generation → \"Nous Subscription\" (recommended); → Browser → \"Browserbase\" (your existing key); → TTS → \"Nous Subscription\" (recommended)". Voice verbatim: "→ pick \"Nous Subscription\" for TTS; → pick a speech-to-text backend (local faster-whisper is free, no setup)".

- **Config / env:** `model.provider`, tool-category selection keys.

- **Rebuild notes:** Before/after comparison table verbatim (Without Portal | With Portal): "1× OpenRouter / Anthropic / OpenAI key in `.env`" | "1× OAuth refresh token, no `.env` keys"; "1× Firecrawl key for web" | "Web routed through gateway"; "1× FAL key for image gen" | "Image gen routed through gateway"; "1× Browser Use / Browserbase key for browser" | "Browser routed through gateway"; "1× OpenAI key for TTS / voice mode" | "TTS routed through gateway"; "5 separate dashboards, top-ups, invoices" | "1 subscription, 1 invoice"; "Cross-machine: replicate all 5 keys" | "Cross-machine: re-OAuth once". That table is the whole value proposition — state credential-count reduction in concrete numbers.

### Manage Hermes Cloud with MCP  `id: docs-rest.guide-manage-hermes-cloud-mcp`
- **Surface:** Docs / Tool
- **Where:** Docs → Guides → **"Manage Hermes Cloud with MCP"** (`guides/manage-hermes-cloud-with-mcp.md`).
- **What it does:** Adds the Hermes Cloud MCP server so the agent can list, inspect, start/stop/restart, create, destroy, and update cloud agent instances in natural language.
- **How it works:** Capability table verbatim (Ask for… | Under the hood): "\"List my cloud agents\"" | "`agents` (list)"; "\"What's the status of `<name>`?\"" | "`agents` (get / status)"; "\"Roughly what is this instance costing?\"" | "`agents` (cost_estimate)"; "\"Start / stop / restart `<name>`\"" | "`agent` (start / stop / restart)"; "\"Spin up a new instance called `<name>`\"" | "`agent` (create)"; "\"Destroy `<name>`\"" | "`agent` (destroy)"; "\"Update the env / image on `<name>`\"" | "`agent` (update_env / update_image)". Steps verbatim: "Step 1: add the server" (+ "Choosing an organization"); "Step 2: verify it connected"; "Step 3: use it"; "Configuration" (+ "Limiting the tool surface"); "Troubleshooting".
- **Inputs / options:** Prerequisites verbatim: "A Nous Portal account with Hermes Cloud access (at least one instance, or the ability to create one)."; "MCP support installed. If you used the standard install script it's already there".
- **Outputs / side effects:** An `mcp_servers` entry with `auth: oauth`; cloud-instance mutations.
- **Config / env:** `mcp_servers.<name>.{url, auth}`; tool filtering via `tools.include`/`exclude`.
- **Edge cases / guards:** Troubleshooting headings verbatim: "The browser shows an org picker and I'm not sure which to choose"; "\"invalid_client\" or \"unknown client\" on connect"; "The tools aren't showing up after adding the server"; "It asks me to log in again"; "Headless / SSH / remote host".
- **Rebuild notes:** Expose fleet management as a small MCP surface (two tools with action verbs) rather than many single-purpose tools; that keeps the schema cheap while covering the whole lifecycle.

### Tutorial: Build a Daily Briefing Bot  `id: docs-rest.guide-daily-briefing-bot`
- **Surface:** Docs
- **Where:** Docs → Guides → **"Tutorial: Build a Daily Briefing Bot"** (`guides/daily-briefing-bot.md`).
- **What it does:** The beginner cron tutorial: test the workflow by hand, schedule it, customise it, then manage the job.
- **How it works:** Sections verbatim: "## What We're Building"; "## Prerequisites"; "## Step 1: Test the Workflow Manually"; "## Step 2: Create the Cron Job" with "### Option A: Natural Language (in chat)" and "### Option B: CLI Slash Command", plus "### The Golden Rule: Self-Contained Prompts"; "## Step 3: Customize the Briefing" with "### Multi-Topic Briefings", "### Using Delegation for Parallel Research", "### Weekday-Only Schedule", "### Twice-Daily Briefings", "### Adding Personal Context with Memory"; "## Step 4: Manage Your Jobs" with "### List All Scheduled Jobs", "### Remove a Job", "### Check Gateway Status"; "## Going Further".
- **Inputs / options:** Both creation paths (chat natural language vs `/cron` slash command); schedule variants for weekday-only and twice-daily.
- **Outputs / side effects:** A recurring cron job delivering a briefing.
- **Config / env:** n/a
- **Edge cases / guards:** "The Golden Rule: Self-Contained Prompts" — a cron agent has no conversation history.
- **Rebuild notes:** Teach automation by making the user run the workflow by hand first; only then schedule the exact prompt that worked.

### Set Up a Team Telegram Assistant  `id: docs-rest.guide-team-telegram-assistant`
- **Surface:** Docs / Platform:Telegram
- **Where:** Docs → Guides → **"Set Up a Team Telegram Assistant"** (`guides/team-telegram-assistant.md`).
- **What it does:** Six-step walkthrough from BotFather to a service-managed team bot with allowlists or DM pairing, a home channel, a personality, and scheduled tasks.
- **How it works:** Sections verbatim: "## Step 1: Create a Telegram Bot"; "## Step 2: Configure the Gateway" ("### Option A: Interactive Setup (Recommended)", "### Option B: Manual Configuration", "### Finding Your User ID"); "## Step 3: Start the Gateway" ("### Quick Test", "### Production: Install as a Service", "### Verify It's Running"); "## Step 4: Set Up Team Access" ("### Approach A: Static Allowlist", "### Approach B: DM Pairing (Recommended for Teams)", "### Security Considerations"); "## Step 5: Configure the Bot" ("### Set a Home Channel", "### Configure Tool Progress Display", "### Set Up a Personality with SOUL.md", "### Add Project Context"); "## Step 6: Set Up Scheduled Tasks" ("### Daily Standup Summary", "### Server Health Check", "### Managing Scheduled Tasks").
- **Inputs / options:** Service management commands verbatim: "Linux — manage the default user service"; "View live logs"; "Keep running after SSH logout"; "Linux servers — explicit system-service commands"; "macOS — manage the service". Pairing management verbatim: "See all pending and approved users"; "Revoke someone's access"; "Clear expired pending codes". Env vars: `TELEGRAM_BOT_TOKEN` ("Telegram bot token from BotFather"), the numeric allowlist ("Your Telegram user ID (numeric)").
- **Outputs / side effects:** A running gateway service; `.env` entries; `SOUL.md` and project context files.
- **Config / env:** Tool-progress modes table verbatim (Mode | What You See): `off` "Clean responses only — no tool activity"; `new` "Brief status for each new tool call (recommended for messaging)"; `all` "Every tool call with details"; `verbose` "Full tool output including command results".
- **Edge cases / guards:** "### Security Considerations" section; DM pairing recommended over a static allowlist for teams.
- **Rebuild notes:** Give a team bot two access models (static allowlist, pairing codes) and make the progress-verbosity a first-class four-value setting — messaging surfaces need less tool chatter than a terminal.

### Tutorial: Build a GitHub PR Review Agent  `id: docs-rest.guide-github-pr-review-agent`
- **Surface:** Docs / Skill
- **Where:** Docs → Guides → **"Tutorial: Build a GitHub PR Review Agent"** (`guides/github-pr-review-agent.md`).
- **What it does:** Builds a scheduled PR reviewer using the `gh` CLI, a custom review skill, and a cron job.
- **How it works:** Sections verbatim: "## Step 1: Verify the Setup"; "## Step 2: Try a Manual Review"; "## Step 3: Create a Review Skill" (the skill body template is transcribed with `# Code Review Guidelines`, `## What to Check`, `## Output Format`, `## Rules`); "## Step 4: Teach It Your Conventions"; "## Step 5: Create the Automated Cron Job" (output template `## PR Reviews — today` / `### [repo] #[number]: [title]`); "## Step 6: Run It On Demand"; "## Going Further" ("### Post Reviews Directly to GitHub", "### Weekly PR Dashboard", "### Multi-Repo Monitoring"); "## Troubleshooting".
- **Inputs / options:** Schedule table verbatim (Schedule | When): `0 */2 * * *` "Every 2 hours"; `0 9,13,17 * * 1-5` "Three times a day, weekdays only"; `0 9 * * 1` "Weekly Monday morning roundup"; `30m` "Every 30 minutes (high-traffic repos)".
- **Outputs / side effects:** A skill file, a cron job, optionally GitHub PR comments.
- **Config / env:** `gh` CLI auth.
- **Edge cases / guards:** Troubleshooting headings verbatim: "\"gh: command not found\""; "Reviews are too generic"; "Cron job doesn't run"; "Rate limits".
- **Rebuild notes:** Encode review standards as a skill (a versioned document) rather than a long cron prompt — the same skill then serves manual and scheduled reviews.

### Automated GitHub PR Comments with Webhooks  `id: docs-rest.guide-webhook-github-pr-review`
- **Surface:** Docs / Gateway/Telegram
- **Where:** Docs → Guides → **"Automated GitHub PR Comments with Webhooks"** (`guides/webhook-github-pr-review.md`).
- **What it does:** Configures the webhook platform so a GitHub `pull_request` event triggers an agent review that is posted back as a PR comment.
- **How it works:** Steps verbatim: "## Step 1 — Enable the webhook platform"; "## Step 2 — Start the gateway" (health check returns `{"status": "ok", "platform": "webhook"}`); "## Step 3 — Register the webhook on GitHub"; "## Step 4 — Open a test PR"; plus "## Local testing with ngrok" (successful POST returns `{"status":"accepted","route":"github-pr-review","event":"pull_request","delivery_id":"..."}`), "## Filtering to specific actions", "## Using a skill for consistent review style", "## Sending responses to Slack or Discord instead", "## GitLab support", "## Security notes", "## Troubleshooting", "## Full config reference".
- **Inputs / options:** Route-field table verbatim (Field | Description): `secret` (route-level) "HMAC secret for this route. Falls back to `extra.secret` global if omitted."; `events` "List of `X-GitHub-Event` header values to accept. Empty list = accept all."; `prompt` "Template; `{field}` and `{nested.field}` resolve from the GitHub payload."; `deliver` "`github_comment` posts via `gh pr comment`. `log` just writes to the gateway log."; `deliver_extra.repo` "Resolves to e.g. `org/repo` from the payload."; `deliver_extra.pr_number` "Resolves to the PR number from the payload."
- **Outputs / side effects:** A PR comment via `gh pr comment`, or a log line, or a Slack/Discord message.
- **Config / env:** `platforms.webhook.extra.routes.<route-name>`, `extra.secret`, `extra.port`, `extra.rate_limit`.
- **Edge cases / guards:** Troubleshooting table verbatim (Symptom | Check): "`401 Invalid signature`" "Secret in config.yaml doesn't match GitHub webhook secret"; "`404 Unknown route`" "Route name in the URL doesn't match the key in `routes:`"; "`429 Rate limit exceeded`" "30 req/min per route exceeded — common when re-delivering test events from GitHub's UI; wait a minute or raise `extra.rate_limit`"; "No comment posted" "`gh` not installed, not on PATH, or not authenticated (`gh auth login`)"; "Agent runs but no comment" "Check the gateway log — if the agent output was empty or just \"SKIP\", delivery is still attempted"; "Port already in use" "Change `extra.port` in config.yaml"; "Agent runs but reviews only the PR description" "The prompt isn't including the `gh pr diff` instruction — the diff is not in the webhook payload"; "Can't see the ping event" "Ignored events return `{\"status\":\"ignored\",\"event\":\"ping\"}` at DEBUG log level only — check GitHub's delivery log (repo → Settings → Webhooks → your webhook → Recent Deliveries)".
- **Rebuild notes:** HMAC-verify per route, template the prompt from payload fields with dotted paths, and rate-limit per route (30 req/min) so replayed deliveries can't stampede the agent.

### Operate the Teams Meeting Pipeline  `id: docs-rest.guide-teams-meeting-pipeline`
- **Surface:** Docs / Platform:Teams
- **Where:** Docs → Guides → **"Operate the Teams Meeting Pipeline"** (`guides/operate-teams-meeting-pipeline.md`).
- **What it does:** The operator runbook for the Microsoft Teams meeting-transcript pipeline: config validation, token health, subscription renewal, job inspection/replay, triage, and go-live checks.
- **How it works:** Core operator commands verbatim: "### Validate the config snapshot"; "### Inspect token health"; "### Inspect subscriptions"; "### Renew near-expiry subscriptions"; "### Automating subscription renewal (REQUIRED for production)" with "#### Option 1: Hermes cron (recommended if you already run the Hermes gateway)", "#### Option 2: systemd timer (recommended for Linux production deployments)", "#### Option 3: Plain crontab", "#### Verifying renewal is working"; "### Inspect recent jobs"; "### Replay a stored job"; "### Dry-run meeting artifact fetches". Runbook sections verbatim: "## Routine Runbook" ("### After first setup", "### Daily or periodic checks", "### Before changing webhook URLs or delivery targets"); "## Failure Triage" ("### No jobs are being created", "### Jobs stay in retry or fail before summarization", "### Summaries are produced but not delivered to Teams", "### Duplicate or unexpected replays"); "## Go-Live Checklist".
- **Inputs / options:** Delivery-mode decision guide verbatim (Mode | Use when | Tradeoff): `incoming_webhook` "you only need simple posting into Teams" "simplest setup, less control"; `graph` "you need channel or chat posting through Graph" "more control, more auth and target config". Operator worksheet fields verbatim: "Public notification URL; Graph tenant ID; Graph client ID; Webhook client state; Transcript resource subscription; Recording resource subscription; Teams delivery mode; Teams chat ID or team/channel; Notion database ID; Linear team ID; Store path override, if any; Owner for daily checks." Change-review worksheet asks e.g. "Are we changing the public webhook URL?".
- **Outputs / side effects:** Renewed Graph subscriptions; replayed jobs; delivered summaries.
- **Config / env:** Graph tenant/client IDs, webhook client state, delivery mode.
- **Edge cases / guards:** Subscription renewal is marked "REQUIRED for production" with three automation options.
- **Rebuild notes:** Any Graph-style webhook integration needs an explicit renewal automation and a fill-in operator worksheet; expiring subscriptions are the number-one silent failure.

### Automation Blueprints catalogue  `id: docs-rest.guide-automation-blueprints`
- **Surface:** Docs
- **Where:** Docs → Guides → **"Automation Blueprints"** (`guides/automation-blueprints.md`).
- **What it does:** A catalogue of ready-to-adapt automations grouped by domain, each with the trigger, prompt and delivery target.
- **How it works:** Trigger table verbatim (Trigger | How | Tool): "**Schedule**" "Runs on a cadence (hourly, nightly, weekly)" "`cronjob` tool or `/cron` slash command"; "**GitHub Event**" "Fires on PR opens, pushes, issues, CI results" "Webhook platform (`hermes webhook subscribe`)"; "**API Call**" "External service POSTs JSON to your endpoint" "Webhook platform (config.yaml routes or `hermes webhook subscribe`)". Blueprint sections verbatim: "## Development Workflow" — "Nightly Backlog Triage", "Automatic PR Code Review", "Docs Drift Detection", "Dependency Security Audit"; "## DevOps & Monitoring" — "Deploy Verification", "Alert Triage", "Uptime Monitor"; "## Research & Intelligence" — "Competitive Repository Scout", "AI News Digest" (output template `## Headlines (3-5 major stories)`, `## Notable Papers (2-3 papers with one-sentence summaries)`, `## Open Source (interesting new repos or major releases)`, `## Industry Moves (funding, acquisitions, launches)`), "Paper Digest with Notes"; "## GitHub Event Automations" — "Issue Auto-Labeling", "CI Failure Analysis", "Auto-Port Changes Across Repos"; "## Business Operations" — "Stripe Payment Monitoring", "Daily Revenue Summary"; "## Multi-Skill Workflows" — "Security Audit Pipeline", "Content Pipeline".
- **Inputs / options:** Cron schedule syntax table verbatim (Expression | Meaning): `every 30m` "Every 30 minutes"; `every 2h` "Every 2 hours"; `0 2 * * *` "Daily at 2:00 AM"; `0 9 * * 1` "Every Monday at 9:00 AM"; `0 9 * * 1-5` "Weekdays at 9:00 AM"; `0 3 * * 0` "Every Sunday at 3:00 AM"; `0 */6 * * *` "Every 6 hours". Plus a "Delivery Targets" quick-reference table (Target | Flag | Notes).
- **Outputs / side effects:** Cron jobs and webhook routes.
- **Config / env:** n/a
- **Edge cases / guards:** n/a
- **Rebuild notes:** A blueprint catalogue is the cheapest onboarding for an automation system — group by domain, and always pair prompt + schedule + delivery in one copyable block. Handoff: the machine-readable catalogue is `reference/automation-blueprints-catalog.mdx` (another shard).

### Delegation & Parallel Work patterns  `id: docs-rest.guide-delegation-patterns`
- **Surface:** Docs / Tool
- **Where:** Docs → Guides → **"Delegation & Parallel Work"** (`guides/delegation-patterns.md`).
- **What it does:** Five patterns for using `delegate_task` effectively, plus the concurrency/depth knobs and inherited tool access.
- **How it works:** Sections verbatim: "## When to Delegate"; "## Pattern: Parallel Research"; "## Pattern: Code Review"; "## Pattern: Compare Alternatives"; "## Pattern: Multi-File Refactoring"; "## Pattern: Gather Then Analyze" (with the split annotated verbatim: "Step 1: Mechanical gathering (execute_code is better here — no reasoning needed)" then "Step 2: Reasoning-heavy analysis (delegation is better here)"); "## Inherited Tool Access"; "## Constraints" → "### Tuning Concurrency and Depth"; "## Tips".
- **Inputs / options:** Config table verbatim (Config | Default | Range | Effect): `max_concurrent_children` `3` `>=1` "Parallel batch size per `delegate_task` call"; `max_spawn_depth` `1` `>=1` "How many delegation levels can spawn further".
- **Outputs / side effects:** Subagent runs with inherited toolsets.
- **Config / env:** `delegation.max_concurrent_children`, `delegation.max_spawn_depth`.
- **Edge cases / guards:** Documented in "## Constraints" and "## Inherited Tool Access".
- **Rebuild notes:** Teach the "mechanical work in code, reasoning work in a subagent" split explicitly — it is the single biggest cost/quality lever in multi-agent workflows.

### Desktop Native Sign-In (RFC 8252)  `id: docs-rest.guide-desktop-native-signin`
- **Surface:** Docs / Desktop app
- **Where:** Docs → Guides → **"Desktop Native Sign-In (RFC 8252)"** (`guides/desktop-native-signin.md`).
- **What it does:** Documents the desktop app's native OAuth PKCE sign-in against a gateway, with capability detection and a webview fallback.
- **How it works:** Sections verbatim: "## Why native sign-in"; "## How it works"; "## Capability detection & fallback"; "## Token lifecycle"; "## For gateway operators".
- **Inputs / options:** Capability table verbatim (`auth_flows` value | Meaning): `["cookie", "native_pkce"]` "Gateway supports native sign-in → the app uses it"; `["cookie"]` "Gateway supports only the legacy flow → the app uses the embedded webview"; "*(field absent)*" "Older gateway → the app uses the embedded webview".
- **Outputs / side effects:** Tokens stored by the app; a gateway session.
- **Config / env:** Gateway-advertised `auth_flows`.
- **Edge cases / guards:** Fallback is automatic and version-tolerant (absent field ⇒ legacy path).
- **Rebuild notes:** Advertise supported auth flows as a list on the server and let the client pick the best it understands — that makes an auth upgrade a non-breaking change.

### OAuth over SSH / Remote Hosts  `id: docs-rest.guide-oauth-over-ssh`
- **Surface:** Docs
- **Where:** Docs → Guides → **"OAuth over SSH / Remote Hosts"** (`guides/oauth-over-ssh.md`).
- **What it does:** Explains how to complete a loopback-callback OAuth login when Hermes runs on a remote machine, via SSH reverse/forward tunnelling.
- **How it works:** TL;DR verbatim: "On your local machine (laptop), in a separate terminal: [start tunnel]"; "In your existing SSH session on the remote machine: → Hermes prints an authorize URL. Open it in a browser on your laptop. → Your browser redirects to 127.0.0.1:43827/callback, the tunnel forwards the request to the remote listener, login completes." Sections verbatim: "## Which Providers Need This"; "## MCP Servers"; "## Why the listener can't just bind 0.0.0.0"; "## Step-by-step: single SSH hop" ("### 1. Start the tunnel from your local machine", "### 2. In a separate SSH session, run the auth command", "### 3. Open the URL in your local browser"); "## Step-by-step: through a jump box"; "## Mosh, tmux, ssh ControlMaster"; "## Troubleshooting".
- **Inputs / options:** Provider table verbatim (Provider | Loopback port | Tunnel needed?): "Spotify" "`43827` (default)" "Yes, when Hermes is remote"; "MCP servers (`auth: oauth`)" "auto-picked per server" "Yes, when Hermes is remote (or paste redirect URL)"; "`xai-oauth` (Grok SuperGrok)" "n/a" "No — device code flow"; "`anthropic` (Claude Pro/Max)" "n/a" "No — paste-the-code flow"; "`openai-codex` (ChatGPT Plus/Pro)" "n/a" "No — device code flow"; "`minimax`, `nous-portal`" "n/a" "No — device code flow".
- **Outputs / side effects:** Tokens written into the remote host's `~/.hermes`.
- **Config / env:** n/a
- **Edge cases / guards:** Troubleshooting headings verbatim: "### `bind [127.0.0.1]:43827: Address already in use`"; "### Authorization timed out waiting for the local callback"; "### Tokens land in the wrong `~/.hermes`".
- **Rebuild notes:** Prefer device-code flows for every new provider; when a loopback callback is unavoidable, document the exact port and the tunnel command, and never bind the listener to `0.0.0.0`.

### Register a Microsoft Graph Application  `id: docs-rest.guide-msgraph-app-registration`
- **Surface:** Docs / Platform:Teams
- **Where:** Docs → Guides → **"Register a Microsoft Graph Application"** (`guides/microsoft-graph-app-registration.md`).
- **What it does:** Six-step Azure app registration for the Teams meeting pipeline, with the exact Graph permissions and a scoping policy.
- **How it works:** Steps verbatim: "## Step 1: Create the App Registration"; "## Step 2: Create a Client Secret"; "## Step 3: Grant Graph API Permissions"; "## Step 4: (Recommended) Scope the App with an Application Access Policy" ("Create a policy scoped to the Hermes app" / "Grant the policy to specific users whose meetings the pipeline may read"); "## Step 5: Write the Credentials to Your Env File"; "## Step 6: Verify the Token Flow"; plus "## Rotating the Client Secret".
- **Inputs / options:** Permission tables verbatim. "### Required for transcript-first summaries": `OnlineMeetings.Read.All` "Read Teams online meeting metadata (subject, participants, join URL)."; `OnlineMeetingTranscript.Read.All` "Read meeting transcripts generated by Teams." "### Required for recording fallback (when a transcript is unavailable)": `OnlineMeetingRecording.Read.All` "Download Teams meeting recordings for offline STT processing."; `CallRecords.Read.All` "Resolve meetings from call records when only the join URL is known." "### Required for outbound summary delivery (Graph mode only)": `ChannelMessage.Send` "Post messages into Teams channels on behalf of the app."; `Chat.ReadWrite.All` "Post messages into 1:1 and group chats (only if you set `chat_id` as the delivery target)." Plus a "### Not recommended" section.
- **Outputs / side effects:** `MSGRAPH_CLIENT_ID`, `MSGRAPH_TENANT_ID`, and a client secret in `.env`.
- **Config / env:** `MSGRAPH_CLIENT_ID`, `MSGRAPH_TENANT_ID`, client secret.
- **Edge cases / guards:** Azure error table verbatim (Azure error | Meaning | Fix): "`AADSTS7000215: Invalid client secret`" "Secret value mismatched or expired." "Generate a new secret in step 2; update `.env`."; "`AADSTS700016: Application not found`" "Wrong `MSGRAPH_CLIENT_ID` or wrong tenant." "Double-check the values from step 1 are from the same app."; "`AADSTS90002: Tenant not found`" "Typo in `MSGRAPH_TENANT_ID`." "Copy the Directory (tenant) ID from the app overview again."; "`insufficient_claims` at call time (not token time)" "Token acquires but Graph returns 401/403." "You skipped step 3 admin-consent, or added permissions but haven't re-consented. Revisit API permissions and click **Grant admin consent** again."
- **Rebuild notes:** Split required permissions by feature so an operator can grant the minimum; recommend an Application Access Policy so a tenant-wide `.Read.All` is scoped to named users.

### Migrate from OpenClaw  `id: docs-rest.guide-migrate-from-openclaw`
- **Surface:** Docs / CLI
- **Where:** Docs → Guides → **"Migrate from OpenClaw"** (`guides/migrate-from-openclaw.md`).
- **What it does:** Imports an OpenClaw install (persona, memory, skills, providers, agent behaviour) into Hermes with a preview-then-confirm flow.
- **How it works:** Quick start verbatim: "Preview then migrate (always shows a preview first, then asks to confirm)"; "Preview only, no changes"; "Full migration including API keys, skip confirmation".
- **Inputs / options:** Option table verbatim (Option | Description): `--dry-run` "Preview only — stop after showing what would be migrated."; `--preset <name>` "`full` (all compatible settings) or `user-data` (excludes infrastructure config). Neither preset imports secrets by default — pass `--migrate-secrets` explicitly."; `--overwrite` "Overwrite existing Hermes files on conflicts (default: refuse to apply when the plan has conflicts)."; `--migrate-secrets` "Include API keys. Required even under `--preset full` — no preset imports secrets silently."; `--no-backup` "Skip the pre-migration zip snapshot of `~/.hermes/` (by default a single restore-point archive is written before apply, under `~/.hermes/backups/pre-migration-*.zip`; restorable with `hermes import`)."; `--source <path>` "Custom OpenClaw directory."; `--workspace-target <path>` "Where to place `AGENTS.md`."; `--skill-conflict <mode>` "`skip` (default), `overwrite`, or `rename`."; `--yes` "Skip the confirmation prompt after preview."
  Persona/memory mapping table verbatim (What | OpenClaw source | Hermes destination | Notes): "Persona" `workspace/SOUL.md` → `~/.hermes/SOUL.md` "Direct copy"; "Workspace instructions" `workspace/AGENTS.md` → "`AGENTS.md` in `--workspace-target`" "Requires `--workspace-target` flag"; "Long-term memory" `workspace/MEMORY.md` → `~/.hermes/memories/MEMORY.md` "Parsed into entries, merged with existing, deduped. Uses `§` delimiter."; "User profile" `workspace/USER.md` → `~/.hermes/memories/USER.md` "Same entry-merge logic as memory."; "Daily memory files" `workspace/memory/*.md` → `~/.hermes/memories/MEMORY.md` "All daily files merged into main memory."
  Skills table verbatim (Source | OpenClaw location | Hermes destination), all four: "Workspace skills" `workspace/skills/` → `~/.hermes/skills/openclaw-imports/`; "Managed/shared skills" `~/.openclaw/skills/` → same; "Personal cross-project" `~/.agents/skills/` → same; "Project-level shared" `workspace/.agents/skills/` → same.
  Model/provider table verbatim: "Default model" `agents.defaults.model` → "`config.yaml` → `model`" "Can be a string or `{primary, fallbacks}` object"; "Custom providers" `models.providers.*` → "`config.yaml` → `custom_providers` (auto-migrated to the canonical `providers:` dict on the next `hermes update` config migration)" "Maps `baseUrl`, `apiType`/`api` — handles both short (\"openai\", \"anthropic\") and hyphenated (\"openai-completions\", \"anthropic-messages\", \"google-generative-ai\") values"; "Provider API keys" `models.providers.*.apiKey` → `~/.hermes/.env` "Requires `--migrate-secrets`."
  Agent-behaviour table verbatim: "Max turns" `agents.defaults.timeoutSeconds` → `agent.max_turns` "`timeoutSeconds / 10`, capped at 200"; "Verbose mode" `agents.defaults.verboseDefault` → `agent.verbose` "\"off\" / \"on\" / \"full\""; "Reasoning effort" `agents.defaults.thinkingDefault` → `agent.reasoning_effort` "\"always\"/\"high\"/\"xhigh\" → \"high\", \"auto\"/\"medium\"/\"adaptive\" → \"medium\", \"off\"/\"low\"/\"none\"/\"minimal\" → \"low\"".
- **Outputs / side effects:** A pre-migration zip under `~/.hermes/backups/pre-migration-*.zip`; imported files and config.
- **Config / env:** As mapped above.
- **Edge cases / guards:** Secrets are never imported by any preset without `--migrate-secrets`; conflicts refuse to apply unless `--overwrite`; skills conflict mode defaults to `skip`.
- **Rebuild notes:** A migration tool should always (a) preview first, (b) snapshot before apply, (c) never move secrets implicitly, and (d) publish an explicit source→destination mapping table with value transformations.

### Pipe Script Output to Messaging Platforms (`hermes send`)  `id: docs-rest.guide-pipe-script-output`
- **Surface:** Docs / CLI
- **Where:** Docs → Guides → **"Pipe Script Output to Messaging Platforms"** (`guides/pipe-script-output.md`).
- **What it does:** Documents `hermes send` for piping stdout or a file to any configured messaging target from a shell script, cron entry, or CI job.
- **How it works:** Quick-start examples verbatim: "Plain text to the home channel for a platform"; "Pipe in stdout from anything"; "Send a file"; "Attach a subject/header line"; "Thread target (Telegram topic, Discord thread)"; "List every configured target"; "Filter by platform". Sections: "## Message Body Resolution"; "## Real-World Examples" ("### Monitoring: Memory / Disk Alerts", "### CI / CD: Build and Test Results", "### Cron: Daily Report", "### Long-Running Tasks: Ping When Done", "### Scripting with `--json` and `--quiet`").
- **Inputs / options:** Flag table verbatim (Flag | Description): `-t, --to TARGET` "Destination."; `message` (positional) "Message text. Omit to read from `--file` or stdin."; `-f, --file PATH` "Read the body from a file. `--file -` forces stdin."; `-s, --subject LINE` "Prepend a header/subject line before the body."; `-l, --list` "List available targets. Optional positional platform filter."; `-q, --quiet` "No stdout on success (exit code only — ideal for scripts)."; `--json` "Emit the raw JSON result of the send."; `-h, --help` "Show the built-in help text." Target-format table verbatim (Format | Example | Meaning): `platform` `telegram` "Send to the platform's configured home channel"; `platform:chat_id` `telegram:-1001234567890` "Specific numeric chat / group / user"; `platform:chat_id:thread_id` `telegram:-1001234567890:17585` "Specific thread or Telegram forum topic"; `platform:#channel` `discord:#ops` "Human-friendly channel name (resolved against the channel directory)"; `platform:+E164` `signal:+15551234567` "Phone-addressed platforms: Signal, SMS, WhatsApp".
- **Outputs / side effects:** A delivered message; exit-code table verbatim (Code | Meaning): `0` "Send (or list) succeeded"; `1` "Delivery failed at the platform level (auth, permissions, network)"; `2` "Usage / argument / config error".
- **Config / env:** Platform credentials in `.env` / `config.yaml`.
- **Edge cases / guards:** Body resolution order documented in "## Message Body Resolution" (positional → `--file` → stdin).
- **Rebuild notes:** A one-shot send CLI with UNIX-shaped exit codes and a `--json` mode is the cheapest integration point for anything already running on a schedule elsewhere.

### Using Hermes as a Python Library  `id: docs-rest.guide-python-library`
- **Surface:** Docs / Core
- **Where:** Docs → Guides → **"Using Hermes as a Python Library"** (`guides/python-library.md`).
- **What it does:** Documents embedding `AIAgent` in a Python program: install, basic usage, full conversation control, toolset selection, multi-turn, trajectories, custom prompts, batch processing, and integration examples.
- **How it works:** Sections verbatim: "## Installation"; "## Basic Usage"; "## Full Conversation Control"; "## Configuring Tools" ("Only enable web tools (browsing, search)" / "Enable everything except terminal access"); "## Multi-turn Conversations" ("First turn" / "Second turn — agent remembers the context"); "## Saving Trajectories" ("Saves to trajectory_samples.jsonl in ShareGPT format"); "## Custom System Prompts"; "## Batch Processing"; "## Integration Examples" ("### FastAPI Endpoint", "### Discord Bot", "### CI/CD Pipeline Step"); "## Key Constructor Parameters"; "## Important Notes".
- **Inputs / options:** Constructor table verbatim (Parameter | Type | Default | Description): `model` `str` `""` "Model in OpenRouter format (defaults to empty; resolved from your hermes config at runtime)"; `quiet_mode` `bool` `False` "Suppress CLI output"; `enabled_toolsets` `List[str]` `None` "Whitelist specific toolsets"; `disabled_toolsets` `List[str]` `None` "Blacklist specific toolsets"; `save_trajectories` `bool` `False` "Save conversations to JSONL"; `ephemeral_system_prompt` `str` `None` "Custom system prompt (not saved to trajectories)"; `max_iterations` `int` `500` "Max tool-calling iterations per conversation"; `skip_context_files` `bool` `False` "Skip loading AGENTS.md files"; `skip_memory` `bool` `False` "Disable persistent memory read/write"; `api_key` `str` `None` "API key (falls back to env vars)"; `base_url` `str` `None` "Custom API endpoint URL"; `platform` `str` `None` "Platform hint (`\"discord\"`, `\"telegram\"`, etc.)".
- **Outputs / side effects:** Trajectory JSONL when enabled; whatever the tools do.
- **Config / env:** Falls back to the user's Hermes config and env vars.
- **Edge cases / guards:** "## Important Notes" section.
- **Rebuild notes:** Expose the agent class with every ambient dependency overridable by constructor argument (model, key, base URL, toolsets, prompt, memory, context files) so an embedder never has to mutate global config.

### Running Hermes on a Personal or Work Machine  `id: docs-rest.guide-secure-work-machine`
- **Surface:** Docs / Config
- **Where:** Docs → Guides → **"Running Hermes on a Personal or Work Machine"** (`guides/secure-hermes-on-a-work-machine.md`).
- **What it does:** Explains what the defaults already protect and how to tighten Hermes on a shared or work machine, plus the checkpoint/rollback undo layer.
- **How it works:** Sections verbatim: "## What the Defaults Already Protect"; "## Tightening for a Shared or Work Machine" ("### Switch approvals to manual", "### Add your own deny rules", "### Sandbox file writes", "### Move command execution off the host", "### If messaging is on: allowlists and pairing"); "## The Undo Layer: Checkpoints and `/rollback`"; "## What This Threat Model Is — and Isn't"; "## A Cautious Starting Config".
- **Inputs / options:** Terminal-backend isolation table verbatim (Backend | Isolation): `local` "None — runs on host (dangerous-command checks apply)"; `docker` "Container — the container itself is the security boundary"; `ssh` "Remote machine — keeps execution on a separate server". Rollback commands verbatim (Command | Description): `/rollback` "List all checkpoints with change stats"; `/rollback diff <N>` "Preview what changed since checkpoint N"; `/rollback <N>` "Restore to checkpoint N (also undoes the last chat turn)"; `/rollback <N> <file>` "Restore a single file from checkpoint N".
- **Outputs / side effects:** A hardened `config.yaml`; checkpoints.
- **Config / env:** Approval mode, deny rules, write sandbox, `terminal.backend`, messaging allowlists in `~/.hermes/.env`.
- **Edge cases / guards:** "## What This Threat Model Is — and Isn't" scopes the guarantees; "A Cautious Starting Config" gives a full copyable block.
- **Rebuild notes:** Publish the default protections *before* the hardening steps so users can reason about marginal risk, and pair every restriction with an undo layer (checkpoints + per-file restore).

- **How it works:** Checks verbatim: "## 1. Check which model the session is actually using"; "## 2. Check context usage" ("Compress the conversation (summarizes history, preserves key context)" / "Or start a fresh session"); "## 3. Verify the detected context length" (fix in `~/.hermes/config.yaml`); "## 4. \"I told it something and it forgot\" — the frozen memory snapshot"; "## 5. Memory is bounded and curated — not a transcript"; "## 6. Check that skills and tools are loaded"; "## 7. Compression side-effects".
- **Inputs / options:** `/model`, `/usage`, `/compress`, `/skills`, `/tools list`, `/context`, `session_search`.
- **Outputs / side effects:** n/a
- **Config / env:** `model.context_length`.
- **Edge cases / guards:** Quick-reference table verbatim (Symptom | First command | Likely cause): "Everything feels less capable" `/model` "Session is on a different model than you think"; "Long session degrading" `/usage` "Context pressure — compress or start fresh"; "Limits hit surprisingly early" "CLI startup line / `/usage`" "Wrong auto-detected context length"; "Forgot what I said this session" "— (by design)" "Frozen memory snapshot — appears next session"; "Forgot last week's discussion" "ask it to `session_search`" "Memory is bounded, curated facts only"; "Lost a specific ability" "`/skills`, `/tools list`" "Skill or toolset not loaded this session"; "Lost old detail after long session" "`/usage`, `/context`" "Compression summarized older history".
- **Rebuild notes:** Most "the model got worse" reports are state problems (wrong model, context pressure, wrong detected window, frozen memory snapshot, compression). Ship a symptom table that maps each to one diagnostic command.

Test It"); "## Per-Platform Skill Management"; "## Skills vs Memory".
