That cost distinction (zero-until-loaded vs constant) is the whole design rationale for separating the two stores.

## User Guide (top level)

### Which File Does What?  `id: docs-rest.ug-which-file-does-what`
- **Surface:** Docs / Config
- **Where:** Docs → User Guide → **"Which File Does What?"** (`user-guide/which-file-does-what.md`, `sidebar_position: 4`).
- **What it does:** One-page map of `SOUL.md`, `USER.md`, `MEMORY.md`, `AGENTS.md` and `.hermes.md` — what each holds, who writes it, when the agent sees it, and where it lives.
- **How it works:** Master table verbatim (File | What it holds | Who writes it | When the agent sees it | Where it lives): **SOUL.md** "The agent's primary identity — personality, tone, communication style, what to avoid stylistically" | "You. Hermes seeds a starter file automatically if one doesn't exist; existing files are never overwritten" | "Slot #1 of the system prompt, at session start" | "`~/.hermes/SOUL.md` (or `$HERMES_HOME/SOUL.md` with a custom home) — never the working directory"; **USER.md** "User profile — your name, role, preferences, communication style, expectations" | "The agent, via the `memory` tool (you can gate saves with `write_approval`, or edit entries via `hermes journey edit`)" | "Injected into the system prompt as a frozen snapshot at session start" | `~/.hermes/memories/`; **MEMORY.md** "Agent's personal notes — environment facts, project conventions, tool quirks, things learned" | "The agent, via the `memory` tool (same gating and editing options as USER.md)" | "Injected into the system prompt as a frozen snapshot at session start" | `~/.hermes/memories/`; **AGENTS.md** "Project instructions, conventions, architecture — commands, ports, paths, repo-specific workflows" | "You (or whoever authors the project)" | "Loaded into the system prompt at startup from your working directory; nested copies are discovered progressively as the agent navigates subdirectories" | "Project working directory + subdirectories"; **.hermes.md / HERMES.md** "Project instructions, like AGENTS.md but Hermes-specific and highest priority" | "You" | "Loaded into the system prompt at startup (first match wins over AGENTS.md)" | "Your project — discovery walks up to the git root".
  Shorthand verbatim: "**SOUL.md** is who the agent *is*…; **USER.md** is who *you* are — the agent maintains it for you.; **MEMORY.md** is what the agent has *learned*…; **AGENTS.md** (or `.hermes.md`) is what the *project* needs…"
- **Inputs / options:** `memory` tool, `write_approval`, `hermes journey edit`, `hermes journey list`, `/personality`.
- **Outputs / side effects:** n/a
- **Config / env:** `HERMES_HOME`.
- **Edge cases / guards:** **":::info One project context file per session"** verbatim: "Only **one** project context type is loaded per session, first match wins: `.hermes.md` → `AGENTS.md` → `CLAUDE.md` → `.cursorrules`. `SOUL.md` is always loaded independently as the agent identity — it is not part of that priority chain." Frozen-snapshot explanation verbatim: "Memory (MEMORY.md and USER.md) is injected into the system prompt as a **frozen snapshot** captured once at session start — when the agent saves something mid-session, the change is persisted to disk immediately but won't appear in the system prompt until the next session starts. This is intentional: it preserves the LLM's prefix cache for performance, and tool responses always show the live state." Two documented mix-ups verbatim: "### \"I put facts about myself in SOUL.md, but USER.md stayed empty\"" — "`SOUL.md` and `USER.md` are separate systems that never feed each other."; "### \"I told it my name mid-session and it acted like it never heard it\"" — "The same applies to edits you make to `SOUL.md` or `AGENTS.md` while a session is running: context is assembled at session start, so restart the session to pick up changes."
- **Rebuild notes:** Publish a five-row ownership table (who writes / when read / where stored) for every ambient file — the frozen-snapshot rule is otherwise indistinguishable from a bug.

### Import from Other Agents (`hermes import-agent`)  `id: docs-rest.ug-import-from-other-agents`
- **Surface:** CLI / Docs
- **Where:** Docs → User Guide → **"Import from Other Agents"** (`user-guide/import-from-other-agents.md`, `sidebar_position: 9`).
- **What it does:** One-command import of a Claude Code (`~/.claude`) or OpenAI Codex CLI (`~/.codex`) setup — instructions, allowlists, MCP servers, skills, memories — with a preview-first plan.
- **How it works:** Commands verbatim: `hermes import-agent` ("auto-detect ~/.claude or ~/.codex"); `hermes import-agent claude-code` ("import from ~/.claude"); `hermes import-agent codex` ("import from ~/.codex"); `hermes import-agent claude-code --dry-run` ("preview only"); `hermes import-agent codex --source /path/to/.codex` ("custom location"); `hermes import-agent claude-code --overwrite --yes` ("replace conflicts, skip prompts").
  Claude Code mapping table verbatim (Claude Code | Hermes): "`CLAUDE.md` (global instructions)" | "Memory entries in `~/.hermes/memories/MEMORY.md`"; "`settings.json` → `permissions.allow` (`Bash(...)` rules)" | "`command_allowlist` in `config.yaml`"; "`settings.json` → `permissions.deny` (`Bash(...)` rules)" | "`approvals.deny` in `config.yaml`"; "`mcpServers` (from `~/.claude.json` and `settings.json`)" | "`mcp_servers` in `config.yaml`"; "`skills/<name>/` (dirs with `SKILL.md`)" | "`~/.hermes/skills/claude-code-imports/<name>/`"; "`commands/*.md` (slash commands)" | "Skipped with a note — convert them into skills". Rule translation verbatim: "Claude's `Bash(npm run test:*)` prefix rules become `npm run test*` globs. Non-`Bash` permission rules (`Read(...)`, `WebFetch`, ...) gate Claude-specific tools and are reported as unmapped rather than imported."
  Codex mapping table verbatim (Codex CLI | Hermes): "`AGENTS.md` (global instructions)" | "Memory entries in `~/.hermes/memories/MEMORY.md`"; "`config.toml` → `[mcp_servers.*]`" | "`mcp_servers` in `config.yaml`"; "`memories/*.md`" | "Memory entries in `~/.hermes/memories/MEMORY.md`"; "`skills/<name>/` (dirs with `SKILL.md`)" | "`~/.hermes/skills/codex-imports/<name>/`".
- **Inputs / options:** `claude-code` / `codex` positional, `--dry-run`, `--source <path>`, `--overwrite`, `--yes`.
- **Outputs / side effects:** Merged memory entries, allowlist/denylist patterns, MCP servers, imported skill directories.
- **Config / env:** `command_allowlist`, `approvals.deny`, `mcp_servers`.
- **Edge cases / guards:** "## What is never imported" verbatim: "**API keys and credentials.** Credential files (`~/.claude/.credentials.json`, `~/.codex/auth.json`) are never read, and MCP server environment variables or headers with secret-looking names (`*_TOKEN`, `*_API_KEY`, `Authorization`, ...) are stripped and listed in the report so you can re-add them deliberately." Behaviour notes verbatim: "**Preview first, always.** The command prints the full plan before applying; in non-interactive sessions it stops at the preview unless you pass `--yes`."; "**Merges, not replaces.** Memory entries are deduplicated against your existing `MEMORY.md`; allowlist/denylist patterns merge with what's already in `config.yaml`."; "**Conflicts are skipped by default.**"; "**Malformed files don't abort the run.** A broken `settings.json` or `config.toml` becomes a per-item error in the report while everything else still imports."
- **Rebuild notes:** Import per-item with a per-item error report, never read credential files, strip secret-shaped values out of anything you do import, and translate permission grammars explicitly (documenting what could not be mapped).

### Managed Scope (`/etc/hermes`)  `id: docs-rest.ug-managed-scope`
- **Surface:** Config / Docs
- **Where:** Docs → User Guide → **"Managed Scope"** (`user-guide/managed-scope.md`, `sidebar_position: 3`).
- **What it does:** Lets an administrator pin config and env values that a non-root user cannot override, for fleet/org deployments.
- **How it works:** Layout verbatim: `/etc/hermes/config.yaml` ("managed config layer (wins over ~/.hermes/config.yaml)") and `/etc/hermes/.env` ("managed env layer (wins over ~/.hermes/.env + shell)"). Ownership verbatim: "owned by `root` (directory mode `0755`, files `0644`): readable by everyone, writable only by an administrator. **That filesystem permission is the enforcement mechanism**." Precedence table verbatim (Tier | config.yaml | .env): 1 "`/etc/hermes/config.yaml` (managed)" "`/etc/hermes/.env` (managed)"; 2 "`~/.hermes/config.yaml` (user)" "`~/.hermes/.env` (user)"; 3 "built-in defaults" "pre-existing shell environment". Merging verbatim: "Merging is **leaf-level**: pinning `model.default` does not freeze the rest of `model.*`."
- **Inputs / options:** `HERMES_MANAGED_DIR` ("The location can be relocated… for containers or non-`/etc` deployments. This is a deployment/bootstrap path knob — like `HERMES_HOME` — set by the same administrator who owns the managed files. It is **never persisted** to any `.env` by Hermes."). Inspection: `hermes config` ("shows a header naming the managed source + the pinned keys"); `hermes doctor` ("reports the resolved managed dir + pinned key counts"). Admin setup transcribed: `sudo mkdir -p /etc/hermes`, `sudo tee /etc/hermes/config.yaml` (example pins `model.provider: nous`, `security.redact_secrets: true`), `sudo tee /etc/hermes/.env` (example `OPENAI_API_BASE=`), `sudo chmod 0755 /etc/hermes`, `sudo chmod 0644 …`.
- **Outputs / side effects:** Refusal message verbatim: "`Cannot set 'model.default': it is managed by your administrator (/etc/hermes/config.yaml) and cannot be changed.`" "The same applies to managed secrets — `hermes config set` / setup will not write a user value for an env key pinned by the managed `.env`."
- **Config / env:** `HERMES_MANAGED_DIR`; any pinned key.
- **Edge cases / guards:** **":::warning"** verbatim: "A user who can set `HERMES_MANAGED_DIR` can repoint managed scope at a directory they control, defeating it… `hermes doctor` reports the *resolved* managed directory so a redirect is visible." Precedence note verbatim: "For the keys it pins, managed scope deliberately wins over the shell environment too — otherwise it would not be \"managed.\" This is the one place that inverts the usual \"an environment variable overrides config.yaml\" rule." Security limits verbatim: "**Enforcement is filesystem permissions only.**…; **The managed `.env` is world-readable** (`0644`), so any local user can read secrets pushed through it. Use it for shared, non-sensitive values…; **The agent's own tools are not hard-blocked from a managed *env* value.**… v1 is a management-convenience boundary against a normal user, not an un-escapable sandbox." Out of scope for v1 verbatim: "A hard boundary that the agent itself cannot escape.; Native managed locations on macOS and Windows (v1 is Linux/POSIX-first).; Drop-in fragment directories (`managed.d/`) for layered policy.; Signed / integrity-checked managed files.; Remote / device-management (MDM) delivery.; Tighter (group-scoped) permissions for managed secrets." Startup behaviour verbatim: "a malformed managed file is logged loudly and ignored — it never blocks startup". Disambiguation verbatim: "A package-manager–managed install (declarative-distro / formula) blocks *all* config mutation… Managed scope is a separate mechanism: it injects *specific immutable values* on a per-key basis."
- **Rebuild notes:** Leaf-level pinning from a root-owned directory, with an explicit statement of what the boundary is and is not; report the *resolved* policy directory in diagnostics so redirection is visible.

### Git Worktrees with Hermes  `id: docs-rest.ug-git-worktrees`
- **Surface:** Docs / CLI
- **Where:** Docs → User Guide → **"Git Worktrees"** (`user-guide/git-worktrees.md`, `sidebar_position: 3`).
- **What it does:** Runs multiple agents safely on the same repository by giving each its own git worktree, branch, and checkpoint history.
- **How it works:** Rationale verbatim: "Hermes treats the **current working directory** as the project root: CLI: the directory where you run `hermes` or `hermes chat`; Messaging gateways: the directory set by `terminal.cwd` in `~/.hermes/config.yaml`." Per-agent isolation verbatim: "Its **own branch and working directory**; Its **own Checkpoint Manager history** for `/rollback`."
  In-session command verbatim: "`/worktree new my-experiment` — Hermes creates `.worktrees/my-experiment/` inside the repo (branch `hermes/my-experiment`, based on the freshly-fetched remote tip unless `worktree_sync: false`), and retargets the session's terminal and file tools into it — no restart needed. Omit the name to get a random `hermes-<id>` tree. `/worktree` alone shows the active tree; `/worktree list` lists all of them. On exit the tree is kept only if it has unpushed commits, exactly like `hermes -w`."
  Manual path verbatim: `git worktree add ../repo-feature feature/hermes-experiment`, then `cd ../repo-feature && hermes`; parallel example creates `../repo-experiment-a` and `../repo-experiment-b`. Effect verbatim: "Works on its own branch…; Writes checkpoints under a different shadow repo hash (derived from the worktree path); Can use `/rollback` independently without affecting the other."
- **Inputs / options:** `/worktree new [name]`, `/worktree`, `/worktree list`, `hermes -w`; `git worktree add`, `git worktree remove`.
- **Outputs / side effects:** New worktree directories and branches; separate checkpoint refs.
- **Config / env:** `worktree_sync`, `terminal.cwd`.
- **Edge cases / guards:** Cleanup notes verbatim: "`git worktree remove` will refuse to remove a worktree with uncommitted changes unless you force it.; Removing a worktree does **not** automatically delete the branch…; Hermes checkpoint data under `~/.hermes/checkpoints/` is not automatically pruned when you remove a worktree, but it is usually very small." Risk of sharing one checkout verbatim: "One agent may delete or rewrite files the other is using.; It becomes harder to understand which changes belong to which experiment."
- **Rebuild notes:** Derive per-workspace state keys (checkpoint refs) from the absolute working-directory path so worktrees isolate automatically, and offer an in-session `/worktree new` that retargets tools without a restart.

### Checkpoints and `/rollback`  `id: docs-rest.ug-checkpoints-rollback`
- **Surface:** Docs / CLI / Config
- **Where:** Docs → User Guide → sidebar **"Checkpoints & Rollback"**, title **"Checkpoints and `/rollback`"** (`user-guide/checkpoints-and-rollback.md`, `sidebar_position: 8`).
- **What it does:** Automatically snapshots the project before destructive operations into a shared shadow git store, and restores it with `/rollback`.
- **How it works:** Opt-in verbatim: "Checkpoints are **opt-in** as of v2 — most users never use `/rollback`, and the shadow-store storage is non-trivial over time, so the default is off." Enable per session with `hermes chat --checkpoints` or globally with `checkpoints.enabled: true`. Store verbatim: "an internal **Checkpoint Manager** that keeps a single shared shadow git repository under `~/.hermes/checkpoints/store/` — your real project `.git` is never touched. Every project the agent works in shares the same store, so git's content-addressable object DB deduplicates across projects and across turns." Mechanism verbatim: "Once per conversation turn (per directory), it: Resolves a reasonable project root for the file.; Initialises or reuses the **single shared shadow store**…; Stages into a per-project index, builds a tree, and commits to a per-project ref (`refs/hermes/<project-hash>`)."
  Triggers verbatim: "**File tools** — `write_file` and `patch`"; "**Destructive terminal commands** — `rm`, `rmdir`, `cp`, `install`, `mv`, `sed -i`, `truncate`, `dd`, `shred`, output redirects (`>`), and `git reset`/`clean`/`checkout`". "The agent creates **at most one checkpoint per directory per turn**."
- **Inputs / options:** Slash commands verbatim (Command | Description): `/rollback` "List all checkpoints with change stats"; `/rollback <N>` "Restore to checkpoint N, keeping your hand-edits (also undoes last chat turn)"; `/rollback <N> --all` "Full restore — overwrites your hand-edits too"; `/rollback diff <N>` "Preview diff between checkpoint N and current state"; `/rollback <N> <file>` "Restore a single file from checkpoint N". CLI verbatim: `hermes checkpoints` "Show total size, project count, per-project breakdown"; `hermes checkpoints status` "Same as bare `checkpoints`"; `hermes checkpoints list` "Alias for `status`"; `hermes checkpoints prune` "Force a sweep: delete orphans/stale, GC, enforce size cap"; `hermes checkpoints clear` "Nuke the entire checkpoint base (asks first)"; `hermes checkpoints clear-legacy` "Delete only the `legacy-*` archives from v1 migration".
- **Outputs / side effects:** Store layout verbatim: `~/.hermes/checkpoints/store/` with `HEAD, objects/` ("git internals (shared across projects)"), `refs/hermes/<hash>` ("per-project branch tip"), `indexes/<hash>` ("per-project git index"), `projects/<hash>.json` ("workdir + created_at + last_touch"), `info/exclude`; plus `.last_prune` ("auto-prune idempotency marker") and `legacy-<ts>/` ("archived pre-v2 per-project shadow repos"). Restore output verbatim: "`✅ Restored to checkpoint a1b2c3d4: before write_file` / `↷ Kept your hand-edits: src/config.py, notes.md` / `Use /rollback <N> --all to restore those too.`"
- **Config / env:** All keys verbatim with their comments: `checkpoints.enabled` "master switch (default: false — opt-in)"; `max_snapshots: 20` "max checkpoints per project (enforced via ref rewrite + gc)"; `max_total_size_mb: 500` "hard cap on total store size; oldest commits dropped"; `max_file_size_mb: 10` "skip any single file larger than this"; `auto_prune: true`, `retention_days: 7`, `min_interval_hours: 24` — "sweep `~/.hermes/checkpoints/` at startup and delete project entries whose last_touch is older than retention_days. Runs at most once per min_interval_hours, tracked via a `.last_prune` marker. This sweep never deletes \"orphan\" entries (working directory not found) — a missing workdir at startup is ambiguous (deleted project vs. an unmounted external volume / network share / VPN not yet up), so orphan cleanup is only ever done via the explicit `hermes checkpoints prune` command… with a confirmation prompt."
- **Edge cases / guards:** Hand-edit preservation verbatim: "`/rollback <N>` restores only the files Hermes itself changed. Every successful `write_file` / `patch` records the file's content hash in an **agent-write ledger**; at restore time, any file whose current contents no longer match what Hermes last wrote (you edited it afterwards, or Hermes never touched it) is **skipped** instead of overwritten… If the ledger is empty…, `/rollback` falls back to the full restore automatically." Guards verbatim: "**Git availability** — if `git` is not found on `PATH`, checkpoints are transparently disabled.; **Directory scope** — Hermes skips overly broad directories (root `/`, home `$HOME`).; **Repository size** — directories with more than 50,000 files are skipped.; **Per-file size cap** — files larger than `max_file_size_mb` (default 10 MB) are excluded…; **Total store size cap** — when the store exceeds `max_total_size_mb` (default 500 MB), the oldest commit per project is dropped round-robin until under the cap.; **Real pruning** — `max_snapshots` is enforced by rewriting the per-project ref and running `git gc --prune=now` afterwards…; **No-change snapshots** — if there are no changes since the last snapshot, the checkpoint is skipped.; **Non-fatal errors** — all errors inside the Checkpoint Manager are logged at debug level; your tools continue to run." v1 migration verbatim: "On first v2 run, any pre-v2 shadow repos are moved into `~/.hermes/checkpoints/legacy-<timestamp>/`… Legacy archives are also swept by `auto_prune` after `retention_days`."
- **Rebuild notes:** One shared bare git store with per-project refs and indexes gives free cross-project dedup; the agent-write ledger (content hash per written file) is what makes "restore only what the agent touched" possible — without it, rollback silently destroys human edits.

### CLI Interface page  `id: docs-rest.ug-cli`
- **Surface:** Docs / CLI
- **Where:** Docs → User Guide → **"CLI Interface"** (`user-guide/cli.md`).
- **What it does:** The user-facing reference for the classic `prompt_toolkit` CLI: how to launch it, the interface layout, every keybinding, slash commands, quick commands, personalities, multi-line input, mid-turn redirect, tool-progress display, session management, background sessions and quiet mode.
- **How it works:** Launch modes verbatim (comments transcribed): "Start an interactive session (default)"; "Single query mode (non-interactive)"; "Single query from a file or stdin — nothing is shell-interpreted, so arbitrary text (quotes, `$(...)`, backticks) arrives verbatim"; "With a specific model"; "With a specific provider"; "With specific toolsets"; "Start with one or more skills preloaded"; "Resume previous sessions"; "Verbose mode (debug output)"; "Isolated git worktree (for running multiple agents in parallel)". Sections verbatim: "### Worktree cleanup"; "### Plugin management"; "## Interface Layout"; "### Status Bar"; "### Session Resume Display"; "## Keybindings"; "### `!` Shell Mode"; "## Slash Commands"; "## Quick Commands"; "## Preloading Skills at Launch"; "## Skill Slash Commands"; "## Personalities"; "## Multi-line Input" + "### Shift+Enter compatibility"; "## Redirecting the Agent Mid-Turn" + "### Busy Input Mode" + "### Suspending to Background"; "## Tool Progress Display" + "### Tool Preview Length"; "## Session Management" ("### Resuming Sessions", "### Session Storage", "### Context Compression"); "## Background Sessions" ("### How It Works", "### Results", "### Use Cases"); "## Quiet Mode".
- **Inputs / options:** Status-bar elements table verbatim (Element | Description): "Model name" "Current model (truncated if longer than 26 chars)"; "Token count" "Context tokens used / max context window"; "Context bar" "Visual fill indicator with color-coded thresholds"; "Cost" "Estimated session cost (or `n/a` for unknown/zero-priced models)"; "🗜️ N" "**Context compression count** — how many times the running session has been auto-compressed. Appears once the first compression fires."; "▶ N" "**Active background tasks** — how many `/bg` prompts are still running in the current session."; "Duration" "Elapsed session time"; "Session title" "Once the session has a title, it appears as a gold badge pinned to the far-right edge. Long titles truncate before displacing the essential model and context fields."; "⚠ YOLO" "**YOLO mode warning** — shown whenever `HERMES_YOLO_MODE` is on (either `hermes --yolo` at launch or `/yolo` toggled mid-session)."
  Context-bar colours verbatim (Color | Threshold | Meaning): Green "< 50%" "Plenty of room"; Yellow "50–80%" "Getting full"; Orange "80–95%" "Approaching limit"; Red "≥ 95%" "Near overflow — consider `/compress`".
  Keybindings table verbatim (Key | Action): `Enter` "Send message"; "`Alt+Enter`, `Ctrl+J`, or `Shift+Enter`" "New line (multi-line input). `Shift+Enter` requires a terminal that distinguishes it from `Enter`… On Windows Terminal, `Alt+Enter` is captured by the terminal (fullscreen toggle); use `Ctrl+Enter` or `Ctrl+J` instead."; `Alt+V` "Paste an image from the clipboard when supported by the terminal"; `Ctrl+V` "Paste text and opportunistically attach clipboard images"; `Ctrl+B` "Start/stop voice recording when voice mode is enabled (`voice.record_key`, default: `ctrl+b`)"; `Ctrl+G` "Open the current input buffer in `$EDITOR` (vim/nvim/nano/VS Code/etc.). Save and quit to send the edited text as the next prompt"; `Ctrl+X Ctrl+E` "Emacs-style alternate binding for the external editor (same behavior as `Ctrl+G`)."; `Ctrl+S` "**Stash the prompt.** Parks the current draft and clears the composer… Press `Ctrl+S` again on an empty composer to bring the draft back (cursor at the end, attached images restored). Repeated presses build a stack… with two or more stashed, `Ctrl+S` opens a browse panel (`↑`/`↓` to navigate, `Enter` to restore, `D` to discard, `Esc` or `Ctrl+S` to close). A `📌 N` badge in the status bar shows how many drafts are parked. Multi-line drafts round-trip exactly, including blank lines. The stash lives in memory for the session only — nothing is written to disk, since drafts often contain secrets."; `Ctrl+C` "Interrupt agent (double-press within 2s to force exit)"; `Ctrl+D` "Exit"; `Ctrl+Z` "Suspend Hermes to background (Unix only). Run `fg` in the shell to resume."; `Tab` "Accept auto-suggestion (ghost text) or autocomplete slash commands"; `!<command>` "**Shell mode** — run a shell command yourself without spending a model turn (e.g. `!git status`, `!pytest -x`)."
  Slash-command examples table verbatim (Command | Description): `/help` "Show command help"; `/model` "Show or change the current model"; `/tools` "List currently available tools"; `/skills browse` "Browse the skills hub and official optional skills"; `/bg <prompt>` "Run a prompt in a separate background session"; `/btw <question>` "Ask a side question about the current conversation without interrupting it"; `/skin` "Show or switch the active CLI skin"; `/voice on` "Enable CLI voice mode (press `Ctrl+B` to record)"; `/voice tts` "Toggle spoken playback for Hermes replies"; `/reasoning high` "Increase reasoning effort"; `/title My Session` "Name the current session"; `/status` "Show session info — model/profile/tokens/duration — followed by a local **Session recap** block (recent turn counts, top tools used, files touched, latest user prompt + assistant reply). Pure local compute; no LLM call."; `/context [all]` "Visual context-usage breakdown — glyph block grid + per-category token table (system prompt / tools / skills / memory / conversation / free space). `/context all` adds per-skill and per-toolset costs."; `/sessions` "Open an interactive session picker right inside the classic CLI (same surface the TUI uses). Type to filter, arrow keys to navigate, Enter to resume."
  Quick commands verbatim: `quick_commands.<name>.type` is `exec` (with `command:`) or `alias` (with `target:`); examples `status` → `systemctl status hermes-agent`, `gpu` → `nvidia-smi --query-gpu=utilization.gpu,memory.used --format=csv,noheader`, `restart` → alias to `/gateway restart`. "These work in both the CLI and messaging platforms (Telegram, Discord, etc.)."
  Skill preloading verbatim: `hermes -s hermes-agent-dev,github-auth`; `hermes chat -s github-pr-workflow -s github-auth`.
  Personalities verbatim: "Built-in personalities include: `helpful`, `concise`, `technical`, `creative`, `teacher`, `kawaii`, `catgirl`, `pirate`, `shakespeare`, `surfer`, `noir`, `uwu`, `philosopher`, `hype`."
- **Outputs / side effects:** Sessions in the session store; quick-command output printed without a model turn.
- **Config / env:** `quick_commands.*`, `voice.record_key`, `HERMES_YOLO_MODE`, `$EDITOR`.
- **Edge cases / guards:** "Commands are case-insensitive — `/HELP` works the same as `/help`. Installed skills also become slash commands automatically." "Every installed skill in `~/.hermes/skills/` is automatically registered as a slash command."
- **Rebuild notes:** The status bar is the whole observability surface: model, context fill with colour thresholds, cost, compression count, background-task count, duration, title and a persistent YOLO warning. The draft stash (in-memory only, stack-shaped, never written to disk) is a small feature with an outsized safety property. Handoff: the exhaustive flag surface lives in the cli-a…cli-f shards and `reference/cli-commands.md`.

### TUI page  `id: docs-rest.ug-tui`
- **Surface:** Docs / TUI
- **Where:** Docs → User Guide → **"TUI"** (`user-guide/tui.md`).
- **What it does:** The user-facing reference for the Ink TUI: launch and resume modes, why it exists, the collapsible banner, requirements, keybindings, TUI-specific slash behaviour, the live session switcher, LaTeX rendering, light-terminal detection, busy indicators, auto-resume, the status line, configuration, sessions, its gateway protocol, and how to go back to the classic CLI.
- **How it works:** Launch commands verbatim (comments transcribed): "Launch the TUI"; "Resume the latest TUI session (falls back to the latest classic session)"; "Resume a specific session by ID or title"; "Resume the latest session for a specific project directory"; "Run source directly — skips the prebuild step (for TUI contributors)". Sections verbatim: "## Why the TUI"; "### Collapsible banner sections"; "## Requirements" + "### External prebuild"; "## Keybindings"; "## Slash commands"; "## Live session switcher"; "## LaTeX math rendering"; "## Light-terminal detection"; "## Busy indicator styles"; "## Auto-resume"; "## Status line"; "## Configuration"; "## Sessions"; "## How the TUI talks to its gateway"; "## Reverting to the classic CLI".
- **Inputs / options:** Banner section defaults verbatim (Section | Default state): "Tools" Open; "Skills" Collapsed; "System Prompt" Collapsed; "MCP Servers" Collapsed.
  TUI slash behaviour table verbatim (Command | TUI behavior): `/help` "Overlay with categorized commands, arrow-key navigable"; "`/sessions` (alias `/switch`)" "Live session switcher — list open TUI sessions, switch between them, close them, or start another one"; `/model` "Modal model picker grouped by provider, with cost hints"; `/skin` "Live preview — theme change applies as you browse"; `/details` "Toggle verbose tool-call details (global or per-section)"; `/usage` "Rich token / cost / context panel"; "`/agents` (alias `/tasks`)" "Observability overlay — live subagent tree with kill/pause controls, per-branch cost / token / file rollups, turn-by-turn history"; `/reload` "Re-reads `~/.hermes/.env` into the running TUI process so newly added API keys take effect without a restart"; "`/mouse [on|off|toggle|wheel|buttons|all]`" "Pick a mouse tracking preset at runtime (also persists to `display.mouse_tracking` in `config.yaml`). `wheel` (1000+1006) keeps scroll-wheel scrolling without the hover events that make tmux spam \"No image in clipboard\" over the prompt row; `buttons` adds drag-to-select; `all` is the default with hover-driven UI."
  Status-line values verbatim (Status | Meaning): `starting agent…` "Session ID is live; tools and skills still coming online. You can type — messages queue and send when ready."; `ready` "Agent is idle, accepting input."; "`thinking…` / `running…`" "Agent is reasoning or running a tool."; `interrupted` "Current turn was cancelled; press Enter to send again."; "`forging session…` / `resuming…`" "Initial connect or `--resume` handshake."
- **Outputs / side effects:** Persists `display.mouse_tracking`; shares the session store with the classic CLI.
- **Config / env:** `display.mouse_tracking`; `HERMES_TUI_DIR` (prebuild path).
- **Edge cases / guards:** External prebuild path documented under "### External prebuild"; light-terminal detection and busy-indicator styles are auto/configurable.
- **Rebuild notes:** Collapse the noisy banner sections by default (only Tools open), give the mouse-tracking mode a runtime switch with a `wheel` preset for multiplexers, and make every long-running overlay (`/agents`) show a killable tree with cost rollups. Handoff: the exhaustive TUI surface is the `tui` shard.

### Profiles: Running Multiple Agents  `id: docs-rest.ug-profiles`
- **Surface:** Docs / CLI / Config
- **Where:** Docs → User Guide → **"Profiles: Running Multiple Agents"** (`user-guide/profiles.md`).
- **What it does:** Documents profiles — independent agents with their own `HERMES_HOME`, config, memory, sessions, skills, and gateway — including creation, aliases, gateways, updating, deletion, completion, and sharing.
- **How it works:** Sections verbatim: "## What are profiles?"; "## Quick start"; "## Creating a profile" ("### Blank profile", "### Clone config only (`--clone`)", "### Clone everything (`--clone-all`)", "### Clone from a specific profile"); "## Using profiles" ("### Command aliases", "### The `-p` flag", "### Sticky default (`hermes profile use`)", "### Knowing where you are"); "## Profiles vs workspaces vs sandboxing"; "## Running gateways" ("### Different bot tokens", "### Safety: token locks", "### Persistent services"); "## Configuring profiles" ("### From the dashboard"); "## Updating"; "## Managing profiles" ("### Naming the default profile"); "## Deleting a profile"; "## Tab completion" (Bash and Zsh); "## How it works"; "## Sharing a profile".
- **Inputs / options:** `hermes profile create` with `--clone`, `--clone-all`, clone-from-a-specific-profile; `-p <name>`; `hermes profile use`; `hermes profile list`; `hermes profile export` / `import`; command aliases per profile. Sharing verbatim: "In chat, run `/export`, hand over the file, and they run `/import` on it"; "Install a whole agent from a git repo"; "Update later when the author ships a new version (keeps your memories + .env)".
- **Outputs / side effects:** Update output example verbatim: "→ Code updated (12 commits)"; "→ Skills synced: default (up to date), coder (+2 new), assistant (+2 new)".
- **Config / env:** `HERMES_HOME` per profile; per-profile `.env` bot tokens.
- **Edge cases / guards:** "### Safety: token locks" prevents two profiles using the same bot token; "## Profiles vs workspaces vs sandboxing" scopes what a profile is not.
- **Rebuild notes:** A profile should be nothing more than a distinct `HERMES_HOME` plus a name, with every stateful path derived from it — that makes cloning, exporting, and per-profile services fall out for free.

### Bot Mode  `id: docs-rest.ug-bot-mode`
- **Surface:** Docs / Desktop app
- **Where:** Docs → User Guide → **"Bot Mode"** (`user-guide/bot-mode.md`).
- **What it does:** Documents named specialist Bots (profiles surfaced as first-class entities in the desktop app) with avatars, routines, groups/group chats, bot-to-bot messaging, and cross-machine operation.
- **How it works:** Sections verbatim: "## The Bots pane"; "## Creating a Bot" ("### Choosing which machine it lives on (\"Create on\")"); "## Avatars"; "## Routines"; "## Groups and group chats"; "## Bot-to-bot messaging" ("### Failed turns retry safely", "### When a delivery fails: typed reasons", "### Messaging across connected machines (the Desktop relay)", "### Bot-initiated DMs across machines (`hermes peer`)"); "## Bots across machines"; "## Turning it off"; "## CLI parity".
- **Inputs / options:** CLI-parity table verbatim (In Bot Mode | From a shell): "Chat with a Bot" | `hermes -p <bot> chat`; "A Bot's files, skills, memory" | `~/.hermes/profiles/<bot>/`; "Routines" | "`hermes cron list` (jobs named `[bot:<name>] …`)"; "Create / inspect profiles" | "`hermes profile create`, `hermes profile list`". Cross-machine DMs use `hermes peer`.
- **Outputs / side effects:** Bot profiles under `~/.hermes/profiles/<bot>/`; cron jobs named `[bot:<name>] …`; group chats.
- **Config / env:** n/a
- **Edge cases / guards:** "### Failed turns retry safely"; "### When a delivery fails: typed reasons"; "## Turning it off".
- **Rebuild notes:** Bot Mode is a *presentation* over profiles + cron + a messaging lane — keeping full CLI parity (every Bot action has a shell equivalent) is what makes that safe to build on.

### Hermes Desktop page  `id: docs-rest.ug-desktop`
- **Surface:** Docs / Desktop app
- **Where:** Docs → User Guide → **"Hermes Desktop"** (`user-guide/desktop.md`).
- **What it does:** The user-facing tour of the native desktop app: install, every surface in the window, updating/uninstalling, the `hermes desktop` CLI, how it works, remote-backend connections, extension, troubleshooting, and building from source.
- **How it works:** Sections verbatim: "## Install"; "## What's in the app" — "### Chat", "### File browser", "### Artifacts", "### Windows, tabs & panes", "### Terminal", "### Git review & worktrees", "### Memory Graph", "### Quick Entry", "### Voice", "### HUD mode", "### Settings & onboarding", "### Management panes", "### Bot Mode (built in)", "### Keyboard & navigation", "### Sessions & profiles"; "## Updating"; "## Uninstalling"; "## CLI reference: `hermes desktop`"; "## How it works"; "## Connecting to a remote backend" — "### The multi-connection registry", "### On the backend (the remote machine)", "### In the app", "### Troubleshooting"; "## Extending the desktop app"; "## Troubleshooting" — "### Failed turns name the failing layer", "### \"The host key has CHANGED since you last connected\" (SSH remote)", "### \"Build desktop app\" stuck on Electron download"; "## Building from source" — "### macOS permissions and local rebuilds (TCC)"; "## See also".
- **Inputs / options:** Remote-backend setup steps transcribed verbatim from the code comments: "1. Set the dashboard login credentials."; "Recommended: a stable signing secret so sessions survive restarts. Without it a random key is generated per boot and you'll be logged out on every restart."; "2. Run the backend bound to a reachable address. The non-loopback bind engages the auth gate; the username/password provider handles login." Repair commands transcribed verbatim: "Force a clean first-launch setup (macOS/Linux)"; "Rebuild a broken Python venv (macOS/Linux)"; "Reset a stuck macOS microphone prompt".
- **Outputs / side effects:** A desktop app connected to a local or remote backend.
- **Config / env:** Dashboard credentials + signing secret on the backend; `HERMES_DESKTOP_*` variables (see `docs-rest.dev-worktree-ui`).
- **Edge cases / guards:** "### Failed turns name the failing layer" (typed error attribution); SSH host-key change warning; Electron download stall; macOS TCC permissions on local rebuilds.
- **Rebuild notes:** Bind-address determines the auth posture (loopback = open, anything else = auth gate), and a *stable* signing secret is required or every restart logs everyone out. Handoff: the exhaustive desktop UI surface is the desktop-a/desktop-b/desktop-main/desktop-settings shards.

### Hermes Docker Setup  `id: docs-rest.ug-docker`
- **Surface:** Docs / Platform:Docker
- **Where:** Docs → User Guide → **"Hermes Docker Setup"** (`user-guide/docker.md`).
- **What it does:** Documents running Hermes from the published container image — gateway mode, dashboard, interactive CLI, volumes, multi-profile supervision, logging, env forwarding, Compose, audio bridge, resource limits, upgrades, adding tools, and local inference connectivity.
- **How it works:** Sections verbatim: "## Quick start"; "## Running in gateway mode"; "## Running the dashboard"; "## Running interactively (CLI chat)"; "## Persistent volumes" + "### Immutable install tree"; "## Multi-profile support" ("### Reaching more than one profile from outside the container", "### Why one container with many profiles, not many containers", "### When you DO want a separate container"); "## Where the logs go"; "## Environment variable forwarding"; "## Docker Compose example"; "## Optional: Linux desktop audio bridge"; "## Resource limits"; "## What the Dockerfile does" ("### `docker exec` automatically drops to the `hermes` user", "### Per-profile gateway supervision"); "## Upgrading"; "## Skills and credential files"; "## Installing more tools in the container" ("### npm or Python tools — use `npx` or `uvx`", "### Other tools (apt packages, binaries) — install and remember", "### Durable installs — build a derived image", "### Complex tools or multi-service stacks — run a sidecar container", "### Broadly useful tools — open an issue or pull request"); "## Connecting to local inference servers (vLLM, Ollama, etc.)" ("### Docker Compose (recommended)", "### Standalone Docker run (no Compose)", "### Verifying connectivity", "### Ollama"); "## Troubleshooting".
- **Inputs / options:** Multi-profile commands with their verbatim comments: "Create a profile — registers the gateway-`<name>` s6 slot."; "Start / stop / restart — dispatches s6-svc; the gateway lifecycle survives docker restart."; "Status — reports `Manager: s6 (container supervisor)` inside the container."; "Remove a profile — tears down the s6 slot too."; external reachability: "Create the profile (registers its gateway-`<name>` s6 slot)"; "Point its API server at a free port (write to the profile's own .env)".
- **Outputs / side effects:** Persistent volumes; s6-supervised per-profile gateways.
- **Config / env:** Env forwarding rules; `config.yaml` base URLs for local inference.
- **Edge cases / guards:** Troubleshooting headings verbatim: "### Container exits immediately"; "### \"Permission denied\" errors"; "### \"Permission denied\" on every `docker exec` (install dir locked to 0700)"; "### Browser tools not working". Update path: Docker installs do not support `hermes update` (see `docs-rest.update-image-provenance`).
- **Rebuild notes:** One container with an in-container supervisor (s6) per profile beats one container per profile for a multi-agent product; document the tool-installation ladder (npx/uvx → apt+remember → derived image → sidecar → upstream) so users pick the durable option deliberately.

- **What it does:** Covers the native Windows install, what the installer does, the feature matrix, shell semantics, UTF-8, editor bindings, login-time gateway, data layout, and Windows-specific pitfalls.

- **Edge cases / guards:** "## Feature matrix" enumerates what is unavailable natively (the PTY-backed dashboard terminal needs a POSIX PTY); "### Why not a Windows Service?"; "## Common pitfalls".
- **Rebuild notes:** Publish a per-feature availability matrix for the platform rather than a blanket "supported" claim, and prefer a login-scoped task over a service when the agent needs a user session.

- **Surface:** Docs / Platform:Windows
- **Where:** Docs → User Guide → **"Windows (WSL2) Guide"** (`user-guide/windows-wsl-quickstart.md`).
- **What it does:** The WSL2 path: why choose it, installing WSL2 and Hermes inside it, the filesystem boundary, networking in both directions, long-term service operation, GPU passthrough, and pitfalls.
- **How it works:** Sections verbatim: "## Why WSL2 (vs. native Windows)"; "## Install WSL2" ("### Distro choice", "### Enable systemd (recommended)", "### Install Hermes inside WSL"); "## Filesystem: crossing the Windows ↔ WSL2 boundary" ("### The two directions", "### Where to put Hermes and your projects", "### Getting files back and forth", "### Line endings, BOMs, and git", "### \"Clone inside WSL or on `/mnt/c`?\""); "## Networking: WSL ↔ Windows" ("### Case 1 — Hermes in WSL talks to a service on Windows", "### Case 2 — Something on Windows (or your LAN) talks to Hermes in WSL"); "## Running Hermes services long-term on Windows" ("### Desktop shortcut for opening Hermes quickly", "### Inside WSL with systemd (recommended)", "### Making WSL itself start on Windows login"); "## GPU passthrough (local models)"; "## Common pitfalls"; "## Where to go next".
- **Inputs / options:** WSL install commands; systemd enablement; port-forwarding/mirrored-networking configuration (see also `docs-rest.providers-wsl2-networking`).
- **Outputs / side effects:** A WSL2 distro running Hermes with optional systemd services.
- **Config / env:** `.wslconfig` networking mode; systemd enablement in `/etc/wsl.conf`.
- **Edge cases / guards:** Filesystem-boundary performance and line-ending/BOM issues; two distinct networking directions with different fixes.
- **Rebuild notes:** Treat the Windows↔WSL boundary as two separate problems (filesystem and network, each with two directions) and answer each with a specific recommendation rather than general advice.

### Profile Distributions: Share a Whole Agent  `id: docs-rest.ug-profile-distributions`
- **Surface:** Docs / CLI
- **Where:** Docs → User Guide → **"Profile Distributions: Share a Whole Agent"** (`user-guide/profile-distributions.md`).
- **What it does:** Packages an entire profile (identity, skills, MCP servers, cron jobs, config) as a git repository others can install and update.
- **How it works:** Sections verbatim: "## Two ways to share a profile"; "## What this means"; "## Why git?"; "## When should you use a distribution?"; "## The lifecycle: author to installer to update"; "## For authors: publishing a distribution" — "### Step 1 — Start from a working profile", "### Step 2 — Add a `distribution.yaml`", "### Step 3 — Create a `.gitignore` before the first commit", "### Step 4 — Push to a git repo", "### Step 5 — Tag versioned releases", "### What the repo looks like", "### Distribution-owned vs user-owned"; "## For installers: using a distribution" — "### Install", "### Source types", "### Override the profile name", "### Fill in env vars".
- **Inputs / options:** `distribution.yaml` declares required env vars — verbatim: "Tell installers which env vars the agent needs. These are checked against the installer's shell and existing .env file so they don't get nagged about keys they already have configured." Source types verbatim: "GitHub shorthand"; "Full HTTPS"; "SSH"; "Self-hosted, GitLab, Gitea, Forgejo — any Git host"; "Private repo using your configured git auth"; "Local directory during development (no git push needed)". Name override example: "Alice" / "Bob (same distribution, different local name)". Env template header verbatim: "Environment variables required by this Hermes distribution. Copy to `.env` and fill in your own values before running."
- **Outputs / side effects:** An installable profile repo; a locally-named profile on install.
- **Config / env:** `distribution.yaml` (`version`, required env vars).
- **Edge cases / guards:** `.gitignore` categories transcribed verbatim as comment headers: "Credentials & secrets — NEVER commit"; "Runtime databases & state"; "User data — NEVER commit"; "Caches & generated artifacts"; "Infrastructure (should not be in profile dir, but safe to exclude)"; "User customization namespace — your local overrides"; "Checkpoints & backups (can be huge)"; "Logs". Versioning verbatim: "Edit distribution.yaml: version: 1.1.0". "### Distribution-owned vs user-owned" defines which files an update may overwrite.
- **Rebuild notes:** Use plain git as the distribution transport, declare required env vars in a manifest so installers are prompted only for what they lack, and publish an explicit distribution-owned vs user-owned split so updates never clobber local memories or `.env`.

### Connecting Desktop to Many Hermes Instances  `id: docs-rest.ug-multi-connection-desktop`
- **Surface:** Docs / Desktop app
- **Where:** Docs → User Guide → **"Connecting Desktop to Many Hermes Instances"** (`user-guide/multi-connection-desktop.md`).
- **What it does:** Documents the desktop app's gateway registry — connecting one app to several local/remote Hermes backends and working across them.
- **How it works:** Sections verbatim: "## Where to find it"; "## The gateway registry"; "## Adding a connection, step by step" + "### Migrating from the single-connection settings"; "## Agents across gateways" + "### Switching and scoping" + "### Sessions and Bot Mode"; "## Updating every instance at once"; "## Security notes"; "## For plugin authors"; "## Troubleshooting".
- **Inputs / options:** Connection entries in the registry (see `host.profileRoutes()` in the desktop SDK for the plugin-visible shape: `profile`, `targetProfile`, `connectionId`, `mode`).
- **Outputs / side effects:** Multiple simultaneous backends addressable from one app.
- **Config / env:** Per-connection endpoint/credentials, held by the app (never crossing the plugin IPC boundary).
- **Edge cases / guards:** "## Security notes"; "### Migrating from the single-connection settings"; "## Troubleshooting".
- **Rebuild notes:** Model connections as a registry with stable ids and route descriptors, so features (agents, sessions, updates, plugins) address a *route* rather than "the" backend.

### Running Many Gateways at Once  `id: docs-rest.ug-multi-profile-gateways`
- **Surface:** Docs / Gateway/Telegram
- **Where:** Docs → User Guide → **"Running Many Gateways at Once"** (`user-guide/multi-profile-gateways.md`).
- **What it does:** Operating several profile gateways on one host — as separate services, or multiplexed into one listener — plus bulk lifecycle, logs, config editing, keep-awake, token safety, updating, and troubleshooting.
- **How it works:** Sections verbatim: "## When to use this"; "## Quick start" (comments verbatim: "Create profiles (once)", "Configure each", "Install each gateway as a managed service", "Start them all"); "## Alternative: one gateway for all profiles (multiplexing)" — "### When to prefer multiplexing", "### How to opt in", "### What changes when multiplexing is on" (with the annotated example "# default profile" / "# the \"coder\" profile, same listener"), "### Serving selected profiles", "### Routing shared-bot chats to profiles (`profile_routes`)"; "## Start, stop, or restart all gateways at once" ("Add or remove profile names here as you create / delete profiles."); "## Manage one profile"; "## Service files"; "## Viewing logs" ("# Default profile" / "# Named profile"); "## Identify what's actually running"; "## Editing configuration" ("or, for everything:"); "## Keeping the host awake" — "### macOS — `caffeinate`" ("Persistent: run in background and forget", "Inspect / stop"), "### Linux — `systemd-inhibit` or `loginctl`" ("Inhibit suspend while a command runs", "Allow user services to keep running after logout (recommended)"); "## Token-conflict safety"; "## Updating the code"; "## Troubleshooting" — "### \"Could not find service in domain for user gui: 501\"", "### Stale PID after a crash", "### Forcing a hard reset of one service" ("# macOS" / "# Linux"), "### Health check".
- **Inputs / options:** `profile_routes` config for routing a shared bot's chats to profiles; per-profile service install/start/stop; bulk scripts.
- **Outputs / side effects:** systemd/launchd units per profile, or one multiplexed listener.
- **Config / env:** `profile_routes`; per-profile `.env` tokens.
- **Edge cases / guards:** "## Token-conflict safety" (scoped locks); macOS `501` domain error; stale PID recovery; forced service reset.
- **Rebuild notes:** Offer both shapes — N services (isolation) and one multiplexed listener (one bot token, many profiles) — and be explicit about what changes when multiplexing is on; `profile_routes` is the piece that makes one shared bot usable by many agents.

- **Inputs / options:** REST examples transcribed verbatim from their comments: "List authenticated providers + curated model lists"; "Read current main + auxiliary assignments"; "Set the main model"; "Override a single auxiliary task"; "Assign one model to every auxiliary task"; "Reset all auxiliary tasks to auto". Custom aliases are defined in `~/.hermes/config.yaml`.

Also "### Mid-session switches and context warnings" and "### Unattended data-training tiers".

- **How it works:** The page is the prose counterpart to the machine-readable key catalogue; other pages link into it by anchor (e.g. `/user-guide/configuration#auxiliary-models`, `#context-compression`, `#quick-commands`, `#openrouter-routing--pareto-code-for-auxiliary-tasks`).
- **Inputs / options:** Every documented `config.yaml` key and its allowed values.
- **Outputs / side effects:** n/a
- **Config / env:** All of `~/.hermes/config.yaml`; `~/.hermes/.env`.
- **Edge cases / guards:** Precedence rules (managed scope > user config > defaults; env vs config, with the managed-scope inversion documented in `docs-rest.ug-managed-scope`).
- **Rebuild notes:** Keep a single narrative configuration page anchored per section so every other doc can deep-link into it rather than restating key semantics. Handoff: the exhaustive per-key inventory (785 leaf keys with defaults, types, and consumers) is the `config-a` / `config-b` shards and the live `GET /api/config/schema`; this entry deliberately covers only the page's role and structure.

## Reference (misc) and site entry pages

### FAQ & Troubleshooting  `id: docs-rest.ref-faq`
- **Surface:** Docs
- **Where:** Docs → Reference → **"FAQ & Troubleshooting"** (`reference/faq.md`, `sidebar_position: 3`).
- **What it does:** The catch-all Q&A and fix list: provider questions, privacy, cost, local models, installation/provider/terminal/messaging/performance/MCP troubleshooting, profiles, and named workflow patterns.
- **How it works:** All headings verbatim. **"## Frequently Asked Questions"** — "### What LLM providers work with Hermes?"; "### Does it work on Windows/Android/Termux/my plataform??"; "### I run Hermes in WSL2. What's the best way to control my normal Windows Chrome?"; "### Is my data sent anywhere?"; "### Can I use it offline / with local models?"; "### How much does it cost?"; "### Can multiple people use one instance?"; "### What's the difference between memory and skills?"; "### Can I use it in my own Python project?". **"## Troubleshooting"** — "### Installation Issues"; "### Provider & Model Issues"; "### Terminal Issues"; "### Messaging Issues"; "### Performance Issues"; "### MCP Issues". **"## Profiles"** — "### How do profiles differ from just setting HERMES_HOME?"; "### Can two profiles share the same bot token?"; "### Do profiles share memory or sessions?"; "### What happens when I run `hermes update`?"; "### How many profiles can I run?". **"## Workflows & Patterns"** — "### Using different models for different tasks (multi-model workflows)"; "### Running multiple agents on one WhatsApp number (per-chat binding)"; "### Controlling what shows up in Telegram (hiding logs and reasoning)"; "### Managing skills on Telegram (slash command limit)"; "### Shared thread sessions (multiple users, one conversation)"; "### Exporting Hermes to another machine"; "### Moving a single profile to another machine"; "### `hermes backup` vs `hermes profile export`"; "### Permission denied when reloading shell after install"; "### Error 400 on first agent run". Plus **"## Still Stuck?"**.
- **Inputs / options:** Provider list verbatim: "**OpenRouter** — access hundreds of models through one API key (recommended for flexibility); **Nous Portal** — Nous Research's subscription gateway — 300+ models plus web/image/TTS/browser through one OAuth login (recommended for newcomers); **OpenAI** — GPT-5.4, GPT-5-codex, GPT-4.1, GPT-4o, etc.; **Anthropic** — Claude models (direct API, OAuth via `hermes auth add anthropic`, OpenRouter, or any compatible proxy); **Google** — Gemini models (direct API via `gemini` provider, OpenRouter, or compatible proxy); **z.ai / ZhipuAI** — GLM models; **Kimi / Moonshot AI** — Kimi models; **MiniMax** — global and China endpoints; **Local models** — via Ollama, vLLM, llama.cpp, SGLang, or any OpenAI-compatible server." Local-model wizard answers verbatim: "Select: Custom endpoint (enter URL manually)"; "API base URL: `http://localhost:11434/v1`"; "API key: `ollama`"; "Model name: `qwen3.5:27b`"; "Context length: `64000` ← Hermes minimum; set this to match your server's actual context window". Migration commands verbatim: `hermes backup` (→ `~/hermes-backup-<timestamp>.zip`), `hermes import ~/hermes-backup-<timestamp>.zip`, `hermes profile export work ./work-backup.tar.gz`, `hermes profile import ./work-backup.tar.gz work`; manual fallback `rsync -av --exclude='hermes-agent' ~/.hermes/ newmachine:~/.hermes/`. Diagnostics named across the troubleshooting sections: `hermes prompt-size` ("See exactly what the fixed prompt costs — breakdown by block (system prompt, skills index, memory, tool schemas). Runs offline."), `/compress`, `hermes config show`, `hermes model`, `hermes gateway status`, `tmux`/`nohup` gateway options ("Option 1: Direct foreground (simplest)", "Option 2: Persistent via tmux (survives terminal close)", "Option 3: Background via nohup").
- **Outputs / side effects:** n/a
- **Config / env:** `~/.hermes/.env`, `~/.hermes/config.yaml`.
- **Edge cases / guards:** Privacy answer verbatim: "API calls go **only to the LLM provider you configure**… Hermes Agent does not collect telemetry, usage data, or analytics. Your conversations, memory, and skills are stored locally in `~/.hermes/`." WSL2 browser answer verbatim: "Prefer an MCP bridge over `/browser connect`… run Hermes inside WSL2; keep using your normal signed-in Chrome on Windows; add `chrome-devtools-mcp` as an MCP server through `cmd.exe` or `powershell.exe`; let Hermes use the resulting MCP browser tools. This is more reliable than trying to force Hermes core browser transport to attach directly across the WSL2/Windows boundary." Backup comparison table verbatim (Feature | `hermes backup` | `hermes profile export`): "**Use Case**" "**Full machine migration**" "**Porting/sharing a specific profile**"; "**Scope**" "Global (entire `~/.hermes` directory)" "Local (single profile directory)"; "**Includes**" "All profiles, global config, API keys, sessions" "Single profile: SOUL.md, memories, sessions, skills"; "**Credentials**" "**Included** (`.env` and `auth.json`)" "**Excluded** (stripped for safe sharing)"; "**Format**" "`.zip`" "`.tar.gz`". Backup consistency verbatim: "`hermes backup` produces a consistent snapshot even while Hermes is actively running. The restored archive excludes machine-local runtime files like `gateway.pid` and `cron.pid`." Sudo-install cleanup verbatim: "Don't use sudo with the installer — it installs to `~/.local/bin`. If you previously installed with sudo, clean up: … Then re-run the standard installer." Error-400 cause verbatim: "Usually a model name mismatch — the configured model doesn't exist on your provider, or the API key doesn't have access to it… If using OpenRouter, make sure your API key has credits."
- **Rebuild notes:** Keep migration semantics in one comparison table (scope, contents, credentials, format) — "which command do I use to move my setup" is the single most repeated support question, and the credentials-included/excluded distinction is the dangerous half.

### CLI Symbols Glossary  `id: docs-rest.ref-cli-symbols`
- **Surface:** Docs / CLI / TUI
- **Where:** Docs → Reference → **"CLI Symbols Glossary"** (`reference/cli-symbols.md`).
- **What it does:** Decodes every glyph the terminal interfaces render, grouped by surface.
- **How it works:** **"## Transcript symbols"** verbatim (Symbol | Meaning): `❯` "Input prompt — where you type. *Themeable* (skins set their own prompt symbol)."; `●` "A tool call. The bullet precedes the tool name and its arguments."; `┊` "Tool-activity rail shown with tool lines. *Themeable* (`tool_prefix`)."; "`✓` / `✗`" "Tool result: succeeded / failed. Appended to the end of a completed tool line."; "`▸` / `▾`" "Collapsed / expanded section chevron (thinking, tools, subagents, banner sections). Click to toggle."; `▍` "Streaming cursor — blinks while the model is still emitting text."; "`│` `├─` `└─`" "Tree rails — connect a parent (a delegation, a journey) to its child entries; `└─` marks the last child."; `◈` "Display-only timeline event (session notices rendered inline, not user messages)."; `◇` "An injected reference block, e.g. `◇ Reference 1/2 — <label>`."; `↳` "The sticky prompt — echoes the user message the agent is currently working on."; "`☐` / `☑` / `•`" "Markdown task-list items (open / done) and plain list bullets."; `▶` "Collapsed `<details>` summary inside rendered markdown."
  **"## Status-bar symbols"** verbatim: "`⠋⠙⠹…` (braille patterns)" "Busy spinner. Thinking and tool phases use different braille animation sets."; "`⚕ 🌀 🤔 ✨ 🍵 🔮`" "Frames of the `emoji` busy-indicator style (`/indicator emoji`). The default style rotates kaomoji faces instead."; "`| / - \\`" "Frames of the `ascii` busy-indicator style."; `⏱` "Per-prompt elapsed time while the turn runs, e.g. `⏱ 12s/3m 45s` (turn time / session time)."; `⏲` "The same timer, frozen after the turn completes."; `cmp N` "The session has been auto-compressed N times."; `▶ N` "N `/bg` tasks currently running."; `⚠ YOLO` "YOLO mode is on (auto-approval). Also shown in the startup banner."; `⛓ N` "N subagents currently active."; `↩ resumes when subagent finishes` "Reassurance shown while you are idle but delegated work is still in flight — the result returns on its own."; `● REC` "Voice mode is recording."; `◉ STT` "Voice recording stopped; speech-to-text is transcribing."; `◉ focus` "Focus view is on (reduced output). Pinned so it never drops off a narrow terminal."; `♥` "Affection flash — Hermes noticed you being nice to it."; "`⚡` / `🔋`" "Battery indicator (opt-in): plugged in / on battery, with percentage."; `N bg` "N background terminal processes tracked in this session."; `N live sessions` "Open TUI sessions in this process — click to open the session switcher."
  **"## Notices"** verbatim: `✓` "Success notice."; `•` "Informational notice."; `⚠` "Warning (also used for credit warnings)."; `✕` "Error notice."
  **"## Approval and confirmation prompts"** verbatim: `⚠ approval required` "A tool wants to run something that needs your explicit yes (bordered panel with the command preview)."; "`⚠` / `?`" "Confirmation dialog title: dangerous action / ordinary question."; `🔐` "Sudo password prompt (input is masked)."; `🔑` "Credential/secret input prompt (input is masked)."
  **"## Subagents overlay (`/agents`)"** verbatim: `●` "Subagent running."; `○` "Queued."; `✓` "Completed."; `■` "Interrupted."; `✗` "Failed."; `⌛` "Timed out."; `⚠` "Errored."; `⚡N` "N currently-active agents in a rollup row."; `▁▂▃▄▅▆▇█` "Activity sparkline — recent event volume per branch."
  **"## Session switcher (`Ctrl+X`)"** verbatim: `✓` "Session idle."; `…` "Starting."; `?` "Waiting for input."; `▶` "Working."; `✎ draft` "The composer in that session holds an unsent draft."
  **"## Pickers and hubs"** verbatim: `▸` "Current selection row (model picker and friends)."; `*` "The currently-active model / provider."; "`●` / `○`" "Provider authenticated / not authenticated (model picker); plugin state fallback (plugins hub)."; "`✓` / `✗`" "Plugin enabled / disabled (plugins hub)."; "`↑ N more` / `↓ N more`" "More rows above/below the visible window of a list."; `┃` "Scrollbar thumb on scrollable overlays."
  **"## Goals"** verbatim: `✓` "Goal complete."; `↻` "Goal continuing — another iteration was scheduled."; `⏸` "Goal paused."
- **Inputs / options:** `/indicator emoji`, `Ctrl+X` (session switcher), `/agents` overlay.
- **Outputs / side effects:** n/a
- **Config / env:** Skins can override themeable glyphs (`tool_prefix`, prompt symbol).
- **Edge cases / guards:** Note verbatim: "Glyphs marked *themeable* below are brand defaults — a skin can override them (for example `tool_prefix` and the prompt symbol). Everything else is fixed in the renderer." Status-bar segments "appear only when relevant and drop off first on narrow terminals" (except `◉ focus`, which is pinned).
- **Rebuild notes:** A dense glyph language needs a published decoder page, and every glyph should carry a one-line meaning plus whether it is themeable — otherwise skins silently break the user's mental model.

  Key Features bullets, all eleven verbatim: "**A closed learning loop** — Agent-curated memory with periodic nudges, autonomous skill creation, skill self-improvement during use, FTS5 cross-session recall with LLM summarization, and [Honcho](https://github.com/plastic-labs/honcho) dialectic user modeling"; "**Runs anywhere, not just your laptop** — 6 terminal backends: local, Docker, SSH, Daytona, Singularity, Modal. ### User Stories & Use Cases page  `id: docs-rest.site-user-stories`
- **Surface:** Docs
- **Where:** Docs site → **"User Stories & Use Cases"** (`website/docs/user-stories.mdx`).
- **What it does:** Renders a collage of real community stories about what people build with Hermes.
- **How it works:** The page is almost entirely a component mount — frontmatter verbatim: `title: User Stories & Use Cases`; `description: Real stories from the Hermes Agent community — what people are actually building, scraped from X, GitHub, Reddit, Hacker News, YouTube, blogs, and podcasts.`; `hide_title: true`; `hide_table_of_contents: true`. Body verbatim: `import UserStoriesCollage from '@site/src/components/UserStoriesCollage';` followed by `<UserStoriesCollage />`.
- **Inputs / options:** None on the page itself; all content comes from the `UserStoriesCollage` React component under `website/src/components/`.
- **Outputs / side effects:** n/a
- **Config / env:** n/a
- **Edge cases / guards:** `hide_title` and `hide_table_of_contents` are set so the collage owns the whole viewport.
- **Rebuild notes:** Keep social proof in a data-driven component rather than hand-maintained markdown, so the page updates without doc edits. Handoff: the collage component's own data source lives under `website/src/components/UserStoriesCollage` and is outside this shard.

- `website/docs/user-guide/features/**` (tools, skills, memory, memory-providers, cron, delegation, hooks, browser, voice-mode, tts, mcp, acp, api-server, tool-gateway, subscription-proxy, provider-routing, fallback-providers, plugins, extending-the-dashboard, web-dashboard, personality, context-files, code-execution, batch-processing, image-generation, web-search, computer-use, goals, skins) — docs-features shard.
- `website/docs/user-guide/messaging/**` (28 platform pages incl. telegram, discord, slack, whatsapp, signal, matrix, mattermost, email, sms, dingtalk, feishu, wecom, wecom-callback, weixin, bluebubbles, buzz, qqbot, yuanbao, homeassistant, teams, teams-meetings, msgraph-webhook, google_chat, line, ntfy, simplex, open-webui, webhooks) — platform shards.

- `website/docs/reference/cli-commands.md`, `slash-commands.md`, `tools-reference.md`, `toolsets-reference.md`, `model-catalog.md`, `skills-catalog.md`, `optional-skills-catalog.md`, `automation-blueprints-catalog.mdx`, `mcp-config-reference.md`, `profile-commands.md`, `environment-variables.md` — cli / tools / config / env shards.

- `website/src/components/UserStoriesCollage` (the React component and its data source behind `user-stories.mdx`) — web/site shard, outside the docs tree.
